# cell-fidelity research log

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 7t outcome: the plating reaction throttles itself, and that is what bends the curve. Late plating per cycle (cycles 90 to 99) over early (2 to 10) falls from 0.555 to 0.297 on `Mohtat2020` and 0.510 to 0.239 on `Ecker2015` as the multiplier goes from 10 to 30. Within each tuned cell that runs fast enough to drift, designs that throttle more drift more (Spearman of T with m +0.83 to +0.88). Across all eight set-conditions, the median throttle orders the median drift (&rho; = &minus;0.81, p = 0.024). The faster cells need more than a longer look: at no window up to 50 cycles does the f = 30 median error get back to f = 10's ten-cycle error. A look scaled to the share of fade observed would stop at about 8 cycles, which is worse. H326, H327 and H330 supported, H328 inconclusive, H329 refuted
- Data: 288 designs (16 on each committed set; 56 on each tuned cell and multiplier, every design in `rows.json` that finished 100 cycles). The plan said 50 per tuned cell (the pool count) and 164 in all, but `rows.json` holds all 56; disclosed, not changed.
- H326 supported: within every set-condition, Spearman(|m &minus; 1|, E10) is +0.79 to +0.99 (all permutation p &lt; 0.001). The ten-cycle error is the R-rate drift, not the transform.
- H327 supported: median T at f = 10 / f = 30 is 0.555 / 0.297 (`Mohtat2020`) and 0.510 / 0.239 (`Ecker2015`). At both multipliers plating is 99.6 to 99.9% of the lithium lost.
- H328 inconclusive: &rho;(T, m) is +0.88 (`Ecker2015` f = 10), +0.87 (`Ecker2015` f = 30) and +0.83 (`Mohtat2020` f = 30), all p &lt; 0.001, but +0.09 (p = 0.51) on `Mohtat2020` at f = 10, where the median m is 1.006 and there is little drift to explain. Not refuted (all above 0).
- H329 refuted: the f = 30 median E<sub>k</sub> at k = 10, 15, 20, 30, 40, 50 is 0.105, 0.111, 0.107, 0.087, 0.074, 0.061 on `Mohtat2020` (target 0.043) and 0.243, 0.238, 0.224, 0.191, 0.158, 0.131 on `Ecker2015` (target 0.087). Neither cell reaches its target by cycle 50. At f = 10 the error is flat in k (0.040 to 0.052 and 0.076 to 0.095): that is the law's floor, not a short look. A rule that scales the look by the share of fade observed would stop at 7.9 and 7.4 cycles on the f = 30 cells, because a cell that throttles has already seen 15 to 17% of its 99-cycle loss by cycle 10 (f = 10: 11 to 12%).
- H330 supported: across the eight set-conditions, &rho;(median T, median |m &minus; 1|) = &minus;0.81, permutation p = 0.024. `NCA_Kim2011` throttles hardest (T 0.037, m 0.820). `Ai2020` is the outlier in mechanism: plating is 2.9% of its loss, so its T describes a small term, and its drift (m 0.996 here) is SEI's.
- Reading: 7s's question has an answer. A faster plating reaction makes a cell lose lithium faster early, and plating slows within 100 cycles (T well below 1). A likely cause, not tested here, is that the early loss pulls the negative electrode's end-of-charge stoichiometry down and so lowers plating's driving force. The law's R rate then falls, m drops below 1 and a ten-cycle line overshoots. The bend continues through cycle 99, so a longer straight line only chases it. For the design-search recipe: on cells whose plating throttles, the fix is a curved term whose bend comes from an early observable of the throttle, not more cycles. 7b's borrowed-gamma curve failed on LG M50 because the bend there was &plusmn;5 to 10% in either direction; here it is one-signed and ordered by T. Post hoc check for the next step, not run: whether T's early analogue (plating per cycle at cycles 6 to 10 over 2 to 6) predicts m out of pool.
- Caveats: T, &phi; and s come from PyBaMM's plating and SEI submodels at one version (26.10.1.1), so the throttle is a property of the simulated reaction, not a measured cell. T uses cycles 90 to 99, so it is a diagnosis, not a forecast input. m and E are on the law's R scale and T on lithium, so H326 and H328 link two views of one trajectory. Eight set-conditions for H330, two of which are the same chemistry at two rates.
- Next: (a) an early throttle observable (plating per cycle, cycles 6 to 10 over 2 to 6) as the bend of a curved ten-cycle forecast, fitted leave-one-pool-out on the tuned cells and scored against 7c's correction; (b) the paper's section 5.3 now says why the tuned cells' forecast degrades (done in this step).
- Output: `experiments/bend7t/results.txt`, `summary.json`; tests `tests/test_bend7t.py` (8).

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 7t (plan): why does the forecast error grow with the plating rate? 7s found the cycle-10 forecast error of the tuned cells rises from f = 10 to f = 30 (median E10 0.045 to 0.105 on `Mohtat2020`, 0.098 to 0.249 on `Ecker2015`) while the drift multiplier m moves off 1 (median 1.003 to 0.806, 0.917 to 0.585), and asked whether the ten-cycle look is too short once fade is fast. This step asks what bends the curve, and how long a look the faster cells need. No new simulation. (plan, written and committed before `report` ran; H326 to H330 are this entry's)
- Data, fixed now (`experiments/bend7t/run.py`, stdlib): every design in `rows.json` of fresh7h (4 committed sets, 16 designs each), plating7r (f = 10) and plating7s (f = 30) (2 cells, 50 each): eight set-conditions, 164 designs. Per design, from `rows.json`: m and E<sub>k</sub> (cell 000, k = 10, 15, 20, 30, 40, 50); from `bench.jsonl`, the cumulative lithium lost L<sub>n</sub> and lost to plating P<sub>n</sub> at the end of cycle n.
- Quantities, fixed now: plating throttle T = [(P<sub>99</sub> &minus; P<sub>90</sub>)/9] / [(P<sub>10</sub> &minus; P<sub>2</sub>)/8], late plating per cycle over early (1 = steady, below 1 = plating slows itself); plating share &phi; = (P<sub>99</sub> &minus; P<sub>2</sub>)/(L<sub>99</sub> &minus; L<sub>2</sub>); observed share s<sub>k</sub> = (L<sub>k</sub> &minus; L<sub>2</sub>)/(L<sub>99</sub> &minus; L<sub>2</sub>). Spearman p values by 20,000 permutations, seed 7201.
- Seen before the plan (disclosed): the 7r and 7s summaries (median E10, q75 E10 and E50, median m per cell); the keys of `bench.jsonl` and that `pl_mol` and `lost_mol` are cumulative end-of-cycle PyBaMM outputs (from `depth/run.py`). No T, &phi; or s<sub>k</sub> has been computed.
- H326 (the error is the drift; a check of the reading): within each of the eight set-conditions, Spearman(|m &minus; 1|, E10) &ge; 0.7. Refuted if below 0.3 on any.
- H327 (plating throttles itself more at the higher rate): median T is lower at f = 30 than at f = 10 on both cells. Refuted if higher on both.
- H328 (the throttle sets each design's drift): within each of the four tuned set-conditions, Spearman(T, m) &ge; 0.5. Refuted if at or below 0 on any.
- H329 (the faster cells need a look about as much longer as they fade faster): on each cell, the shortest window k at which the f = 30 median E<sub>k</sub> is at most the f = 10 median E10 (linear between windows) is at most 25 cycles (10 times the fade ratio of about 2.5). Refuted if no window up to 50 reaches it on either cell. Reported beside it: the f = 30 window whose median s<sub>k</sub> matches f = 10's median s<sub>10</sub>, the look a share-scaled rule would choose.
- H330 (the same mechanism across chemistries): across the eight set-conditions, Spearman(median T, median |m &minus; 1|) &le; &minus;0.5. Refuted if at or above 0.
- Risks named now: L and P are PyBaMM outputs of one model and version (26.10.1.1), so a throttle is a property of the simulated plating law, not a measured cell; m and E are on the law's R scale, T and s on lithium; eight set-conditions is a small sample for H330, and the committed sets have 16 designs each against 50 on the tuned cells; H326 is close to definitional (m is the R-rate drift the forecast ignores), so it is a check that the error is the drift and not the transform.

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 7s outcome: 7r's across-chemistry interval does not survive the other passing multiplier. With f = 30 instead of 10 on `Mohtat2020` and `Ecker2015`, the threshold rule is dearer than a uniform run length on both tuned cells (+7.50 and +3.50 cycles per design; 7 of 10 folds dearer, 1 cheaper), and the six-set interval of theta minus U goes back across zero: &minus;1.17 cycles per design, set bootstrap &minus;6.19 to +3.75 (f = 10: &minus;3.73, &minus;6.94 to &minus;0.57). The rule now meets its target on 9 of 10 new folds (f = 10: 5 of 10), so the two multipliers trade a saving for a miss rather than showing a saving at matched reliability. H322 inconclusive, H323 inconclusive, H324 refuted, H325 supported
- Bench: 112 of 112 designs finished 100 cycles (median 16.5 s per design, about 8 minutes on 4 workers). Pools 10, 8, 8, 8, 16 of designs 0 to 49 on each cell, as in 7r.
- H322 inconclusive: cycle-10 forecast error q75 0.132 on `Mohtat2020` (bar met) and 0.294 on `Ecker2015` (over the 0.2 bar, far under the refutation at 1); medians 0.105 and 0.249, both above f = 10's (0.045, 0.098). The drift multiplier moves off 1 (median m 0.806 and 0.585; f = 10: 1.003 and 0.917), and 7c's correction switches from O1 to O3 on both cells (|&rho;| 0.54 and 0.53 on pools 1 to 3).
- H323 inconclusive: 30 paired folds, mean &minus;1.17, set bootstrap &minus;6.19 to +3.75; sign test 15 cheaper, 11 dearer, p = 0.557. Not refuted, since the mean is still below zero. theta50: &minus;0.83, &minus;6.03 to +4.25.
- H324 refuted: the two cells' 10-fold mean of theta minus U is +5.50 (bootstrap over the two sets +3.50 to +7.50). U's nested margin picks 40 cycles on every fold of both cells and meets all 10. theta runs to 47.5 to 50 cycles on 7 of 10 folds; it is cheaper only on `Ecker2015` pool 1 (30 cycles), and misses the target on that fold.
- H325 supported: theta meets 5 of 5 folds on `Mohtat2020` (U 5 of 5) and 4 of 5 on `Ecker2015` (U 5 of 5).
- Reading: at f = 10 the rule was cheaper on the tuned cells partly by missing (Mohtat2020 2 of 5); at f = 30, where both cells fade about 2.5 times as fast, the forecast is noisier, the rule flags most designs as risky and runs them to 50 cycles, and a uniform 40 cycles meets the target on every fold. So whether the tuned cells help or hurt the across-chemistry claim depends on a plating rate the programme chose. The honest statement for the paper is the four-set one (&minus;4.50, &minus;8.50 to +0.25, spanning zero), with the tuned-cell results as a sensitivity range: six-set interval &minus;6.94 to &minus;0.57 at f = 10 and &minus;6.19 to +3.75 at f = 30. 7r's "first interval that excludes zero" is withdrawn as a headline.
- Caveats: two multipliers are two points; the 20 committed folds are shared with 7r, so the two six-set intervals are not independent; the bootstrap over the two new sets alone has two clusters, so its interval (+3.50 to +7.50) is descriptive only; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1).
- Next: (a) why the forecast error grows with the plating rate (is the ten-cycle look too short once fade is fast, and would the rule's look length need to scale with fade?); (b) the paper's results and abstract to state the four-set interval with the f = 10 and f = 30 sensitivity range (done in `paper/discussion.md` in this step).
- Output: `experiments/plating7s/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `choose.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `rows.json`; tests `tests/test_plating7s.py` (5).

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 7s (plan): is 7r's six-set interval a property of one plating multiplier? 7r kept f = 10 on `Mohtat2020@3x` and `Ecker2015@2x` because it was the smallest multiplier that passed the probe rule, and with those two tuned cells the set-bootstrap interval of theta minus U fell below zero for the first time (&minus;6.94 to &minus;0.57). f = 30 also passed the probe on both cells (median fade 0.107 and 0.120, inside the committed sets' 4.8 to 17%) and was not benched. If the claim is about the rule, and not about where the plating rate happened to be set, it should survive the other passing multiplier. (plan, written and committed before `bench` ran; H322 to H325 are this entry's)
- Lever, fixed now (`experiments/plating7s/run.py`): 7r's code unchanged, with the multiplier fixed at f = 30 on both cells instead of being chosen by the probe rule. Bench designs 0 to 55 to cycle 100, then 7r's `rows` and `report` (7p's arithmetic: pools 10, 8, 8, 8, 16 of the first 50 that finish; 7c's correction; 7h's `refit` lopo5 for theta, theta50 and U; pairing with 7h's 20 committed folds; set-clustered bootstrap, 20,000 draws, seed 7101). No new rule and no new parameter.
- Seen before the plan (disclosed): only 7r's probe rows at f = 30 (designs 0 to 3, 20 cycles). No f = 30 run past cycle 20 has been made.
- H322 (the forecast law works at f = 30): the cycle-10 forecast error q75 is at most 0.2 on both cells. Refuted if above 1 on either.
- H323 (the interval survives the other multiplier): with the f = 30 cells' 10 lopo5 folds added to 7h's 20, the set-bootstrap 95% interval of theta minus U lies below zero. Refuted if the six-set mean is zero or above.
- H324 (the tuned cells still favour the rule): the mean of theta minus U over the two cells' 10 folds is negative (f = 10: &minus;2.20). Refuted if positive.
- H325 (the target is met at f = 30): theta meets the tail target on at least 4 of 5 lopo5 folds on each cell. Refuted if 2 or fewer on either. (At f = 10 Mohtat2020 met 2 of 5; a faster-fading cell may give the nested margin more signal per pool.)
- Risks named now: two multipliers are still two points, so passing here shows the claim is not tied to f = 10, not that it holds for every plating rate; the 20 committed folds are shared with 7r, so the two six-set intervals are not independent; six clusters for the bootstrap; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); if fewer than 50 designs finish on a cell, 7p's pool rule leaves the last pool short, as in 7p.

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 7r outcome: a tenfold faster plating reaction makes `Mohtat2020` and `Ecker2015` fade like the committed sets (4.2% and 5.0% of n<sub>Li</sub> lost from cycle 2 to 20), the forecast law works on both (cycle-10 error q75 0.074 and 0.201, committed sets 0.06 to 0.12), and with them as a fifth and sixth set the across-chemistry interval of theta minus U moves below zero for the first time: &minus;3.73 cycles per design, set bootstrap &minus;6.94 to &minus;0.57 (four sets: &minus;8.50 to +0.25). But on `Mohtat2020` the threshold rule meets its tail target on only 2 of 5 folds (the uniform rule on 3), so part of that saving is a miss. H317, H318 and H320 supported, H319 inconclusive, H321 refuted
- Control (`control.json`): with f = 1 the wrapper reproduces 7p's probe rows on all 8 probe designs exactly (max relative difference in lithium lost, every cycle, 0.0).
- H317 supported: f = 10 is the smallest multiplier that passes on both cells (median fade 0.0423 and 0.0503); f = 30 also passes (0.107, 0.120) and f = 100 overshoots the 20% cap (0.237, 0.225). All 32 probe runs finished and plated.
- H318 supported: fade rises strictly with f on both cells; log-log slope from f = 1 to 100 is 0.85 (Mohtat2020) and 0.81 (Ecker2015), so lithium loss is a little less than proportional to the plating rate.
- Bench: 112 of 112 designs finished 100 cycles (about 12 minutes on 3 workers), against 45 of 56 on `Ramadass2004`. Pools 10, 8, 8, 8, 16 of designs 0 to 49 on each cell.
- H319 inconclusive: q75 E10 0.074 on `Mohtat2020` (bar 0.2 met) and 0.201 on `Ecker2015` (just over the bar, far under the refutation at 1); medians 0.045 and 0.098. The drift multiplier is near 1 (median m 1.003 and 0.917), unlike `Ramadass2004` (median 74), and 7c's correction picks O1 on both (|&rho;| 0.51 and 0.38 on pools 1 to 3).
- H320 supported: 30 paired folds (20 committed, 10 new), theta minus U mean &minus;3.73 cycles per design, set bootstrap 95% &minus;6.94 to &minus;0.57; sign test 17 cheaper, 6 dearer, p = 0.035. Per set: Ai2020 &minus;7.00, Chen2020 +2.67, Ecker2015 &minus;3.40, Mohtat2020 &minus;1.00, NCA_Kim2011 &minus;10.00, OKane2022 &minus;3.67. theta50 stays across zero (&minus;6.84 to +3.96).
- H321 refuted: theta meets the target on 5 of 5 folds on `Ecker2015` (42.6 cycles against U's 46.0, also 5 of 5) but on 2 of 5 on `Mohtat2020` (23.0 cycles; U 3 of 5 at 24.0). Both rules miss pools 2 and 3 there; theta also misses pool 4, where it stops at 45 cycles and U at 50 meets. Over all 30 folds theta meets 26 and U 27, so one of the 30 paired savings (Mohtat2020 pool 4, &minus;5 cycles) is bought with a miss.
- Reading: the paper's across-chemistry claim now has an interval that excludes zero, on six sets, two of them with a tuned plating rate. That is the honest form of the claim: the rule is cheaper on average across six electrode chemistries at matched fade, it meets its target about as often as a uniform run length (26 against 27 of 30 folds), and on the lowest-error cell (Mohtat2020, q75 E10 0.074) neither rule's nested margin transfers between pools reliably. Not shown: that the saving holds on cells whose plating rate was not chosen to make them fade.
- Caveats: f = 10 was chosen by the probe rule, so the two new sets are tuned cells, not published parameterisations; four probe designs set the choice; six clusters for the bootstrap; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); the design-0 check at f = 10 was seen before the plan (disclosed there).
- Next: (a) why the nested margin fails on Mohtat2020 pools 2 and 3 for both rules (a low-error cell where the 75th percentile is set by one or two designs per pool); (b) the f = 30 cells as a robustness check of H320 (same code, no new rule), so the interval is not a property of one multiplier; (c) write the six-set interval and its caveat into `paper/results.md` and the abstract.
- Output: `experiments/plating7r/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `choose.json`, `control.json`, `probe.jsonl`, `bench.jsonl`, `rows.json`; tests `tests/test_plating7r.py` (5).

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 7r (plan): a pre-registered sweep of the plating reaction rate on `Mohtat2020` and `Ecker2015`. 7p found these cells barely fade under the programme's protocol (0.48% and 0.53% of n<sub>Li</sub> lost from cycle 2 to 20, against 4.8 to 17% on the four committed sets) and 7q excluded the one cell that did. Plating is 96 to 99.9% of what they lose. If the plating exchange-current density alone is scaled until they fade like the committed sets, does the forecast law hold on them, and do they move the across-chemistry interval off zero? (plan, written and committed before `probe` ran; H317 to H321 are this entry's)
- Correction to 7p's caveat, found while writing this plan: the protocol copies only the degradation keys a set lacks from OKane2022 (`transfer/run.py` `params`). `Mohtat2020` ships its own constant plating exchange-current density, 1e-3 A/m<sup>2</sup>; `Ecker2015` ships OKane2020's F k c<sub>e</sub> with k = 1e-10 m/s (OKane2022's k is 1e-9). Both ship their own SEI parameters, equal to OKane2022's in the keys checked (solvent diffusivity 2.5e-22 m<sup>2</sup>/s, initial thickness 5 nm). So "OKane2022's degradation parameters on every set" was not right for these two cells.
- Lever, fixed now (`experiments/plating7r/run.py`): the plating exchange-current density, whatever form the cell ships, multiplied by f in (3, 10, 30, 100), nothing else changed; 5zk's x-resolved DFN (SEI + plating) child run unchanged through a wrapper. Conditions fixed now: 7p's lowest-current condition of each cell, `Mohtat2020@3x` and `Ecker2015@2x`. Values of f above 1 take each cell outside its published parameterisation: the result is a sweep over a plating rate, reported as such, and a kept cell is "this chemistry's OCPs and transport with a faster plating reaction", not a measured Mohtat or Ecker cell.
- Checked before the plan (disclosed): with f = 1 the wrapper reproduces 7p's probe row for design 0 of both cells bit for bit (lithium lost at cycle 20, 0.0049654 and 4.9796e-5 mol); on design 0 alone, f = 10 gave 7.9% (Mohtat2020) and 1.8% (Ecker2015) lost from cycle 2 to 20, against 0.93% and 0.18% at f = 1. No other run was looked at.
- Probe and choice, fixed now (`probe`, `choose`): designs 0 to 3 of 7p's pool (Sobol seed 41), 20 cycles; per cell the smallest f whose four designs all finish, all plate in cycles 2 and 10, and have a median loss from cycle 2 to 20 in [3%, 20%] of n<sub>Li</sub> (7p's rule, with an upper bound at the committed sets' range rounded up). At most one f per cell.
- Bench and report, fixed now: per kept cell, designs 0 to 55 to cycle 100, then 7p's `rows` and its report arithmetic unchanged (pools 10, 8, 8, 8, 16 of the first 50 that finish; 7c's correction; 7h's `refit` lopo5 for theta, theta50 and U; pairing with 7h's 20 committed folds; set-clustered bootstrap, 20,000 draws, seed 7101). The balance and OCP tables are 7p's, since they do not depend on the plating rate.
- H317 (a faster plating reaction makes each cell fade like the committed sets): on both cells some f passes the probe rule. Refuted if neither cell passes at any f.
- H318 (fade is close to proportional to the plating rate): with 7p's f = 1 median as the first point, the median probe fade rises strictly with f on both cells and the log-log slope from f = 1 to f = 100 lies in [0.5, 1.5]. Refuted if it is not rising on either cell.
- H319 (the forecast law works on the tuned cells): the cycle-10 forecast error q75 is at most 0.2 on every kept cell (committed sets 0.06 to 0.12). Refuted if above 1 on any.
- H320 (the interval moves off zero): with the kept cells' lopo5 folds added to 7h's 20, the set-bootstrap 95% interval of theta minus U lies below zero. Refuted if the all-set mean is zero or above.
- H321 (the rule meets its target on the tuned cells): theta meets the tail target on at least 4 of 5 lopo5 folds on every kept cell. Refuted if 2 or fewer on any.
- Risks named now: a tuned plating rate is a free parameter chosen to make the cells fade, so a working rule on them shows breadth over electrode chemistry at matched fade, not over real cells; the fade band of the probe is four designs at 20 cycles; six sets (four committed plus two) is still a small cluster count; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); if only one cell is kept, H320 has five clusters, as in 7q.

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 7q outcome: PyBaMM's `Ramadass2004` cathode fit has three poles inside 0 < y < 1 (at y = 0.2453, 0.3740, 0.8888), put there by its `stretch = 1.13`, and no table repair makes the forecast law work on it. The DFN cells do not stop at the pole: cycle 2's charge ends anywhere from y = 0.61 to 0.97 (median 0.884, just under the 0.8888 pole). Reading the law through the running-maximum envelope cuts the cycle-10 forecast error q75 from 12.6 to 0.90, still eight times the committed sets' 0.06 to 0.12, with a drift multiplier of median 74 where the committed sets sit near 1. Added as a fifth set, it makes the threshold rule dearer (+8.4 cycles per design) and widens the across-chemistry interval to &minus;7.53 to +3.81. H313 refuted, H314 and H316 inconclusive, H315 supported on its letter only
- Scan (`experiments/poles7q/scan.json`): the table is PyBaMM 26.10.1.1's `lico2_ocp_Ramadass2004` to 5.0e-8 V. Each pole sits beside a numerator zero (0.2488, 0.3721, 0.8872), so the spikes are narrow (the last pair is 0.0016 wide). In the fit's own variable t = 1.13 y the last pole is at 1.0043, so without the stretch the fit would have one pole-free stretch from about t = 0.42 to 1. The envelope changes 354 of 401 table points on Ramadass2004 and none on the other seven tabulated cells.
- H313 refuted: 9 of 45 designs end cycle 2's charge in (0.8888, 0.9188]. The cell charges a median 9.8% of its cathode per cycle, against 28 to 44% on the committed sets (7h's 64 designs: Ai2020 0.44, NCA_Kim2011 0.36, Chen2020 0.29, OKane2022 0.28), so the band is narrow, but it is not pinned above the pole. 23 of 45 end below it. Post hoc reading, not tested: the spike is too narrow for the solver's steps to resolve, so the DFN steps across it. The 11 solver failures of 7p may be runs that landed on it.
- H314 inconclusive: q75 E10 0.895 (bar 0.5, refutation above 2); median 0.64; E50/E10 median 0.48 (7p 0.91). The envelope is right for a cell that stops above the pole and wrong for the half that cross it, where it holds the voltage above 8.8 V.
- H315 supported on the letter: max |&rho;| 0.402 (O2; O1 0.08, O3 0.39), against a 0.4 bar. But the drift multiplier m it tracks has median 74 (committed sets near 1), and 7c's line through it gives a median |m<sub>C</sub> &minus; 1| of 3.5e21, so the correction is numerically meaningless on this cell.
- H316 inconclusive: five sets, 25 folds, theta minus U mean &minus;1.92 cycles per design, set bootstrap &minus;7.53 to +3.81; sign test 14 cheaper, 9 dearer, p = 0.405. On the repaired set theta and theta50 meet 5 of 5 folds at 48.4 cycles, U meets 5 of 5 at 40.0, so the threshold rule is dearer on every fold (+8.4).
- Post hoc (not in the plan): a second repair, Up bridged linearly across each pole-zero pair &plusmn;0.01 (`REPAIR=bridge`, `rows_bridge.json`), is worse: E10 median 1.38, q75 about 12.4, close to 7p's raw table. Neither the cell that stops nor the cell that crosses is what the law assumes.
- Reading: the breadth limit now has a mechanism. `Ramadass2004` as shipped is not a fifth chemistry for this protocol. Its cathode OCP has a pole in the cycling range that the DFN half-resolves, so the simulated cell is an artefact of the fit and not a cell an engineer would build. The paper keeps four sets and excludes Ramadass2004 for a measured, checkable reason. For anyone benchmarking across PyBaMM's parameter sets: scan each OCP function for sign changes of its denominator on 0 to 1 (`scan`, stdlib) before benching, as a pre-check beside 7p's fade probe.
- Caveats: one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); y<sub>end</sub> is an average-stoichiometry estimate that ignores the overpotential at cut-off and assumes each discharge ends at y = min(1, n<sub>Li</sub>/Q<sub>p</sub>); the poles were found before the plan (disclosed in the plan entry); the bridge variant is post hoc; we did not check whether the stretch and its effect are documented upstream.
- Next: write the exclusion and its measured reason into the paper (done in this step, `paper/discussion.md` 5.4); breadth now has to come from the one route left open: a pre-registered sweep of SEI and plating rate constants on `Mohtat2020` and `Ecker2015` until they fade like the committed sets, reported as a sweep.
- Output: `experiments/poles7q/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `scan.json`, `rows.json`, `rows_bridge.json`; tests `tests/test_poles7q.py` (5).

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 7q (plan): the Ramadass2004 cathode OCP has poles inside the cycling range. 7p found the ten-cycle forecast law broken on `Ramadass2004@1x` and read it, post hoc, as a positive OCP table that "changes direction 7 times". Is the break a property of the triage rule, or of that table, and does repairing the table the law reads let the 45 benched designs join the across-chemistry interval as a fifth set? (plan, written and committed before `rows` or `report` ran; H313 to H316 are this entry's)
- Found while writing the plan (`experiments/poles7q/run.py scan`, stdlib, run before this entry): the table is PyBaMM 26.10.1.1's `lico2_ocp_Ramadass2004`, a degree-10 even rational fit evaluated at 1.13 y (`stretch = 1.13`, the negative/positive area overhang), to a max error of 5.0e-8 V on the 401-point grid. Its denominator vanishes at y = 0.2453, 0.3740 and 0.8888, each beside a numerator zero (0.2488, 0.3721, 0.8872), so the fit carries three near-cancelling pole pairs in 0 < y < 1. In the fit's own variable t = 1.13 y the last pole is at t = 1.0043, just outside [0, 1]: the stretch is what moves it inside. No other table of the eight the programme has tabulated has a turn in Up (OKane2022's Un has 18 small ones and works).
- Mechanism, fixed now: a constant-current charge starts at the discharged end (y near 1) and stops when the terminal voltage reaches 4.2 V. Coming down from y = 1 the OCP rises without bound as y approaches 0.8888 from above, so the DFN cell should stop just above the pole and cycle a narrow band. The law's balance code (window, capacity, eta) solves over the whole grid and can land below the pole, where the table is meaningless for this cell.
- Repair, fixed now (`rows`): Up replaced by its running maximum from y = 1 downward (the highest voltage a charge from the discharged end has met by y; monotone, and equal to Up wherever Up is monotone from the top, so it changes no other set's table). Every other input, the 45 designs, 7p's code path (`lawfloor` and `drift7c` `_job`), its pools (10, 8, 8, 8, 11) and 7c's correction are unchanged. No new simulation.
- Report, fixed now (`report`, stdlib): 7p's report code on the repaired rows (7c's correction, then 7h's `refit` lopo5 for theta, theta50 and U), paired with 7h's 20 committed folds, set-clustered bootstrap (20,000 draws, seed 7101). Reference for the charge band: every finished 7h design on the four committed sets, same arithmetic.
- H313 (the cell cycles just above the pole): on every one of the 45 designs, the end of cycle 2's charge, y<sub>end</sub> = min(1, n<sub>Li</sub>/Q<sub>p</sub>) &minus; Q<sub>ch</sub>/(F Q<sub>p</sub>), lies in (0.8888, 0.9188]. Refuted if fewer than half do.
- H314 (the envelope repairs the forecast): the cycle-10 forecast error q75 on the repaired rows is at most 0.5 (7p: 12.6; committed sets 0.06 to 0.12). Refuted if above 2.
- H315 (7c's drift signal exists once the table is repaired): on pools 1 to 3, the largest |Spearman| between O1, O2, O3 and m is at least 0.4. Refuted if below 0.2.
- H316 (the interval moves off zero): with the repaired set as a fifth set (25 folds), the set bootstrap 95% interval of theta minus U lies below zero. Refuted if the five-set mean is zero or above.
- Risks named now: the repaired cell is still a cell that cycles about a tenth of its cathode (a physically odd cell, though it is what PyBaMM simulates), so a working forecast on it says the law reads the simulator correctly, not that the set is a good fifth chemistry; the 11 designs that failed in the solver may be the ones that crossed the pole, so the 45 are the survivors; y<sub>end</sub> ignores the overpotential at cut-off and assumes the discharge ends at y = min(1, n<sub>Li</sub>/Q<sub>p</sub>); five sets is still a small cluster count for a bootstrap; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1).

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7p outcome: the across-chemistry interval did not move, because PyBaMM's other cells do not supply a usable fifth set. Of eight candidate conditions on four cells no triage step had used, only `Ramadass2004@1x` fades enough (median 5.0% of n<sub>Li</sub> lost from cycle 2 to 20). `Mohtat2020`, `Ecker2015` and `Marquis2019` lose 0.08 to 0.61% even at 3x to 4x current, under an eighth of the committed sets' 4.8 to 17%. On the one kept set the ten-cycle lithium-loss forecast breaks (cycle-99 error q75 12.6, against 0.06 to 0.12 on the committed sets), every rule stops at cycle 10, and the pooled theta-minus-U interval is &minus;7.53 to +0.33, still spanning zero. H310 supported but vacuous; H309, H311 and H312 inconclusive
- Probe (`experiments/sets7p/probe.jsonl`, `choose.json`): 32 runs of 20 cycles, about 2 minutes. Median lithium lost from cycle 2 to 20 as a share of n<sub>Li</sub>: Mohtat2020 3x 0.0048, 4x 0.0061; Ecker2015 2x 0.0053, 3x 0.0017; Marquis2019 2x 0.0016, 3x 0.0008; Ramadass2004 1x 0.0504 (kept); Ramadass2004 2x dropped (1 of 4 failed). All 31 finished runs plate (plating is 96 to 99.9% of the lithium lost), so the cells plate, just slowly. On Ecker2015 and Marquis2019 more current means less fade, presumably because the charge reaches its cut-off sooner. Only one set was kept, so the plan's fallback applied: run what passed.
- Bench: 56 designs of `Ramadass2004@1x` to cycle 100, about 10 minutes on 3 workers; 45 finished, 11 failed inside the solver in their first cycles (IDA error test failures, as `Ai2020@0.3x` in 5h), dropped and counted. Pools 10, 8, 8, 8, 11 (the plan's rule leaves the last pool short). Tests (6) ran before `report`, including a control: the report's stdlib correction run on the four committed sets' 200 rows gives their committed drift estimates to within 1e-9.
- The forecast law does not transfer to this cell: the cell-000 lithium table R(L) jumps from about 0.005 to 1.6e6 by 3.2% of n<sub>Li</sub> lost on the first design looked at (j = 1), the cycle-10 forecast error has a median of 1.45, and extending to cycle 50 barely helps (E50/E10 median 0.91). The drift multiplier m is therefore meaningless (median S<sub>C</sub> 9.9e6), the observables do not track it (|&rho;| at most 0.27; H312 inconclusive), and every rule's best policy is "stop everything at cycle 10", which meets the target on 5 of 5 folds because nothing beats it (H310 supported, but it tests nothing).
- H309 inconclusive: theta minus U over 25 folds (20 committed, 5 new) has a mean of &minus;3.60 cycles per design, set-clustered bootstrap &minus;7.53 to +0.33 (four sets: &minus;4.50, &minus;8.50 to +0.25); sign test unchanged (14 cheaper, 4 dearer, p = 0.031) since every new fold ties. H311 inconclusive (the new set's mean difference is exactly 0).
- Post hoc (not a hypothesis of the plan): Ramadass2004 is the only cell of eight whose positive-electrode OCP table on the 0 to 1 stoichiometry grid changes direction (7 times; range &minus;2.6 to 57 V), and its negative OCP reaches 1729 V at the grid edge. The forecast law inverts these tables over the whole grid, so a fit that is only valid inside the cell's own window corrupts the table. NCA_Kim2011's positive table also reaches extreme values at the edges (&minus;187 to 11 V) yet is monotone and works, so it looks like the turns, not the edge values, that matter. This reading is not tested.
- Reading: the paper's across-chemistry claim cannot be firmed up from PyBaMM's stock parameter sets under this protocol. Three of the four unused cells barely degrade with the copied SEI and plating parameters, and the fourth breaks the forecast law upstream of the triage rule. What this run adds is a measured reason for the paper's breadth limit, plus a pre-check (OCP monotonicity and a 20-cycle fade probe) that any next cell must pass before it is benched. More breadth needs either degradation parameters that make these cells fade (a sweep of the SEI and plating rate constants, reported as such) or new conditions on the committed cells (different protocols or temperatures, which are not new chemistries).
- Caveats: one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); OKane2022's degradation parameters on every set (5p), which is the likely reason three cells barely fade; a 4-design, 20-cycle probe; the 11 failed Ramadass2004 designs could be the most degrading ones (the solver failed in their first cycles), so the kept 45 could be a biased sample; the OCP reading is post hoc.
- Next: write the breadth limit into the paper's discussion (four sets, with the measured reason), then either (a) a pre-registered sweep of the SEI and plating rate constants on Mohtat2020 and Ecker2015 until they fade like the committed sets, or (b) the forecast law with OCP tables clipped to each cell's stoichiometry window, rerun on the 45 Ramadass2004 designs with no new simulation.
- Output: `experiments/sets7p/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `choose.json`, `probe.jsonl`, `bench.jsonl`, `rows.json`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`; tests `tests/test_sets7p.py` (6).

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7p (plan): a fifth and sixth parameter set for the across-chemistry interval. The paper's threshold rule beat a uniform run length on 14 of 20 lopo5 folds (sign test p = 0.031), but bootstrapped over the four parameter sets its mean saving ran from &minus;8.5 to +0.25 cycles per design. Do two cells no triage step has used, put through the same code, move that interval off zero? (plan, written and committed before the probe or the bench ran; H309 to H312 are this entry's)
- Candidates, fixed now, in this order (`experiments/sets7p/run.py`, `CANDIDATES`): Mohtat2020 at 3x and 4x, Ecker2015 at 2x and 3x, Marquis2019 at 2x and 3x, Ramadass2004 at 1x and 2x (multiples of 5b's 48.7 A/m2). Earlier milestones (5k edge, 5g gate) ran Mohtat2020 at 2x and 3x, Ecker2015 at 1.5x and 2x and Marquis2019 at 2x to cycle 100 and saw at most 2.7% energy fade, against 16 to 30% lithium loss on the four committed sets, so these cells may not fade enough; that is what the probe decides. ORegan2022, Prada2013, Chen2020_composite and Xu2019 fail inside the design mapping (5i), so they are not candidates.
- Probe rule, fixed now (`probe`, `choose`): per candidate, the first 4 designs of a fresh 64-design Sobol pool (seed 41, used by no milestone) run 20 cycles with 5zk's bench; a candidate is kept if all 4 finish, every one plates in cycles 2 and 10 (so O1 and O2 exist), and the median lithium lost from cycle 2 to 20 is at least 3% of n<sub>Li</sub> (5c's fading rule; the committed sets sit at 4.8 to 17%). The first two kept, at most one per cell, are the new sets. If fewer than two pass, the step reports that and runs what passed.
- Bench, fixed now (`bench`, `rows`): per kept condition, designs 0 to 55 of the same pool to cycle 100 with 5zk's x-resolved DFN (SEI + plating) child, unchanged, `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`, 3 workers; then 7h's rows code (lawfloor's e at every window, 7c's O1 to O3 and m, cell 000). The first 50 that finish 100 cycles, in design order, form five pools of 10, 8, 8, 8 and 16 (the committed sets' 5zk, 5zy, 5zz, 7g, 7h sizes).
- Report, fixed now (stdlib): 7c's correction (observable picked by |Spearman| with m, line by least squares) leave-one-out on pools 1 to 3, fitted on them for pools 4 and 5; then 7h's `refit` for theta (K1 free), theta50 and U, each pool held out in turn with its nested margin from the other four (lopo5, 5 folds per set). The interval: per fold, theta's DFN cycles per design minus U's, pooled with 7h's 20 committed folds, mean and a set-clustered bootstrap (20,000 draws, seed 7101, as `paper/tables.py`).
- H309 (the interval moves off zero): over six sets, the bootstrap 95% interval of theta minus U lies below zero. Refuted if the six-set mean is zero or above.
- H310 (the rule still meets its target on new cells): theta meets the tail target on at least 4 of 5 lopo5 folds on each new set. Refuted if it meets 2 or fewer on either.
- H311 (the saving carries): theta's mean cycles minus U's, over each new set's 5 folds, is below zero on both. Refuted if above zero on both.
- H312 (7c's drift signal exists on new cells): on pools 1 to 3 of each new set, the largest |Spearman| between O1, O2, O3 and m is at least 0.4. Refuted if below 0.2 on both.
- Risks named now: low-fade cells give small E and a uniform rule that is cheap to satisfy, so a null here may say more about the cells than the rule; two sets of 50 designs move a set-clustered bootstrap with six clusters, still few; the new pools are all fresh (no reuse), unlike the committed sets' first three pools; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); not benched through the runner (wall time), which 7n and 7o showed tracks the cycle count to about 1%.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7o outcome: refitting the triage policy on state-read observables changes nothing a designer would see. Over all 200 designs the policy and its evaluation ever used, the state read moves the drift estimate by a median 1.2e-5 (max 3.2e-4), every set keeps its observable, the thresholds move in the fifth significant figure, the 64 held-out 7h decisions are identical, and the paper's numbers stand: 3 of 4 sets met at 40.0 cycles (20% saved) on 7h, lopo5 19 of 20 at 43.5 cycles (13% saved). The runner now ships a policy fitted on its own scorer. H306 to H308 supported; H305 refuted on its own bar (the old pools' looks do not reproduce bit for bit), with no effect downstream
- Bench: 200 designs, 200 processes, about 10 minutes on 3 workers, all ok first time, `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`. Tests (5) ran before `report`; `run.py` unchanged after the plan commit. Ten-cycle looks took a median 2.93 s (583 s in all).
- H305 refuted: the 7g and 7h looks reproduce their committed observables bit for bit (all 96), but every 5zk design and 48 of 64 5zy and 5zz designs differ slightly. O1 by at most 2.3e-5 relative; O3 by more than the 1e-3 bar on five designs (5zk `Chen2020` j = 11, 15, 23, 31 and `OKane2022` j = 23; worst 1.4e-2, a log ratio of small lithium-loss differences). The old pools were simulated as 100-cycle experiments in their own benches; the ten-cycle look takes different adaptive steps (post hoc reading, not tested). The committed control through the same code reproduces the committed mC to 3.7e-14 and 7h's verdicts exactly, and this bench's trapezoid variant gives the same policy outcomes as the committed rows, so the mismatch reaches nothing downstream.
- H306 supported: the picked observable is unchanged on all four sets (Ai2020 O1, Chen2020 O2, OKane2022 O2, NCA_Kim2011 O1). The state read moves O1 by a median 6.5e-6 relative and O2 by 4.5e-4 (max 18%, O2 being a small difference of two centroids), which moves mC by a median 1.2e-5 (max 3.2e-4, NCA_Kim2011).
- H307 supported: the state-refit replay thresholds are 0.04462, 0.02596, 0.03123 and 0.21172 (trapezoid 0.04463, 0.02594, 0.03123, 0.21169); on state scores they make the same decision as replay7h on trapezoid scores on 64 of 64 7h designs (62 of 62 away from &theta;, 2 of 2 near it), and meet the target on the same three sets (Chen2020 missed, as in 7h).
- H308 supported: lopo5 under the state read 19 of 20 folds met at 43.50 cycles, identical to the trapezoid lopo5 (and 7h's).
- Reading: the quadrature of the plating integral is not a degree of freedom the triage result depends on: the scorer that the runner can afford (two state vectors per cycle, 7n) and the one the paper's analysis used give the same decisions, policies and headline numbers to the precision anyone would report. The shipped runner now pairs its scorer with a policy fitted on it (`experiments/state7o/triage.py`, `policy.json`: replay7h_state for replays, deploy_state fitted on all five pools for users), which removes the one caveat 7n left. The remaining open issue for the paper is breadth, not the read: four parameter sets, an across-chemistry interval that still spans zero.
- Caveats: same four parameter sets and five pools as 7h; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); irreversible plating only; the deploy_state policy is fitted on all five pools, so it has no held-out score of its own (lopo5 is its estimate).
- Next: the fifth and sixth parameter sets for the across-chemistry interval, benched through `experiments/state7o/triage.py` so accuracy and wall time come from the same runs.
- Output: `experiments/state7o/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `policy.json`; tool `experiments/state7o/triage.py`; tests `tests/test_state7o.py` (5).

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7o (plan): refit the triage policy on state-read observables. 7n's lean runner reads node plating from the dead-lithium state (the solver's exact integral), but its policy's lines and thresholds were fitted on trapezoid observables, so the shipped runner pairs one scorer with another's tuning. Re-running the first ten cycles of all 200 designs the policy and its evaluation ever used and refitting everything downstream of the observables on the state read: does the held-out triage, and the paper's lopo5 headline, survive the change of quadrature? (plan, written and committed before the bench ran; H305 to H308 are this entry's)
- Bench, fixed now (`experiments/state7o/run.py bench`): every design of 5zk (40), 5zy (32), 5zz (32), 7g (32) and 7h (64), a 10-cycle DFN (SEI + plating) look through 7m's `_setup`, each in its own process, `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`, 3 workers. From one solution, O1, O2, O3 read two ways: from the dead-lithium state change per cycle (7n's read, mesh nodes) and with 7m's trapezoid on output points. The cycle-10 and cycle-50 errors (E) and the drift multiplier m are unchanged (they come from the 100-cycle runs).
- Report (stdlib): 7c's correction refit per variant (leave-one-out on the set's 26 earlier designs for 5zk, 5zy and 5zz; fitted on the 26 for 7g and 7h), then 7h's theta50 rule with its nested margin, refit on 5zk, 5zy, 5zz and 7g and scored on 7h (the policy 7m's runner replays), and lopo5 (20 folds). Control: the committed observables through the same code reproduce the committed mC to 5.2e-14 and 7h's numbers (3/4 met at 40.0 cycles; lopo5 19/20 at 43.5), checked before the bench. `run.py policy` writes `policy.json` (replay7h_state, deploy_state) for the runner.
- Probe while writing the code (not part of the bench), 5zk `Chen2020` j = 7: the trapezoid read of the new look differs from the committed row by 3e-7 (O1), 2.3e-4 (O2) and 4.0e-5 (O3) relative, so the old pools do not reproduce bit for bit (7m found 7h's rows exact); the state read moves O2 by 18% relative (&minus;5.07e-5 against &minus;4.30e-5), which through Chen2020's line (slope &minus;2.38) moves the drift estimate by 1.8e-5, against a threshold of 0.026. Look 1.39 s.
- H305 (the looks reproduce the committed observables): this bench's trapezoid O1 and O3 within 1e-3 relative of the committed rows on the median and on every design, O2 within 1e-2 on the median. Refuted if any design's O1 or O3 differs by more than 1e-2.
- H306 (the correction barely moves): the observable each set's correction picks is the same under both reads on all four sets, and the drift estimate mC moves by a median at most 1e-3 (state against trapezoid, all 200 designs). Refuted if the pick changes on two or more sets.
- H307 (the refit policy keeps the held-out triage): the state-refit replay policy, on state scores, makes the same stop/extend decision as replay7h on trapezoid scores on at least 62 of the 64 7h designs and on every one whose trapezoid score is more than 1% from &theta;, and meets the target on the same sets (Ai2020, OKane2022, NCA_Kim2011; Chen2020 missed). Refuted if the number of sets met differs.
- H308 (the headline holds): lopo5 under the state read within one fold and one cycle of the trapezoid lopo5 of this bench (met folds and mean DFN cycles per design). Refuted if it differs by three or more folds or more than three cycles.
- Risks named now: the old pools' looks are not bit-identical to their committed rows (probe above), so the trapezoid variant of this bench, not the committed rows, is the like-for-like comparison, and the committed control is reported beside it; the theta50 pick is a discrete argmin over training scores, so a small score shift can move &theta; to a neighbouring design's score and flip designs near it; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1), irreversible plating only.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7n outcome: reading the drift score from the dead-lithium state, and only the observable the policy uses, cuts the read from a median 0.20 s to 0.009 s (1.09 to 0.043 cycle equivalents), keeps every stop/extend decision (62 of 62 away from &theta;, 2 of 2 near it), and lifts the runner's wall-time saving on the held-out 7h pool from 14.0% to 17.2% per design (12.6% of total compute). The runner now sits 0.010 from the cycle model, against 0.041. H301 to H304 supported
- Bench: 64 designs, 128 stage processes, about 13 minutes on 3 workers, all ok first time, `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`. Tests (6) ran before `report`; `run.py` and `triage.py` unchanged after the plan commit. One wording fix to the plan after the commit: its sign (the dead-lithium change is the integral of minus the plating current over F; the code and the probe were already so).
- H301 supported: lean score against 7m's runner score on the same design, relative difference median 9.6e-5, max 1.9e-3 (`Chen2020`, where the policy reads O2, a small difference of two centroids; the picked observable itself differs by up to 9.1%, median 9.4e-5). Decisions agree on all 64. The paired check confirms the run itself is unchanged: 7m's read of this bench's own look solution gives 7m's score to 3.9e-14, and the mesh nodes equal `x_n` exactly. The two readers differ only in integration: the state change is the solver's exact integral, 7m's is the trapezoid rule on output points.
- H302 supported: the read costs a median 0.043 cycle equivalents (max 0.215) against 1.086 for 7m's read in this bench's b; per set 6 to 11 ms against 7m's 138 to 278 ms. (7m's read run cache-warm in the same process after the timed run took a median 0.036 s, so even its cheapest form is four times the lean one.)
- H303 supported: mean t<sub>T</sub>/t<sub>A</sub> 0.828, a 17.2% saving per design (Ai2020 9.0%, Chen2020 32.7%, OKane2022 8.2%, NCA_Kim2011 19.0%); 12.6% of total compute (587 s against 671 s); 12.1% without `Chen2020`, the set whose accuracy target 7h missed (7m: 9.5%). Extended designs ran a median 1.007 times A (IQR 0.97 to 1.03; 7m 1.026), stopped ones 0.319 (7m 0.364).
- H304 supported: predicted (c = 4.77, f = 0.750) 0.817, measured 0.828, gap 0.010 (7m 0.041). The container ran a little faster than in 7m (c 4.77 against 5.24), which moves the prediction by only 0.002, so the gain is the read, not the load.
- Reading: the runner now delivers what the cycle model says, less about one percent. What is left between the 19% cycle saving and the 17% wall saving is the fixed cost every design pays (c &asymp; 5 cycles) and nothing else measurable. The read is also more exact than 7m's: it takes the solver's own integral of the plating rate instead of a trapezoid on output points. The policy's lines were fitted on trapezoid observables, which moved one score by 0.2% and no decision; a policy refit on state-read observables would remove even that.
- Caveats: one pool (7h, 16 designs per set) on a shared 3-worker container; the accuracy side is still 7h's; the dead-lithium identity holds for PyBaMM's irreversible plating option only (reversible options would add the plated-lithium state); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1).
- Next: the fifth and sixth parameter sets for the across-chemistry interval, benched through this runner so accuracy and wall time come from the same runs; refit the policy on state-read observables.
- Output: `experiments/lean7n/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `bench.jsonl`; tool `experiments/lean7n/triage.py`; tests `tests/test_lean7n.py` (6).

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7n (plan): a lean score read for the triage runner. 7m's runner read the drift score by integrating the x-resolved plating current over every output step of cycles 2 and 10 and reading ten cycles of lithium loss, a median 1.26 cycle equivalents per design, most of its 0.041 gap to the cycle model. Does reading only what the policy needs, from the state, keep every decision and recover that cycle? (plan, written and committed before the bench ran; H301 to H304 are this entry's)
- Runner, fixed now (`experiments/lean7n/triage.py`): 7m's rule, policy and in-place extension unchanged. The score read computes only the set's picked observable (no set's policy uses O3, so the lithium-loss series is not read before the decision); a cycle's node plating is the change in `Negative dead lithium concentration` across the cycle (two state vectors), which in PyBaMM's irreversible plating option is the exact time integral of minus the plating current divided by F, where 7m used the trapezoid rule on output points; node positions come from the mesh.
- Probe while writing the code (not part of the bench), `Chen2020` j = 8: node plating from the state change equals 7m's trapezoid integral times F to within 7e-5 per node; O2 differs by 9% relative (it is a small difference of two centroids, so the trapezoid error shows), the score by 1.6e-4 relative, the same decision (stop); the read took 0.008 s against 7m's 0.142 s on that design.
- Bench (`run.py bench`): 7m's, unchanged: all 64 designs of the 7h pool, policy `replay7h`, per design T (this runner) and A (a fresh 50-cycle run, lean recorder), each in its own process, order alternated by design, `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`, 3 workers. T also reads 7m's full observables from its own look solution after the timed run (a paired check; never in `seconds`).
- H301 (the lean read keeps the decisions): the lean score within 1e-3 relative of 7m's runner score on the median design and 1e-2 on every design, and its decision equal to 7m's on every design whose 7m score is more than 1% from &theta; (those within 1% reported). Refuted if any such decision differs.
- H302 (the read is cheap): the score read costs a median at most 0.2 cycle equivalents of the design's b (stopped designs: the set's median b over extended designs). Refuted above 0.5.
- H303 (the saving rises): mean per-design t<sub>T</sub>/t<sub>A</sub> gives a saving of at least 16% of U50's wall time (7m: 14.0%). Refuted if below 7m's 14.0%.
- H304 (the gap to the cycle model closes): |mean t<sub>T</sub>/t<sub>A</sub> &minus; (c + 10 + 40 f)/(c + 50)| at most 0.02 (7m: 0.041). Refuted above 0.04.
- Risks named now: the policy's lines and threshold were fitted on trapezoid observables, so the lean score is a slightly different scorer (H301 measures how much); one shared 3-worker container, whose load differs from 7m's bench; the paired in-process 7m read runs cache-warm after the timed run, so it is a floor on 7m's cost, not a fair timing (7m's own bench is the comparison); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1), and the dead-lithium identity holds for the irreversible plating option only (the runner would need the plated-lithium state too for the reversible options).

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7m outcome: the triage runner, built as a tool (`experiments/runner7m/triage.py`, one live PyBaMM `Simulation` per design, scored at cycle 10, stopped or extended in place), reproduces the offline triage exactly on all 64 designs of the 7h pool and saves 14.0% of wall time per design against running each to cycle 50 (9.7% of total compute), under a policy that never saw the pool. Scoring costs 1.26 cycle equivalents, more than the 0.5 planned. H297, H298 and H300 supported, H299 inconclusive
- Bench: 64 designs, 128 stage processes, about 13 minutes on 3 workers, all ok first time, `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1` as planned. Tests (9) ran before `report`; `run.py` and `triage.py` unchanged after the plan commit.
- H297 supported: runner score against 7h's offline score, relative difference median 6.0e-15, max 3.2e-13; decisions agree on 62 of 62 away from &theta; and on both designs within 1% of it (`Chen2020` j = 53, `NCA_Kim2011` j = 54). The runner's last-cycle lithium loss equals the 7h bench's at the same cycle on every design (difference 0). So 7h's accuracy numbers carry over to the runner unchanged: the &theta;50 refit policy met the tail target on 3 of 4 sets (`Chen2020` missed, q75 1.58 times the target).
- H298 supported: per-design mean t<sub>T</sub>/t<sub>A</sub> 0.860 against 0.819 from the cycle model (c = 5.24, f = 0.750; gap 0.041). Extended designs ran a median 1.026 times A (IQR 0.997 to 1.061), stopped ones 0.364 (IQR 0.297 to 0.405).
- H299 inconclusive: reading the x-resolved plating at cycles 2 and 10 and computing the score took a median 0.20 s (IQR 0.16 to 0.25), 1.26 cycle equivalents (max 3.28, on `Chen2020`, whose cycles are cheapest). That is most of the gap in H298 (0.75 &times; 1.26 / 55.2 = 0.017) and the extended designs' 2.6% overrun.
- H300 supported: 14.0% of U50's wall time saved on the per-design mean (Ai2020 5.7%, Chen2020 27.5%, OKane2022 6.9%, NCA_Kim2011 15.9%). Weighted by each design's run time, 9.7% (526 s against 583 s), because the stopped designs are the cheap ones. Without `Chen2020`, the set whose target was missed, the per-design saving is 9.5%.
- Reading: the paper's rule now exists as a runnable tool that gives the same answer as the analysis and saves what the cycle model says it should, less the cost of reading the score. The saving is smaller than the 19% cycle figure for three named reasons: the fixed cost every design pays (c &asymp; 5), the score read (about one cycle), and a pool where 75% of designs are flagged. A cheaper score read (asking PyBaMM for the plating variable at two cycles only, or a lean output-variable list) would recover about a cycle per design.
- Caveats: one pool (7h, 16 designs per set) on a shared 3-worker container; T and A in separate processes; the accuracy side is 7h's, not re-measured; the `deploy` policy (threshold refit on all five pools) is shipped but not benched; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1).
- Next: cut the score's read cost (restrict output variables, or read the plating from the solution's state vector), then the fifth and sixth parameter sets for the across-chemistry interval.
- Output: `experiments/runner7m/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `policy.json`; tool `experiments/runner7m/triage.py`; tests `tests/test_runner7m.py` (9).

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7m (plan): the triage runner as an artefact. 7l showed that a `Simulation` extended in place pays no second fixed cost, so the paper's rule can run as one process per design: solve 10 cycles, score the drift, stop or keep solving to 50. Does that runner, built as a tool (`experiments/runner7m/triage.py`), reproduce the offline triage decisions and deliver the wall-time saving the cycle model predicts, on a pool its policy never saw? (plan, written and committed before the bench ran; H297 to H300 are this entry's)
- Runner, fixed now: per design, a DFN (SEI + plating) `Simulation` with a 10-cycle experiment, solved once; O1, O2, O3 from that solution's cycle-2 and cycle-10 node plating and lithium loss (7c's definitions); S = |c<sub>0</sub> + c<sub>1</sub> O &minus; 1| with the set's observable and line frozen in `policy.json`; if S &ge; &theta;, the same object is solved in place from its last solution, 10 cycles at a time, to cycle 50. Nothing is written to disk.
- Policy `replay7h`, fixed now (`run.py policy`, stdlib, from committed rows only): 7c's correction fitted on the set's 104 earlier designs (the line 7h's rows used) and 7h's refit &theta;50 threshold (picked on 5zk, 5zy, 5zz and 7g with its nested margin; never on 7h). A second policy, `deploy`, refits the threshold on all five pools for users; it is not benched here.
- Bench (`run.py bench`): all 64 ok designs of the 7h pool; per design, each in its own process, T (the runner) and A (a fresh 50-cycle run, 7l's A, lean recorder), order alternated by design; `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`, 3 workers. Offline, `replay7h` flags 48 of the 64 (f = 0.75); 2 designs score within 1% of &theta;. A probe on `Chen2020` j = 8 while writing the code (not part of the bench) gave O1, O2, O3 equal to 7h's rows to every printed digit, S = 0.02567 against &theta; = 0.02594 (stop), 1.37 s.
- H297 (the runner reproduces the offline triage): the runner's S within 1e-6 relative of 7h's offline S on the median design and 1e-3 on every design, and its decision equal to the offline one on every design whose offline S is more than 1% from &theta; (those within 1% are reported). Refuted if any such decision differs.
- H298 (wall time follows the decision): the mean per-design ratio t<sub>T</sub>/t<sub>A</sub> within 0.05 of the cycle model (c + 10 + 40 f)/(c + 50), with c the median a/b of the extended designs (b = (t<sub>A</sub> &minus; t<sub>look</sub>)/40, a = t<sub>look</sub> &minus; 10b) and f the runner's flagged share. Refuted if the gap exceeds 0.10.
- H299 (scoring is cheap): computing the observables and the score costs a median at most 0.5 cycle equivalents of the design's b (for stopped designs, the median b of the set's extended designs). Refuted above 2.
- H300 (a held-out wall-time saving): the mean t<sub>T</sub>/t<sub>A</sub> is at most 0.90 (a saving of at least 10% of U50's wall time on a pool the policy never saw). Refuted if the saving is below 5%.
- Risks named now: a shared 3-worker container (per-design times carry noise of the size of a fixed cost); the score's accuracy consequence (q75 against target) is not re-measured, it is 7h's (&theta;50 refit met 3 of 4 sets), valid only if H297 holds; T and A run in separate processes, so a stopped design pays no comparison against a 10-cycle fresh run; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1), whose in-place `solve(starting_solution=...)` reuse is an implementation detail.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7l outcome: keeping one PyBaMM `Simulation` alive and extending it in place makes the cycle-10 look free. Reaching cycle 50 through a 10-cycle look plus four in-place extensions took a median 1.000 times one uninterrupted 50-cycle run (IQR 0.95 to 1.06; 7k's restart from file 1.22), an extra 0.02 cycle equivalents against a fresh fixed cost of 6.34, with the cycle-50 state identical. Priced in, the continued triage saving is 16.9% of wall time, its x = 0 ceiling, and 17.1% scaled to 7j's runner. H293 to H296 supported
- Bench: 16 designs (7k's picks), 48 stage processes, about 4 minutes on 3 workers, all ok first time. Tests ran before `report`; `run.py` unchanged after the plan commit. Run with `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`, which the plan did not name (7k did not set it); every comparison is within this bench, but its A ran a median 0.78 times 7k's A, so absolute times and a/b (6.34 here, 11.79 in 7k) differ between the two benches.
- H293 supported: cycle-50 total lithium lost of D (10-cycle chunks) is bit-identical to A on all 16 designs (and cycle-50 capacity); E (1-cycle chunks) within 4.9e-6 (median over D and E 3.6e-14).
- H294 supported: extension cost x<sub>D</sub>/b median 0.02 cycles (IQR &minus;2.85 to 3.50) against a/b 6.34 (IQR 4.30 to 7.44). D was slower than A on 8 of 16 designs, faster on 8: the difference is timing noise.
- H295 supported: with c = 6.34 and x = 0.02 the triage saves 16.9% of U50's wall time in this bench's units (ceiling at x = 0, 16.9%); scaled to 7j's runner, 17.1%, 7j's continue column.
- H296 supported: a 1-cycle-chunk runner (E) costs a median 1.46 extra cycles over 49 extra solve calls (0.03 per call; E slower than A on 10 of 16), under a quarter of one restart. Per-call cost does not grow with the solution's length (calls for cycles 41 to 50 cost 0.97 of the mean, cycles 11 to 20 0.98; later dearer on 6 of 16).
- Reading: 7k's lost saving was the price of rebuilding the model, not of continuing. The model's fixed cost is paid in `Simulation` construction and the first solve (in D the first 10 cycles took 4.75 s and each later 10 cycles about 3.2 s on `Ai2020` j = 9); calling `solve(starting_solution=...)` again on the same object reuses the built and discretised step models, so continuation is free. The paper's practical claim is now whole for a runner shaped this way: run each design in its own long-lived `Simulation` in 1- or 10-cycle chunks, score the drift at cycle 10, and either stop or keep solving. Because the absolute threshold needs no batch (7h), each design's process can decide by itself, with no checkpoint on disk (7k's were 904 MB).
- Caveats: 16 designs on a shared 3-worker container (per-design extension cost spans &plusmn;4 cycles of noise; the median is what is tested); b is fitted per design from A and D's first solve; the lean recorder, not the x-resolved child, is timed; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1), whose in-place behaviour is an implementation detail rather than a documented contract; `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1` as above.
- Next: build the triage runner itself as an artefact (per design: chunked solve, drift score at cycle 10 from the frozen 7f threshold, stop or extend), tested end to end on a handful of designs; then the fifth and sixth parameter sets for the across-chemistry interval.
- Output: `experiments/keep7l/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `bench.jsonl`; tests `tests/test_keep7l.py` (7).

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7l (plan): does a runner that keeps the built DFN model alive make the cycle-10 look free? 7k found that resuming through PyBaMM's `starting_solution` in a new `Simulation` rebuilds the model and pays the fixed cost again (13.4 cycle equivalents against 11.8 fresh), which takes the continued triage saving from 17.1% to about &minus;1%. This step extends one `Simulation` in place and times it against an uninterrupted 50-cycle run on the same 16 designs (plan, written and committed before the bench ran; H293 to H296 are this entry's)
- Bench, fixed now (`experiments/keep7l/run.py bench`): 7k's 16 designs (its `jobs()`, unchanged), same DFN with SEI + plating, parameters, experiment steps and lean recorder, PyBaMM 26.10.1.1. Per design, each in its own process: A, a fresh 50-cycle experiment (as 7k's A); D, a `Simulation` built with a 10-cycle experiment, solved once (the triage look, t<sub>D10</sub>), then solved four more times on the same object from its own last solution (`sim.solve(starting_solution=sol)`) to cycle 50; E, a 1-cycle experiment solved 50 times in a chain the same way (a runner that can stop after any cycle). Stage order rotated by design (A D E, D E A, E A D). Timed from `Simulation` construction to the end of the last solve.
- Derived per design: b = (t<sub>A</sub> &minus; t<sub>D10</sub>) / 40; fresh fixed cost a = t<sub>D10</sub> &minus; 10b; extension cost x<sub>D</sub> = (t<sub>D</sub> &minus; t<sub>A</sub>) / b and x<sub>E</sub> = (t<sub>E</sub> &minus; t<sub>A</sub>) / b, in cycle equivalents (what reaching cycle 50 through the look costs beyond one 50-cycle run).
- A same-process probe on one `Chen2020` design while writing the code (not part of the bench): 10 cycles in 1.26 s, each further 10-cycle solve 0.47 to 0.63 s, cycle-50 lithium lost identical to a 50-cycle run.
- H293 (state carries over in place): cycle-50 total lithium lost of D and E within 1% of A's on the median (D and E pooled) and within 5% on every one. Refuted otherwise.
- H294 (no second fixed cost): median x<sub>D</sub> at most half the median a/b. Refuted if at least the median a/b.
- H295 (the continued saving comes back): in this bench's own units (c = median a/b, resume cost = median x<sub>D</sub> floored at 0, 7j's pooled 40.47 cycles and flagged share 0.762) the triage saves at least 12% of U50's wall time (the ceiling at x = 0 is about 15% for c near 12). Refuted below 8%. (Own units throughout, which fixes 7k's H292 mixing two runners' costs.)
- H296 (a cycle-by-cycle runner is cheaper than a restart): median x<sub>E</sub> at most the median a/b. Refuted if larger.
- Risks named now: 16 designs on a shared 3-worker container, where a fixed cost of 1 to 6 s is of the order of the noise; b fitted from two runs per design, one of them inside D; repeated `starting_solution` solves may copy the growing solution (E would show it as a per-call cost rising with the cycle count); the lean recorder, not the x-resolved child, is timed.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7k outcome: a resumed PyBaMM DFN run reproduces the uninterrupted cycle-50 state exactly, but pays the fixed cost again, so the continued triage saving 7j priced at 17.1% of wall time falls to about &minus;1% (8% if scaled to 7j's runner). Resuming 16 7h designs from a saved cycle-10 solution cost 1.22 times one uninterrupted 50-cycle run (IQR 1.17 to 1.24); the resume's own fixed cost was 13.4 cycle equivalents against 11.8 for a fresh run. H290 supported, H291 and H292 refuted
- Bench: 16 designs (4 per set, `random.Random(5301)` from the 7h bench), 64 stage processes, about 6 minutes on 3 workers, all ok first time; stage order alternated by design. Tests ran before `report`; `run.py` changed after the plan commit only in `report` and in reading 7j's constants from `paper/cost.json` instead of rounded copies (the child, picks and verdict rules unchanged).
- H290 supported: cycle-50 total lithium lost, resumed (B, across processes) against uninterrupted (A): median relative difference 1.6e-6, max 5.1e-5; in one process (C) identical to B; cycle-50 discharge capacity within 6.9e-5. A's cycle-50 lithium loss equals the 7h bench's x-resolved run on every design (difference 0), so the lean recorder changes nothing but the cost.
- H291 refuted: per-design line through A (50 cycles) and B1 (10 cycles): fresh fixed cost a/b median 11.79 (IQR 10.93 to 14.03); resume fixed cost a&prime;/b (load + new `Simulation` + solve 11 to 50) median 13.38 (IQR 10.87 to 15.07), at least the fresh one. In one process, building a second `Simulation` for cycles 11 to 50 still costs 8.24 (IQR 3.75 to 11.27); C took 1.075 times A. Loading the saved solution is not the cost (median 0.94 s); building the model is. The saved full cycle-10 `Solution` is a median 904 MB on disk.
- H292 refuted: with a&prime;/b = 13.38 and 7j's c = 5.66, f = 0.762, triage saves &minus;1.2% of U50's wall time (break-even a&prime;/b 12.5). Units check, not pre-registered: H292 mixes this bench's per-cycle cost (lean recorder, about half the x-resolved child's b, which is why a/b is 11.8 here and 5.66 in 7j) with 7j's c. In this bench's own units the saving is &minus;1.1%; with the resume cost scaled to 7j's runner (5.66 &times; 1.13) it is 8.3%. Both fall below the 10% refutation line.
- Reading: the state carries over, so a resumed run gives the same answer; the price is wrong. With PyBaMM's public interface a resume rebuilds and discretises the model, which is the fixed cost, so "continue" costs about what "restart" minus the ten redone cycles costs. The paper's practical claim narrows again: the 19% cycle saving is a wall-time saving only in a runner that keeps the built model alive between the cycle-10 look and the extension (one long-lived simulation per design, or the solver called directly with design parameters as inputs), which this programme has not built. Against that, a uniform 50-cycle run needs none of it.
- Caveats: 16 designs on a shared 3-worker container (a fixed cost of 1 to 6 s per run is of the order of the noise; every design's B1 + B2 exceeded A, 16 of 16); b is fitted per design from two runs; the resume uses PyBaMM 26.10.1.1's public `starting_solution` path, not a hand-built solver reuse; the lean recorder, not the x-resolved child, is what was timed.
- Next: a runner that keeps the model built (build once with design parameters as `inputs`, or a 50-cycle experiment stopped and continued inside one `Simulation`), timed against A on the same designs; then the fifth and sixth parameter sets.
- Output: `experiments/resume7k/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `bench.jsonl`; tests `tests/test_resume7k.py` (7).

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7k (plan): does a cycle-10 DFN run resume to cycle 50 at the price 7j assumed? 7j found the triage saving is 17.1% of wall time if a flagged design's cycle-10 run is continued and a 4.3% loss if it is restarted, but never tested continuation. This step resumes real runs and measures the resume's own fixed cost and whether the resumed cycle-50 state matches an uninterrupted run (plan, written and committed before the bench ran; H290 to H292 are this entry's)
- Bench, fixed now (`experiments/resume7k/run.py bench`): 4 designs per set drawn by `random.Random(5301)` from the 7h bench's ok designs (16 in all), same model, parameters and experiment as 5zk's child (DFN, SEI + plating, PyBaMM 26.10.1.1), but a lean recorder (total lithium lost and discharge capacity per cycle) so every condition pays the same post-processing. Per design, each in its own process: A, a fresh run to cycle 50; B1, a fresh run to cycle 10 whose solution is saved to disk; B2, a new process that loads it and solves cycles 11 to 50 with `starting_solution`; C, cycles 1 to 10 then 11 to 50 in one process (separates process and build cost from the solver). Timed from `Simulation` construction to the end of `solve` (as 7j's `seconds`), plus whole-process time. A and B run in alternating order by design.
- Derived per design: b = (t<sub>A</sub> &minus; t<sub>B1</sub>) / 40, fresh fixed cost a = t<sub>B1</sub> &minus; 10b, resume fixed cost a&prime; = t<sub>B2</sub> &minus; 40b, all in cycle equivalents (/b).
- H290 (state carries over): the resumed cycle-50 total lithium lost is within 1% of the uninterrupted run's on the median design and within 5% on every design. Refuted otherwise.
- H291 (no second fixed cost): the median a&prime;/b is at most half the median a/b. Refuted if it is at least the median a/b (the resume pays the whole fixed cost again).
- H292 (the continued saving survives): with the median a&prime;/b priced in (wall time c + 40.47 + f a&prime; against c + 50, f = 0.762, c = 5.66, 7j's pooled fresh-pool rate), the triage saving stays at or above 15%. Refuted below 10% (break-even at a&prime;/b = 12.5).
- Risks named now: 16 designs on a shared 3-worker container, so timing noise can be of the size of a fixed cost; b is fitted from two runs per design; `starting_solution` may not carry every state (the SEI and plating variables), which H290 would catch.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7j outcome: priced in wall time, the triage saving holds only if flagged runs are continued. A DFN run's fixed cost is worth 5.66 cycles (192 paired runs; 2.63 on a second runner), and the frozen threshold rule flags 76% of designs, so its 19.1% saving in DFN cycles is 17.1% of wall time when a flagged design's cycle-10 run is continued to cycle 50, and a 4.3% loss when it is restarted from cycle 0 (break-even at 72% flagged). Discussion section and three figures written
- Measured (`paper/cost.py`, stdlib, < 1 s, descriptive): fixed cost a/b from t(k) = a + bk through each of 5l's 192 designs run to cycle 10 and to cycle 100 (`experiments/fade/bench.jsonl`): median 5.66, IQR 4.65 to 6.18 (per set 5.14 to 6.37); cross-check by least squares on the per-cycle end times of 2a's 49 thirty-cycle DFN runs (`experiments/horizon`): 2.63, IQR 2.04 to 3.01. 100-cycle x-resolved child runs in the 7g and 7h benches: median 8.9 s (`Chen2020`) to 29.1 s (`Ai2020`).
- Table 2 in time (continue / restart): 7f nested &theta; 18.0% / &minus;3.0%; 7g frozen &theta; 15.0% / &minus;7.3%; 7h frozen &theta; 19.3% / &minus;1.3%; 7h refits 21.9% / +2.3%; lopo5 &theta; 11.7% / &minus;11.9%, R50 14.1% / &minus;8.4%; uniform rules are one run per design, so U lopo5 saves 3.6% either way. Varying the fixed cost over its IQR moves the continued savings by under 0.5 points.
- Written: `paper/discussion.md` (section 5: the recipe, the saving in wall time, why ten cycles say anything, limitations and next steps); `paper/figures.py` draws Figure 1 (Table 1), Figure 2 (Table 2, hit rate with Wilson interval against cycles) and Figure 3 (continue against restart) from `tables.json` and `cost.json`; `results.md` points to it.
- Reading: the paper's practical claim needs a condition it did not state. The threshold rule saves by leaving about a quarter of the designs at cycle 10, so nearly every design pays a fixed cost; the saving survives only if the cycle-10 solution is checkpointed and resumed. A uniform run length needs no such machinery, which narrows the practical gap between the two rules wherever runs cannot be resumed.
- Caveats: the fixed cost is measured on two runners, neither the x-resolved child the triage was scored with (it was only ever run to cycle 100); the wall times come from a shared 3-worker cloud container, so they price relative, not absolute, cost; the continuation numbers assume a resumed run costs the same per cycle and yields the same cycle-50 state, untested here; the flagged share assumes extension to cycle 50 (7f/7g free-K1 policies with K1 = 40 or 45 flag slightly more, which only affects the restart column).
- Next: a continuation test (rerun a few 7h designs to cycle 10, resume to 50 with `starting_solution`, compare wall time and cycle-50 lithium loss with the uninterrupted runs); then a fifth and sixth parameter set for the across-chemistry interval.
- Output: `paper/cost.txt`, `paper/cost.json`, `paper/discussion.md`, `paper/fig1_insample.svg`, `paper/fig2_outofbatch.svg`, `paper/fig3_time.svg`; tests `tests/test_paper_cost.py` (7).

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7j (paper step, plan): the discussion section, one figure per results table, and the triage saving priced in wall-clock DFN time. What does a modelling engineer get back, in CPU-hours rather than cycles, if they adopt the nested-margin threshold rule? (descriptive; no hypothesis is tested)
- Planned: `paper/figures.py` (stdlib SVG, from `paper/tables.json`): Figure 1 for Table 1 (in-sample saving by rule and set), Figure 2 for Table 2 (hit rate with Wilson interval against DFN cycles per design for every out-of-batch look); `paper/cost.py`: seconds per DFN cycle from the `seconds` and `ok_cycles` fields of the committed `bench.jsonl` files (7g, 7h), per set, to convert 7g to 7h's cycle savings into time; `paper/discussion.md` (section 5).
- Risks named now: bench timings come from a shared 3-worker cloud container, so they price relative time, not a workstation's absolute speed; runs to cycle 100 include the setup cost, which a per-cycle average spreads.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7i (paper step): the results section, 5zk to 7h, with intervals. Pooled over the two fresh pools the frozen threshold policies meet 18 of 24 (Wilson 0.55 to 0.88, set-clustered bootstrap 0.67 to 0.88) at 40.5 DFN cycles per design (19% saved, bootstrap 12% to 27%); on lopo5 the threshold rule is cheaper than the uniform one on 14 of 20 folds (sign test p = 0.031), but a bootstrap over the four sets puts the mean saving of 4.5 cycles at &minus;8.5 to +0.25
- Written: `paper/results.md` (sections 4.1 to 4.8: pools and target, 7a's error budget, 7b's law floor, 7c's drift score, 7d and 7e in hindsight, 7e to 7h out of batch, results that did not hold, threats to validity); `paper/tables.py` regenerates Table 1 (in-sample) and Table 2 (every out-of-batch look) from `experiments/*/summary.json` in 0.2 s, stdlib; tests `tests/test_paper_tables.py` (7) check it reproduces the counts, cycles and worst misses 7f, 7g and 7h logged.
- Computed for the section, after every milestone had reported (descriptive, no hypothesis): Wilson 95% on each hit count; pooled fresh-pool hit rate of 7f's frozen &theta; policies on 7g and 7h, 18/24 = 0.75, with a bootstrap over the 8 (pool, set) clusters, since a set's three policies share one fresh pool; the paired lopo5 cycle difference &theta; &minus; U per fold: mean &minus;4.50, cheaper 14, dearer 4, tied 2, exact sign p = 0.0309, bootstrap over the 4 sets &minus;8.50 to +0.25; both rules meet on 18 folds, each alone on 1.
- Reading: the paper's claim is now sized. The saving replicates (two blind looks, each 9 of 12) and the threshold rule beats uniform fold by fold, but with four parameter sets the across-chemistry interval still spans zero; adding sets helps the paper more than adding pools. The worst miss on a fresh pool is 1.58 times the target (`Chen2020`, the most permissive 7f policy, missed on both fresh pools).
- Caveats: the bootstraps resample 8 and 4 clusters, too few for a percentile interval to be more than indicative; only 4 of lopo5's 20 folds are blind; all caveats of 7f to 7h carry over.
- Output: `paper/results.md`, `paper/tables.txt`, `paper/tables.json`; tests `tests/test_paper_tables.py`.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7h outcome: on 64 more designs no step had simulated, 7f's frozen threshold policies meet the tail target in 9 of 12 for the third time running (now at 39.3 DFN cycles per design, 21% saved against all-50), and 7g's refit policies meet 4 of 4 at 37.8. The new batch-relative threshold (R50) meets 4 of 4 at 40.6 cycles, the absolute ones 3 of 4 at 40.0; over all five pools by leave-one-pool-out, every drift-score rule meets 16 to 19 of 20 folds at 42 to 44 cycles, the uniform rule 19 of 20 at 48.0.
