The relative threshold does not make the flagged share more stable (H287 refuted). H285, H288 and H289 supported, H286 inconclusive (4 of 4 met, at 40.6 cycles against its 40 line), H287 refuted
- Bench: 64 DFN runs to cycle 100 through 7g's runner, about 9 minutes on 3 workers; 62 ok first time, 2 (`Ai2020` j = 50, 52) killed for memory and re-run with 1 worker, both ok (the failed attempts stay in `bench.jsonl` as `ok: false`). Tests ran before `rows` and `report`; both run once; `run.py` unchanged after the plan commit (`figure.py` added after the report).
- Fresh pool (16 designs per set): all-50 q75 0.039, 0.045, 0.055, 0.020; all-10 q75 1.6 to 3.6 times higher. Median S<sub>C</sub> on 7h sits back near the 104's on every set (`Ai2020` 0.098 against 0.057 to 0.077, and 0.120 on 7g; `Chen2020` 0.026 against 0.025 to 0.029, and 0.047 on 7g): the scale jump 7g saw did not recur.
- H285 supported (9 of 12, 39.3 cycles): misses `Chen2020` from 5zk (0.089 against 0.056 at 25 cycles), `OKane2022` from 5zz (0.072 against 0.068), `NCA_Kim2011` from 5zk (0.031 against 0.025, 22.5 cycles). `Chen2020` 5zk and `NCA_Kim2011` 5zk also missed on 7g: the two most permissive 7f policies miss every time.
- H286 inconclusive: R50 refit4 meets 4 of 4 at 40.6 cycles (19% saved), 0.6 over its line. Its margins fell back to 1.00 on two sets (no grid value met all four inner folds), and it flags 88% to 97% of training designs on three sets.
- H287 refuted: mean |flagged share on 7h &minus; on training| 0.095 for R50 against 0.069 for &theta;50. With the scale steady on 7h there was little drift to absorb, and the median of 16 designs moves the relative cut by itself.
- H288 supported (3 against 3): with four training pools the free-K1 rule picks K1 = 50 on every set anyway, so &theta;50 and &theta;-tight are the same policy here (identical rows).
- H289 supported: R50 meets 18 of 20 lopo5 folds at 42.1 cycles (16% saved). Absolute threshold 19 of 20 at 43.5 (13%), fraction 16 at 43.2, uniform 19 at 48.0 (4%).
- Reading: the triage saving has now replicated on two fresh batches nothing had simulated: 9 of 12 frozen policies met each time, at 17% (7g) and 21% (7h) fewer DFN cycles, and the same misses recur on the two most permissive policies. With more pools to set the margin, the extension length settles at 50 by itself and the threshold rules meet 19 of 20 held-out pools at 13% saved, where a uniform run length meets 19 at 4%: about three times the saving at the same hit rate. The batch-relative threshold is not needed (it does no better and needs a batch); the absolute threshold on the drift score stays the paper's rule. For the paper: report the three looks (7f reused, 7g and 7h fresh) and lopo5 as the headline, with the honest saving a seventh to a fifth of the DFN budget. Next: write the results section (5zk to 7h).
- Caveats: 16 designs per set on 7h (q75 is the 4th largest), the 4 refit4 folds are 4 tests, lopo5's 16 folds holding out an earlier pool were seen before (only the 4 holding out 7h are blind); R50's design was informed by 7g's misses; one law form and &kappa; (25.3); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/fresh7h/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `fresh7h.svg`; tests `tests/test_fresh7h.py`.

(plan, written and committed before the fresh bench ran; H285 to H289 are this entry's)

- H287 (the mechanism: scale-free flagging): the mean over sets of |flagged share on 7h &minus; flagged share on training| is smaller for R50 than for &theta;50. Refuted if larger.
- H288 (K1 = 50 is enough): refit4 &theta;50 meets on at least as many sets as refit4 &theta;-tight. Refuted if fewer.
- H289 (R50 out of batch, all pools): R50 meets at least 15 of 20 lopo5 folds. Refuted if 11 or fewer.

- Risks named now: 16 designs per set (q75 is the 4th largest); the 4 refit4 folds are 4 tests; the relative rule needs a batch (it cannot score a lone design, which the absolute rule can); median of a small pool is itself noisy; 7h's S<sub>C</sub> is fitted on all 104 while the 104's own is leave-one-out (the scale difference R50 is meant to absorb); one law form and &kappa; (25.3); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set; one PyBaMM version; modelled fade only.

Refit on all three earlier pools, the threshold rule meets 3 of 4 sets at 41.2 cycles; the uniform rule meets 4 of 4 at 47.5 (5% saved). In hindsight on each fresh pool the cheapest threshold policy costs 35.0, 32.5, 25.0, 30.0 cycles and the cheapest uniform 50, 40, 50, 50, so the forward rules keep about half of what was there to keep.

The threshold and the fraction remain tied; the fraction is immune to the score's scale drift, the threshold needs no batch. For the paper: 20% (7f) and 17 to 19% (7g) are the honest savings, with 9 of 12 met both times. Realised cost: the fresh pool's DFN cycles per design.

- H282 (refit on three pools): refit &theta;-tight meets on at least 3 of 4 sets at a mean of at most 40 cycles per design. Refuted if it meets on 1 or none, or costs more than 45.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7f outcome: a margin chosen by nested leave-one-pool-out turns the triage rule from 4 of 12 held-out pools met to 9 (absolute threshold on the drift score) or 10 (flag fraction), at 40.0 DFN cycles per design against 50 for running every design to cycle 50, a 20% saving. The uniform rule with the same margin choice always lands on 50 cycles (12 of 12, nothing saved), and adding a margin to the flag fraction alone (F+&Delta;) meets only 6. H277 and H280 supported (H280 at its 20% line), H279 refuted (threshold 9 against fraction 10), H278 inconclusive (threshold misses 3 more folds than uniform but costs 10 fewer cycles)
- Analysis: stdlib, 0.2 s, tests run before the report (`tests/test_margin7f.py`; zero-margin U-tight and F-tight reproduce 7e's 12 transfer folds), report run once; `run.py` unchanged after the plan except the H278 refutation clause, corrected to the plan's wording before the report ran.
- Zero margin (reproduces 7e and adds the threshold rule): threshold 3 of 12 met at 26.5 cycles, fraction 4 at 27.3, uniform 7 at 41.7.
- Nested margin: threshold 9 of 12 at 40.0 cycles (misses `Ai2020` 5zz at 1.08 times the target, `OKane2022` 5zz at 1.16, `NCA_Kim2011` 5zk at 1.01); fraction 10 at 40.0 (misses `Ai2020` 5zk at 1.40, 5zz at 1.08); F+&Delta; 6 at 34.2 (the extension length K1 picked at zero margin is too short on most folds, and a larger f cannot fix that); uniform 12 at 50.0 (every fold's margin ends at U50).
- H277 supported (9 of 12). H278 inconclusive (9 against 12 met; 40.0 against 50.0 cycles): the plan's two refutation clauses both need the threshold rule to cost no less, and it costs less. H279 refuted (9 against 10). H280 supported: best rule meeting at least 9 is threshold or fraction (tied at 39.97 cycles per design), 20.07% saved against U50, on the plan's 20% line.
- Inner folds: 5 of 12 outer folds found no t&prime; on the grid that met both inner folds for the threshold rule (fell back to t&prime; = 1.00), 3 of 12 for the fraction rule, 0 for uniform. The grid's most conservative point is still too permissive on `OKane2022` for the threshold rule.
- Post hoc, descriptive (after the report): the threshold rule's worst held-out miss is 1.16 times the target, the fraction rule's 1.40; uniform's worst held-out q75 is 0.80 times the target, so U50 overshoots the accuracy it was asked for by 20% on every fold. The tie at 39.97 cycles is a coincidence of two different fold-by-fold profiles (per-fold costs differ on 6 folds).
- Reading: with a margin set out of batch, triage keeps a fifth of the DFN budget and meets the held-out tail target about as often as one would accept from a 12-fold check (9 or 10 of 12), while the uniform rule, given the same margin procedure, cannot save anything: no run length shorter than 50 cycles holds across pools. The in-sample savings of 7d and 7e (33% to 61%) shrink to 20% out of batch, which is the honest number for the paper. Threshold against fraction is not settled (one fold apart); the threshold rule has the attraction that it needs no batch to rank against, so it applies to one design at a time in a sequential search. Next: the fourth fresh pool (new DFN runs to cycle 99 on 8 designs per set) to score the nested rules once more with no reuse; the paper's results section (5zk to 7f).
- Caveats: 8 to 12 designs per pool, inner folds pick on 8 to 12 designs; margin grids coarse (t&prime; to 1.00, &Delta; to 0.50); S<sub>C</sub> is 7c's leave-one-out correction fitted on all pools' drift, so pools share information (flatters both triage rules); one law form and &kappa; (25.3); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/margin7f/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `margin7f.svg`; tests `tests/test_margin7f.py`.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7f (a triage rule with an out-of-batch margin): 7e's triage policy, picked on two design pools, met the third pool's tail target in 4 of 12 folds. Does a margin chosen by nested leave-one-pool-out, or an absolute flag threshold on the drift score instead of a flag fraction, bring the held-out hit rate up while keeping the DFN cycles saved? (plan, written and committed before any rule below was applied to a held-out pool; H277 to H280 are this entry's)
- Question: 7e's next step: set the triage rule's margin out of batch, by leave-one-pool-out, and score it by held-out target misses and cycles together.
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/margin7f/run.py report`; stdlib, no new simulation, reads 7e's join of 7d's and 7b's rows through `frontier7e/run.py`'s `load`, cell 000, the 104 designs, target 1.25 &times; the pool's own all-50 q75 as 7e's H276). Four rules, each a function of training designs and a margin m:
  - U-tight: the cheapest uniform U(k) whose training q75 is at most t&prime; &times; training q75(U50); m = t&prime; in {1.25, 1.20, 1.15, 1.10, 1.05, 1.00} (most to least permissive).
  - F-tight: the cheapest 7e triage policy T(K1, f, S<sub>C</sub>) meeting t&prime; on training, same grid; applied to the held-out pool as the fraction f of that pool ranked by S<sub>C</sub> (7e's transfer).
  - F+&Delta;: the T(K1, f) picked at t&prime; = 1.25, applied with f + &Delta; (clamped at 1), &Delta; in {0, 0.05, ..., 0.50}.
  - &theta;-tight (primary): for K1 in {15, 20, 30, 40, 50} and an absolute threshold &theta; on S<sub>C</sub> = |m&#770;<sub>C</sub> &minus; 1| (candidates: each training design's S<sub>C</sub>, and +&infin; for flag none), flag every design with S<sub>C</sub> &ge; &theta;; the cheapest (K1, &theta;) meeting t&prime; on training (training cost 10 + (K1 &minus; 10) &times; flagged share; ties to the larger &theta;, then smaller K1), applied unchanged to the held-out pool.
  - No policy meeting t&prime; on training means U50. Margin choice by nested leave-one-pool-out: in each of 7e's 12 outer folds (set, held-out pool), the two training pools A, B give two inner folds (pick on A, score on B at 1.25 &times; B's all-50 q75, and the reverse); m is the most permissive grid value whose two inner folds both meet, or the most conservative if none does. The rule at that m is then picked on A &cup; B and applied to the held-out pool. Score per fold: met (held-out q75 at or below the target) and the held-out pool's realised DFN cycles per design (10 + (K1 &minus; 10) &times; the share of that pool flagged; k for U(k)). Reference rules: U50 (always meets, 50 cycles) and each rule at zero margin (U-tight and F-tight at m = 1.25 reproduce 7e's transfer, tested).
- H277 (the primary rule hits): &theta;-tight with the nested margin meets the held-out target in at least 9 of 12 folds. Refuted if 6 or fewer.
- H278 (it pays against uniform): &theta;-tight misses no more folds than U-tight with its nested margin and costs fewer mean held-out cycles over the 12 folds. Refuted if it misses more folds and costs no less, or costs more and misses no fewer.
- H279 (threshold beats fraction): &theta;-tight meets more folds than F-tight (both nested). Refuted if fewer.
- H280 (the margin is worth its cycles against always-50): the best of the four nested rules that meets at least 9 of 12 folds saves at least 20% of DFN cycles against U50 averaged over the 12 folds. Refuted if no rule meets 9, or if every one that does saves less than 10%.
- Already seen: 7e's zero-margin transfer (S<sub>C</sub> fraction 4 of 12, uniform 7 of 12) and its post hoc note that held-out targets vary fivefold across pools of one set. Not seen: any margin other than zero, any threshold rule, any inner fold.
- Risks named now: 8 to 12 designs per pool, so each inner fold picks on one pool of 8 to 12 designs and a held-out q75 is one or two designs; the margin grids are coarse and the most conservative t&prime; = 1.00 may still miss (the training q75 of U50 is not the held-out one); S<sub>C</sub> is 7c's leave-one-out correction fitted on all pools' drift (shared information across pools flatters any S<sub>C</sub> rule); a fresh fourth pool, which 7e also asked for, needs new DFN runs to cycle 99 and is not in this step.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7e outcome: in hindsight, drift-score triage is cheaper than any uniform run length at almost every tail target (strictly cheaper at 14, 18, 21 and 21 of 21 targets; mean DFN cycles saved against all-50 55%, 61%, 33%, 46% against uniform's 36%, 39%, 27%, 18% and random triage's 48%, 52%, 30%, 31%). A 30-cycle extension is not enough at 7d's target on three of four sets (`Chen2020` needs U30 at 30 cycles against 16 for 15% extended to 50; `OKane2022` and `NCA_Kim2011` cannot meet it at all with K1 &le; 30). But the policy does not transfer: picked on two design pools and applied to the third, it meets the held-out target in 4 of 12 folds, against 7 of 12 for a uniform run length picked the same way. H273 and H275 supported, H274 and H276 refuted
- Analysis: stdlib, 6 s, tests run before the report, report run once; `run.py` unchanged after the plan. Reproduces 7d: the K1 = 50 slice at t = 1.25 gives 7d's S<sub>C</sub> f* on all four sets (tested).
- H273 supported (4 of 4): T(S<sub>C</sub>) strictly cheaper than U at 14, 18, 21, 21 of 21 targets (`Ai2020`, `Chen2020`, `OKane2022`, `NCA_Kim2011`).
- H274 refuted (1 of 4 at or below; more on 3): at t = 1.25, cheapest T(S<sub>C</sub>) with K1 &le; 30 against K1 = 50: 28.0 against 32.0 (`Ai2020`, 30@0.90), 30.0 against 16.0 (`Chen2020`), unmet against 48.0 (`OKane2022`), unmet against 28.0 (`NCA_Kim2011`). The uniform q75 at k = 30 is still 1.3 to 1.8 times the k = 50 one (0.042, 0.071, 0.063, 0.040 against 0.027, 0.057, 0.040, 0.022): on three sets the 30-cycle line is too short to reach 1.25 times all-50 even for every design.
- H275 supported (4 of 4): mean saved T(S<sub>C</sub>) against T(random) 55% and 48%, 61% and 52%, 33% and 30%, 46% and 31%; oracle 70%, 70%, 41%, 49%.
- H276 refuted (4 of 12 folds met; uniform 7 of 12): S<sub>C</sub> met on `Ai2020` 5zy, `Chen2020` 5zz, `OKane2022` 5zy, `NCA_Kim2011` 5zk. Three folds where uniform met and S<sub>C</sub> missed: `Ai2020` 5zk and 5zz, `NCA_Kim2011` 5zz (q75 0.060 against target 0.031 with half the pool extended to 50: a missed design).
- Post hoc, descriptive (after the report): over the 12 folds the transferred S<sub>C</sub> policies cost 27.3 DFN cycles per design on average against 41.7 for the transferred uniform ones, and their held-out q75 averages 1.18 times the target against 0.97. Held-out targets vary fivefold across pools of one set (`Ai2020` 0.017 to 0.058), so the q75 of 8 designs (the second largest) is a noisy target in its own right.
- Reading: the frontier says triage is the right shape for a design search's fidelity budget, and that the extension has to be long: 30 cycles do not buy 50-cycle tail accuracy on these cells, so the gain comes from running few designs long, not many designs a little longer. But the savings 7d and this milestone report are in hindsight: the flagged fraction that meets the target on one batch of designs undershoots on the next, more often than a uniform run length does, because a triage policy is betting on which few designs carry the tail and a small batch's tail is one or two designs. A usable rule needs a margin set on held-out batches (a larger f than the in-sample one, or a fixed flag threshold on S<sub>C</sub> rather than a fraction), scored by out-of-batch target misses as well as cycles. Next: that rule, with the margin chosen by leave-one-pool-out and checked on a fourth fresh pool; and the paper's results section (5zk to 7e).
- Caveats: 24 to 28 designs per set and 8 to 12 per pool; costs are least over many policies in hindsight within the set (the in-sample curves), which H276 shows overstates what a forward choice keeps; S<sub>C</sub> is 7c's leave-one-out correction fitted on all pools' 99-cycle drift, so even the transfer folds share information across pools (it flatters S<sub>C</sub>, so the refutation stands); e<sub>K1</sub> from 7b's lines; one law form and &kappa; (25.3); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/frontier7e/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `frontier7e.svg`; tests `tests/test_frontier7e.py`.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7e (the triage frontier): how long should the flagged designs run, and how many DFN cycles does triage save across a range of tail-error targets rather than at one? (plan, written and committed before any policy below was computed with an extension other than 50 cycles or a target other than 1.25; H273 to H276 are this entry's)
- Question: 7d scored one policy family (flag a fraction f by 7c's drift score S<sub>C</sub>, extend those to cycle 50) at one target (1.25 times the all-50 q75). Its next step: whether the triage holds with a 30-cycle extension and a tighter target, scored as a curve of cycles saved against target.
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/frontier7e/run.py`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation, reads 7d's `triage7d/rows.json` joined with 7b's `lawfloor/rows.json` for e at k = 15, 20, 30, 40, 50): policies on each set, cell 000 primary. Uniform U(k): every design gets the k-cycle line, k in {10, 15, 20, 30, 40, 50}, cost k. Triage T(K1, f, score): the round(f n) designs with the largest score get the K1-cycle line, the rest the ten-cycle line, K1 in {15, 20, 30, 40, 50}, f on 7d's 0.05 grid, cost 10 + (K1 &minus; 10) f per design. Scores S<sub>C</sub> (primary) and random (expected q75 over 2,000 draws, `random.Random(79)`, as 7d); oracle by the design's own gain e<sub>10</sub> &minus; e<sub>K1</sub> as a ceiling. Targets t &times; q75(U(50)), t in {1.00, 1.05, ..., 2.00} (21 targets). For each family, the cost to meet a target is the least cost of any of its policies whose q75 is at or below it (hindsight within the set, as 7d's f*); unmet counts as 50 cycles. Saved = 1 &minus; cost/50; a family's curve is saved against t, and its score the mean over the 21 targets.
- H273 (triage beats uniform): T(S<sub>C</sub>) strictly cheaper than U at at least 11 of 21 targets on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on 1 or none.
- H274 (a 30-cycle extension is enough at 7d's target): at t = 1.25, the cheapest T(S<sub>C</sub>) policy with K1 &le; 30 costs no more than the cheapest with K1 = 50 on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if it costs more on at least 3.
- H275 (better than random over the whole curve): mean saved of T(S<sub>C</sub>) above T(random)'s on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if at or below on at least 3.
- H276 (the choice transfers across pools): leave one pool out (5zk, 5zy, 5zz; 12 set-pool folds): pick the cheapest T(S<sub>C</sub>) policy (K1, f) meeting t = 1.25 on the other two pools' designs (S<sub>C</sub> ranks within the training designs and, for the held-out pool, within that pool), apply it to the held-out pool, and score it against 1.25 times that pool's all-50 q75. Supported if met in at least 8 of 12 folds; refuted if in 4 or fewer. Uniform U(k) picked the same way reported alongside.
- Already seen: e at k = 10 and 50 per design and 7d's q75 curves for K1 = 50 at t = 1.25; 7b's set medians at every k. Not seen: any triage policy with K1 &lt; 50, any target other than 1.25, any per-pool policy.
- Risks named now: 24 to 28 designs per set (8 to 12 per pool, so a held-out q75 is one or two designs); taking the least cost over many policies in hindsight flatters triage more than uniform (more policies to choose from), which H276 partly checks; e<sub>K1</sub> is not monotone in K1 for some designs (7b), so a shorter extension can beat a longer one by chance; one law form and &kappa;.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7d outcome: on two of four parameter sets, simulating only the designs a ten-cycle drift score flags keeps the 50-cycle forecast's tail accuracy for about half the DFN cycles. With the real ten-cycle forecast, the upper-quartile cycle-99 lithium-loss error falls from 11.4% to within 1.25 times the all-50 figure (5.7%) once 15% of `Chen2020`'s designs are extended (68% of the all-50 cycles saved; random flags need 50%, saving 40%), and on `NCA_Kim2011` once 45% are (44% saved against random's 20%). On `OKane2022` no score saves anything (95% must be extended, as for random flags), and on `Ai2020` it saves 36% against random's 24%. With every input measured, the cycle-2 plating depth alone beats the full correction on LG M50 (20% extended against 60%). H269, H271 and H272 supported, H270 inconclusive
- Analysis: stdlib, 2 s, tests run before the report, report run once; `run.py` unchanged after the plan. Reproduces 7b and 7c: each design's ten-cycle error equals 7c's line error on all 104 designs and both cells (to 12 places, tested); the all-10 and all-50 policies are 7b's k = 10 and k = 50 lines.
- H269 supported (3 of 4): q75 e<sub>000</sub> at f = 0.25, S<sub>C</sub> against random: `Ai2020` 0.043 and 0.043 (S<sub>C</sub> marginally worse, 0.0434 against 0.0429), `Chen2020` 0.066 and 0.087, `OKane2022` 0.078 and 0.082, `NCA_Kim2011` 0.060 and 0.066; oracle 0.032, 0.066, 0.070, 0.042.
- H270 inconclusive (2 of 4 at f* &le; 0.5, 1 above 0.75): S<sub>C</sub>'s f* 0.55, 0.15, 0.95, 0.45 (cycles saved 36%, 68%, 4%, 44%), against targets 0.034, 0.071, 0.050, 0.028 (all-10 q75 0.045, 0.114, 0.100, 0.075; all-50 0.027, 0.057, 0.040, 0.022).
- H271 supported (3 of 4): random flags' f* 0.70, 0.50, 0.95, 0.75 (24%, 40%, 4%, 20% saved); `OKane2022` a tie. Oracle f* 0.25, 0.10, 0.85, 0.40 (60%, 72%, 12%, 48%), so S<sub>C</sub> is within 0.05 of the oracle on `Chen2020` and `NCA_Kim2011`.
- H272 supported: cell 111 f* on `Chen2020` and `OKane2022`, S<sub>1</sub> 0.20 and 0.20 (64% saved), S<sub>C</sub> 0.60 and 0.60 (32%), random 0.50 and 0.45, oracle 0.10 and 0.10. For the real forecast (cell 000) S<sub>1</sub> is worse than S<sub>C</sub> on three sets (f* 0.50, 0.30, 1.00, 0.80).
- Reported: the q75 curves are not monotone in f: extending a design does not always help (e<sub>50</sub> &gt; e<sub>10</sub> on 4, 3, 3 and 1 designs of the four sets, cell 000), so on `Chen2020` S<sub>C</sub>'s q75 sits at 0.066 from f = 0.2 to 0.9 and only reaches the all-50 0.057 at f = 1; on `Ai2020` the q75 at f = 0.9 (0.022) is below the all-50 one (0.027).
- Reading: a design search does not have to pick between ten cycles for every candidate and fifty. 7c's drift score, known at cycle 10, ranks which designs' ten-cycle forecasts will be badly wrong well enough to buy most of the 50-cycle tail accuracy with a fraction of the cycles on `Chen2020` and `NCA_Kim2011`, and it does no worse than random elsewhere. Where it fails (`OKane2022`), the oracle fails too (85% extended): the tail there is spread over most designs, so there is nothing to triage. The score that flags best depends on what the forecast's error is made of: with measured inputs the error is the law's drift and the plating depth alone flags it (H272); with the real forecast the inputs' error adds in, and the full correction, which picks its observable per fold, does better. Next: the paper's results section from 5zk to 7d (error budget, the law floor, the drift and this triage as its four tables); then whether the triage holds with a 30-cycle extension and a tighter target, scored as a curve of cycles saved against target rather than one point.
- Caveats: 24 to 28 designs per set, so q75 is one design's error and f* moves by whole designs; one target (1.25 times all-50) and a 0.05 grid; the flags rank within a set, a batch decision a live search makes only once its ten-cycle runs are in; S<sub>C</sub> is 7c's leave-one-out correction, so its fit used the other designs' 99-cycle drift; O1 is a model internal; one law form and &kappa; (25.3); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/triage7d/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `triage7d.svg`; tests `tests/test_triage7d.py`.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7d (flag and extend): if only the designs a ten-cycle score flags are simulated to cycle 50, how much of the 50-cycle forecast's tail accuracy does a design search keep, and how many DFN cycles does it save? (plan, written and committed before any triage policy below was computed on these designs; H269 to H272 are this entry's)
- Question: 7c found that cycle-2 plating depth and the bend of lithium loss predict which designs' fade-law rate drifts, and post hoc that a correction built on them halves the upper-quartile ten-cycle error, while 7b found that a 50-cycle line removes most of the real forecast's error (cell 000 set medians 0.025, 0.056, 0.067, 0.040 at k = 10 against 0.010, 0.027, 0.021, 0.014 at k = 50). 7c's next step: a decision rule that sends only flagged designs to a longer simulation, scored on DFN cycles saved at a tail-error target.
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/triage7d/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation): join 7b's `lawfloor/rows.json` (e at k = 10 and 50, cells 000 and 111) and 7c's `drift7c/rows.json` (m&#770;<sub>C</sub>, O1) on (cond, j, pool), all 104 designs. Each design's forecast costs k DFN cycles. Policy F(f, score): within a set, the fraction f of designs with the largest score get the 50-cycle line (e<sub>50</sub>), the rest keep the ten-cycle line (e<sub>10</sub>); cost per design 10 + 40 f. Scores, all known at cycle 10: S<sub>C</sub> = |m&#770;<sub>C</sub> &minus; 1| (7c's leave-one-out drift correction for that cell, primary), S<sub>1</sub> = |O1 &minus; median O1 of the set's other designs|. Controls: R(f), random flags (expected quantile over 2,000 draws, `random.Random(79)`); O(f), oracle flags by the design's own gain e<sub>10</sub> &minus; e<sub>50</sub> (a ceiling). f on the grid 0, 0.05, ..., 1 (flag count round(f n)). Tail metric: q75 of e over the set (7c's `quant`, index int(0.75 n)); target per set and cell: 1.25 times the q75 of the all-50 policy. f* is the smallest grid f whose q75 meets the target; cycles saved = 1 &minus; (10 + 40 f*)/50. Primary cell 000 (the real forecast); cell 111 reported with the same rule.
- H269 (the score finds the tail): at f = 0.25, F(S<sub>C</sub>)'s q75 e<sub>000</sub> below R(0.25)'s on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on 1 or none.
- H270 (it saves cycles): F(S<sub>C</sub>)'s f* &le; 0.5 (at least 40% of the all-50 cycles saved) on at least 3 of 4 sets, cell 000. Refuted if f* &gt; 0.75 on at least 3.
- H271 (better than random): F(S<sub>C</sub>)'s f* below R's f* on at least 3 of 4 sets, cell 000. Refuted if above or equal on at least 3.
- H272 (plating depth alone suffices on LG M50): F(S<sub>1</sub>)'s f* &le; F(S<sub>C</sub>)'s on both `Chen2020` and `OKane2022`, cell 111. Refuted if above on both.
- Already seen: e<sub>10</sub>, e<sub>50</sub> per design (7b), m&#770;<sub>C</sub> and O1 per design and their correlations with m (7c), 7c's post hoc tail quantiles under its correction. Not seen: any score's ranking against e<sub>10</sub> &minus; e<sub>50</sub>, any triage policy, any f*.
- Risks named now: 24 to 28 designs per set, so q75 is the 7th-largest error or so and moves by whole designs; a design whose drift is large but whose 50-cycle line is no better gains nothing from extension; S<sub>C</sub> predicts the drift of the law under measured inputs (cell 111) better than the real forecast's whole error; the target (1.25 times all-50) is one choice, and f* on a 0.05 grid is coarse.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7c outcome: ten cycles do tell which LG M50 designs will drift. With every input measured, each design's drift multiplier m tracks where the plating sits at cycle 2 (Spearman &rho; = 0.80 on `Chen2020` and `OKane2022`, Holm-adjusted permutation p = 0.0003; plating nearer the separator, faster rate) and the bend of lithium loss itself (&rho; = &minus;0.73, &minus;0.72). A correction built on it does not lower the set-median floor (1.8% to 2.3%, 1.9% to 2.1%), because the typical design drifts by 4%, about the correction's own error, but post hoc it halves the upper quartile (8.0% to 3.5%, 7.3% to 3.0%). On the real forecast it beats the line on all four sets. H265 and H268 supported, H266 refuted, H267 inconclusive
- Analysis: stdlib, 15 s (`rows`, 4 cores), tests run before the report, report run once; `run.py` unchanged after the plan apart from the post hoc `posthoc` subcommand added after the report. Reproduces 7b: the line's e at k = 10 equals 7b's on all 104 designs and cells (to 12 places, tested); the design's own m reproduces cycle 99 (e<sub>own m</sub> 0.000 at every set median, both cells).
- H265 supported: O1 (cycle-2 plating depth) &rho; = 0.796 (`Chen2020`) and 0.797 (`OKane2022`), Holm p 0.0003 on both; O3 &minus;0.726 and &minus;0.715 (Holm p 0.0006); O2 (the depth's change to cycle 10) &minus;0.01 and &minus;0.08. On `Ai2020` &rho; 0.37, &minus;0.27, &minus;0.34 (Holm p 0.24); `NCA_Kim2011` &minus;0.08, &minus;0.18, &minus;0.31 (none significant).
- H266 refuted (C/L 1.30 and 1.07): set median e<sub>111</sub> L, M, C: `Chen2020` 0.018, 0.021, 0.023; `OKane2022` 0.019, 0.022, 0.021. C beats L on 14 and 16 of 28 designs. H267 inconclusive (C below M on `OKane2022` only).
- H268 supported (4 of 4): e<sub>000</sub> L to C 0.025 to 0.023 (`Ai2020`), 0.056 to 0.055, 0.067 to 0.046, 0.040 to 0.014 (`NCA_Kim2011`, 22 of 24 designs). For cell 000 every fold picks O2 on LG M50 and O1 on the other two sets.
- Reported: m (cell 111) set medians 1.232, 0.985, 0.994, 0.904 (IQRs 1.21 to 1.27, 0.95 to 1.02, 0.94 to 1.03, 0.87 to 0.98). On `Ai2020` the constant drift M is the best ten-cycle forecast found so far for cell 111 (0.007, against 7b's borrowed bend 0.048 and C's 0.018): its drift is shared, as 7b found. Per pool, C beats L on LG M50 in the two fresh pools (0.029 and 0.026 against 0.033 and 0.028 for 5zy; 0.026 and 0.019 against 0.032 and 0.034 for 5zz) and ties in 5zk.
- Post hoc, descriptive (after the report, not hypothesised; `posthoc.txt`): e<sub>111</sub> quantiles L to C, `Chen2020` q75 0.080 to 0.035, q90 0.101 to 0.065; `OKane2022` q75 0.073 to 0.030, q90 0.090 to 0.053; the max is unchanged (0.117 to 0.113). m's spread is heavy-tailed (sd 0.12 against an IQR of &plusmn;0.04): C cuts its leave-one-out RMSE from 0.130 to 0.094 and 0.116 to 0.084 but not its median absolute error (0.043, 0.043; 0.050, 0.037). O1's correlation holds inside each pool (&rho; 0.56, 0.86, 1.00 on `Chen2020`; 0.52, 0.90, 1.00 on `OKane2022`, n = 8 to 12), so it is not a pool artefact.
- Reading: 7b's per-design drift on LG M50 is not noise. Where the plating sits at cycle 2 tells, before cycle 11, which designs' law rate will speed up or slow down over the next 90 cycles, and by roughly how much. Most designs drift too little (about 4%) for any ten-cycle correction to beat the line, which is why the median floor stays near 2%; a quarter drift by 10 to 25%, and those are the ones the correction catches, halving the upper-quartile error. For a design search that is the useful half: the risk of a badly wrong forecast is concentrated in designs whose cycle-2 plating sits far from the set's typical depth, and those can be flagged or simulated longer. On `Ai2020` the drift is shared (a constant m does best), on `NCA_Kim2011` it is small and unexplained. Next: the paper's results section from 5zk to 7c (the error budget, the floor and this tail correction as its tables); then a decision rule that sends only the flagged designs to a longer simulation, scored on DFN calls saved at a tail-error target.
- Caveats: 24 to 28 designs per set and three observables; C chooses its observable inside each fold; the quantile result is post hoc; O1 is a model internal (node plating) a real cell exposes only through a diagnosis; one law form and one &kappa; (25.3); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/drift7c/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `posthoc.txt`, `drift7c.svg`; tests `tests/test_drift7c.py`.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7c (the per-design drift): does a ten-cycle observable predict how far each design's law rate drifts by cycle 99, and does a correction built on it close the LG M50 floor that 7b found a longer window and a borrowed bend cannot? (plan, written and committed before any observable below was correlated with any drift on these designs; H265 to H268 are this entry's)
- Question: 7b found that on LG M50, with every input measured (cell 111), R(L) grows at a constant rate per cycle at the set median, but each design's rate drifts a few per cent either way over 99 cycles, and that drift, not a bend, is the 1.8 to 1.9% ten-cycle floor. 7b's next step: whether that per-design drift tracks something visible in ten cycles (where the plating sits, how it moves, how lithium loss itself bends).
- Target, per design and cell: the drift multiplier m, defined by R(99) = a + 10 s + 89 s m, with (a, s) the ten-cycle line through R(L<sub>n</sub>), n = 2..10, and R(99) = R(L<sub>DFN</sub>(99)) on the cell's table (m = 1 is 7a's/7b's line L; the forecast at m is inverted as L is). Ten-cycle observables, fixed now: O1, the plating-weighted depth (centroid, 1 = separator) of cycle 2's node plating (5zk's `centroid`); O2, its change from cycle 2 to cycle 10; O3, the bend of lithium loss itself, ln((L<sub>10</sub> &minus; L<sub>6</sub>)/(L<sub>6</sub> &minus; L<sub>2</sub>)).
- Method (`experiments/drift7c/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation): 7b's `tables` imported unchanged (cells 000 and 111), all 104 `ok` x-resolved designs (5zk 40, 5zy 32, 5zz 32), &kappa; = 25.3, e = |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|. Spearman &rho; of m (cell 111) with O1 to O3 within each set; two-sided permutation p (10,000 shuffles, `random.Random(73)`), Holm over the three observables per set. Corrections at ten cycles, leave one design out within the set: C, m&#770; from a least-squares line of m on the one observable with the largest |&rho;| on the other designs (chosen inside each fold); M, m&#770; = the other designs' median m (a constant drift, the control: what a shared bend gives). Cell 000 gets the same with its own m.
- H265 (the drift is predictable from ten cycles): on both LG M50 sets (`Chen2020`, `OKane2022`) some observable has |&rho;| &ge; 0.5 with Holm-adjusted p &lt; 0.05. Refuted if no observable reaches |&rho;| &ge; 0.3 on either set.
- H266 (a correction closes the floor): C's set median e<sub>111</sub> &le; 0.75 times L's on both LG M50 sets. Refuted if C's is above L's on both.
- H267 (beyond a shared drift): C's set median e<sub>111</sub> below M's on both LG M50 sets. Refuted if above on both.
- H268 (it carries to the real ten-cycle forecast): C's set median e<sub>000</sub> below L's on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on 1 or none.
- Already seen: 7b's late/early slope ratios (set medians and IQRs, cell 111 and 000), its finding that the drift's sign varies by design, and 5zk's centroids on its 40 designs. Not seen: m itself, O2, O3, or any correlation of any observable with any drift.
- Risks named now: 24 to 28 designs per set, so |&rho;| = 0.5 is near the detection limit; choosing the observable inside each fold costs power; O1 to O3 are model internals (node plating, lithium loss) a real cell exposes only through a diagnosis; m is a ratio to the ten-cycle slope and inherits that slope's noise.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7b outcome: on LG M50 the law floor is not a bend and a longer window does not remove it. With every input measured, the set-median cycle-99 error moves from 1.8% to 1.6% (`Chen2020`) and 1.9% to 1.4% (`OKane2022`) as the law's line window grows from 10 to 50 cycles, while the real ten-cycle forecast falls from 5.6% to 2.7% and 6.7% to 2.1%: 5zm's longer-window gain on LG M50 comes from the inputs, not the law. The measured R(L) is a straight line in cycle number at the set median (late slope 0.99 times the ten-cycle slope) but scatters &plusmn;5 to 10% design by design, so a bend borrowed from other designs makes it worse (T beats L on 15 of 28 and 5 of 28). `Ai2020` is the exception: its R bends upward on every design (late slope 1.24 to 1.34 times), and the borrowed bend cuts its floor from 8.3% to 4.8% (23 of 24 designs). H261 supported, H263 inconclusive, H260, H262 and H264 refuted
- Analysis: stdlib, 15 s (`rows`, 4 cores), tests run before the report, report run once; `run.py` unchanged after the plan apart from the post hoc `posthoc` subcommand added after the report. Reproduces 7a: the line at k = 10 equals 5zu's and 7a's cells 000 and 111 on all 104 designs (to 1e&minus;15, tested).
- H260 refuted (1 of 4): set median e<sub>111</sub> at k = 10 and 50: `Ai2020` 0.083 and 0.020, `Chen2020` 0.018 and 0.016, `OKane2022` 0.019 and 0.014, `NCA_Kim2011` 0.011 and 0.007. On LG M50 the floor is flat to k = 40 (0.018 to 0.024, 0.017 to 0.021).
- H261 supported (4 of 4): the free quadratic through cycles 2..10 is worse than the line everywhere (e<sub>111</sub> 0.129, 0.082, 0.100, &infin; on `NCA_Kim2011`, where its ten-cycle &gamma; of about &minus;0.015 per cycle puts the parabola's peak near cycle 33, so 14 of 24 forecasts cannot be inverted at cycle 99); the ten-cycle &gamma;'s set median is 9 to 100 times the 99-cycle one, with the opposite sign on `NCA_Kim2011`.
- H262 refuted (1 of 4): T's e<sub>111</sub> 0.048, 0.021, 0.026, 0.011 against L's 0.083, 0.018, 0.019, 0.011. H263 inconclusive (0.021, 0.026: neither &le; 0.015 nor both &gt; 0.03). H264 refuted (1 of 4): T's e<sub>000</sub> 0.027, 0.079, 0.079, 0.025 against NS 0.025, 0.056, 0.067, 0.040 (`NCA_Kim2011` the one win, 23 of 24 designs).
- Reported: O, the design's own 99-cycle &gamma; with the ten-cycle line's a and s, reaches only 0.058, 0.019, 0.017, 0.013 for e<sub>111</sub>: even a known quadratic bend does not remove the LG M50 floor, because the ten-cycle slope itself is off. &gamma; set medians (cell 111, per cycle): 0.00088, &minus;0.00015, &minus;0.00007, 0.00012, with IQRs spanning zero on every set except `Ai2020`. e<sub>000</sub> falls with the window on every set (k = 50: 0.010, 0.027, 0.021, 0.014). Per pool, 5zk's 40 have the lowest LG M50 floor at every window, as 7a found at k = 10.
- Post hoc, descriptive (after the report, not hypothesised; `posthoc.txt`): a line through all of cycles 2..99 leaves e<sub>111</sub> 0.003 to 0.005, so R(L) with measured inputs is nearly linear in cycle number over the whole run; a ten-cycle line through cycles 11..20 instead of 2..10 does no better (0.019, 0.019 on LG M50), so the first cycles are not a transient to skip. Slope of R over cycles 51..99 against 2..10, cell 111: `Ai2020` 1.27 (IQR 1.24 to 1.34), `Chen2020` 0.99 (0.90 to 1.03), `OKane2022` 0.99 (0.89 to 1.05), `NCA_Kim2011` 0.94 (0.86 to 1.06). For the real forecast (cell 000) the late slope departs further and in both directions (`NCA_Kim2011` 0.69, `OKane2022` 1.08).
- Reading: the law is right on LG M50 in the sense that matters: with true inputs R(L) grows at a constant rate per cycle to within a few per cent over 99 cycles. What a ten-cycle line cannot know is each design's own few-per-cent drift in that rate, and the sign of the drift varies by design, so neither a set bend nor a longer line removes it until the window covers most of the run. That puts the ten-cycle forecast's irreducible error on LG M50 at about 2 to 3% (set medians 1.8 and 1.9% over all 104 designs, 2.8 to 3.4% on the fresh pools, against the 0.5% a line through all 99 cycles leaves), and the fresh pools show it is not a window problem: their floor at k = 50 is still 1.8 to 3.7%. The large window gains of 5zm on the real forecast are input gains: a longer line averages out the node lever's and the drift's errors (cell 000's slopes drift by 10 to 30%). `Ai2020` has a real, shared bend (its R accelerates by a quarter over the run on every design), which is why measured inputs looked worse there in 5zu and 7a: a ten-cycle line under-reads its acceleration, and a borrowed bend recovers about half of it. For a design search: fit the law's line through 30 to 50 cycles, as before, and expect it to fix the inputs rather than the law; on `Ai2020`-like cells, add a bend borrowed from a few fully simulated designs. Next: the paper's results section from 5zk to 7b (the error budget and this floor as its two tables); then whether the per-design rate drift on LG M50 tracks a ten-cycle observable (SEI share of the loss, the separator-node plating fraction).
- Caveats: one law form (line or quadratic in n) and one &kappa; (25.3); the quadratic's &gamma; is a crude bend model; T borrows within the set from pools of 24 to 28; inputs are model internals with cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/lawfloor/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `posthoc.txt`, `lawfloor.svg`; tests `tests/test_lawfloor.py`.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7b (the law floor): how does the all-measured forecast error e<sub>111</sub> fall as the law's line window grows from 10 to 50 cycles, and does a curved term, its bend borrowed from the set's other designs, close it at ten cycles? (plan, written and committed before any window other than 10, 30 and 50 cycles or any curved term was computed on these designs; H260 to H264 are this entry's)
- Question: 7a found that with every input measured (cell 111) the ten-cycle forecast still misses cycle-99 lithium loss by 3.2 to 3.4% on held-out LG M50 designs, more than half of the forecast's error there: the law's own term, a straight line in R(L) = &int; exp(&kappa; du) Q<sub>0</sub>/Q dl against cycle number through cycles 2..10, extrapolated to cycle 99. 7a's next step: the law floor itself on all 104 x-resolved designs, how e<sub>111</sub> falls as the window grows, and whether a curved term (5zp's bend) closes it at ten cycles.
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/lawfloor/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation): 5zu's cell tables (`experiments/budget/run.py`, `_row`'s construction, cells 000 and 111) on every `ok` design of `depth` (5zk, 40), `freshnode` (5zy, 32) and `rangeline` (5zz, 32); &kappa; = 25.3; e = |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|. (a) Window: the line through cycles 2..k, k &isin; {10, 15, 20, 30, 40, 50}. (b) Curved term: R(n) = a + s (n + &gamma; n<sup>2</sup>) through cycles 2..10 with &gamma; fixed, a and s by least squares, read at cycle 99 and inverted as the line is. Variants: L (line, 7a's), Q (free quadratic, &gamma; fitted on cycles 2..10 alone), T (transferred: &gamma; = median over the same set's other designs, leave one design out, of each design's c/b from a quadratic a + b n + c n<sup>2</sup> fitted to its own cell table's R(L<sub>n</sub>) over cycles 2..99), O (oracle: the design's own &gamma; from cycles 2..99, a ceiling, not a forecast). Each cell uses its own tables' &gamma; (T for cell 000 borrows cell-000 bends). A forecast that cannot be inverted (R beyond the table) counts as e = &infin; in medians. Primary: set medians over all 104 designs (24, 28, 28, 24); reported per pool too.
- Already seen: e<sub>111</sub> at k = 10 on all 104 (7a: set medians 0.083, 0.018, 0.019, 0.011 for `Ai2020`, `Chen2020`, `OKane2022`, `NCA_Kim2011`); 5zm's and 5zr's 30- and 50-cycle errors for other cells on 5zk's 40 (e.g. 5zr's e<sub>A50</sub> 0.032, 0.020, 0.017, 0.011), which suggest longer windows help; 5zp's statement that the law bends even with the true rise. Not seen: e<sub>111</sub> at any k &gt; 10, any &gamma;, any curved forecast.
- H260 (a longer window removes the floor): set median e<sub>111</sub> at k = 50 &le; half its k = 10 value on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on 1 or none.
- H261 (the bend is not identifiable from ten cycles): Q's set median e<sub>111</sub> above L's on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on 1 or none.
- H262 (a borrowed bend closes part of the floor): T's set median e<sub>111</sub> below L's on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on 1 or none.
- H263 (it closes the LG M50 floor): T's set median e<sub>111</sub> &le; 0.015 on both `Chen2020` and `OKane2022`. Refuted if above 0.03 on both.
- H264 (it carries to the real ten-cycle forecast): T's set median e<sub>000</sub> below L's (NS) on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on 1 or none.
- Reported, not hypothesised: the per-set &gamma; spread (median, IQR), O's ceiling, e<sub>000</sub> against k, and T's wins over L design by design.
- Risks named now: a quadratic in n extrapolated 89 cycles past a ten-cycle fit amplifies any error in &gamma;; &gamma; may differ by design within a set as 5zw's node gains did; on `Ai2020` cell 111 is already worse than cell 000, so a closer law may not help e<sub>000</sub> there.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7a outcome: on the 64 held-out designs every direction of 5zu's error budget replicates, but its sizes do not. The node lever's OCP rise is still the largest input on `OKane2022` and `NCA_Kim2011`, the drift on `Chen2020`, the capacity factor is spent everywhere, and on `Ai2020` measuring the inputs still makes the forecast worse (2.9% to 8.3%). But with every input measured, the forecast still misses cycle-99 lithium loss by 3.4% on `OKane2022` and 3.2% on `Chen2020`, against 1.5% and 1.4% on 5zk's 40: out of sample, more than half of the ten-cycle error on LG M50 (set median shares 0.57 and 0.62) is the law's own term, not any input. Both fresh pools agree on the LG M50 floor (e<sub>111</sub> 0.033 and 0.032 on `Chen2020`, 0.028 and 0.034 on `OKane2022`). On `NCA_Kim2011` the lever is the error in every pool (&phi;<sub>M</sub> 0.038, largest on 14 of 16; e<sub>100</sub> 0.006). Paper table, all 104 designs (24, 28, 28, 24): e<sub>000</sub> 0.025, 0.056, 0.067, 0.040; e<sub>111</sub> 0.083, 0.018, 0.019, 0.011; &phi;<sub>M</sub> &minus;0.017, 0.009, 0.019, 0.035; &phi;<sub>D</sub> &minus;0.025, 0.016, 0.008, &minus;0.000; &phi;<sub>q</sub> &minus;0.004, 0.000, 0.000, 0.001 (`experiments/heldbudget/results.txt`).

(2) Per-design e<sub>111</sub> on the two LG M50 sets, 5zk's 24 against the fresh 32: Mann&ndash;Whitney two-sided p = 0.028 (normal approximation), per set p = 0.08 (`Chen2020`) and 0.16 (`OKane2022`); `Ai2020` 0.95, `NCA_Kim2011` 0.58. So 5zk's draw had a lower law floor than either fresh pool, by more than chance would usually give on LG M50, and nothing in how it was drawn (`random.Random(18)`) selects for that.
- Reading: the budget's ranking of inputs is a property of the cell, and it survives designs that never informed it: lever on `OKane2022` and `NCA_Kim2011`, drift on `Chen2020`, nothing from the capacity factor, cancellation on `Ai2020`. Its sizes were flattering. On fresh LG M50 designs the measurable inputs together close 2.3 (`OKane2022`) and 3.2 (`Chen2020`) points of a 5.7 to 6.8% error, and the rest is the law: a straight line in R against cycle number through cycles 2..10 at &kappa; = 25.3, which 5zo found drifts by 9 to 13% over 100 cycles even with the true lever. That is why 5zv to 5zz's lever fixes stopped paying off on fresh designs (5zz: the measured-OCP ceiling beats NS on only 14 of 32): a perfect lever lands on the law floor, and on fresh designs the floor is about half the error. For a design search the advice is unchanged and now better founded: fit the law's line through 30 to 50 cycles (5zm), which shortens the extrapolation the floor comes from. For the paper: the 104-design table is the error budget, with the in-sample and held-out columns side by side. Next: the law floor itself, on all 104 designs: how e<sub>111</sub> falls as the line's window grows from 10 to 50 cycles, and whether a curved term (5zp's bend) closes it at ten cycles; then the paper's results section from 5zk to 7a.
- Caveats: 16 held-out designs per set from the same pools, chemistry, current and Sobol space; H256 and H258 leaned on cells 000 and 100 already seen; Shapley medians need not sum to the gap's median; the floor share is a median of per-design ratios and exceeds 1 where measuring makes things worse (`Ai2020`); the post hoc test is two-sided, unplanned and approximate; inputs are model internals with cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/heldbudget/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `rounded.txt`, `heldbudget.svg`; tests `tests/test_heldbudget.py`.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 5zz outcome: on a second fresh pool of 32 DFN designs the thickness line does not hold. Fitted on all 72 earlier designs and used only inside their thickness range (29 of 32 fresh designs are), it beats the uniform node lever's set median on `NCA_Kim2011` only (5.3% to 3.7%) and is worse on `Ai2020` (1.2% to 3.4%), `Chen2020` (4.5% to 4.7%) and `OKane2022` (5.1% to 6.2%); design by design it wins 14 of 29 in range. More training designs do improve the line itself (pooled beats 5zk-only on 3 of 4 sets), but not enough. On this pool even the measured-OCP ceiling beats the uniform lever on only 14 of 32 designs, so outside `NCA_Kim2011` the node lever is not what limits the ten-cycle forecast. H254 supported, H252, H253 and H255 refuted
- Bench: 32 DFN runs to cycle 100, about 6 minutes on 3 workers, all 32 ok first time, committed at full precision (6.8 MB). Tests ran before `rows` and `report`; both were run once; nothing in `run.py` changed after the plan commit. The figure script was written while the bench ran.
- Pooled training: 16, 20, 20, 16 designs; Spearman(L<sub>n</sub>, h<sub>sep</sub>) &minus;0.29, 0.40, 0.21, 0.70 (as listed in the plan). Fresh designs in range: 6, 8, 8, 7 of 8.
- H252 refuted (1 of 4): set median e<sub>NR</sub> against e<sub>NS</sub>: 0.034 against 0.012 (`Ai2020`), 0.047 against 0.045 (`Chen2020`), 0.062 against 0.051 (`OKane2022`), 0.037 against 0.053 (`NCA_Kim2011`).
- H253 refuted: NL beats NS on 14 of the 29 in-range designs (one-sided sign test p = 0.64).
- H254 supported (3 of 4): set median e<sub>NL</sub> against e<sub>NL40</sub>: 0.034 against 0.026 (`Ai2020`, worse), 0.047 against 0.054, 0.062 against 0.065, 0.047 against 0.048.
- H255 refuted: on `OKane2022` e<sub>NR</sub> 0.062 is above e<sub>NS</sub> 0.051 (halfway mark (0.051 + 0.046)/2).
- Reported: set medians e<sub>NT</sub> 0.023, 0.081, 0.061, 0.045; e<sub>C</sub> 0.085, 0.070, 0.046, 0.007; e<sub>Ts</sub> 0.013, 0.076, 0.058, 0.023. NL beats NS on 2, 5, 3, 4 of 8 designs; NT on 1, 2, 4, 7. Worst fresh design under NS, NL, NR: 14.7, 19.4, 14.7% (`Ai2020`); 13.3, 8.9, 8.9% (`Chen2020`); 10.3, 8.9, 8.9% (`OKane2022`); 8.4, 8.8, 8.8% (`NCA_Kim2011`): restricting to the range removes the extrapolation tail on `Ai2020` and the line trims the worst design on `Chen2020` and `OKane2022`, but the medians move the wrong way. Lever errors N, T, L40, L, Ts (mV): 6.14, 2.50, 4.55, 2.51, 2.51; 4.28, 2.37, 4.26, 4.86, 1.52; 6.33, 5.49, 5.01, 5.38, 2.01; 25.97, 17.88, 21.49, 20.78, 1.27. 5zy's fourfold lever gain on `NCA_Kim2011` (19.9 to 4.9 mV) did not replicate (26.0 to 20.8).
- Post hoc, descriptive (computed after the report from the committed rows, not hypothesised): the measured-OCP ceiling C beats NS on 14 of 32 designs here and 23 of 32 in 5zy (37 of 64); on this pool it is worse than NS at the set median on `Ai2020` (8.5% against 1.2%) and `Chen2020` (7.0% against 4.5%) and beats it on all 8 `NCA_Kim2011` designs. Over both fresh pools NL beats NS on 33 of 64 designs (p = 0.45).
- Reading: 5zy's set-level gains for the thickness line were mostly the luck of one draw. On a second draw from the same pools, with more training designs and the extrapolation guard 5zy asked for, the line loses to the uniform lever at the median on three of four sets and is a coin flip design by design. The reason is upstream of the profile: replacing the uniform lever with the design's own measured anode OCP rise (C) does not reliably improve the forecast either outside `NCA_Kim2011`, so on `Ai2020`, `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` the ten-cycle error left after 5zm is dominated by other terms (the line extrapolation of the plating exponent and the law's other inputs), and errors in the node lever partly cancel them in either direction. `NCA_Kim2011` is the exception throughout 5zv to 5zz: the lever is its error (C 0.7%), and a profile helps it on both fresh pools. For a design search: do not use a borrowed node-gain profile, except possibly on cells where the measured lever dominates; fit the law's line through 30 to 50 cycles. This closes the node-profile thread (5zv to 5zz). Next: the paper's error budget on all 104 x-resolved designs (5zk's 40 and the two fresh pools), splitting the ten-cycle error into lever, exponent and law terms, to name what limits the forecast on the three sets where the lever does not.
- Caveats: 8 fresh designs per set; the same pools, chemistry, current and Sobol space as the training designs; `Ai2020`'s pooled Spearman is negative (named in the plan); node OCPs and stoichiometries are model internals; cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/rangeline/bench.jsonl`, `rows.json`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rangeline.svg`; tests `tests/test_rangeline.py`.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zy outcome: on 32 fresh DFN designs the profiles learned on 5zk's 40 still help, but by less, and the pre-registered gate fires only where it matters least. The thickness line has the lowest set-median ten-cycle forecast error of every profile on three of four sets (`OKane2022` 7.2% to 5.6%, `Chen2020` 7.5% to 6.5%, `NCA_Kim2011` 3.3% to 2.5%; `Ai2020` 4.4% to 5.0%), yet the gate (training Spearman &ge; 0.5) fires on `NCA_Kim2011` only (0.73; `OKane2022` 0.46), so the gated forecast leaves `OKane2022` at the uniform lever. 5zw's median profile replicates on `OKane2022` (7.2% to 6.2%). Design by design no profile beats the uniform lever reliably (NL 19 of 32, NK 17, NT 16). H248 and H251 supported, H249 and H250 inconclusive
- Bench: 32 DFN runs to cycle 100, about 5 minutes on 3 workers; `Ai2020` j = 30 was killed for memory (exit &minus;9) and finished on a single-worker retry, so all 32 designs are in. Rows (49 s, 4 cores) and the report were run once after tests passed. Then, before any reading, one change was tried and undone: rounding `bench.jsonl` to 7 significant digits (as 5zk did) moved `Chen2020`'s medians by up to 0.6 points (e<sub>NK</sub> 7.0 to 7.6%), because per-cycle increments are differences of cumulative totals; the bench was re-run at full precision (2 workers, 32 of 32 ok) and reproduced the first report character for character, and that full-precision file is the one committed (6.8 MB). So 5zk's rounded file is a reproducibility risk for any step that differences its cumulative totals; 5zk to 5zx's printed numbers came from the same rounded file they were run on, so they reproduce, but they carry that rounding.
- Gate (training designs, computed after the plan): Spearman(L<sub>n</sub>, h<sub>sep</sub>) &minus;0.45 (`Ai2020`), 0.42 (`Chen2020`), 0.46 (`OKane2022`), 0.73 (`NCA_Kim2011`). The gate fires on `NCA_Kim2011` only; 5zx's figure had suggested `OKane2022` too.
- H248 supported (1 of 1 gated set): `NCA_Kim2011` set median e<sub>NG</sub> = e<sub>NL</sub> 0.025 against e<sub>NS</sub> 0.033.
- H249 inconclusive: `OKane2022` e<sub>NL</sub> 0.056, below e<sub>NS</sub> 0.072 but above the halfway mark (0.072 + 0.015)/2 = 0.044.
- H250 inconclusive: NL beats NS on 6 of the 8 gated designs (one-sided sign test p = 0.145; the plan named 12 of 16 if two sets were gated).
- H251 supported: `OKane2022` e<sub>NT</sub> 0.062 against e<sub>NS</sub> 0.072.
- Reported: set medians e<sub>NS</sub>, e<sub>NT</sub>, e<sub>NK</sub>, e<sub>NL</sub>, e<sub>C</sub>, e<sub>Ts</sub>: `Ai2020` 0.044, 0.054, 0.056, 0.050, 0.036, 0.038; `Chen2020` 0.075, 0.075, 0.070, 0.065, 0.041, 0.046; `OKane2022` 0.072, 0.062, 0.067, 0.056, 0.015, 0.047; `NCA_Kim2011` 0.033, 0.028, 0.029, 0.025, 0.003, 0.011. Wins over NS, all 32: NT 16, NK 17, NL 19 (p = 0.19). Lever errors N, T, K, L (mV): 2.28, 0.94, 1.33, 0.67; 4.36, 3.55, 3.25, 3.72; 7.06, 2.37, 2.09, 3.05; 19.94, 17.83, 16.71, 4.92: every transferred profile reads the cycle-99 OCP rise better than the uniform lever on every set, as in 5zw. Fresh designs outside the training thickness range: 1, 4, 4, 3 of 8.
- Reading: the profiles' lever gains hold out of sample (H240's pattern replicates on all four sets), and on `NCA_Kim2011` the thickness line cuts the lever error fourfold (19.9 to 4.9 mV) and the forecast error by a quarter. But a better lever buys less forecast than it did in-sample: the fresh `OKane2022` designs have a much lower measured-OCP ceiling (1.5% against 3.5% on 5zk's designs), so most of their ten-cycle error is the lever, and the profiles close a third or less of it. The errors are heavy-tailed design by design: on `OKane2022` the line takes four designs from 6 to 8% to 1 to 4% and misses two by 14 to 15%, both at or below the training range's thin end (80 and 93 &mu;m against a training minimum of 92), where a line in thickness extrapolates. The pre-registered gate was the weak part: 0.5 was a guess and `OKane2022` sat just under it (0.46), so the gated forecast threw away the set's largest median gain. For a design search the advice changes little: fit the law's line through 30 to 50 cycles; on cells like `NCA_Kim2011`, where the separator-node gain rises clearly with anode thickness, a thickness-line profile from a few fully simulated designs shortens that to ten cycles; elsewhere it is a set-level improvement with large single-design misses, not a rule. Next: restrict the line to designs inside the training thickness range (fall back to NS outside), pre-registered on another fresh draw; and the paper's error-budget table (the 5zu budget on these 32 designs as its held-out column).
- Caveats: 8 fresh designs per set; the same pools, chemistry, current and Sobol space as the training designs, so this is transfer across designs, not chemistries or protocols; the gate threshold was chosen, not tuned, and its training statistic was known only after the plan; node OCPs and stoichiometries are model internals; cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/freshnode/bench.jsonl`, `rows.json`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `freshnode.svg`; tests `tests/test_freshnode.py`.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zy: do the node-gain profiles learned on 5zk's 40 designs carry the ten-cycle forecast to designs they never saw? A held-out test on a fresh DFN pool of 32 designs, with the thickness line pre-registered behind a monotonicity gate (plan, written and committed before the fresh bench ran and before the gate's training statistic was computed; H248 to H251 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zw's set-median late-gain profile (T) and 5zx's per-node thickness line (L) were judged leave-one-design-out on the 40 designs that motivated them, and L was reported, not hypothesised. 5zx's own next step: pre-register the thickness line, with a monotonicity check on the training designs that falls back to the uniform node lever, on designs the programme has never x-resolved. Does it hold out of sample?
- Bench, fixed now (`experiments/freshnode/run.py bench`): 8 fresh designs per set, `random.Random(5248)` picks from the same 64-design pools 5z drew from (`crossset` `Chen2020@1.5x`, `OKane2022@1.5x`, `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x`, `calmap` `Ai2020@0.45x`), excluding all of 5z's `random.Random(18)` picks and any design without a committed `ok` source run and balance row: `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` j = 5, 13, 19, 20, 22, 33, 38, 50; `NCA_Kim2011` and `Ai2020` j = 11, 18, 19, 20, 30, 36, 47, 55. Each run is 5zk's `depth/run.py child` unchanged (DFN, SEI + irreversible plating, 100 cycles, 20 negative-electrode nodes, PyBaMM 26.10.1.1, one fresh process per run). A design that fails is reported and not replaced.
- Forecasts, fixed now (`rows`, stdlib): training = 5zk's 40 designs, their late gains h<sub>i</sub> read unchanged from `experiments/nodetransfer/rows.json`; every profile is fitted on all training designs of the fresh design's set and read nothing of the fresh design past cycle 10 (its design vector and anode thickness are known before simulation). T = node-wise median of the training h<sub>i</sub>; K = median over the 3 training designs nearest in 5zx's scaled design cube; L = 5zx's per-node least-squares line of h<sub>i</sub> in anode thickness, clipped to [0, 3]; G (primary) = L on a set whose training designs have Spearman(L<sub>n</sub>, h<sub>sep</sub>) &ge; 0.5 (the gate), else the uniform node lever N. Each through 5zw's `_second` unchanged (&kappa; = 25.3, line through cycles 2..10 on 5zm's tables, q<sub>S</sub>, no drift); NS, C (measured-OCP ceiling) and Ts (own late gains, reads cycle 99) from 5zw's `_first` unchanged. e = |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|; set medians over the fresh designs.
- Already seen: everything 5zk to 5zx report on the 40 training designs, including 5zx's reading that the separator-node late gain rises with anode thickness on `OKane2022` and `NCA_Kim2011` and not on `Ai2020` and `Chen2020` (a figure, not the Spearman statistic; so the gate is expected to fire on those two sets, and the test is on the fresh designs, not the gate). The fresh designs' booked totals at their earlier pool currents were in the source pools' benches (5zb to 5zg read those pools); none was ever x-resolved, and no node gain, lever or ten-cycle forecast of this kind has been computed on any of them.
- H248 (the gated line holds out of sample): on every set where the gate fires, set median e<sub>NG</sub> &lt; e<sub>NS</sub>. Refuted if on none of them (untestable if the gate fires nowhere).
- H249 (`OKane2022` reaches halfway): fresh set median e<sub>NL</sub> &le; (e<sub>NS</sub> + e<sub>C</sub>)/2 on `OKane2022`. Refuted if above e<sub>NS</sub>.
- H250 (design by design, gated sets): e<sub>NL</sub> &lt; e<sub>NS</sub> on enough gated designs for a one-sided sign test p &le; 0.05 (12 of 16 if the gate fires on two sets). Refuted if on at most half.
- H251 (5zw's median profile replicates on `OKane2022`): fresh set median e<sub>NT</sub> &lt; e<sub>NS</sub> there. Refuted if above.
- Reported, not hypothesised: NK and NL on every set and their win counts against NS; the training Spearman per set; lever errors of N, T, K, L, Ts; the separator-node gain error of T, K, L; fresh designs outside the training thickness range.
- Caveats fixed now: 8 fresh designs per set is a small test; the designs come from the same pools and the same Sobol design space as the training designs, so this tests transfer across designs of one chemistry and current, not across chemistries or protocols; the gate threshold 0.5 is chosen, not tuned; node OCPs and stoichiometries are model internals; cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zx outcome: matching the borrowed node-gain profile to the held-out design's three nearest neighbours does not beat 5zw's set-wide median. It keeps `OKane2022`'s gain (6.7% to 4.5%, median 4.1%) and predicts the separator node's late gain better on three sets, but leaves `Chen2020` (6.5%) and `NCA_Kim2011` (4.1%) as bad as the median, beating it on one set of four. A per-node line in anode thickness (reported, not hypothesised) does better on the two sets where the late gain rises with thickness: 2.7% on `OKane2022` (below the 3.5% measured-OCP ceiling) and 1.5% on `NCA_Kim2011` (NS 3.0%), but 3.7% on `Ai2020` (NS 2.3%) and 6.7% on `Chen2020`. H246 supported, H244 and H245 refuted, H247 inconclusive
- Analysis: stdlib, about 40 s on 4 cores (`rows`), tests run before the report, report run once. After the plan only the test file's float comparison in `test_scale_maps_bounds_to_unit_cube` changed (exact equality to almostEqual); `run.py` did not. Reproduces 5zw: NT recomputed with 5zw's profiles through the same `_second` equals 5zw's on all 40 designs (to 12 places, tested); every K and L gain lies on [0, 3].
- H244 refuted (1 of 4): set median e<sub>NK</sub> against e<sub>NT</sub> is 0.023 against 0.022 (`Ai2020`), 0.065 against 0.065 (`Chen2020`), 0.045 against 0.041 (`OKane2022`), 0.041 against 0.042 (`NCA_Kim2011`). NK beats NT on 5/8, 8/12, 8/12, 4/8 designs.
- H245 refuted (0 of 2): e<sub>NK</sub> 0.065 against e<sub>NS</sub> 0.054 on `Chen2020`, 0.041 against 0.030 on `NCA_Kim2011`.
- H246 supported: on `OKane2022` set median e<sub>NK</sub> 0.045, below the halfway mark 0.051.
- H247 inconclusive: NK beats NS on 22 of 40 designs (1/8, 8/12, 8/12, 5/8).
- Reported: set median lever error N, T, K, L: 3.52, 1.73, 2.03, 2.09 mV (`Ai2020`); 5.08, 3.70, 3.47, 4.22 (`Chen2020`); 5.89, 5.31, 3.40, 4.28 (`OKane2022`); 23.24, 19.41, 17.97, 10.67 (`NCA_Kim2011`). Set median e<sub>NL</sub> 0.037, 0.067, 0.027, 0.015 against e<sub>C</sub> 0.035, 0.039, 0.035, 0.010; NL beats NS on 1/8, 8/12, 9/12, 6/8 designs (24 of 40). Set-median |t<sub>20</sub> &minus; h<sub>20</sub>| at the separator node, T, K, L: 0.25, 0.23, 0.71 (`Ai2020`); 0.34, 0.18, 0.29 (`Chen2020`); 0.32, 0.18, 0.30 (`OKane2022`); 0.18, 0.18, 0.11 (`NCA_Kim2011`). Median distance to the third neighbour in the unit design cube: 0.75, 0.61, 0.61, 0.74.
- Reading: the neighbours are not near (third-neighbour distance 0.6 to 0.75 of the cube's side), the risk the plan named, and although they predict the separator node's gain about twice as well as the median on `Chen2020` and `OKane2022`, that does not reach the forecast: the set medians of NK and NT agree to half a point everywhere and NK's wins over NS (8 of 12 on `Chen2020`) sit beside a worse median, so the misses are a few large ones (`Chen2020` designs at 148 to 172 &mu;m are 9 to 17% off under every lever). The thickness line is the more useful lead: on `OKane2022` and `NCA_Kim2011` the separator node's late gain rises with anode thickness (figure), and a two-parameter line per node fitted on 7 to 11 other designs takes the forecast to or below the measured-OCP ceiling. It fails where the gain is not monotone in thickness (`Ai2020`'s 2.55 at 102 &mu;m and 0.23 to 0.26 above 139 &mu;m; `Chen2020`'s 0.00 at 169 &mu;m), so the line is a set-specific find made after the plan, not yet a fix. For a design search the advice is unchanged: fit the law's line through 30 to 50 cycles; on `OKane2022`-like cells, a node-gain profile from a few fully simulated designs (median or thickness line) shortens that to ten cycles. Next: pre-register the thickness line, with a monotonicity check on the training designs that falls back to NS, on a fresh DFN pool (needs PyBaMM, absent from this container); and the error budget as the paper's table.
- Caveats: leave-one-design-out on one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zw; NL was reported, not hypothesised, and its success is judged after seeing it; k = 3 and the thickness feature were fixed before looking, not tuned; several separator-node late gains sit on the grid ends (0.00 on two `OKane2022` and one `Chen2020` design, 3.00 on three `NCA_Kim2011` designs), so those gains are weakly determined; the designs are not a random sample; node OCPs and stoichiometries are model internals; cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/nodematch/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `nodematch.svg`; tests `tests/test_nodematch.py`.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zx: does a node-gain profile matched to the held-out design carry the cycle-99 forecast where 5zw's set-wide median did not? A leave-one-design-out test of a nearest-neighbour profile and of a per-node line in anode thickness (plan, written and committed before any matched profile, lever or forecast below was computed; H244 to H247 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zw's set-median late-gain profile (T) took `OKane2022`'s ten-cycle forecast from 6.7% to 4.1% but made `Chen2020` (5.4 to 6.5%) and `NCA_Kim2011` (3.0 to 4.2%) worse, where the separator node's late gain spreads widely across designs (IQR 0.91..1.34, 2.46..3.00). With each design's own late gains a constant-gain lever reaches the measured-OCP ceiling on every set (Ts), so the open problem is predicting the profile from the design. A design search knows each design's parameters before simulating it; does conditioning the borrowed profile on them recover the design-specific part?
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/nodematch/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation; 5zk's 40 designs; 5zw's `_ctx`, `_second`, late gains h<sub>i</sub> and its NS, NT, C imported or read from `experiments/nodetransfer/rows.json` unchanged). Design vector x = (positive thickness, positive porosity, negative porosity, relative N/P ratio) from `bench.jsonl`, scaled to the unit cube by `energy_cell.cell.BOUNDS` (50..150 &mu;m, 0.20..0.40, 0.20..0.40, 0.90..1.30). K (primary): t<sub>i</sub> = node-wise median of h<sub>i</sub> over the k = 3 other designs of the same set nearest in scaled Euclidean distance (ties by design index). L: t<sub>i</sub> = a per-node least-squares line of h<sub>i</sub> against anode thickness L<sub>n</sub> (from `bench.jsonl`) over the other designs of the set, evaluated at the held-out L<sub>n</sub> and clipped to 5zv's gain grid [0, 3]. Forecasts NK = (K, q<sub>S</sub>) and NL = (L, q<sub>S</sub>) at &kappa; = 25.3, line through cycles 2..10 on 5zm's tables, no drift, through 5zw's `_second` unchanged; NS, NT and the ceiling C as 5zw (reproduced from its rows). Lever error and e as 5zw; set medians over 8, 12, 12, 8 designs.
- Already seen: everything 5zk to 5zw report on these designs, including every design's late gains h<sub>i</sub>, T, NT, NS, C and Ts, and the separator-node spread. Not seen: any design's distance to another, any nearest-neighbour or thickness-line profile, NK or NL on any design, or how h<sub>i</sub> varies with any design parameter. Risk named now: 8 to 12 designs in four dimensions are sparse, so the 3 nearest neighbours of a design are not near and K may be no better than a noisier median; L spends two parameters per node on 7 to 11 points and can extrapolate at the thickness extremes.
- H244 (conditioning beats the set median): set median e<sub>NK</sub> &lt; e<sub>NT</sub> on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H245 (repairs the sets T hurt): set median e<sub>NK</sub> &le; e<sub>NS</sub> on both `Chen2020` and `NCA_Kim2011`. Refuted if e<sub>NK</sub> &gt; e<sub>NS</sub> on both.
- H246 (keeps `OKane2022`): set median e<sub>NK</sub> on `OKane2022` at most (e<sub>NS</sub> + e<sub>C</sub>)/2 (5zw's halfway mark, 0.051). Refuted if above e<sub>NS</sub>.
- H247 (design by design): e<sub>NK</sub> &lt; e<sub>NS</sub> on at least 26 of 40 designs (one-sided sign test p &asymp; 0.04). Refuted if on at most 20.
- Reported, not hypothesised: NL's lever and forecast errors and counts; set medians of the lever errors of K and L; the set-median absolute error of the separator node's predicted gain |t<sub>20</sub> &minus; h<sub>20</sub>| for T, K and L; the median distance to the third neighbour; counts NK&lt;NT and NL&lt;NS per set.
- Caveats fixed now: leave-one-design-out on one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zw, so held out from the profile's fit but not from the programme's earlier choices (5zw's failure motivated this test); k = 3 and the thickness feature are chosen before looking, not tuned; the designs are not a random sample; node OCPs and stoichiometries are model internals; cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zw outcome: a node-gain profile carried from the other designs of a set takes `OKane2022`'s cycle-99 lithium-loss forecast from 6.7% to 4.1% (set median; the measured-OCP ceiling is 3.5%), and reads the OCP rise better than the uniform lever on all four sets, but it makes `Chen2020` (5.4 to 6.5%) and `NCA_Kim2011` (3.0 to 4.2%) worse and beats the uniform lever on 23 of 40 designs. Relaxing the design's own ten-cycle gains at a rate chosen on the other designs helps `OKane2022` too (4.7%, 9 of 12) but is no better than both NS and NG on three sets. H240 and H241 supported, H242 refuted, H243 inconclusive
- Analysis: stdlib, 34 s on 4 cores (`rows`), tests run before the report, report run once, nothing in `experiments/nodetransfer/run.py` changed after the plan apart from the test file's float comparison. &Delta;s was fixed in the plan to the line-extrapolated L&#770;<sub>99</sub> before any code ran. Reproduces 5zv: NS, NG and C equal 5zv's on all 40 designs (to 12 places), the ten-cycle gains are identical, and R at &rho; = &infin; equals G (tested).
- H240 supported (4 of 4): set median lever error, T against N, 1.73 against 3.52 mV (`Ai2020`), 3.70 against 5.08 (`Chen2020`), 5.31 against 5.89 (`OKane2022`), 19.41 against 23.24 (`NCA_Kim2011`).
- H241 supported: on `OKane2022` set median e<sub>NT</sub> 0.041, below the halfway mark (0.067 + 0.035)/2 = 0.051; NT beats NS on 7 of 12 designs there.
- H242 refuted (1 of 4): set median e<sub>NR</sub> 0.025, 0.040, 0.047, 0.057 against min(e<sub>NS</sub>, e<sub>NG</sub>) 0.023, 0.039, 0.067, 0.023; only `OKane2022` passes.
- H243 inconclusive: NT beats NS on 23 of 40 designs (2/8, 8/12, 7/12, 6/8).
- Reported: set median e<sub>NT</sub> 0.022, 0.065, 0.041, 0.042 against e<sub>NS</sub> 0.023, 0.054, 0.067, 0.030. The in-sample constant-gain lever with the design's own late gains (Ts, reads cycle 99) reaches 0.021, 0.039, 0.030, 0.021, at or below the measured-OCP ceiling C on every set, and its lever error is 1.1 to 1.9 mV: a constant per-node gain is enough if it is the right one. Set-median late gains, collector to separator: `Ai2020` 0.85 ... 1.07; `Chen2020` 0.61 ... 1.17; `OKane2022` 0.52, 0.58, 0.56, 0.78, 0.96, 0.78, 0.72, 1.01; `NCA_Kim2011` 1.07 ... 2.83. Separator-node IQR across designs: 1.04..1.10, 0.91..1.34, 0.79..1.23, 2.46..3.00. The LOO &rho;: &infin; on all 8 `Ai2020` designs, 10 on 11 of 12 `Chen2020`, 0.5 or 1 on `OKane2022`, and 0.5 on 3 and 5 on 5 `NCA_Kim2011` designs (unstable).
- Reading: the late gains (0.5 to 1.0 on `OKane2022`) sit well above 5zv's ten-cycle gains (0.1 to 0.8): the node lag shrinks with lithium lost, as 5zv's failure implied, and once the per-node gains are right a constant gain is enough (Ts). Two designs of the same chemistry share enough of that late profile on `OKane2022` that a median carried across designs recovers 2.6 of the 3.2 points between NS and the measured-OCP ceiling. It does not carry on `Chen2020` and `NCA_Kim2011`, where the separator node's late gain spreads widely across designs (IQR 0.91 to 1.34; 2.46 to 3.00, at the grid end), so the median erases the design-specific part, the risk the plan named. The per-design win counts (23 of 40) say the transfer is a set-level fix on `OKane2022`, not a rule. For a design search the advice is: with a few fully simulated designs of the same chemistry, carry their node-gain profile into the ten-cycle forecast on `OKane2022`-like cells; otherwise fit the law's line through 30 to 50 cycles. Next: condition the transferred profile on the design (nearest-neighbour or a regression of h<sub>i</sub> on thickness, porosity and rate), and a held-out pool of new DFN designs for T and G4 (needs PyBaMM, absent from this container).
- Caveats: leave-one-design-out on one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zv, so held out from the profile's fit but not from the programme's earlier choices; the designs are not a random sample; gains weakly determined on plateau nodes and `NCA_Kim2011`'s separator gain sits on the grid end (3.00); node OCPs and stoichiometries are model internals; cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/nodetransfer/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `nodetransfer.svg`; tests `tests/test_nodetransfer.py`.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zw: does a node-gain profile learned on the other designs of a set carry the cycle-99 forecast, where 5zv's own ten-cycle gains did not? A leave-one-design-out test of a transferred late gain and of a relaxing gain (plan, written and committed before any late gain, relaxation, lever or forecast below was computed; H240 to H243 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zv's per-node gains fitted on cycles 3..10 under-read `OKane2022`'s cycle-99 OCP rise (lever error 14.8 against N's 5.9 mV): the node lag is not a constant fraction of the uniform shift. A design search that has fully simulated a few designs of a chemistry can learn how the node lag evolves from them. Two ways to use that, both held out design by design within a set: (T) carry the other designs' late per-node gain profile; (R) let the design's own ten-cycle gains relax toward 1 with a shared rate chosen on the other designs.
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/nodetransfer/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation; 5zk's 40 designs; 5zv's `fit_gain`, `gain_lever` and its forecast pipeline imported unchanged). Late gain h<sub>i</sub> per design and node = 5zv's `fit_gain` over cycles 3..99 (reads past cycle 10, so used only for other designs). T: constant gains t<sub>i</sub> = median over the other designs of the same set of h<sub>i</sub> (leave one design out); lever G with gains t<sub>i</sub>. R: path gains g<sub>i</sub>(l) = 1 + (g<sub>i</sub> &minus; 1) exp(&minus;max(0, s(l) &minus; s(L<sub>10</sub>))/(&rho; &Delta;s)), g<sub>i</sub> the design's own 5zv gain, &Delta;s = s(L&#770;<sub>99</sub>) &minus; s(L<sub>10</sub>), with L&#770;<sub>99</sub> the lithium lost at cycle 99 extrapolated by a least-squares line through cycles 2..10 (no DFN read past cycle 10), &rho; on the grid 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, &infin; chosen per held-out design as the value minimising the median forecast error e over the other designs of its set; lever R(l) = &Sigma; w<sub>i</sub> [U<sub>n</sub>(x<sub>i</sub>(2) + g<sub>i</sub>(l) s(l)) &minus; U<sub>n</sub>(x<sub>i</sub>(2) + g<sub>i</sub>(0) s(0))]; &rho; = &infin; is 5zv's G (tested), &rho; &rarr; 0 is N past cycle 10.
