Forecasts NT = (T, q<sub>S</sub>) and NR = (R, q<sub>S</sub>) at &kappa; = 25.3, line through cycles 2..10 on 5zm's tables, no drift; NS, NG and the ceiling C as in 5zv (reproduced). Lever error |du(L<sub>99</sub>) &minus; du(L<sub>2</sub>) &minus; M<sub>w,99</sub>| in mV; e = |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|; set medians over 8, 12, 12, 8 designs.
- Already seen: everything 5zk to 5zv report on these designs, including N's, G's and G4's lever errors, NS, NG, NG4 and C. Not seen: any late gain h<sub>i</sub>, any transferred profile, any relaxation or &rho;, NT or NR on any design. Risk named now: within a set the designs differ in thickness, porosity and rate (5zk), so node i is a different depth and lag on each; a median profile may average away exactly the design-specific lag that matters, and 8 to 12 designs is a small training pool.
- H240 (the transferred profile reads the OCP rise): set median T lever error below N's on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H241 (and carries `OKane2022`): set median e<sub>NT</sub> at most (e<sub>NS</sub> + e<sub>C</sub>)/2 on `OKane2022`. Refuted if above e<sub>NS</sub>.
- H242 (relaxation does no harm and some good): set median e<sub>NR</sub> &le; min(e<sub>NS</sub>, e<sub>NG</sub>) on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H243 (design by design): e<sub>NT</sub> &lt; e<sub>NS</sub> on at least 26 of 40 designs (one-sided sign test p &asymp; 0.04). Refuted if on at most 20.
- Reported, not hypothesised: set-median late gains h<sub>i</sub> by node, the spread (IQR) of h<sub>i</sub> across designs at the separator node, the &rho; chosen per set (median), counts NT&lt;NS and NR&lt;NS per set, and T's error with the design's own h<sub>i</sub> (an in-sample ceiling for a constant-gain lever, reads cycle 99).
- Caveats fixed now: leave-one-design-out on one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zv, so held out from the profile's fit but not from the programme's earlier choices; the designs are not a random sample of a design space; node OCPs and stoichiometries are model internals; cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zv outcome: a per-node gain fitted on cycles 3..10 does not carry the ten-cycle forecast. On `OKane2022` the gains fall well below 1 (plating-weighted mean 0.61), the lever under-reads the cycle-99 OCP rise by 14.8 mV (N: 5.9) and the forecast is 6.9% off against NS's 6.7%; it helps `Chen2020` (5.4 to 3.9%, the measured-OCP ceiling) and `NCA_Kim2011` (3.0 to 2.3%) and beats NS on 22 of 40 designs. Gains on the four separator-side nodes only (G4, reported, not hypothesised) take `OKane2022`'s set median to 4.1% but beat NS on only 6 of 12 designs there and 20 of 40 overall, and make `Chen2020` worse (6.9%). H237 and H238 refuted, H236 and H239 inconclusive
- Analysis: stdlib, 40 s (`rows`), tests run before the report, report run once, nothing in `experiments/nodegain/run.py` changed after the plan. Reproduces 5zu: NS equals cell 000 and C equals cell 100 on all 40 designs (to 12 places), and G with every gain 1 equals 5zn's N (tested).
- H236 inconclusive (2 of 4): set median lever error at cycle 99, G against N, is 6.58 against 3.52 mV (`Ai2020`), 2.52 against 5.08 (`Chen2020`), 14.83 against 5.89 (`OKane2022`), 17.20 against 23.24 (`NCA_Kim2011`).
- H237 refuted: on `OKane2022` set median e<sub>NG</sub> 0.069 against e<sub>NS</sub> 0.067.
- H238 refuted: the halfway mark was 0.051 (NS 0.067, ceiling C 0.035).
- H239 inconclusive: NG beats NS on 22 of 40 designs (4/8, 7/12, 6/12, 5/8).
- Reported: set median e of NS, NG, NG4, C is 0.023, 0.025, 0.025, 0.035 (`Ai2020`); 0.054, 0.039, 0.069, 0.039 (`Chen2020`); 0.067, 0.069, 0.041, 0.035 (`OKane2022`); 0.030, 0.023, 0.015, 0.010 (`NCA_Kim2011`). G4's lever error is 1.36, 6.25, 5.07, 11.28 mV. NG4 beats NS on 2/8, 4/12, 6/12, 8/8 designs. Set-median gains from the collector (node 1) to the separator (node 20), every third node and the last: `Ai2020` 0.93 ... 0.45, 0.19; `Chen2020` 0.67 ... 0.96, 1.00; `OKane2022` 0.16, 0.33, 0.13, 0.83, 0.82, 0.49, 0.17, 0.75; `NCA_Kim2011` 1.14 ... 0.90, 3.00. Plating-weighted mean gain 0.79, 0.96, 0.61, 1.27.
- Reading: over cycles 3..10 the anode nodes' local OCP rises more slowly than the uniform shift predicts on three sets, most on `OKane2022` (gains 0.1 to 0.8), but extrapolating that ratio to cycle 99 over-corrects: N over-reads early (5zo) and under-reads late less than G does. So the node lag is not a constant fraction of the shift, the same failure as 5zq's drift line, and the risk the plan named. On `Chen2020` the gain lever reaches the measured-OCP ceiling (3.9%), which says its OCP share (5zu's &phi;<sub>M</sub> 2.1) is recoverable from ten cycles there. G4's set-median win on `OKane2022` is a median of mixed designs (6 of 12) and found after the plan, so it is a lead, not a result; NCA_Kim2011 is the one set where G4 wins everywhere (8 of 8). For a design search the advice is unchanged: fit the law's line through 30 to 50 cycles. Next: a gain that is allowed to relax with lithium lost (two parameters per node, or one shared relaxation) tested on held-out designs, and G4 pre-registered on fresh designs before it is called a fix.
- Caveats: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zu, so a check on known designs, not a held-out test; G4 was reported, not hypothesised; s moves by under a tenth of its cycle-99 value within cycles 3..10, so gains are weakly determined on flat plateau nodes (ties go to 1; `NCA_Kim2011`'s separator node sits on the grid end, 3.00); node OCPs and stoichiometries are model internals; cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/nodegain/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `nodegain.svg`; tests `tests/test_nodegain.py`.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zv: does a ten-cycle per-node gain on the node lever's shift forecast the plating-weighted OCP rise, and close `OKane2022`'s OCP share of the ten-cycle error? (plan, written and committed before any gain, lever or forecast below was computed; H236 to H239 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zu put the largest share of `OKane2022`'s ten-cycle error (&phi;<sub>M</sub> 3.3 of a 5.6-point gap) in the node lever's OCP part: 5zn's lever moves every node's stoichiometry by the same uniform shift s(l), and 5zo found its over-read sits at the deepest-lithiated, separator-side nodes where the plating is. A node whose local stoichiometry moves less (or more) than the uniform shift is invisible to that lever. Cycles 2..10 already show each node's measured OCP rise, so a per-node gain on s can be calibrated there without reading past cycle 10.
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/nodegain/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation; 5zk's 40 designs, 5zu's, 5zt's and 5zn's code imported unchanged). For each node i, gain g<sub>i</sub> = the value on the grid 0.00, 0.01, ..., 3.00 minimising &Sigma;<sub>n=3..10</sub> [U<sub>n</sub>(x<sub>i</sub>(2) + g s(L<sub>n</sub>)) &minus; U<sub>n</sub>(x<sub>i</sub>(2)) &minus; (u<sub>i</sub>(n) &minus; u<sub>i</sub>(2))]<sup>2</sup>, fitted in OCP space so the graphite plateau's flat steps cannot blow up an inverse (ties go to the gain nearest 1). Gain lever G(l) = &Sigma; w<sub>i</sub> [U<sub>n</sub>(x<sub>i</sub>(2) + g<sub>i</sub> s(l)) &minus; U<sub>n</sub>(x<sub>i</sub>(2) + g<sub>i</sub> s(0))], cycle-2 weights; with g<sub>i</sub> = 1 it is 5zn's N (tested). Variant G4: gains on the four separator-side nodes only (5zo's split), g<sub>i</sub> = 1 elsewhere. Forecasts at &kappa; = 25.3, line through cycles 2..10, on 5zm's tables, no drift term, 5zs's q<sub>S</sub>: NS (5zu's cell 000, reproduced), NG = (G, q<sub>S</sub>), the primary, NG4 = (G4, q<sub>S</sub>), and 5zu's cell 100 (measured M<sub>w</sub>, q<sub>S</sub>; reproduced) as the ceiling. Lever error at cycle 99: |du(L<sub>99</sub>) &minus; M<sub>w,99</sub>| in mV. Forecast error e = |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|; set medians over 8, 12, 12, 8 designs.
- Already seen: everything 5zk to 5zu report on these designs, including N's and the measured M<sub>w</sub> at cycles 10 and 99 (set medians), cells 000 and 100 and 5zo's node over-read at cycle 10. Not seen: any per-node gain, G or G4 at any cycle, NG or NG4 on any design. Risk named now: the gain is a straight-line scaling of a shift fitted over cycles in which s moves by under a tenth of its cycle-99 value, so a node whose lag grows or shrinks later is extrapolated wrongly, the same failure 5zq's drift line had.
- H236 (the gain reads the OCP rise better): set median |G(L<sub>99</sub>) &minus; M<sub>w,99</sub>| below N's on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H237 (and carries `OKane2022`'s forecast): set median e<sub>NG</sub> &lt; e<sub>NS</sub> on `OKane2022`. Refuted if e<sub>NG</sub> &gt; e<sub>NS</sub>.
- H238 (half the OCP share): on `OKane2022` set median e<sub>NG</sub> at most (e<sub>000</sub> + e<sub>100</sub>)/2 (halfway from NS to the measured-OCP ceiling). Refuted if above e<sub>000</sub>.
- H239 (design by design): e<sub>NG</sub> &lt; e<sub>NS</sub> on at least 26 of 40 designs (one-sided sign test p &asymp; 0.04). Refuted if on at most 20.
- Reported, not hypothesised: set medians of g<sub>i</sub> by node (collector to separator), the plating-weighted mean gain, NG4's lever and forecast errors, and the counts NG&lt;NS, NG4&lt;NS, NG&lt;NG4 per set.
- Caveats fixed now: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zu, so a check on known designs, not a held-out test; the hypotheses lean on 5zo's and 5zu's directions; node OCPs and stoichiometries are model internals; cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zu outcome: on `OKane2022` the ten-cycle forecast's remaining error is the node lever's OCP part, not the drift; on `Chen2020` it splits evenly between the two; the capacity factor's share is nil on every set. Of the gap between the best ten-cycle forecast and the all-measured diagnosis (6.7% against 1.5% on `OKane2022`), measuring the plating-weighted OCP rise buys 3.3 points (Shapley, set median) and the drift 1.1; on `Chen2020` (5.4% against 1.4%) the two buy 2.1 and 2.0. On `Ai2020` measuring the inputs makes the forecast worse (2.3% to 8.0%): there the node lever's errors cancel the law's. H232 and H234 supported, H233 and H235 inconclusive
- Analysis: stdlib, 35 s (`rows`), report run once, nothing in `experiments/budget/run.py` changed after the plan. Reproduces 5zs, 5zt and 5zp: cell 000 equals NS, 010 equals ND and the all-measured cell 111 equals PQ (built 5zp's way, to 6 places) on all 40 designs, and each design's three Shapley values sum to its gap (tested).
- H232 supported: on `OKane2022` set median &phi;<sub>M</sub> = 0.033, &phi;<sub>D</sub> = 0.011, &phi;<sub>q</sub> = &minus;0.001 (gap 0.056); M has the largest &phi; on 10 of 12 designs.
- H233 inconclusive (rank 2 of 3): on `Chen2020` &phi;<sub>M</sub> = 0.021, &phi;<sub>D</sub> = 0.020, &phi;<sub>q</sub> = &minus;0.000 (gap 0.041); D has the largest &phi; on 7 of 12 designs, M on 4. The two are tied to within a point.
- H234 supported (4 of 4 at most 0.01): set median &phi;<sub>q</sub> is &minus;0.003, &minus;0.000, &minus;0.001, 0.003; measuring the factor raises the error on 6 of 8, 6 of 12, 7 of 12 and 2 of 8 designs.
- H235 inconclusive: set median |I<sub>MD</sub>| is 0.002 on `Chen2020` and 0.011 on `OKane2022` (signed 0.002 and 0.000): on `Chen2020` the lever and drift fixes add; on `OKane2022` they interact design by design but with no common sign.
- Reported: set median e by cell (000, 001, 010, 011, 100, 101, 110, 111): `Ai2020` 0.023, 0.020, 0.049, 0.063, 0.035, 0.040, 0.071, 0.080; `Chen2020` 0.054, 0.063, 0.024, 0.027, 0.039, 0.040, 0.017, 0.014; `OKane2022` 0.067, 0.069, 0.065, 0.058, 0.035, 0.041, 0.022, 0.015; `NCA_Kim2011` 0.030, 0.035, 0.030, 0.035, 0.010, 0.011, 0.010, 0.011. On `NCA_Kim2011` the measured OCP rise alone (100) reaches the all-measured 1.0% (&phi;<sub>M</sub> 0.025 of a 0.028 gap; the drift is nil there, 5zt). On `Ai2020` the gap is &minus;0.069: &phi;<sub>M</sub> &minus;0.020 (negative on 8 of 8 designs) and &phi;<sub>D</sub> &minus;0.038 (6 of 8), so the forecast inputs' errors offset the law's line error and NS's 2.3% is partly luck, as 5zp found for its overpotential oracle (4.2 to 8.7%).
- Reading: the one-input ceilings of 5zs and 5zt are confirmed and placed. The capacity factor is spent everywhere. What the ten-cycle forecast lacks on LG M50 is the path of the plating-weighted plating overpotential, and which half of it matters is set-dependent: its OCP part (the node lever's forecast of how the anode's local OCP rises at the plating nodes) on `OKane2022`, its OCP part and its non-OCP drift equally on `Chen2020`. 5zo already placed `OKane2022`'s node-lever over-read at the separator half of the anode, so the next forecast to try is the node lever's OCP rise at the separator nodes, not another drift term. For a design search the advice is unchanged: fit the law's line through 30 to 50 cycles. Next: a ten-cycle forecast of the separator-side OCP rise (5zo's split) on `OKane2022`; why measured inputs make `Ai2020` worse (the law's line, 5zo's bend); and the budget as the paper's error-budget table.
- Caveats: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zt, so a decomposition on known designs, not a held-out test; H232 and H233 leaned on directions 5zo's O and 5zt's ND suggested; Shapley medians need not sum to the gap's median; M<sub>w</sub> and D<sub>w</sub> are model internals with cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/budget/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `budget.svg`; tests `tests/test_budget.py`.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zu: an error budget for the ten-cycle forecast. Of the gap between the best ten-cycle forecast (NS) and the all-measured diagnosis (PQ), how much is the node lever's OCP part, the non-OCP plating-overpotential drift and the capacity factor? (plan, written and committed before any of the new cells was computed; H232 to H235 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zq to 5zt each tried a ten-cycle forecast of one input and measured it against a one-input ceiling: the factor (5zs's NM moves N by under a point on three sets), the drift (5zt's ND takes `Chen2020` from 5.4 to 2.4% but `OKane2022` only from 6.7 to 6.5%). One-at-a-time ceilings do not add when the error is an absolute log ratio and the inputs' errors offset, so they cannot say where `OKane2022`'s 6.5% lives. A full factorial with a Shapley split does, and it is the error-budget table the paper needs.
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/budget/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation; 5zk's 40 designs; 5zt's, 5zs's, 5zq's and 5zo's code imported unchanged). Three switches, each forecast (0) or measured (1): M = N's node-lever OCP rise (5zn) or the measured plating-weighted OCP rise M<sub>w</sub> (5zo's oracle); D = no drift (as NS) or the measured non-OCP drift D<sub>w</sub> = E<sub>w</sub> &minus; M<sub>w</sub> (5zq, cycle-2 weights); q = 5zs's saturating ten-cycle factor q<sub>S</sub> or the measured factor q<sub>M</sub> (5zp). Eight cells mdq at &kappa; = 25.3, line through cycles 2..10 on 5zm's tables; e = |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|. 000 is NS, 010 is ND, 111 is 5zp's PQ (M<sub>w</sub> + D<sub>w</sub> = E<sub>w</sub>; tested). Per design: Shapley values &phi;<sub>M</sub>, &phi;<sub>D</sub>, &phi;<sub>q</sub> of measuring each input (the drop in e; they sum to e<sub>000</sub> &minus; e<sub>111</sub>), and the M&ndash;D interaction at q<sub>S</sub>, I<sub>MD</sub> = e<sub>110</sub> &minus; e<sub>100</sub> &minus; e<sub>010</sub> + e<sub>000</sub>. Set medians over 8, 12, 12, 8 designs.
- Already seen: NS (000), ND (010) and PQ (111) on every design, and NM (N with q<sub>M</sub>, which is cell 001); 5zo's O (measured M<sub>w</sub> with the law's own factor, not q<sub>S</sub>: 0.042, 0.038, 0.034, 0.012) and 5zr's PE (measured E<sub>w</sub> with q<sub>E</sub>). So H232 and H233 lean on directions those suggest (O well below N on `OKane2022`; ND well below NS on `Chen2020`); what is new is the attribution with the other inputs measured, the factor's share in the presence of the others, and the interaction. Not seen: cells 100, 110, 011, 101 on any design, any Shapley value or interaction.
- H232 (the OCP lever carries `OKane2022`): set median &phi;<sub>M</sub> is the largest of the three on `OKane2022`. Refuted if it is the smallest.
- H233 (the drift carries `Chen2020`): set median &phi;<sub>D</sub> is the largest of the three on `Chen2020`. Refuted if it is the smallest.
- H234 (the factor is spent): |set median &phi;<sub>q</sub>| at most 0.01 on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if above 0.02 on 3 or more.
- H235 (the inputs' errors offset): set median |I<sub>MD</sub>| at least 0.01 on both LG M50 sets. Refuted if below 0.005 on both.
- Reported, not hypothesised: all eight cells' set medians, the gap e<sub>000</sub> &minus; e<sub>111</sub>, signed I<sub>MD</sub>, and per set how many designs each input has the largest &phi; and how many it has a negative &phi; (measuring it makes the forecast worse on average).
- Caveats fixed now: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zt, so a decomposition on known designs, not a held-out test; Shapley medians need not sum to the gap's median; M<sub>w</sub> and D<sub>w</sub> are model internals with cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zt outcome: the drift forecast improves, the lithium-loss forecast does not. Tying the non-OCP plating-overpotential drift to the falling charge overpotential forecasts its cycle-99 value closer than 5zq's ten-cycle line on every set (set median error 3.5, 6.0, 4.9 mV against 13.0, 8.7, 7.1 on `Ai2020`, `Chen2020`, `OKane2022`), but fed to the node lever it is worse than no drift term on all four sets (7.6% against 5.4% on `Chen2020`, 7.9% against 6.7% on `OKane2022`) and beats the law's lever on 17 of 40 designs. The measured drift itself takes `Chen2020` from 5.4 to 2.4% but `OKane2022` only from 6.7 to 6.5%. H228 supported, H229 and H230 refuted, H231 inconclusive
- Analysis: stdlib, 80 s (`rows`), report run once, nothing in `experiments/etadrift/run.py` changed after the plan. Reproduces 5zs and 5zq: A, N and NS equal 5zs's committed values on all 40 designs, and D<sub>10</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>) and D<sub>99</sub> equal 5zq's (tested).
- H228 supported (4 of 4): set median |D<sub>&eta;</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>) &minus; D<sub>99</sub>| is 3.49 against the line's 12.98 mV (`Ai2020`), 6.00 against 8.71 (`Chen2020`), 4.85 against 7.09 (`OKane2022`), 0.02 against 0.02 (`NCA_Kim2011`, where the drift is nil; strictly lower in the unrounded medians).
- H229 refuted (0 of 2): set median e<sub>FS</sub> against e<sub>NS</sub> is 0.076 against 0.054 (`Chen2020`) and 0.079 against 0.067 (`OKane2022`).
- H230 refuted: FS beats A on 17 of 40 designs (4/8, 6/12, 4/12, 3/8).
- H231 inconclusive (one set each way): e<sub>ND</sub> is 0.024 on `Chen2020` (NS 0.054; ND beats NS on 11 of 12 designs) and 0.065 on `OKane2022` (NS 0.067; 9 of 12).
- Reported: FL (5zq's line with q<sub>S</sub>) is 0.067, 0.074, 0.095, 0.030; FH (the line held past cycle 10) is 0.017, 0.054, 0.075, 0.030, the best ten-cycle variant on `Ai2020` (NS 0.023) and equal to NS on LG M50; FS beats FL on 31 of 40 designs but NS on only 12. ND on `Ai2020` is 0.049 against NS's 0.023: there the measured drift makes the node lever worse, so N's OCP part already absorbs it. The held line is closer still to the measured cycle-99 drift than the tie (3.06, 3.35, 3.15 mV). The tie's ten-cycle slope k<sub>&eta;</sub> is 0.082, 0.048, 0.029 mV of drift per mV of &eta; fall; refitted over cycles 2..99 with the measured &eta; (post hoc) it is 0.231, &minus;0.043 and &minus;0.057: on LG M50 the drift goes negative (&minus;2.50, &minus;2.58 mV at cycle 99) while &eta; keeps falling, so the tie that holds over ten cycles has the wrong sign over the run, the risk the plan named.
- Reading: on LG M50 every ten-cycle drift term tried (5zq's line, the held line, the &eta;-tied drift) forecasts a positive drift at cycle 99 where the DFN gives a negative one, and adding it to the node lever makes the forecast worse in proportion to how much drift it adds; leaving it out (NS) or freezing it at cycle 10 (FH) is best. The ceiling splits the two LG M50 sets: on `Chen2020` the measured drift takes the error from 5.4 to 2.4%, so the drift is the lever there and the ten cycles hold no sign of its turn-over; on `OKane2022` it barely moves the error, so something else (the node lever's OCP part, or the law's form) carries its 6.5%. So the ten-cycle forecast's remaining error on LG M50 is not recoverable from a one-parameter tie to &eta;, and is only half the drift. For a design search the advice is unchanged: fit the law's line through 30 to 50 cycles. Next: what sets the drift's turn-over on `Chen2020` (the plating-overpotential split by node, whether it follows SEI growth at the separator), and why the measured drift leaves `OKane2022` at 6.5%; 5zp's cycle-n weights on `Ai2020` remain open.
- Caveats: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zs; the tie is the simplest physically shaped drift (one slope), not the best possible; D is a model internal and &eta; a symmetric cut-off shift (5zc), CC charge only; cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/etadrift/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `etadrift.svg`; tests `tests/test_etadrift.py`.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zt: does tying the non-OCP plating-overpotential drift to the falling charge overpotential forecast it from cycles 2..10 better than 5zq's straight line, and does it carry the ten-cycle forecast on LG M50? (plan, written and committed before any of the new forecasts was computed; H228 to H231 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zs closed the capacity factor (q<sub>S</sub> within 2.4 to 4.8% of the measured factor at cycle 99; the measured factor itself moves the node lever N by under a point on three sets) and left the ten-cycle forecast's error on LG M50 (5.4 to 7.6%) in the lever: the non-OCP part D of the plating-weighted plating overpotential, which 5zq's ten-cycle line forecast with the wrong sign at cycle 99 (+2.50 and +0.87 mV against &minus;2.50 and &minus;2.58) because D rises over cycles 2..10 and turns over later. 5zs named the next step: a physically shaped forecast of D tied to the charge overpotential &eta;, which falls and levels off over the same range. Two questions: does a D that follows &eta;'s saturating fall forecast cycle-99 D better than the line, and is D the lever at all (N plus the measured D, ND, a ceiling that reads cycle 99)?
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/etadrift/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation; 5zk's 40 designs, 5zs's, 5zq's and 5zp's code imported unchanged). D<sub>n</sub> = E<sub>w,n</sub> &minus; M<sub>w,n</sub> as 5zq (cycle-2 weights, D<sub>2</sub> = 0). &eta;<sub>S</sub> = 5zs's saturating ten-cycle fit, unchanged. Tie: D<sub>&eta;</sub>(l) = k<sub>&eta;</sub> (&eta;<sub>S</sub>(L<sub>2</sub>) &minus; &eta;<sub>S</sub>(l)) for l &gt; L<sub>2</sub>, with k<sub>&eta;</sub> the least-squares slope through the origin of D<sub>n</sub> on &eta;<sub>S</sub>(L<sub>2</sub>) &minus; &eta;<sub>S</sub>(L<sub>n</sub>), n = 2..10 (one parameter, no fit past cycle 10). Comparators: D<sub>10</sub>, 5zq's line (reproduced), and D<sub>10H</sub>, that line held at its L<sub>10</sub> value beyond. Forecasts at &kappa; = 25.3, line through cycles 2..10, on 5zm's tables, all with 5zs's q<sub>S</sub>: A, N, NS (reproduced), FS = (N + D<sub>&eta;</sub>, q<sub>S</sub>) the primary, FL = (N + D<sub>10</sub>, q<sub>S</sub>), FH = (N + D<sub>10H</sub>, q<sub>S</sub>), and ND = (N + measured D, q<sub>S</sub>; reads cycle 99, a diagnosis). Error e = |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|; set medians over 8, 12, 12, 8 designs.
- Already seen: everything 5zk to 5zs report on these designs, including 5zq's D at cycles 10 and 99 and D<sub>10</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>) (set medians), its F = (N + D<sub>10</sub>, q<sub>10</sub>), and 5zs's &eta;<sub>S</sub>, q<sub>S</sub> and NS. Not seen: k<sub>&eta;</sub>, D<sub>&eta;</sub> or D<sub>10H</sub> at any cycle, and FS, FL, FH or ND on any design. The risk is named now: a tie to a monotone, levelling &eta; can rise and level but cannot turn over, so on LG M50 it can at best shrink the over-read, not reach D's sign at cycle 99.
- H228 (the tie beats the line in the drift): set median |D<sub>&eta;</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>) &minus; D<sub>99</sub>| below |D<sub>10</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>) &minus; D<sub>99</sub>| on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H229 (and carries the forecast on LG M50): set median e<sub>FS</sub> &lt; e<sub>NS</sub> on both LG M50 sets (`Chen2020`, `OKane2022`). Refuted if on neither.
- H230 (design by design): e<sub>FS</sub> &lt; e<sub>A</sub> on at least 26 of 40 designs (one-sided sign test p &asymp; 0.04). Refuted if on at most 20.
- H231 (the drift is the lever on LG M50): set median e<sub>ND</sub> at most 0.03 on both LG M50 sets. Refuted if above 0.05 on both.
- Reported, not hypothesised: e of FL, FH, ND against A, N, NS, and design-by-design counts FS&lt;A, FS&lt;N, FS&lt;NS, FS&lt;FL, FH&lt;NS, ND&lt;NS; set medians of k<sub>&eta;</sub>, the measured D at 10 and 99 and its largest-magnitude value, each forecast at L<sub>99</sub> and its error; and, post hoc, k<sub>&eta;</sub> refitted over cycles 2..99 with the measured &eta;, to show whether the tie holds along the run.
- Caveats fixed now: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zs, so this is a check on known designs, not a held-out test; D is a model internal (plating overpotential at the nodes) and &eta; a symmetric cut-off shift standing in for the charge overpotential (5zc), CC charge only; cycle-2 weights; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zs outcome: the capacity factor is done; the ten-cycle forecast's remaining error is the lever. A saturating ten-cycle fit of the charge overpotential brings the window factor to within 2.4 to 4.8% of the measured cycle-99 factor on all four sets (5zr's line: 3.6 to 7.6%), and the node lever fed the measured factor itself (NM, the ceiling) moves the cycle-99 lithium-loss error by at most 0.7 points on three sets (&minus;0.2, +0.4, &minus;0.7) and 1.3 on `NCA_Kim2011`. With the saturating factor the node lever beats the law's own lever on 22 of 40 designs. H224, H225 and H226 supported, H227 inconclusive
- Analysis: stdlib, 19 s (`rows`), report run once, nothing in `experiments/saturate/run.py` changed after the plan. Reproduces 5zr: A, N, NE and q<sub>E</sub>'s error equal its committed values on all 40 designs (tested).
- H224 supported (3 of 4): set median |ln(q<sub>S</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>)/q<sub>M</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>))| is 0.024 against q<sub>E</sub>'s 0.076 (`Ai2020`), 0.045 against 0.067 (`Chen2020`), 0.043 against 0.068 (`OKane2022`), 0.048 against 0.036 (`NCA_Kim2011`).
- H225 supported (4 of 4 at most 0.05). q<sub>S</sub> reads 2.00, 1.45, 1.44 and 1.87 against measured 1.92, 1.54, 1.53 and 2.00.
- H226 supported (3 of 4 at most 0.01, none above 0.02): set median e<sub>NM</sub> against e<sub>N</sub> is 0.020 against 0.023 (`Ai2020`), 0.063 against 0.059 (`Chen2020`), 0.069 against 0.076 (`OKane2022`), 0.035 against 0.048 (`NCA_Kim2011`); NM beats N on 4/8, 6/12, 8/12, 6/8 designs.
- H227 inconclusive: NS beats A on 22 of 40 designs (6/8, 8/12, 5/12, 3/8), against NE's 14 in 5zr.
- Reported: set median e<sub>NS</sub> is 0.023, 0.054, 0.067, 0.030 (A: 0.083, 0.082, 0.059, 0.022; N: 0.023, 0.059, 0.076, 0.048; NE: 0.028, 0.054, 0.081, 0.057); NS beats N on 2/8, 6/12, 8/12, 5/8 and NE on 6/8, 6/12, 5/12, 5/8. On `NCA_Kim2011` NS (0.030) is below the measured-factor ceiling NM (0.035): factor errors and lever errors offset there, so a better factor is not always a better forecast. The hold q<sub>H</sub> is closer still to the measured factor on three sets (0.016, 0.031, 0.021, 0.059), but its forecast NH (0.022, 0.068, 0.076, 0.034) is no better than N on LG M50. AS (A's lever with q<sub>S</sub>) is 0.068, 0.098, 0.079, 0.016: the law's own lever with a better factor is worse on LG M50, as AE was in 5zr. &eta;<sub>S</sub> at L<sub>99</sub> reads 120, 368, 373 and 163 mV against the 132, 372, 382 and 185 the cycle-99 charge needs (5zr's line 106, 341, 349, 155; the hold 143, 379, 390, 177). The fitted decay length is 1.3, 15.1, 4.1 and 0.74 times L<sub>10</sub> &minus; L<sub>2</sub> (set medians), and 20 of 40 designs' &lambda; sits on a grid end (2/8, 8/12, 6/12, 4/8), mostly the long end on LG M50, where the fit is close to 5zr's line.
- Reading: three steps (5zq, 5zr, 5zs) took the ten-cycle forecast of the charge-capacity factor from 16 to 51% off at cycle 99 to 2.4 to 4.8% off, and NM shows that even the exact factor would change the node lever's cycle-99 error by under a point on three of four sets. So the capacity factor is no longer where the ten-cycle forecast's error lives. With N, the forecast is 2.3% off on `Ai2020` and 3.0 to 4.8% on `NCA_Kim2011`, but 5.4 to 7.6% on LG M50, where 5zp's measured plating overpotential (with the measured factor) reached 1.4 to 1.5%: on LG M50 the error is the lever, the non-OCP part of the plating overpotential that 5zq's straight line could not follow (it rises, then turns over). For a design search the advice is unchanged: fit the law's line through 30 to 50 cycles. Next: a physically shaped ten-cycle forecast of the non-OCP plating-overpotential drift on LG M50 (for example tied to the falling charge overpotential &eta;, which shares its turn-over), and 5zp's cycle-n weights on `Ai2020`.
- Caveats: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zr; half the designs' decay lengths sit on a grid end, so the saturating fit is weakly determined and on LG M50 often a line; the hold variant was chosen after seeing 5zr's post hoc &eta;; &eta; is a symmetric cut-off shift (5zc), CC charge only; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/saturate/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `saturate.svg`; tests `tests/test_saturate.py`.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zs: does a saturating ten-cycle fit of the charge overpotential close the window factor's late under-read, and is any capacity-factor gain left for the node lever? (plan, written and committed before any of the new forecasts was computed; H224 to H227 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zr's window factor q<sub>E</sub> forecast the cycle-99 charge-capacity factor within 3.6 to 7.6%, but its post hoc path showed the error opening late (&minus;3.5 to &minus;6.6% by cycles 75 to 99 on LG M50) because its ten-cycle &eta; line keeps falling where the measured &eta; levels off; and fed to the law with 5zn's node lever N it changed N's error by under 0.01. Two questions follow: does a saturating &eta; (one whose fall levels off) close that late under-read from cycles 2..10 alone, and, more decisive for the programme, is there any capacity-factor gain left for N at all? The second is answered by N with the measured factor q<sub>M</sub> (NM, a ceiling that reads cycle 99): if NM is N to within 0.01, no forecast of the factor can help N, and the ten-cycle forecast's remaining error is the lever.
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/saturate/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation; 5zk's 40 designs, 5zr's code imported unchanged). &eta;<sub>n</sub> as 5zr, n = 2..10. Saturating fit &eta;<sub>S</sub>(l) = c + d exp(&minus;(l &minus; L<sub>2</sub>)/&lambda;): &lambda; on a fixed grid of 60 log-spaced values from 0.25 to 20 times L<sub>10</sub> &minus; L<sub>2</sub>, c and d by least squares for each &lambda;, the &lambda; with the least squared error kept (the long end of the grid is close to 5zr's line), clamped to [0, 0.49 (v<sub>hi</sub> &minus; v<sub>lo</sub>)]. Hold &eta;<sub>H</sub>(l): 5zr's line up to L<sub>10</sub>, held at its L<sub>10</sub> value beyond (no fit past the line). q<sub>S</sub>, q<sub>H</sub> = 5zr's `window_factor` with &eta;<sub>S</sub>, &eta;<sub>H</sub>. Forecasts at &kappa; = 25.3, line through cycles 2..10, on 5zm's tables: A, N, NE (reproduced), NS, the primary (N's du, q<sub>S</sub>), NH (N, q<sub>H</sub>), AS (A's du, q<sub>S</sub>), and NM (N, q<sub>M</sub>; reads cycle 99, a diagnosis). Error e = |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|; set medians over 8, 12, 12, 8 designs.
- Already seen: everything 5zk to 5zr report on these designs, including 5zr's post hoc set medians of the &eta; cycle 99 needs (132, 372, 382, 185 mV) against &eta; at cycle 10 (144, 382, 392, 178): the hold variant was chosen knowing these, so it is reported, not hypothesised. Not seen: any saturating fit, q<sub>S</sub> or q<sub>H</sub> at any cycle, and NM, NS, NH or AS on any design.
- H224 (saturation beats the line in the factor): set median |ln(q<sub>S</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>)/q<sub>M</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>))| below 5zr's |ln(q<sub>E</sub>/q<sub>M</sub>)| on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H225 (and reaches it closely): set median |ln(q<sub>S</sub>/q<sub>M</sub>)| at cycle 99 at most 0.05 on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if above 0.10 on 3 or more.
- H226 (no capacity-factor gain is left for the node lever): set median |e<sub>NM</sub> &minus; e<sub>N</sub>| at most 0.01 on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if above 0.02 on 3 or more.
- H227 (design by design): e<sub>NS</sub> &lt; e<sub>A</sub> on at least 26 of 40 designs (one-sided sign test p &asymp; 0.04). Refuted if on at most 20.
- Reported, not hypothesised: e of NH, AS, NM against A, N and NE, and design-by-design counts NS&lt;A, NS&lt;N, NS&lt;NE, NH&lt;N, NM&lt;N, AS&lt;A; set medians of q<sub>S</sub>, q<sub>H</sub> at L<sub>99</sub>, |ln(q<sub>H</sub>/q<sub>M</sub>)|, &eta;<sub>S</sub> and &eta;<sub>H</sub> at L<sub>99</sub> against the &eta; cycle 99 needs, the fitted &lambda; over L<sub>10</sub> &minus; L<sub>2</sub>, and how many designs' &lambda; sits on a grid end.
- Caveats fixed now: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zr, so this is a check on known designs, not a held-out test; a three-parameter fit to nine points over a short range of lithium lost is weakly determined, which the grid-end count reports; &eta; is a symmetric cut-off shift standing in for the charge overpotential (5zc), CC charge only; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zr outcome: yes for the factor, no for the forecast. The electrode-balance window with a ten-cycle line in the charge overpotential forecasts the cycle-99 charge-capacity factor within 3.6 to 7.6% on all four sets, where 5zq's straight line missed by 16 to 51%; but fed to the law with the node lever it beats 5zd's lever on only 14 of 40 designs (set medians 2.8% on `Ai2020` against 8.3%, 5.4% on `Chen2020` against 8.2%, 8.1% on `OKane2022` against 5.9%, 5.7% on `NCA_Kim2011` against 2.2%), and is no better than the node lever with the law's own factor. The balance alone, with &eta; held, is closer still on three sets (2.4 to 4.0%). H220 and H221 supported, H222 inconclusive, H223 refuted
- Analysis: stdlib, 18 s (`rows`), report run once, nothing in `experiments/window/run.py` changed after the plan apart from adding the `posthoc` command after the verdicts. Reproduces 5zq: A, N, NQ and PQ equal its committed values on all 40 designs (tested).
- H220 supported (4 of 4 at most 0.10): set median |ln(q<sub>E</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>)/q<sub>M</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>))| is 0.076 (`Ai2020`), 0.067 (`Chen2020`), 0.068 (`OKane2022`), 0.036 (`NCA_Kim2011`); q<sub>E</sub> reads 1.86, 1.41, 1.40 and 1.87 against measured 1.92, 1.54, 1.53 and 2.00.
- H221 supported (4 of 4): against 5zq's straight line's 0.512, 0.161, 0.157 and 0.232.
- H222 inconclusive (2 of 4): set median e<sub>NE</sub> against e<sub>A</sub> is 0.028 against 0.083 (`Ai2020`), 0.054 against 0.082 (`Chen2020`), 0.081 against 0.059 (`OKane2022`), 0.057 against 0.022 (`NCA_Kim2011`).
- H223 refuted: NE beats A on 14 of 40 designs (6/8, 5/12, 2/12, 1/8).
- Reported: against the node lever with the law's factor (N: 0.023, 0.059, 0.076, 0.048) NE changes the set median by +0.005, &minus;0.005, +0.005 and +0.009 and wins on 19 of 40 designs (2/8, 6/12, 7/12, 4/8), so the window factor adds nothing to N. AE (A's lever with q<sub>E</sub>) beats A on `Ai2020` (0.068, 7/8) and `NCA_Kim2011` (0.020, 6/8) and loses on LG M50 (0.101 and 0.094, 2/12 each). FE (5zq's F lever with q<sub>E</sub>) is 0.086, 0.082, 0.100, 0.057, no better than 5zq's F. PE (measured overpotential with q<sub>E</sub>, a diagnosis) is 0.060, 0.023, 0.034, 0.013 against PQ's 0.080, 0.014, 0.015, 0.011 and 5zp's P (law's factor) 0.087, 0.026, 0.029, 0.012: on `Chen2020` the window keeps a quarter of what the measured factor gained over the law's (0.026 to 0.023, against PQ's 0.014), and on `OKane2022` it loses ground (0.034 against 0.029). The balance alone (q<sub>B</sub>, &eta; held at the law's ten-cycle mean, i.e. the law's own factor renormalised at L<sub>2</sub>) misses the measured factor at cycle 99 by 0.040, 0.034, 0.024 and 0.071, closer than q<sub>E</sub> on all but `NCA_Kim2011`. The effective charge overpotential falls with cycling on every set: &eta; at cycles 2 and 10 is 151 and 144 mV (`Ai2020`), 388 and 382 (`Chen2020`), 397 and 392 (`OKane2022`), 196 and 178 (`NCA_Kim2011`), a ten-cycle slope of &minus;0.57 to &minus;1.20 mV per mmol lost; the line reads 106, 341, 349 and 155 mV at cycle 99, where the cycle-99 charge needs 132, 372, 382 and 185 (`posthoc.txt`). With the lines through 2..50, NE is 0.015, 0.035, 0.027, 0.012 and AE 0.026, 0.030, 0.017, 0.007, against A's 0.032, 0.020, 0.017, 0.011.
- Post hoc (`run.py posthoc`, `posthoc.txt`, after the verdicts): along the path, on LG M50 q<sub>E</sub> stays within 1.1% of the measured factor to cycle 50 and falls to &minus;3.5 to &minus;6.6% by cycles 75 to 99, as the &eta; line keeps falling where the measured &eta; has levelled; q<sub>B</sub> over-reads by 2.2 to 5.1% from cycle 20 to 75 on LG M50 and by 2 to 4% on `Ai2020` throughout. So the law's own factor is high by a few per cent in the middle of the run, the window with the &eta; line is low by a few per cent at the end, and neither error is large.
- Reading: the capacity factor's upward curve, which 5zq's straight line missed, is the open-circuit electrode balance: lost lithium slides the window along the two OCP curves, and that is nonlinear in lithium lost. The law already carries this through its own factor, which is within 2.4 to 7.1% of the measured one at cycle 99. So 5zq's "the capacity factor is the larger failure" was a failure of the straight line, not of the law, and resistance growth does not end the charge earlier in these runs: the effective charge overpotential falls by 6 to 18 mV over ten cycles and then levels. Getting the factor right to within a few per cent does not carry the forecast, though: NE is N to within 0.01 in set median, and PE keeps little or none of PQ's gain on LG M50, so the factor's last few per cent along the path still move cycle-99 lithium loss by one to two points. On LG M50 the ten-cycle forecast's remaining error is the lever: the law's du, where 5zp's measured plating overpotential took the error from 3.8 to 2.6% (`Chen2020`) before the factor mattered. For a design search the advice is unchanged: fit the law's line through 30 to 50 cycles (A<sub>50</sub> 1.1 to 3.2%). Next: a ten-cycle forecast of the plating-weighted plating overpotential's path from the node states (5zn's node lever applied to &eta;<sub>pl</sub> rather than the node OCP, or a saturating fit of E<sub>w</sub>), and a saturating &eta; (its fall levels off) in q<sub>E</sub>; 5zp's cycle-n weights on `Ai2020` remain open.
- Caveats: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zq; &eta; is a symmetric cut-off shift standing in for the charge overpotential (5zc); CC charge only; 60-point window integration; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/window/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `posthoc.txt`, `rows.json`, `window.svg`; tests `tests/test_window.py`.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zr: does the electrode-balance map, with a ten-cycle line in the charge overpotential, forecast the capacity factor that 5zq's straight line under-read? (plan, written and committed before any of the new forecasts was computed; H220 to H223 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zq found that the measured charge-capacity factor q<sub>ch</sub>(2)/q<sub>ch</sub>(n) curves upward in lithium lost after cycle 10, so a ten-cycle straight line under-reads it at cycle 99 on every set (1.20 to 1.59 against 1.53 to 2.00), and named a physically shaped forecast as the next step: the constant-current charge shrinks because lost lithium slides and narrows the open-circuit window (5r's electrode-balance map, design only) and because a growing overpotential &eta; ends the step earlier. Both are nonlinear in lithium lost through the OCP curves, so the map may bend where a line cannot. The question is whether the map, fed only a ten-cycle line in &eta;, keeps the gain 5zp's measured factor gave.
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/window/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation; 5zk's 40 designs, 5zq's, 5zp's and 5zc's code imported unchanged). Per cycle n = 2..10, &eta;<sub>n</sub> = 5zc's `solve_eta`(n<sub>Li</sub> &minus; L<sub>n</sub>, q<sub>ch</sub>(n)): the symmetric shift of both cut-offs that puts 5r's open-circuit window capacity at the measured constant-current charge Ah. Ten-cycle line &eta;<sub>10</sub>(l) = a + s l, least squares on (L<sub>n</sub>, &eta;<sub>n</sub>), n = 2..10, clamped to [0, 0.49 (v<sub>hi</sub> &minus; v<sub>lo</sub>)] (as 5ze's &eta; drift). Window factor q<sub>E</sub>(l) = Q<sub>&eta;10(L2)</sub>(n<sub>Li</sub> &minus; L<sub>2</sub>) / Q<sub>&eta;10(l)</sub>(n<sub>Li</sub> &minus; l) for l &gt; L<sub>2</sub>, 1 below, with Q<sub>&eta;</sub> 5zc's `cap_eta`. For comparison the balance alone, q<sub>B</sub>(l), the same with &eta; held at the law's ten-cycle mean (the law's own factor renormalised at L<sub>2</sub>). Forecasts at &kappa; = 25.3, line through cycles 2..10, on 5zm's tables: A and N (reproduced), NE, the primary (N's du, q<sub>E</sub>), AE (A's du, q<sub>E</sub>), FE (5zq's F du, N + D<sub>10</sub>, with q<sub>E</sub>), and PE (5zp's measured overpotential du<sub>P</sub> with q<sub>E</sub>; reads cycle 99, a diagnosis of whether q<sub>E</sub> can stand in for the measured factor in 5zp's PQ). Error e = |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|; set medians over 8, 12, 12, 8 designs.
- Already seen: everything 5zk to 5zq report on these designs (A, N, F, NQ, PQ, q<sub>M</sub> at cycle 99, q<sub>10</sub>, D, the curvatures), and the law's &eta; as a ten-cycle mean of &eta;<sub>n</sub>. Not seen: any &eta;<sub>n</sub> series or &eta; line on these designs, q<sub>E</sub> or q<sub>B</sub> at any cycle past 10, and any forecast built from them. 5ze fitted an &eta; line on other pools (the levers read at &eta;(l)), not as a capacity factor.
- H220 (the window forecast reaches the measured factor): set median |ln(q<sub>E</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>)/q<sub>M</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>))| at most 0.10 on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if above 0.25 on 3 or more (5zq's H218 bars).
- H221 (it beats the straight line): set median |ln(q<sub>E</sub>/q<sub>M</sub>)| at cycle 99 below 5zq's |ln(q<sub>10</sub>/q<sub>M</sub>)| on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H222 (and carries the forecast): set median e<sub>NE</sub> below e<sub>A</sub> on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H223 (design by design): e<sub>NE</sub> &lt; e<sub>A</sub> on at least 26 of 40 designs (one-sided sign test p &asymp; 0.04). Refuted if on at most 20.
- Reported, not hypothesised: e of AE, FE, PE against A, N, NQ and PQ; NE and AE with the line through 2..30 and 2..50; set medians of &eta;<sub>2</sub>, s (mV per mmol), q<sub>E</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>), q<sub>B</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>) and |ln(q<sub>B</sub>/q<sub>M</sub>)| at 99 (how much of the measured factor the balance alone explains); NE against N design by design.
- Caveats fixed now: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zq, so this is a check on known designs, not a held-out test; &eta; is a symmetric shift of both cut-offs standing in for the charge overpotential (5zc), and the constant-current charge ends at the upper cut-off only; open-circuit tables at 60-point integration; NE needs a cell model's node states at cycle 2 (for N), while AE needs only the design, the OCP curves and measured charge Ah; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zq outcome: no. Straight-line forecasts from cycles 2..10 of the two inputs 5zp fed by oracle keep little of its gain. With the node lever plus a ten-cycle drift line and a ten-cycle capacity-factor line (F), the ten-cycle forecast misses cycle-99 lithium loss by 5.7% on `Chen2020` (5zd's lever A: 8.2%, oracle PQ: 1.4%), 7.2% on `OKane2022` (A 5.9%), 10.1% on `NCA_Kim2011` (A 2.2%) and 7.0% on `Ai2020` (A 8.3%); F beats A on 15 of 40 designs. The capacity factor's line under-reads cycle 99 on every set (1.20 to 1.59 against 1.53 to 2.00), and the drift's line has the wrong sign at cycle 99 on both LG M50 sets. H217 refuted, H216, H218 and H219 inconclusive
- Analysis: stdlib, 13 s (`rows`), report run once, nothing in `experiments/drift/run.py` changed after the plan. Reproduces 5zn and 5zp: A, N and PQ equal the committed values on all 40 designs (difference 0.0, tested).
- H216 inconclusive (2 of 4): set median e<sub>F</sub> against e<sub>A</sub> is 0.057 against 0.082 (`Chen2020`), 0.070 against 0.083 (`Ai2020`), 0.072 against 0.059 (`OKane2022`), 0.101 against 0.022 (`NCA_Kim2011`).
- H217 refuted: F beats A on 15 of 40 designs (4/8, 8/12, 2/12, 1/8).
- H218 inconclusive (none at most 0.10, one above 0.25): set median |ln(q<sub>10</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>)/q<sub>M</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>))| is 0.161 (`Chen2020`), 0.157 (`OKane2022`), 0.232 (`NCA_Kim2011`), 0.512 (`Ai2020`). The line under-reads on every set: forecast 1.35, 1.34, 1.59 and 1.20 against measured 1.54, 1.53, 2.00 and 1.92. The charge-capacity factor curves upward in lithium lost after cycle 10.
- H219 inconclusive (2 within a quarter of |M<sub>w,99</sub>|, none above half): set median |D<sub>10</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>) &minus; D<sub>99</sub>| is 8.71 mV against |M<sub>w,99</sub>| 30.5 (`Chen2020`), 7.09 against 29.8 (`OKane2022`), 0.02 against 128.9 (`NCA_Kim2011`, where the drift is nil), 12.98 against 46.6 (`Ai2020`). On LG M50 the line forecasts +2.50 and +0.87 mV at cycle 99 where the DFN gives &minus;2.50 and &minus;2.58: the risk the plan named. The drift rises over cycles 2..10 (0.56 and 0.26 mV at cycle 10) and turns over later.
- Reported: AQ (A's lever with q<sub>10</sub>) is worse than A on three sets (0.150 against 0.082 on `Chen2020`, 0.128 against 0.059 on `OKane2022`, 0.060 against 0.083 on `Ai2020` the exception); NQ 0.055 and 0.068 on LG M50, close to F; G (E<sub>w</sub>'s own ten-cycle line with q<sub>10</sub>) is the best ten-cycle variant on LG M50 (0.053, 0.055) and the worst on the other two (0.121, 0.159). F beats N on 10 of 40 (1/8, 3/12, 6/12, 0/8). The share of the A-to-PQ gap F closes is 0.37 on `Chen2020` and &minus;0.30 on `OKane2022` (on `Ai2020` and `NCA_Kim2011` PQ is not much better than A, so the share is meaningless there: 4.35 and &minus;6.94). F with the line through 2..30 and 2..50 (the input fits kept on 2..10): 0.043/0.030 (`Chen2020`), 0.049/0.032 (`OKane2022`), 0.056/0.035 (`NCA_Kim2011`), 0.063/0.040 (`Ai2020`), against A's 0.046/0.020, 0.028/0.017, 0.013/0.011, 0.039/0.032: a longer line does not rescue the inputs' ten-cycle fits. Set median |c<sub>F</sub>| is 0.17 to 0.29 on three sets and 0.86 on `NCA_Kim2011`, against PQ's 0.03 to 0.24.
- Reading: 5zp's diagnosis stands (the law's form is close to right on LG M50 when fed the two measured inputs), but the inputs themselves are not linear in lithium lost over the first ten cycles' range, so ten cycles cannot hand them to the law by a straight line. The capacity factor is the larger failure: it accelerates after cycle 10 on every set, and a line that under-reads it makes A's forecast worse, not better, because A's own law-side factor already rises faster. The drift is small (under 5 mV at cycle 99 against 30 to 130 mV of OCP rise) but enters the exponent at 25.3 V<sup>&minus;1</sup>, and its turn-over means any early extrapolation over-reads it. For a design search 5zm's advice still holds: fit the law's line through 30 to 50 cycles. The next step is a physically shaped forecast of the capacity factor: q<sub>ch</sub> falls as lithium loss shrinks the window and as resistance growth ends the constant-current step earlier, and 5r's electrode-balance map already gives the first part from the design alone; fed that map's q(L) in place of q<sub>10</sub>, does the forecast keep PQ's gain?
- Caveats: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zp; cycle-2 weights; end-of-charge values only; &eta;<sub>pl</sub> and node OCPs are model internals; through-origin straight lines are the simplest forecasts, not the best possible ones; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/drift/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `drift.svg`; tests `tests/test_drift.py`.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zq: can cycles 2..10 forecast the two inputs 5zp fed by oracle? A ten-cycle forecast of the non-OCP plating-overpotential drift and the charge-capacity factor, against 5zd's and 5zn's levers (plan, written and committed before any of the new forecasts was computed; H216 to H219 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zp found that on LG M50 the law's straight line in R against cycle number holds to |c| of 0.03 to 0.04 when it is fed the measured plating overpotential and the measured charge-capacity factor (PQ, ten-cycle error 1.4 and 1.5%), and that "what a ten-cycle forecast lacks there is a forecast of those two, not a different law". PQ reads cycle 99. This step asks whether the simplest forecasts of the two that read only cycles 2..10 keep enough of PQ's gain to beat the levers a ten-cycle forecast can actually use (5zd's A and 5zn's node lever N).
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/drift/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation; 5zk's 40 designs, 5zp's and 5zo's code imported unchanged). D<sub>n</sub> = E<sub>w,n</sub> &minus; M<sub>w,n</sub>, the non-OCP part of the plating-weighted plating-overpotential rise from cycle 2 (5zp's E<sub>w</sub> less 5zo's M<sub>w</sub>, cycle-2 weights; D<sub>2</sub> = 0). Ten-cycle drift D<sub>10</sub>(l) = k<sub>D</sub>(l &minus; L<sub>2</sub>) for l &gt; L<sub>2</sub>, 0 below, k<sub>D</sub> the least-squares slope through the origin of D<sub>n</sub> on L<sub>n</sub> &minus; L<sub>2</sub>, n = 2..10. Ten-cycle capacity factor q<sub>10</sub>(l) = 1 + k<sub>q</sub>(l &minus; L<sub>2</sub>) for l &gt; L<sub>2</sub>, 1 below, k<sub>q</sub> the same fit of q<sub>ch</sub>(2)/q<sub>ch</sub>(n) &minus; 1, n = 2..10. Forecasts at &kappa; = 25.3, line through cycles 2..10, on 5zm's tables: A (5zd's lever, law's capacity factor; reproduced), N (5zn's node lever, law's factor; reproduced), F, the primary (du = N + D<sub>10</sub>, q<sub>10</sub>), AQ (A's du, q<sub>10</sub>), NQ (N, q<sub>10</sub>), G (du = E<sub>10</sub>(l) = k<sub>E</sub>(l &minus; L<sub>2</sub>), the same through-origin fit of E<sub>w,n</sub> itself, q<sub>10</sub>), and PQ (5zp's diagnosis, reproduced). Error e = |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|; set medians over 8, 12, 12, 8 designs. Only PQ reads past cycle 10.
- Already seen: everything 5zk to 5zp report on these designs (A, N, O, P, OQ, PQ, E and M at cycles 10 and 99, q<sub>ch</sub>(2)/q<sub>ch</sub>(99), the curvatures). Not seen: any fit over cycles 2..10 of D, E or the capacity factor, and any forecast built from one.
- H216 (the ten-cycle forecast of both inputs beats the law's lever): set median e<sub>F</sub> below e<sub>A</sub> on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H217 (design by design): e<sub>F</sub> &lt; e<sub>A</sub> on at least 26 of 40 designs (one-sided sign test p &asymp; 0.04). Refuted if on at most 20.
- H218 (the capacity factor extrapolates): set median |ln(q<sub>10</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>)/q<sub>M</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>))| at most 0.10 on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if above 0.25 on 3 or more.
- H219 (the drift extrapolates): set median |D<sub>10</sub>(L<sub>99</sub>) &minus; D<sub>99</sub>| at most a quarter of the set median |M<sub>w,99</sub>| on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if above half of it on 3 or more. (5zp's E/M of 0.91 to 1.10 at cycle 99 against E above M at cycle 10 suggests D changes sign on LG M50, which a line through the origin cannot follow; that is the risk this tests.)
- Reported, not hypothesised: e of AQ, NQ, G against A, N, F and PQ; F against N design by design; F with the line through cycles 2..30 and 2..50 (the fits of D and q kept on 2..10); k<sub>D</sub> and k<sub>q</sub> set medians; the share of the A-to-PQ gap F closes, (e<sub>A</sub> &minus; e<sub>F</sub>)/(e<sub>A</sub> &minus; e<sub>PQ</sub>) on set medians.
- Caveats fixed now: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zp, so this is a check on known designs, not a held-out test; cycle-2 weights; end-of-charge values only; &eta;<sub>pl</sub> and node OCPs are model internals, so F needs a cell model's node states at cycles 2..10, while q<sub>10</sub> needs only measured charge capacity; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only. 5zp's other next step (cycle-n weights on `Ai2020`) is left for a later step.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zp outcome: on LG M50 the law's line bends because it holds two things at cycle 2, the non-OCP part of the plating overpotential and the capacity factor; fed both as the DFN measured them, the ten-cycle forecast misses cycle-99 lithium loss by 1.4% (`Chen2020`) and 1.5% (`OKane2022`) against 5zo's 3.8 and 3.4%, and the line's bend falls from 0.10 and 0.087 to 0.030 and 0.038. Either alone does little, and on `Ai2020` the overpotential makes it worse (bend 0.13 to 0.27, error 4.2 to 8.7%). The bend is not 5zm's coarse table. H212 supported (narrowly), H213 and H214 inconclusive, H215 supported
- Analysis: stdlib, 26 s (`rows`), report run once, nothing in `experiments/rate/run.py` changed after the plan. Reproduces 5zo: O's error and curvature equal 5zo's committed values on all 40 designs (tested).
- H212 supported (3 of 4), but the third set carries no information: set median |c<sub>P</sub>| against |c<sub>O</sub>| is 0.080 against 0.100 (`Chen2020`), 0.080 against 0.087 (`OKane2022`), 0.1085 against 0.1086 (`NCA_Kim2011`, where the plating overpotential's rise equals the OCP's, E/M = 1.00 at cycle 99, so P is O) and 0.271 against 0.133 (`Ai2020`).
- H213 inconclusive (2 of 4): e<sub>P</sub> against e<sub>O</sub> 0.026 against 0.038 (`Chen2020`), 0.029 against 0.034 (`OKane2022`), 0.012 against 0.012 (`NCA_Kim2011`, 0.01240 against 0.01234), 0.087 against 0.042 (`Ai2020`). Design by design P beats O on 8/12, 7/12, 3/8, 2/8.
- H214 inconclusive (2 of 4): the measured capacity factor alone lowers |c| on `Ai2020` (0.117) and `NCA_Kim2011` (0.070) and raises it on both LG M50 sets (0.118, 0.100): it flips the bend's sign there (signed c 0.020 to &minus;0.063 on `Chen2020`, 0.065 to &minus;0.042 on `OKane2022`).
- H215 supported: the 600-cell table moves set median |c<sub>O</sub>| by 0.001, 0.002, 0.004 and 0.000; errors move by at most 0.002.
- Reported: PQ, both measured, gives e 0.014 (`Chen2020`), 0.015 (`OKane2022`), 0.011 (`NCA_Kim2011`), 0.080 (`Ai2020`) and |c| 0.030, 0.038, 0.069, 0.239; PQ beats O on 9/12, 9/12, 6/8, 2/8 designs. On LG M50 that ten-cycle error matches what 5zo needed a fifty-cycle line for (O<sub>50</sub> 0.016, 0.015). P's line through 30 and 50 cycles: 0.017/0.013 (`Chen2020`), 0.019/0.014 (`OKane2022`), 0.012/0.010 (`NCA_Kim2011`), 0.049/0.024 (`Ai2020`). The plating-overpotential rise against the OCP rise at cycle 99 (E/M) is 0.92 and 0.91 on LG M50, 1.00 on `NCA_Kim2011`, 1.10 on `Ai2020`; at cycle 10 E exceeds M on every set but `NCA_Kim2011` (3.27 against 2.88 mV on `Chen2020`). The measured capacity factor q<sub>ch</sub>(2)/q<sub>ch</sub>(99) is 1.53 to 2.00.
- Reading: on LG M50 the two held inputs pull the bend opposite ways, so each alone moves it little or past zero. Given the law's capacity factor, the measured overpotential leaves the signed set-median bend positive (0.067 on `Chen2020`, 0.066 on `OKane2022`); the measured capacity factor alone pushes it negative (&minus;0.063, &minus;0.042); with both it is &minus;0.009 and 0.001. The plating overpotential rises faster than the OCP over the first ten cycles (3.27 against 2.88 mV on `Chen2020`) and slower by cycle 99 (E/M 0.92); how each moves R's early and late slopes is not decomposed here. With both, the law's straight line holds to |c| of 0.03 to 0.04, and a ten-cycle line is as good as 5zo's fifty-cycle one. So the law's rate form, R(L) = &int; exp(&kappa; du) Q<sub>0</sub>/Q dl, is close to right on LG M50 when du is the full plating overpotential and Q the delivered charge; what a ten-cycle forecast lacks there is a forecast of those two, not a different law. `Ai2020` is the exception: its overpotential rises 10% more than its OCP by cycle 99 and feeding that in doubles the bend, so on that set something else (the plating's location moving off its cycle-2 weights, 5zk, is the first suspect) offsets it. Next: forecast the non-OCP overpotential drift and the charge-capacity factor from cycles 2..10 (for example the resistance growth rate times the charge current), and test cycle-n weights on `Ai2020`.
- Caveats: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zo; cycle-2 weights; end-of-charge values only; &eta;<sub>pl</sub> and node OCPs are model internals; P, OQ, PQ read cycle 99 by construction and are diagnoses; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/rate/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `rate.svg`; tests `tests/test_rate.py`.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zp: does the law's line bend because it holds the plating overpotential's non-OCP part, or the capacity factor, at cycle 2? An overpotential oracle, a measured-capacity oracle and a grid check on 5zo's bend (plan, written and committed before any of the new forecasts or curvatures was computed; H212 to H215 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zo found that even fed the measured plating-weighted OCP rise (oracle O), the law's line in R against cycle number bends by c = ln(s<sub>late</sub>/s<sub>early</sub>) of 0.09 to 0.13 in set-median size, which leaves 1.2 to 4.2% of the ten-cycle forecast's error, and named the law's rate form as the next suspect. With du set to the measured OCP rise, two inputs of the law are still held at their cycle-2..10 form: (a) the kinetic driving force, which the law takes to rise only by the OCP rise, while the plating reaction overpotential also moves with the intercalation overpotential and film drop as resistance grows; (b) the capacity factor Q<sub>&eta;</sub>(0)/Q<sub>&eta;</sub>(L), computed on a window shortened by an &eta; fixed from cycles 2..10. 5zk's records hold the end-of-charge plating reaction overpotential of every node on every cycle (`eta_pl_node`, never used so far) and each cycle's charge time, so both can be replaced by what the DFN did. One more candidate is ours: 5zm's tables put about two of 60 cells inside cycles 2..10, so part of c may be the grid.
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/rate/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation; 5zk's 40 designs, 5zo's code imported unchanged). E<sub>w,n</sub> = &Sigma; w<sub>i</sub>(2) (&eta;<sub>pl,i</sub>(n) &minus; &eta;<sub>pl,i</sub>(2)), the plating-weighted rise of the end-of-charge plating overpotential from cycle 2 at cycle-2 weights (5zl's weighting, with &eta;<sub>pl</sub> in place of the node OCP). Lever du<sub>P</sub>: 5zo's `oracle_du` on the points (L<sub>n</sub>, E<sub>w,n</sub>), n = 2..99. Measured capacity factor q<sub>M</sub>(l): linear through (L<sub>n</sub>, q<sub>ch</sub>(2)/q<sub>ch</sub>(n)), n = 2..99, q<sub>ch</sub> the constant-current charge Ah; 1 below L<sub>2</sub> (a constant factor there shifts R by a constant the intercept absorbs); beyond L<sub>99</sub> the least-squares slope over n = 90..99. Forecasts at &kappa; = 25.3, line through cycles 2..10, on 5zo's tables: O (du<sub>O</sub>, law's qr; 5zo's, reproduced), P (du<sub>P</sub>, law's qr), OQ (du<sub>O</sub>, q<sub>M</sub>), PQ (du<sub>P</sub>, q<sub>M</sub>), and O<sub>fine</sub> (O on the same span with 600 cells instead of 60). Curvature c as 5zo (`curvature`). Error |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|. Set medians over 8, 12, 12, 8 designs. All of P, OQ, PQ read cycle 99: diagnoses, not forecasts.
- Already seen: everything 5zk to 5zo report on these designs (A, N, O, W, c<sub>O</sub>, node OCPs, plating shares, charge times as they enter q<sub>ch</sub> in &eta;). Not seen: any &eta;<sub>pl</sub> series, any forecast or curvature with du<sub>P</sub> or q<sub>M</sub>, any table finer than 60 cells. Before writing this plan one cycle-2 &eta;<sub>pl</sub> row of one design was printed to fix the sign (positive at the collector, where nothing plates, and negative next to the separator), nothing else.
- H212 (the driving force straightens the line): set median |c<sub>P</sub>| below set median |c<sub>O</sub>| on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H213 (and carries the forecast): set median e<sub>P</sub> below e<sub>O</sub> on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H214 (the capacity factor bends the line): set median |c<sub>OQ</sub>| below |c<sub>O</sub>| on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H215 (the bend is not the grid): |set median |c<sub>O,fine</sub>| &minus; set median |c<sub>O</sub>|| at most 0.02 on all 4 sets. Refuted if above 0.05 on 2 or more.
- Reported, not hypothesised: PQ's error and |c|; P with the line through 2..30 and 2..50; the signed set-median c of each variant; the set-median ratio E<sub>w,99</sub>/M<sub>w,99</sub> (how much of the driving-force rise is the OCP); e and c of P on the fine grid.
- Caveats fixed now: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zo; cycle-2 weights; end-of-charge values only, while plating runs over the late part of each charge; &eta;<sub>pl</sub> and node OCPs are model internals; all variants read cycle 99 by construction; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zo outcome: about half of the ten-cycle forecast's error on LG M50 is the lever's and half is the law's line. Fed the measured plating-weighted OCP rise (an oracle lever that reads every cycle), the forecast at 25.3 with its line through cycles 2..10 misses cycle-99 lithium loss by 3.8% on `Chen2020`, 3.4% on `OKane2022`, 1.2% on `NCA_Kim2011` and 4.2% on `Ai2020`, against 8.2, 5.9, 2.2 and 8.3% for 5zd's lever; the R slope still drifts by 9 to 13% between cycles 2..10 and 50..99 with the true lever. The node lever's `OKane2022` over-read sits at the separator, not the collector (collector half's share 0.21; 0 of 12 designs). H209 supported, H210 inconclusive, H211 refuted
- Analysis: stdlib, 19 s (`rows`), report run once, nothing in `experiments/oracle/run.py` changed after the plan apart from adding the `posthoc` command after the verdicts. Reproduces 5zn: A, N and W equal 5zn's committed values on all 40 designs (tested).
- H209 supported (3 of 4): set median error of O against A and N is 0.038 against 0.082 and 0.059 (`Chen2020`), 0.034 against 0.059 and 0.076 (`OKane2022`), 0.012 against 0.022 and 0.048 (`NCA_Kim2011`); on `Ai2020` O's 0.042 halves A's 0.083 but loses to N's 0.023. Design by design O beats both on 18 of 40 (0/8, 9/12, 5/12, 4/8).
- H210 inconclusive (1 at most 0.10, none above 0.25): set median |c| is 0.133 (`Ai2020`), 0.1000 (`Chen2020`, 0.10002, over the line by rounding), 0.087 (`OKane2022`), 0.109 (`NCA_Kim2011`). Signed set medians are 0.13, 0.02, 0.06 and 0.01: the drift is design by design and of either sign on three sets, positive (the rate in R accelerating) on `Ai2020`.
- H211 refuted (0 of 12): on `OKane2022` all 12 designs have a positive cycle-10 over-read (set median 2.29 mV, collector half 0.49), and the collector half's share is 0.21.
- Reported: O with its line through 30 and 50 cycles is 0.029 and 0.014 (`Ai2020`), 0.031 and 0.016 (`Chen2020`), 0.023 and 0.015 (`OKane2022`), 0.012 and 0.010 (`NCA_Kim2011`), against A's 0.039/0.032, 0.046/0.020, 0.028/0.017, 0.013/0.011: the true lever helps most where the line is short. 5zl's W (A's lever at &kappa;<sub>w</sub>, also reading cycle 99) beats O on LG M50 (0.025 and 0.019) because its exponent is fitted to the whole path and so absorbs some of the line's drift; they tie on `NCA_Kim2011` (0.012). The node split on the other sets: the collector half's share of a positive over-read is 0.21 (`Ai2020`) and 0.12 (`Chen2020`); `NCA_Kim2011`'s over-read is negative on 4 of 8 designs. The per-node Spearman of (P<sub>i</sub> &minus; M<sub>i</sub>) on x<sub>i</sub>(2) is &minus;0.07, &minus;0.35, &minus;0.30, &minus;0.41: unweighted, the node lever over-reads the low-stoichiometry (collector-side) nodes more, but little plating sits there.
- Post hoc (`run.py posthoc`, `posthoc.txt`, after the verdicts): x<sub>i</sub>(2) rises from the collector to the separator on every set (`OKane2022` 0.39 to 0.68), and the four separator-side nodes carry 0.56 of `OKane2022`'s positive over-read (0.74 on `Ai2020`, 0.27 on `Chen2020`). So 5zn's suspect was the right nodes by stoichiometry but the wrong ones by weight: the over-read that matters is where the plating is, at the deepest-lithiated nodes next to the separator.
- Reading: the two parts 5zn named are about equal on LG M50. A perfect plating-weighted lever takes the ten-cycle error from 8.2 to 3.8% on `Chen2020` and from 5.9 to 3.4% on `OKane2022`; what is left is the law's straight line in R against cycle number, whose slope moves by about 10% across the run even with the true lever, and no lever read from cycle 2 can remove that. On `Ai2020` the node lever beats the oracle, so its 5zn win there is partly an under-read early (&rho;<sub>node,10</sub> 0.67) cancelling the line's positive drift (c = +0.13), not a better lever. For a design search, 5zm's advice holds and is now explained: fitting the line through 30 to 50 cycles buys more than any lever on LG M50. The next step is the law's rate form: test whether c follows the drift of the end-of-charge overpotential &eta; (the law holds &eta; at its cycles-2..10 value while resistance grows) or the capacity factor Q<sub>0</sub>/Q.
- Caveats: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zn; cycle-2 weights; end-of-charge node OCPs are a model internal; the oracle reads cycle 99 by construction; R tables with about two cells inside cycles 2..10 (5zd's span); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/oracle/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `posthoc.txt`, `rows.json`, `oracle.svg`; tests `tests/test_oracle.py`.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 5zo: is the ten-cycle forecast's remaining error in the lever or in the law's constant-rate line? An oracle-lever decomposition, and a node-by-node split of the `OKane2022` over-read (plan, written and committed before any oracle forecast, curvature or node split was computed; H209 to H211 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zn found the node lever reads the plating-weighted rise right by cycle 99 on three sets but the ten-cycle forecast at 25.3 still misses by 0.02 to 0.08, and that the miss has "a second part outside the lever's level: its shape beyond the first ten cycles' range". Two places can hold that part. Either the lever (any lever read from cycle 2) mis-predicts the rise far from cycle 10, or the law itself, a straight line in R(L) = &int; exp(&kappa; du) Q<sub>0</sub>/Q dl against cycle number, bends even when its du is the true plating-weighted rise. Feeding the law the measured rise (an oracle lever that reads every cycle) separates the two: if the oracle forecast is accurate from ten cycles, the residual is the lever's; if it is not, no better lever will fix the forecast and the law's rate form is next. 5zn also left open why the node lever over-reads `OKane2022`'s early rise (5.49 against 3.00 mV at cycle 10); its named suspect is the nodes nearest the current collector, whose x<sub>i</sub>(2) is lowest.
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/oracle/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation; 5zk's 40 designs in `depth/bench.jsonl`, 5zn's balance, &eta;, tables, node lever and forecast code imported unchanged). Oracle lever du<sub>O</sub>(l): linear interpolation of the points (L<sub>n</sub>, M<sub>w,n</sub>), n = 2..99, with M<sub>w,2</sub> = 0 (5zl's plating-weighted end-of-charge OCP rise from cycle 2 at cycle-2 weights), du<sub>O</sub> = 0 below L<sub>2</sub> (a constant there only shifts R by a constant, which the line's intercept absorbs), and beyond L<sub>99</sub> extended with the least-squares slope of M<sub>w</sub> on L over n = 90..99. Forecast O = 5zf's `forecast` at &kappa; = 25.3, line through cycles 2..10, with 5zm's tables and du replaced by du<sub>O</sub>; it reads cycle 99, so it is a diagnosis, the best any lever can give this law. Curvature: on O's R table, s<sub>early</sub> = least-squares slope of R<sub>O</sub>(L<sub>n</sub>) on n over n = 2..10, s<sub>late</sub> over n = 50..99, c = ln(s<sub>late</sub>/s<sub>early</sub>) (0 for a law whose line holds). Node split at cycle 10: per node i, the node lever's predicted rise P<sub>i</sub> = U<sub>n</sub>(x<sub>i</sub>(2) + s(L<sub>10</sub>)) &minus; U<sub>n</sub>(x<sub>i</sub>(2)) against the measured M<sub>i</sub> = u<sub>i</sub>(10) &minus; u<sub>i</sub>(2), weighted over-read o<sub>i</sub> = w<sub>i</sub>(2)(P<sub>i</sub> &minus; M<sub>i</sub>); the collector half is nodes 1 to 10 of 20 (`x_n` from the collector), and its share is &Sigma;<sub>collector</sub> o<sub>i</sub> / &Sigma;<sub>all</sub> o<sub>i</sub>, taken only where the total is positive. A and N are 5zn's (5zd's lever and the node lever at 25.3, line through 2..10). Error |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|, set medians over 8, 12, 12, 8 designs.
- Already seen: every quantity 5zk to 5zn report on these designs (A, N, the one-point variant, 5zl's &kappa;<sub>w</sub> forecast W, &rho;<sub>node,k</sub>, set-median rises at cycle 10). Not seen: any forecast with du<sub>O</sub>, any R curvature, any node-resolved over-read.
- H209 (the residual is the lever's): O's set median error is below both A's and N's on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1 (then the law's line, not the lever, holds most of the ten-cycle miss).
- H210 (the law's line holds given the true lever): set median |c| at most 0.10 on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if above 0.25 on 3 or more.
- H211 (`OKane2022`'s over-read sits at the collector): on `OKane2022`, among designs with a positive total over-read at cycle 10, the collector half carries more than half of it on at least two thirds. Refuted if on at most one third.
- Reported, not hypothesised: O with the line through cycles 2..30 and 2..50; O against W (both read cycle 99); the sign of c per set; the node split on all four sets; the per-design Spearman of (P<sub>i</sub> &minus; M<sub>i</sub>) on x<sub>i</sub>(2) across the 20 nodes, set medians.
- Caveats fixed now: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zn; cycle-2 weights; end-of-charge node OCPs are a model internal; 5zm's R tables put only about two of their 60 cells inside cycles 2..10 (5zd's span, kept so O compares with A and N), so O inherits that resolution; the oracle reads cycle 99 by construction and is not a forecast; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zn outcome: the plateau explains the measured rise but not the forecast. Read at each node's cycle-2 stoichiometry, the law's uniform shift reproduces the plating-weighted OCP rise to within 2 to 16% by cycle 99 on three sets (slope 1.00 `Ai2020`, 0.98 `Chen2020`, 1.18 `NCA_Kim2011`; 0.84 `OKane2022`), against the law's own lever's 1.35 to 1.49 on LG M50, and on `Chen2020` its rise from cycle 2 to 10 is 3.25 mV against 2.88 measured and 0.42 for the law. But the ten-cycle forecast at the physical exponent with this lever beats 5zd's on 2 of 4 sets (`Ai2020` 0.083 to 0.023, `Chen2020` 0.082 to 0.059) and loses on `OKane2022` and `NCA_Kim2011` (0.059 to 0.076, 0.022 to 0.048); 22 of 40 designs. H204 to H208 all inconclusive
- Analysis: stdlib, 16 s (`rows`), report run once, nothing in `experiments/plateau/run.py` changed after the plan was committed. Reproduces 5zm and 5zl: A's set medians (0.083, 0.082, 0.059, 0.022) and 5zl's error at &kappa;<sub>w</sub> (0.085, 0.025, 0.019, 0.012) equal the committed values. A test checks that the node lever equals 5zd's lever when every node sits at the window top.
- H204 inconclusive (2 at most 0.15, none above 0.3): set median |ln &rho;<sub>node,99</sub>| 0.024 (`Ai2020`), 0.095 (`Chen2020`), 0.221 (`OKane2022`), 0.167 (`NCA_Kim2011`).
- H205 inconclusive (2 at most 0.3, none above 0.6): |ln &rho;<sub>node,10</sub>| 0.409, 0.220, 0.556, 0.160. The ratio still drifts with k (set medians &rho;<sub>node,k</sub> at k = 10, 30, 99: `Ai2020` 0.67, 0.91, 1.00; `Chen2020` 0.81, 0.85, 0.98; `OKane2022` 0.57, 0.65, 0.84; `NCA_Kim2011` 0.99, 1.08, 1.18), but far less than 5zm's law-lever ratio (25.3 &times; ratio 148 to 30 on `Chen2020`, a factor of 5; here 1.2).
- H206 inconclusive (2 of 4): N against A 0.023 against 0.083 (`Ai2020`), 0.059 against 0.082 (`Chen2020`), 0.076 against 0.059 (`OKane2022`), 0.048 against 0.022 (`NCA_Kim2011`).
- H207 inconclusive: N beats A on 22 of 40 designs (7/8, 9/12, 5/12, 1/8; one-sided sign test p = 0.32).
- H208 inconclusive: N over 5zl's diagnostic error is 2.41 (`Chen2020`) and 3.93 (`OKane2022`).
- Reported, not hypothesised: the plating-weighted cycle-2 stoichiometry sits 0.15 to 0.22 above the law's window top on three sets and 0.04 on `NCA_Kim2011` (x<sub>w</sub> 0.94 against 0.72 on `Ai2020`, 0.62 against 0.47 on `Chen2020`, 0.61 against 0.46 on `OKane2022`, 0.44 against 0.40 on `NCA_Kim2011`), and the node x<sub>i</sub>(2) span 0.36 to 0.46 of the stoichiometry range through the thickness. The one-point variant (one reading at x<sub>w</sub>) gives 0.091, 0.027, 0.044, 0.024: better than A on both LG M50 sets and close to 5zl's diagnosis on `Chen2020` (0.027 against 0.025), worse on `Ai2020`. N at 25.3 &rho;<sub>node,10</sub> gives 0.068, 0.056, 0.039, 0.075. These were read after the verdicts and are not tests; no variant wins on all four sets.
- Reading: 5zm's mechanism is right about the rise. On LG M50 and `Ai2020` the plating sits well above the law's window top in stoichiometry (deeper in lithiation, off the plateau the window top reads), and the law's own lithium-to-stoichiometry shift read there reproduces the measured plating-weighted rise on `Chen2020` from cycle 10 (3.25 against 2.88 mV, where the law reads 0.42) and to within 2% at cycle 99. So the 1.35 to 1.49 factor 5zl found on LG M50 is mostly a reading-point error, not a different plating physics. What it does not do is forecast better everywhere at 25.3: on `OKane2022` the node lever over-reads the early rise (5.49 against 3.00 mV) and the forecast overshoots, and on `NCA_Kim2011`, where the law's lever already read the rise right, swapping it loses half the accuracy (its rise is the same at cycle 10, 44.3 against 44.6 mV, so the difference is in how the lever extrapolates to the cycle-99 loss, which is 30 L(10) away). The forecast error therefore has a second part outside the lever's level: its shape beyond the first ten cycles' range. For a design search the advice of 5zm stands (fit the law's line through 30 to 50 cycles at 25.3); the node lever is worth using on `Ai2020`-like cells (thick, plateau-free top of charge), where it cuts the ten-cycle error by a factor of 3.6 on 7 of 8 designs.
- Next: test the lever's extrapolation directly (the node lever's predicted rise at cycle 99 against the measured, given only cycles up to 10), and whether the OKane2022 overshoot comes from the nodes nearest the collector, whose x<sub>i</sub>(2) is lowest.
- Caveats: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zm; cycle-2 weights; end-of-charge node OCPs are a model internal; inverting U<sub>n</sub> on the LG M50 plateau is ill-conditioned; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/plateau/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rows.json`, `plateau.svg`; tests `tests/test_plateau.py`.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zn: does a plateau-aware lever, the law's own uniform stoichiometry shift read at each node's cycle-2 stoichiometry on the OCP curve, reproduce the plating-weighted rise from cycle 2 and fix the ten-cycle forecast at the physical exponent? (plan, written and committed before the new lever was computed on any design; H204 to H208 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zm found the plating-weighted OCP rise outruns the law's lever early on LG M50 (25.3 &times; the pointwise ratio 148 at cycle 10 on `Chen2020`, 30 at cycle 99) and named the cause: the law reads dU at the top of its uniform-stoichiometry window, which sits on the graphite plateau, while the plating sits at other stoichiometries through the thickness. The next step it named is a lever that reads the plating-weighted stoichiometry on the OCP curve. If the law's mapping of lost lithium to a stoichiometry shift is right and only the reading point is wrong, such a lever needs only cycle 2's node state, so it is available to a ten-cycle forecast, unlike 5zl's &kappa;<sub>w</sub>.
