- Method, fixed now (`experiments/plateau/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation; 5zk's 40 designs and 5zl's balance, &eta; and tables unchanged): x<sub>i</sub>(2) = U<sub>n</sub><sup>&minus;1</sup>(u<sub>i</sub>(2)), each negative-electrode node's end-of-charge OCP at cycle 2 inverted on the set's U<sub>n</sub> table (a running-minimum envelope, since the LG M50 tables have flat steps; linear interpolation). The law's uniform shift s(l) = x<sub>hi</sub>(n<sub>Li</sub> &minus; l; v<sub>hi</sub> &minus; &eta;) &minus; x<sub>hi</sub>(n<sub>Li</sub> &minus; L<sub>2</sub>; v<sub>hi</sub> &minus; &eta;) (5zd's window top). The node lever dU<sub>node</sub>(l) = &Sigma; w<sub>i</sub>(2) U<sub>n</sub>(x<sub>i</sub>(2) + s(l)) minus its value at l = 0, w the cycle-2 plating shares (5zk). D<sup>node</sup><sub>n</sub> = dU<sub>node</sub>(L<sub>n</sub>) &minus; dU<sub>node</sub>(L<sub>2</sub>); &rho;<sub>node,k</sub> = least-squares slope through the origin of 5zl's M<sub>w,n</sub> on D<sup>node</sup><sub>n</sub> over n = 3..k. Forecast N: 5zf's `forecast` (line through cycles 2..10, &kappa; = 25.3) with 5zm's tables but du replaced by dU<sub>node</sub>; A is 5zm's A<sub>10</sub> (5zd's lever, 25.3, line through 2..10). Error |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|, set medians over 8, 12, 12, 8 designs.
- Already seen: 5zl's &kappa;<sub>w</sub> and its errors, 5zm's A errors, pointwise ratios and early exponents on these designs; the U<sub>n</sub> tables' shape (checked for monotonicity only). Not seen: any x<sub>i</sub>(2), dU<sub>node</sub>, &rho;<sub>node</sub> or forecast N.
- H204 (the uniform shift read at the nodes reproduces the measured rise): set median |ln &rho;<sub>node,99</sub>| at most 0.15 on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if above 0.3 on 3 or more. (5zl's law lever gives |ln(&kappa;<sub>w</sub>/25.3)| of 0.40 and 0.30 on LG M50.)
- H205 (and it does so early, where 5zm's ratio did not): set median |ln &rho;<sub>node,10</sub>| at most 0.3 on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if above 0.6 on 3 or more.
- H206 (the honest ten-cycle forecast): N's set median error is below A's on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H207 (design by design): N beats A on at least 26 of 40 designs (one-sided sign test p &lt; 0.05). Refuted if on 20 or fewer.
- H208 (it closes most of the gap to 5zl's diagnosis): on both LG M50 sets N's set median error is at most 1.5 &times; 5zl's error at &kappa;<sub>w</sub> (0.025 `Chen2020`, 0.019 `OKane2022`). Refuted if above 3 &times; on both.
- Reported, not hypothesised: a one-point variant (x<sub>w</sub>(2) = U<sub>n</sub><sup>&minus;1</sup> of the plating-weighted cycle-2 OCP, one reading point instead of the node sum), &rho;<sub>node,k</sub> at k = 20, 30, 50, the plating-weighted x<sub>i</sub>(2) against the window top, and N with &kappa; = 25.3 &rho;<sub>node,10</sub>.
- Caveats fixed now: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk to 5zm; cycle-2 weights; end-of-charge node OCPs are a model internal, so a forecast that uses them asks what a ten-cycle DFN run can tell a design search, not what a lab test records; inverting U<sub>n</sub> on a plateau is ill-conditioned (a small OCP error moves x a lot, though the lever then reads the same flat region); the uniform shift assumes every node loses stoichiometry at the window top's rate; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zm outcome: the plating-weighted lever cannot be read early on LG M50. Cut at k cycles, its exponent is 100 (the clamp) on `Chen2020` at 10, 20 and 30 cycles and 49 to 75 on `OKane2022`, against 38 and 34 at 99, because the plating-weighted OCP rise outruns the law's lever early and the ratio falls all the way to cycle 99 (pointwise, 25.3 &times; ratio is 148, 81, 58, 30 at cycles 10, 30, 50, 99 on `Chen2020`). On `NCA_Kim2011` it is flat from cycle 10 (28.5 to 26.0), and on `Ai2020` it settles by 30 cycles (24.0 against 25.2). A forecast at the early exponent loses to the physical 25.3 given the same cycles on 4 of 4 sets at 10 cycles and 3 of 4 at 30. H200 and H202 refuted, H201 and H203 inconclusive
- Analysis: stdlib, 19 s (`rows`), report run once. Reproduces 5zl exactly (&kappa;<sub>w,99</sub> and the 25.3 error with a ten-cycle line equal 5zl's committed values on all 40 designs, tested). No design fell back to 25.3 (D carried signal on all 40 at every budget). After the verdicts the `rows` step was rerun once to add one per-design field for the post hoc (&kappa; from the pointwise cycle-99 ratio); the report's numbers did not change.
- H200 refuted (0 of 4): at k = 10 the set median error of B (the law at &kappa;<sub>w,10</sub>) against A (at 25.3) is 0.289 against 0.083 (`Ai2020`), 0.171 against 0.082 (`Chen2020`, where &kappa;<sub>w,10</sub> is clamped at 100), 0.066 against 0.059 (`OKane2022`) and 0.041 against 0.022 (`NCA_Kim2011`).
- H201 inconclusive (2 at most 0.15, 2 above 0.3): set median |ln(&kappa;<sub>w,30</sub>/&kappa;<sub>w,99</sub>)| is 0.067 (`Ai2020`), 0.681 (`Chen2020`), 0.728 (`OKane2022`), 0.037 (`NCA_Kim2011`).
- H202 refuted (1 of 4): at k = 30, B against A is 0.034 against 0.039 on `Ai2020` (the one win), 0.133 against 0.046, 0.152 against 0.028 and 0.019 against 0.013.
- H203 inconclusive: within-set Spearman(&kappa;<sub>w,30</sub>, &kappa;*) is 0.83 (`Ai2020`), 0.23 (`Chen2020`), 0.87 (`OKane2022`), &minus;0.24 (`NCA_Kim2011`, whose &kappa;* spans only 18.5 to 28.9, as 5zl).
- Not hypothesised, in the table: the physical-exponent forecast with its line fitted through more cycles improves steadily on every set (A at k = 10, 30, 50: `Chen2020` 0.082, 0.046, 0.020; `OKane2022` 0.059, 0.028, 0.017; `Ai2020` 0.083, 0.039, 0.032; `NCA_Kim2011` 0.022, 0.013, 0.011). Keeping the ten-cycle line and only the early exponent (C) is no better than B.
- Post hoc (`run.py posthoc`, `posthoc.txt`, after the verdicts): 25.3 &times; the pointwise ratio (plating-weighted rise) / (law's rise) from cycle 2 to k, set medians at k = 10, 20, 30, 50, 99: `Chen2020` 148, 111, 81, 58, 30; `OKane2022` 63, 79, 79, 51, 29; `Ai2020` 52, 27, 21, 23, 29; `NCA_Kim2011` 28.5, 27.4, 27.0, 26.5, 26.0. B beats A on 3, 4, 4, 3 of 12 `Chen2020` designs at the four budgets.
- Reading: on LG M50 the ratio 5zl found is not a constant of the design that early cycles reveal. In the first tens of cycles the OCP where the plating sits rises several times faster than the law's uniform-stoichiometry lever (whose window sits on the graphite plateau, 0.4 mV of rise by cycle 10 on `Chen2020` against 2.9 mV plating-weighted), and the two converge only as lost lithium pushes the law's window off the plateau. So 5zl's &kappa;<sub>w</sub> is a path average dominated by late cycles, and the forecast law's missing piece on LG M50 is the shape of its lever near the plateau, not one exponent. On `NCA_Kim2011` (no plateau at the top of charge) the exponent is the physical one to within 10% from cycle 10. For a design search the practical answer is the plain one: run the DFN 30 to 50 cycles and fit the law's line at 25.3; that halves to quarters the ten-cycle error on every set, and no early exponent beats it on LG M50. The next mechanism step is a lever that reads the plating-weighted stoichiometry on the OCP curve (a plateau-aware dU), tested against the pointwise ratio's decay above.
- Caveats: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs), all seen in 5zk and 5zl; cycle-2 weights; end-of-charge node OCPs are a model internal; the A-versus-B comparison gives both the same cycles, but A at k &gt; 10 is not the programme's ten-cycle forecast; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/horizon10/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `posthoc.txt`, `rows.json`, `horizon.svg`; tests `tests/test_horizon10.py`.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zm: how many cycles does the plating-weighted lever need before it sets the plating exponent? A horizon test on 5zk's x-resolved records, with every forecast given the same cycles as its exponent (plan, written and committed before any early-window ratio or forecast was computed; H200 to H203 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zl found that 25.3 &times; (plating-weighted end-of-charge OCP rise) / (the law's lever rise), fitted over cycles 3..99, lands within 3 to 12% of the oracle &kappa;* on three of four sets. That reads cycle 99, so it is a diagnosis. 5zi's H186 found the separator-node lever unreadable in ten cycles on LG M50 (0.0 mV of rise on `Chen2020` by cycle 10). The practical question is how early the plating-weighted lever settles: if it can be read by cycle 20 or 30, a design search can stop its DFN runs there and forecast cycle 99 with a per-design exponent instead of 5zh's 80 earlier 100-cycle runs per set.
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/horizon10/run.py`, `rows`, `report`; stdlib, no new simulation): per design on 5zk's 40 designs, 5zl's &eta;, &kappa;* and tables unchanged (`wlever` `_row` inputs). For a budget of k cycles, k &isin; {10, 20, 30, 50}: &kappa;<sub>w,k</sub> = 25.3 &times; the least-squares slope through the origin of M<sub>w,n</sub> on D<sub>n</sub> over n = 3..k (5zl's quantities, cut at k), clamped to [0, 100]; when D carries no signal (5zi's `ratio` returns None) the exponent falls back to 25.3 (count reported). Two forecasts of L(99) with the same budget: A<sub>k</sub> = the law at 25.3 with its line fitted through cycles 2..k; B<sub>k</sub> = the law at &kappa;<sub>w,k</sub> with its line fitted through cycles 2..k (5zf's `forecast` with `k`). Error |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|, set medians over 8, 12, 12, 8 designs. &kappa;<sub>w,99</sub> is 5zl's &kappa;<sub>w</sub>.
- Already seen: 5zl's &kappa;<sub>w</sub>, &kappa;* and errors at 25.3 and &kappa;<sub>w</sub> (line through 2..10) on these designs; 5zi's ten-cycle separator-node result on other pools. Not seen: any M<sub>w</sub> or D cut before cycle 99, any forecast with a line through more than ten cycles.
- H200 (ten cycles are enough, the honest forecast): at k = 10 the set median error of B is below A's on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1. (Expected to be hard on LG M50, after 5zi's H186.)
- H201 (the lever settles by 30 cycles): the set median |ln(&kappa;<sub>w,30</sub>/&kappa;<sub>w,99</sub>)| is at most 0.15 on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if above 0.3 on 3 or more.
- H202 (an early read pays): at k = 30 the set median error of B is below A's on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- H203 (it still ranks designs): within-set Spearman(&kappa;<sub>w,30</sub>, &kappa;*) at least 0.4 on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if under 0.15 on 3 or more.
- Reported, not hypothesised: the same at k = 20 and 50, the fallback counts, and B with the line through cycles 2..10 only (exponent from k cycles, the law's ten-cycle fit unchanged).
- Caveats fixed now: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs, 5z's picks), all seen in 5zk/5zl; cycle-2 weights; end-of-charge node OCPs are a model internal, not a cell measurement, so this asks how long a DFN run must be, not what a lab test must record; &kappa;* reads cycle 99 by construction; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zl outcome: read on the same designs, the plating-weighted OCP lever does set the plating exponent. On 5z's 40 designs, 25.3 times (plating-weighted end-of-charge OCP rise) / (the law's lever rise) lands within 3 to 12% of the oracle &kappa;* on LG M50 and `NCA_Kim2011` (set medians 37.7 against 36.6, 34.2 against 32.3, 26.4 against 23.6) and 34% high on `Ai2020` (25.2 against 18.8, where the separator node gives 43.6). It is closer to &kappa;* than the separator-node reading on 37 of 40 designs (sign test p = 10<sup>&minus;8</sup>) and ranks designs within a set (Spearman 0.83 to 0.95 on three sets). 5zk's refutation came from comparing pools at other currents. H198 and H199 supported, H195 to H197 inconclusive
- Analysis: stdlib, 14 s (`rows`), report run once, nothing in `experiments/wlever/run.py` changed after the plan apart from adding the post hoc command after the verdicts. All 40 designs gave &kappa;* and both levers; 3 LG M50 designs have &kappa;<sub>sep</sub> = 0 (the separator node's OCP does not rise past cycle 2), and none has &kappa;<sub>w</sub> = 0.
- H195 inconclusive: set median &kappa;<sub>sep</sub> here against 5zi's &kappa;<sub>path</sub> is 43.6 against 73.5 on `Ai2020` (41% off), 40.5 against 33.0, 37.5 against 36.6 and 17.3 against 22.6. 5z's `Ai2020` pool runs at 0.45 C-multiple; 5zi's six `Ai2020` pools at 0.525 to 0.65, whose separator-node rise is 102 to 136 mV against 72 mV here, while the law's lever rises about the same (41 to 43 mV, 41 here).
- H196 inconclusive: set median &kappa;* is 18.8 (5zi 20.8), 36.6 (52.3, 30% off), 32.3 (38.1) and 23.6 (23.0). `Chen2020`'s &kappa;* spreads widely within the pool (9.7 to 75.6).
- So 5zk's H193 compared a depth ratio from one pool with &kappa;<sub>path</sub> and &kappa;* from pools at other currents, and both sides of that comparison moved. Its "refuted" stands as stated but its reading (the set differences sit in the law's lever, not the measured one) does not survive the same-design test.
- H197 inconclusive (4 of 4 sets closer, `Ai2020` misses the 25% line): set median |ln(&kappa;<sub>w</sub>/&kappa;*)| 0.307, 0.148, 0.119, 0.056 against the separator node's 0.839, 0.531, 0.439, 0.252. `Ai2020`'s set median &kappa;<sub>w</sub> is 34% above its &kappa;*; two of its eight designs (j 15 and 31, &kappa;<sub>w</sub> 36 and 68 against 24 and 29) carry most of that.
- H198 supported: within-set Spearman(&kappa;<sub>w</sub>, &kappa;*) is 0.95 (`Ai2020`), 0.83 (`Chen2020`), 0.94 (`OKane2022`) and &minus;0.17 (`NCA_Kim2011`, where &kappa;* spans only 18.5 to 28.9). The separator node gives 0.93, 0.38, 0.40, 0.90.
- H199 supported (3 of 4; a diagnosis, the lever reads cycle 99): set median forecast error |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))| at &kappa;<sub>w</sub> is 0.025 on `Chen2020` (0.082 at 25.3), 0.019 on `OKane2022` (0.059) and 0.012 on `NCA_Kim2011` (0.022); on `Ai2020` 0.085 against 0.083. At &kappa;<sub>sep</sub> it is worse than 25.3 on every set (0.261, 0.101, 0.084, 0.048).
- Post hoc (`run.py posthoc`, `posthoc.txt`, after the verdicts): &kappa;<sub>w</sub> is closer to &kappa;* than &kappa;<sub>sep</sub> on 8/8, 11/12, 11/12 and 7/8 designs (37 of 40, one-sided sign test p = 10<sup>&minus;8</sup>); the forecast at &kappa;<sub>w</sub> beats 25.3 on 28 of 40 (p = 0.008; 11 of 12 on `Chen2020`, 4 of 8 on `Ai2020` and `NCA_Kim2011`) and beats &kappa;<sub>sep</sub> on 36 of 40.
- Reading: the plating exponent the law needs is, to within 3 to 12% on three of four cells, the physical &alpha;F/RT scaled by how much faster the OCP rises where the plating is than the law's uniform-stoichiometry lever. On LG M50 the law's lever under-reads that rise by a factor of 1.35 to 1.49 (the forecast needs 1.28 to 1.44); on `NCA_Kim2011` it reads it right. So &kappa;* is a measured quantity, as 5zi hoped, but measured through the thickness, not at the separator node. It still needs cycle 99's OCPs: the next question is whether the plating-weighted rise can be forecast from ten cycles or from the open-circuit balance, and why `Ai2020` (the one cell modelled with 34 parallel electrode pairs) stays a third high.
- Caveats: one pool per set (8, 12, 12, 8 designs) at one current per set; seen data (cycle 99 is in both the lever and &kappa;*); cycle-2 weights; end-of-charge OCPs; the charge Ah from the constant-current charge time; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/wlever/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `posthoc.txt`, `rows.json`, `wlever.svg`; tests `tests/test_wlever.py`.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zl: on the same designs, is the plating exponent the forecast needs 25.3 times the ratio of the plating-weighted OCP rise to the law's lever rise? A per-design test on 5zk's x-resolved records, which carry everything the law needs (plan, written and committed before any ratio or &kappa;* on these designs was computed; H195 to H199 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zk read the lever where the plating is and found it does not set &kappa;*, but the comparison was across pools: the measured depth ratio came from 5z's 40 designs, &kappa;<sub>path</sub> and &kappa;* from 5zi's 768 others. Its reading put the remaining set differences on the law's side of the ratio and named the next step: compare the law's lever dU<sub>&eta;</sub>(L<sub>n</sub>) with the plating-weighted measured OCP rise, per cycle, on 5z's designs. 5zk's records hold the lost lithium per cycle and the constant-current charge time (so the charge Ah is I t<sub>ch</sub>/3600, the experiment has no voltage hold), which is all 5zd's &eta; and 5zf's oracle need. So &kappa;*, the law's lever and both measured levers can be computed on the same 40 designs, with no new simulation.
- Method, fixed now (`experiments/wlever/run.py`, `rows`, `report`): per design, 5zd's &eta; from cycles 2..10 and 5zc's `du_eta` unchanged (balance from the pool 5z drew the design from: `crossset`, `calmap` for `Ai2020`); D<sub>n</sub> = dU<sub>&eta;</sub>(L<sub>n</sub>) &minus; dU<sub>&eta;</sub>(L<sub>2</sub>); M<sub>sep,n</sub> = u<sub>sep</sub>(n) &minus; u<sub>sep</sub>(2) (5zi's lever); M<sub>w,n</sub> = &Sigma; w<sub>i</sub>(2)(u<sub>i</sub>(n) &minus; u<sub>i</sub>(2)) (5zk's M<sub>w</sub>, now at every cycle); &rho; = least-squares slope through the origin over n = 3..99 and &kappa; = 25.3&rho; clamped to [0, 100], as 5zi; &kappa;* = 5zf's oracle; forecast errors |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))| at 25.3, &kappa;<sub>sep</sub>, &kappa;<sub>w</sub> and &kappa;*. Set medians over 8 (`Ai2020`), 12, 12 and 8 designs.
- Already seen: 5zk's set medians of M<sub>sep</sub> and M<sub>w</sub> at cycle 99 and their ratio (0.64, 0.81, 0.78, 1.21); 5zi's set medians of &kappa;<sub>path</sub> and &kappa;* on other pools. Not seen: &eta;, D<sub>n</sub>, &kappa;* or any &kappa; on these designs.
- H195 (5zi's separator lever transfers to 5z's pool): the set median &kappa;<sub>sep</sub> is within 25% of 5zi's set median &kappa;<sub>path</sub> (73.5, 33.0, 36.6, 22.6) on all four sets. Refuted if more than 50% off on any.
- H196 (so does &kappa;*): the set median &kappa;* is within 25% of 5zi's (20.8, 52.3, 38.1, 23.0) on all four sets. Refuted if more than 50% off on any. (If H195 or H196 fails, 5zk's cross-pool H193 rested on a mismatch.)
- H197 (the plating-weighted lever explains &kappa;* better, design by design): the set median of |ln(&kappa;<sub>w</sub>/&kappa;*)| is below that of |ln(&kappa;<sub>sep</sub>/&kappa;*)| on at least 3 of 4 sets, and `Ai2020`'s set median &kappa;<sub>w</sub> is within 25% of its set median &kappa;*. Refuted if below on at most 1 set.
- H198 (it ranks designs): the within-set Spearman(&kappa;<sub>w</sub>, &kappa;*) is at least 0.4 on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if under 0.15 on 3 or more.
- H199 (as a correction it helps the forecast, a diagnosis since it reads cycle 99): the set median forecast error at &kappa;<sub>w</sub> is below the one at 25.3 on at least 3 of 4 sets. Refuted if on at most 1.
- Caveats fixed now: one pool per set (7 to 12 designs, 5z's picks); the slope through the origin weights late cycles; cycle-2 weights for M<sub>w</sub>; end-of-charge OCPs, not the time profile; the charge Ah from the charge time at constant current (checked in a test, not against a recorded Ah, which these runs did not keep); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zk outcome: the plating is spread through the negative electrode (the separator node carries 8 to 19% of it at cycle 2, the plating-weighted depth is 0.53 to 0.72 of the thickness), and that spread explains 5z's `NCA_Kim2011` index miss (the separator node's slowdown misses the electrode's by 0.28 in log there, 0.015 on LG M50). It does not explain the plating exponent: read where the plating is, the lever shrinks to 0.64 of the separator node's on `Ai2020` and 0.78 to 0.81 on LG M50, so the implied exponent moves from 73 to 47 on `Ai2020` (&kappa;* 21) and further from &kappa;* on `Chen2020` (27 against 52) and `OKane2022` (28 against 38). H191, H192 and H194 supported; H193 refuted
- Bench: 40 DFN runs to cycle 100, about 6 minutes on 3 workers; `Ai2020` design 7 was killed for memory (exit &minus;9) with 3 workers and finished on a single-worker retry, so `Ai2020` has 8 designs (5z had 7). Report run once on the 40; one change after it, before any reading: the committed `bench.jsonl` was rounded to 7 significant digits to halve its size (8.3 to 4.7 MB); re-running the report on it moved no printed number past the fourth digit (worst booked-plating reproduction 0 to 2.6 &times; 10<sup>&minus;7</sup>).
- H194 supported: on all 39 designs 5z also ran, the cycle-99 booked plating reproduces to 2.6 &times; 10<sup>&minus;7</sup> relative, the separator-node end-of-charge OCP to under 0.0001 mV, and the log change of plating from the integrated node currents matches 5z's booked per-cycle log change within 0.0014. The 20-node mesh is uniform (widths equal to 2 &times; 10<sup>&minus;15</sup>).
- H191 supported: set-median separator-node share of cycle 2's plating `Ai2020` 0.124, `Chen2020` 0.194, `OKane2022` 0.194, `NCA_Kim2011` 0.076. The median node share rises steadily toward the separator on `Ai2020` and LG M50 (collector node 0.016 to 0.032), and is nearly flat on `NCA_Kim2011` (0.047 at the collector, 0.045 mid-electrode, 0.076 at the separator) (`depth.svg`); on every set most of the plating is inside the electrode: the plating-weighted depth is 0.62 (`Ai2020`), 0.72 (both LG M50) and 0.53 (`NCA_Kim2011`) of the thickness from the collector.
- H192 supported: set median |&Delta;<sub>sep</sub> &minus; &Delta;<sub>tot</sub>| is 0.282 on `NCA_Kim2011` (total &minus;4.42, separator node &minus;3.95), 0.015 and 0.014 on LG M50, 0.053 on `Ai2020`. On `NCA_Kim2011` the plating moves toward the separator over the 100 cycles (separator share 0.076 to 0.141, depth 0.53 to 0.63) because the inner nodes slow faster, so more than half of 5z's 0.49 index miss there is location; the rest is the index's end-of-charge form (medians, so the split is approximate).
- H193 refuted: depth ratios (median M<sub>w</sub> / median M<sub>sep</sub>) are 0.644 (`Ai2020`; refutation line 0.425), 0.813 (`Chen2020`), 0.777 (`OKane2022`) and 1.207 (`NCA_Kim2011`), giving &kappa;<sub>w</sub> 47.3, 26.8, 28.4 and 27.3 against &kappa;* 20.8, 52.3, 38.1 and 23.0: within 25% on `NCA_Kim2011` only. Cycle-99 weights give the same picture (0.637, 0.760, 0.730, 1.183).
- Reading: what each set would need from a depth correction is a factor of 0.28 on `Ai2020`, 1.58 on `Chen2020`, 1.04 on `OKane2022` and 1.02 on `NCA_Kim2011`. Reading the lever where the plating is gives 0.64, 0.81, 0.78 and 1.21: the right direction on `Ai2020` only, too weak there, and wrong on `Chen2020`. Together with 5zj (the separator node's other terms give a similar 0.59 on `Ai2020` and 0.80 on LG M50), two independent corrections of the lever's location and terms move the sets the same way, so the set differences in &kappa;* are not in where or how the plating potential is read. They are in the other side of the ratio: the law's own lever dU<sub>&eta;</sub>(L), its uniform-stoichiometry window, and how it maps lost lithium to a potential on each set's OCP curve. That is the next step: compare the law's dU<sub>&eta;</sub>(L<sub>n</sub>) against the plating-weighted measured OCP rise per cycle on 5z's designs (both are now recorded), which needs no new simulation.
- Caveats: one pool per set (7 to 12 designs, 5z's picks); &kappa;<sub>path</sub> and &kappa;* are set medians from other pools (5zb to 5zg), so H193 is a set-level comparison, as 5zj's; the weighted rise uses end-of-charge OCPs, not the time profile; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); 20 nodes in the negative electrode (5zj's doubled mesh moved the particle loss by 0.1%, not checked here for the profile); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/depth/bench.jsonl` (per node and cycle: plating over the cycle and over the charge, end-of-charge OCP, surface potential difference and plating overpotential), `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `depth.svg`; tests `tests/test_depth.py`.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zk: where through the negative electrode does the plating happen, and does reading the lever there give the exponent the forecast needs? x-resolved plating currents on 5z's re-run designs over 100 cycles (plan, written and committed before the bench; H191 to H194 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zi found that the separator-surface OCP lever explains the plating exponent &kappa;* on `OKane2022` and `NCA_Kim2011` but overshoots on `Ai2020` (lever-implied 73 against &kappa;* 21) and undershoots on `Chen2020` (33 against 52); 5zj found that the separator node's other terms halve the `Ai2020` overshoot and push the other sets further off. Every lever so far is read at one node, the separator side of the negative electrode. If the plating is spread through the thickness, the potential that slows it is the plating-weighted one, not the separator node's, and the set differences may be where the plating sits.
- Bench, fixed now (`experiments/depth/run.py`): 5z's 40 designs (`random.Random(18)` picks from `crossset` `Chen2020@1.5x` 12, `OKane2022@1.5x` 12, `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x` 8 and `calmap` `Ai2020@0.45x` 8; 5z lost `Ai2020` design 7 to a solver failure), DFN with SEI (solvent-diffusion limited) + irreversible plating to cycle 100, PyBaMM defaults (20 nodes in the negative electrode), one fresh process per run. Recorded per cycle and per negative-electrode node: the plating current integrated over the whole cycle, P<sub>i</sub>(n) = &minus;&int; j<sub>pl,i</sub> dt; and at end of charge the OCP u<sub>i</sub>, the surface potential difference and the plating reaction overpotential. Also the booked plating and lithium-loss totals and the node positions.
- Quantities (cycles 2 and 99, 1-based, as 5z): w<sub>i</sub>(n) = P<sub>i</sub>(n) / &Sigma;P (the mesh is uniform in x; node widths are checked from the recorded positions); the separator-node share s(n) = w<sub>last</sub>(n); &Delta;<sub>tot</sub> = ln(&Sigma;P(99) / &Sigma;P(2)); &Delta;<sub>sep</sub> = ln(P<sub>last</sub>(99) / P<sub>last</sub>(2)); M<sub>sep</sub> = u<sub>last</sub>(99) &minus; u<sub>last</sub>(2); M<sub>w</sub> = &Sigma; w<sub>i</sub>(2) (u<sub>i</sub>(99) &minus; u<sub>i</sub>(2)), the OCP rise where the plating was at cycle 2. The set's depth ratio is median(M<sub>w</sub>) / median(M<sub>sep</sub>) over its designs (a ratio of medians, because M<sub>sep</sub> is near zero on some LG M50 designs, 5zj), and the depth-read exponent is &kappa;<sub>w</sub> = 5zi's set-median &kappa;<sub>path</sub> &times; that ratio (`Ai2020` 73.5, `Chen2020` 33.0, `OKane2022` 36.6, `NCA_Kim2011` 22.6), against 5zi's set-median &kappa;* (20.8, 52.3, 38.1, 23.0).
- Already seen: 5z's separator-node records on these designs (its rate index misses the per-cycle plating change by a median 0.49 in log on `NCA_Kim2011`, 0.069 on `Ai2020`, 0.015 on LG M50), 5zi's &kappa;<sub>path</sub> and &kappa;*, 5zj's terms. Not seen: anything resolved in x.
- H191 (the plating is spread): at cycle 2 the separator node carries under half of the electrode's plating (set median of s(2)) on all four sets. Refuted if over 0.8 on any.
- H192 (location explains 5z's `NCA_Kim2011` miss): the set median of |&Delta;<sub>sep</sub> &minus; &Delta;<sub>tot</sub>| is at least 0.2 on `NCA_Kim2011` and at most 0.1 on both LG M50 sets. Refuted if it is under 0.1 on `NCA_Kim2011` (then the miss is the index's end-of-charge form, not where the plating is).
- H193 (the lever read where the plating is): &kappa;<sub>w</sub> within 25% of &kappa;* on at least 3 of the 4 sets, `Ai2020` among them. Refuted if `Ai2020`'s &kappa;<sub>w</sub> stays above 1.5 &kappa;* (31.3), i.e. a depth ratio above 0.425 there.
- H194 (the re-runs are 5z's runs): on every design the cycle-99 booked plating and the separator-node end-of-charge OCP reproduce 5z's records within 0.1% and 0.1 mV, and &Delta;<sub>tot</sub> from the integrated node currents matches the log change of 5z's booked per-cycle plating within 0.02. Refuted if any design misses by 10 times those.
- Caveats fixed now: one pool per set, 7 to 12 designs; the 5zi &kappa;<sub>path</sub> and &kappa;* come from other pools (5zb to 5zg) than these designs (5z's), so H193 compares set-level quantities, as 5zj's terms did; cycle-2 weights for M<sub>w</sub> (the plating moves over 100 cycles; the cycle-99 weighting is reported, not hypothesised); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.


## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zj outcome: `Ai2020`'s "unbooked" lithium loss is plating, as 5p and 5za had already shown. This plan missed that and re-asked a closed question; the ledger replicates it by a second route (the side currents, integrated directly, pass the lithium that leaves the particles to 0.01%), and a doubled mesh moves it by 0.1%. The question 5zi actually left open, which separator-node terms give back `Ai2020`'s steep surface-OCP lever, has a partial answer from 5z's committed records: the intercalation overpotential and the shape of the charge profile give back about 41% of it (about 20% on LG M50), which halves the `Ai2020` overshoot (lever-implied exponent 73 to 43 against &kappa;* 21) and moves LG M50 further off. The separator node does not set &kappa;*. H187, H189 and H190 supported; H188 refuted
- Planning error, stated first: the plan said the gap's source was unseen and needed naming. It had been named in 5p (`experiments/attribution/UPSTREAM.md`: PyBaMM 26.10.1.1 books SEI and plating per electrode pair, `L_y L_z`, but totals lithium over `A_cc = L_y L_z n_parallel`, and `Ai2020` has 34 electrodes in parallel), and 5za's outcome confirmed it on 128 runs and corrected 5z. The plan was written from the newest entries and the README's next step without searching the log for "parallel". So H187 and H188 re-test a known result. They are logged as replications, not findings.
- Bench: 32 ten-cycle DFN runs, 3 workers, about 2 minutes; then 2 refined runs, which the plan fixed at x 40/40/40, r 60/60 with rtol = atol = 10<sup>&minus;8</sup> over ten cycles. Those were killed for memory (exit &minus;9) twice with 3 workers and once with 1. A doubled mesh (x and r all 40) at 10 cycles and 10<sup>&minus;8</sup> was killed too. Deviation, made before any refined number was read: the refined runs use the doubled mesh, default tolerances and 3 cycles, compared against the base runs at cycle 3. The report was run once on the 30 base runs and once more with the two refined runs added; nothing else in `run.py report` changed.
- H187 supported (a replication): on all eight `Ai2020@0.45x` designs the charge the negative side reactions pass, &int; &minus;a j L<sub>n</sub> A<sub>cc</sub> dt / F from PyBaMM's own volumetric currents, equals the lithium lost from the particles to 0.01% (median 18.26 mmol by cycle 10). The booked side-reaction loss is 1/34.000 of it (0.537 mmol) on every design. Lost from the electrolyte: 0.000.
- H188 refuted (a replication of 5p): SEI alone loses 0.048 mmol in ten cycles against 18.26 with both reactions. Plating alone loses 18.29. On `Ai2020` the loss is plating.
- H189 supported: the doubled mesh changes the cycle-3 particle loss by 0.11% and 0.07% (designs 7 and 12). The base run of design 7 reproduces 5za's recorded 0.0053285 mol to the last digit.
- H190 supported: on the six control designs (`Chen2020`, `OKane2022`, `NCA_Kim2011`, one electrode pair each) booked and lost agree to under 0.01%.
- Post hoc, the question 5zi left (`run.py terms`, stdlib, on 5z's committed per-design records; their pool medians were seen before this step): 5z split the cycle-2-to-99 change of the separator-node plating exponent into terms. As shares of the surface-OCP term, the intercalation overpotential gives back 0.21 on `Ai2020` (0.20 and 0.24 on LG M50, &minus;0.01 on `NCA_Kim2011`). The charge-profile shape (the time-integrated rate index minus the end-of-charge terms and the charge time) gives back 0.23 on `Ai2020` (range 0.18 to 0.36, all seven designs) against 0.02 and 0.04 on LG M50, and it adds 0.22 on `NCA_Kim2011`. So what is left of the OCP lever is 0.59 on `Ai2020`, 0.82 and 0.80 on LG M50 and 1.19 on `NCA_Kim2011`. Rescaling 5zi's set-median lever-implied exponent by that ratio gives `Ai2020` 43 (from 73; &kappa;* 21), `Chen2020` 27 (from 33; &kappa;* 52), `OKane2022` 29 (from 37; &kappa;* 38) and `NCA_Kim2011` 27 (from 23; &kappa;* 23). Every set but `Ai2020` moves away from its &kappa;*.
- Reading: `Ai2020` is a plating cell like the others, and everything fitted on it from 5u on fits plating on a consistent scale (`balance.jsonl`'s n<sub>Li</sub> = 0.2520 mol equals PyBaMM's A<sub>cc</sub>-scaled particle total for design 7). Its odd exponent has a partial physical reason: on `Ai2020` the plating spreads over more of the charge, so the end-of-charge node over-reads its slowdown, and the intercalation overpotential grows. Neither is enough, and on the other sets the same correction hurts. So &kappa;* is not a property of the separator node. The next mechanism step is a through-thickness one: integrate PyBaMM's plating current over x and the charge (as this milestone's ledger already does for the total) and split its cycle-2-to-99 change by node, on 5z's re-run designs. If the spread in x carries the set differences, the lever should be read where the plating is, not at the separator.
- Caveats: ten cycles for the ledger (the bookkeeping factor is structural and exact, so the horizon does not matter for H187); 3 cycles and default tolerances for the mesh check; the post hoc terms come from 5z's four pools (other pools than 5zi's 24) and compare cycles 2 and 99, not 5zi's slope over 3 to 99; shares are unstable on LG M50 designs whose OCP term is near zero (medians are shown, ranges in `terms.txt`); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1).
- Output: `experiments/ledger/bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `terms.txt`, `terms.json`; tests `tests/test_ledger.py`.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zj: where does `Ai2020`'s unbooked lithium loss go? A lithium ledger of PyBaMM's DFN over ten cycles on 5z's designs, set against the charge the side reactions actually pass (plan, written and committed before the bench; H187 to H190 are this entry's)
- Question: 5z found that on `Ai2020@0.45x` only 2.9% of PyBaMM's "Total lithium lost [mol]" at cycle 99 is booked to plating and 0.0% to SEI, while on the other three sets the booked side reactions are 98.7 to 100% of it; a 3-cycle check on one design saw 0.0053 mol leave the particles against 0.00016 mol booked, and nothing lost with SEI and plating both off. 5zi then found `Ai2020` the one set the separator-surface lever cannot explain. Every `Ai2020` result from 5u on fits this unbooked quantity, so its physics has to be named before `Ai2020` counts as a second cell.
- Bench, fixed now (`experiments/ledger/run.py`): DFN to cycle 10, one fresh process per run, at 5z's designs: all eight `Ai2020@0.45x` picks (`random.Random(18)`, as 5z) in three variants (*both* = SEI solvent-diffusion limited + irreversible plating, as every 100-cycle milestone; *sei* only; *plating* only); the first two of them again with *both* on a doubled-or-more mesh (x 40/40/40, r 60/60 against the default 20s) and IDAKLU rtol = atol = 10<sup>&minus;8</sup>; and the first two of 5z's picks on each control pool (`Chen2020@1.5x`, `OKane2022@1.5x`, `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x`), *both*. Recorded per cycle: every term of PyBaMM's lithium ledger (lithium in each electrode and the electrolyte; lost in total, from particles, from electrolyte, to side reactions; booked to SEI, SEI on cracks, plating, loss of active material in each electrode), and the charge the negative electrode's SEI, SEI-on-cracks and plating currents pass, &int; &minus;a j L<sub>n</sub> A dt / F from their x-averaged volumetric interfacial current densities (A the parallel electrode area).
- Already seen: 5z's 100-cycle shares and its one 3-cycle check (above). [Added at the outcome: this was wrong. 5p and 5za had already explained the gap; see the outcome entry.] Not seen: any ledger term other than the totals, the passed charge, any single-reaction variant or any refined run.
- H187 (the booking variables under-count): on every `Ai2020` design (*both*) the passed side-reaction charge at cycle 10 is within 10% of the lithium lost from the particles. Refuted if it is within 10% of the booked side-reaction loss on at least half of them (then the side reactions really are small and the particles lose lithium another way).
- H188 (SEI, not plating): in the *sei* variant the cycle-10 particle loss is at least 80% of the *both* variant's on every `Ai2020` design. Refuted if under 50% on any.
- H189 (not numerical): the refined runs change the cycle-10 particle loss by under 5% on both designs. Refuted if by over 20% on either.
- H190 (`Ai2020` only): on the six control designs the booked side-reaction loss is within 5% of the particle loss at cycle 10. Refuted if outside 20% on any.
- Caveats fixed now: ten cycles, not 100 (the 3-cycle check says the gap is there from the start); eight `Ai2020` designs from one pool; x-averaged currents (exact for the integral over x); OKane2022's SEI and plating parameters only where `Ai2020` lacks its own (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1). This milestone places the gap; if it is a bookkeeping or parameter inconsistency, naming the parameter is the step after.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zi outcome: the lever ratio explains the plating exponent the forecast needs on two of the four sets. On `OKane2022` and `NCA_Kim2011`, 25.3 times (measured separator-surface OCP rise) / (the law's lever rise) lands within 25% of the oracle &kappa;* on all 12 pools (ratio 0.80 to 1.13). On `Chen2020` it explains half to nine-tenths of &kappa;* (0.50 to 0.89), and on `Ai2020` it is three times too high (2.75 to 3.77), so across the 24 pools it does not order &kappa;* (Spearman &minus;0.10). It cannot be read in ten cycles on LG M50, whose surface OCP has not moved by cycle 10 (0.0 mV on `Chen2020`). H185 supported, H184 and H186 refuted, H182 and H183 inconclusive
- Analysis: 768 committed designs (24 pools), about 74 s for `rows` on 4 cores, report run once; nothing in `experiments/lever/run.py` changed after the plan. Every design gave a &rho;<sub>path</sub> and a &rho;<sub>10</sub>.
- H182 inconclusive (7 of 12; supported at 10, refuted below 6): all six `OKane2022` pools match (&kappa;<sub>path</sub>/&kappa;* 0.82 to 1.08), but only one `Chen2020` pool does (0.89; the others 0.50 to 0.70). Pool medians: measured cycle-2-to-99 surface rise 30.8 to 39.4 mV on LG M50 against the law's 21.1 to 29.5 mV.
- H183 inconclusive (6 of 12; supported at 8, refuted below 4): all six `NCA_Kim2011` pools match (0.80 to 1.13; measured rise 107 to 146 mV against the law's 134 to 151 mV, so the lever is about right and so is 25.3). None of the six `Ai2020` pools does: its surface OCP rises 102 to 136 mV while the law's lever rises 41 to 43 mV, which implies &kappa; = 55 to 82 where the forecast needs 20 to 25.
- H184 refuted: Spearman of pool &kappa;<sub>path</sub> against pool &kappa;* over the 24 pools is &minus;0.095, because `Ai2020` has the highest &kappa;<sub>path</sub> and the lowest &kappa;*.
- H185 supported (just): the median within-pool Spearman(&kappa;<sub>path</sub>, &kappa;*) over the 12 LG M50 pools is 0.423 (`OKane2022` 0.33 to 0.72, `Chen2020` 0.03 to 0.46). Not hypothesised but in the table: within-pool ranks are strongest where the level is wrong, `Ai2020` 0.80 to 0.89, and 0.60 to 0.87 on `NCA_Kim2011`.
- H186 refuted (0 of 12): over cycles 2 to 10 the LG M50 surface OCP rises 0.0 mV on every `Chen2020` pool and 0.5 to 2.7 mV on `OKane2022`, so &kappa;<sub>10</sub> is near 0 on `Chen2020` (pool medians 0.0 to 0.2) and 3 to 27 on `OKane2022`, and the forecast at &kappa;<sub>10</sub> errs by 0.136 to 0.270 against 25.3's 0.059 to 0.109.
- Post hoc (after the verdicts, from the same table): the forecast at &kappa;<sub>path</sub> beats 25.3 on 3 `Chen2020`, 2 `OKane2022` and 4 `NCA_Kim2011` pools and on no `Ai2020` pool (0.235 to 0.319 against 0.056 to 0.081), and never beats 5zg's set calibration from 160 earlier runs. So even with cycle 99's own surface potential the lever ratio is a weaker forecast than one calibrated number per set.
- Reading: where the law's lever under-reads the loaded separator-surface potential (`OKane2022`) or reads it about right (`NCA_Kim2011`), the exponent it needs is the physical one rescaled by that ratio, which is the mechanism 5zf proposed. `Chen2020` is part of the way there: the ratio accounts for 50 to 89% of the steeper lever. `Ai2020` breaks it completely: its surface OCP rises three times as fast as the law's lever, yet the forecast needs about the physical exponent, so on that set the OCP at the separator surface is not the potential that slows plating. The plating overpotential is &phi;<sub>s</sub> &minus; &phi;<sub>e</sub>, not U, and 5za already saw that `Ai2020` "predicts the surface rise exactly" while doing harm. The next mechanism step therefore needs pybamm: decompose the plating overpotential at the separator node (the surface OCP, the intercalation overpotential and the electrolyte potential) on re-run `Ai2020` and `Chen2020` designs, as 5z did on LG M50 at two cycles. A ten-cycle forecast cannot use this lever on LG M50, because the graphite surface sits on its plateau through cycle 10.
- Caveats: seen data throughout; one node (the separator side); the slope through the origin weights late cycles; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/lever/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `lever.svg`; `rows.json` (regenerated by `rows`) not committed; tests `tests/test_lever.py`.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zi: is the plating exponent the ten-cycle forecast needs the physical &alpha;F/RT scaled by how much faster the separator-surface potential rises than the law's lever? A test of the mechanism 5zf to 5zh left open, on the 768 committed designs (plan, written and committed before any ratio was computed; H182 to H186 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zf to 5zh found that 5zd's law needs a per-set exponent (oracle &kappa;* 30 to 57 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> on LG M50, about 20 to 23 on the controls, against &alpha;F/RT = 25.3), and 5zh that calibrating it costs about 80 earlier long runs before it never loses to physics. The law reads the potential lever dU<sub>&eta;</sub>(L), the rise of the negative OCP at the top of the &eta;-shortened window. If the true lever that slows plating, the loaded separator-surface OCP u(n) the DFN records at end of charge (5z), rises &rho; times as fast as dU<sub>&eta;</sub>, the law's effective exponent is 25.3&rho;. If that holds, &kappa;* is a measured quantity, not a calibration.
- Data: no new simulation. All 24 committed pools of 5zb to 5zg (32 designs each); every record carries u(n) for cycles 1 to 100 (checked: 768 of 768). Per design, with 5zd's &eta; from cycles 2..10 and 5zc's `du_eta` unchanged: D<sub>n</sub> = dU<sub>&eta;</sub>(L<sub>n</sub>) &minus; dU<sub>&eta;</sub>(L<sub>2</sub>), M<sub>n</sub> = u(n) &minus; u(2); &rho;<sub>path</sub> = the least-squares slope through the origin of M<sub>n</sub> on D<sub>n</sub> over n = 3..99 (a diagnosis: it uses cycle 99); &rho;<sub>10</sub> = the same over n = 3..10 (a forecast); &kappa;<sub>path</sub> = 25.3&rho;<sub>path</sub>, &kappa;<sub>10</sub> = 25.3&rho;<sub>10</sub>, clamped to [0, 100]. &kappa;* is 5zf's oracle (`experiments/kappa/run.py`), unchanged. Script `experiments/lever/run.py` (`rows`, `report`).
- Already seen before this plan: &kappa;* per pool (5zf to 5zh); 5zd's H161 pool medians of the measured cycle-99 surface rise (39 and 36 mV on its LG M50 pools, 118 mV on `Ai2020`) and of the &eta; window's absolute error in forecasting it (21 and 17 mV; 75 mV on `Ai2020`), which hint that the law's lever is about half the measured one on LG M50 but do not give its sign or size on the controls; 5zf's law lever rise in ten cycles (under 1 mV on LG M50, 40 to 50 mV on `NCA_Kim2011`). Not seen: any &rho;, per design or per pool.
- H182 (the mechanism on LG M50): the pool median &kappa;<sub>path</sub> within 25% of the pool median &kappa;* on at least 10 of the 12 LG M50 pools. Refuted if on fewer than 6.
- H183 (the mechanism on the controls): the same on at least 8 of the 12 `Ai2020` and `NCA_Kim2011` pools. Refuted if on fewer than 4.
- H184 (it orders the sets): Spearman of pool median &kappa;<sub>path</sub> against pool median &kappa;* over the 24 pools at least 0.6. Refuted below 0.3.
- H185 (it explains the within-set spread that 5zh found costly): the median over the 12 LG M50 pools of the within-pool Spearman(&kappa;<sub>path</sub>, &kappa;*) at least 0.4. Refuted below 0.15.
- H186 (readable in ten cycles): the forecast at &kappa;<sub>10</sub> has a lower pool median |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))| than at 25.3 on at least 9 of the 12 LG M50 pools. Refuted if on fewer than 6. (Expected to be hard: 5zf found the law's lever moves under 1 mV in ten cycles there.) The 5zg *med* calibration from the set's other 160 designs is reported alongside, not hypothesised.
- Caveats fixed now: seen data throughout; &rho; compares the separator-node OCP (one node) with a uniform-stoichiometry lever, so a match says the law's lever is mis-scaled, not why; the slope through the origin weights late cycles most; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zh outcome: ten earlier 100-cycle runs buy the per-set plating exponent's typical gain (every pool's median error within 0.007 of the 160-run calibration), but not its reliability: at ten runs the 90th-percentile pool still loses to the physical exponent on 10 of 24 pools (4 of 12 LG M50), and it takes about 80 runs before that tail is gone. One earlier campaign at another current is as good as scattered runs. H178 to H180 supported, H177 and H181 refuted
- Analysis: 768 committed designs (24 pools), 500 resamples per cell, seed 5, about 6 s after the 1-minute `rows` step; the report was run once. One mechanical fix after the plan, before the report: the script registered its imported module in `sys.modules` so the worker pool could pickle the worker function (the first `rows` call failed on that, before any number was produced). Grid interpolation against the exact forecast at k = 160: largest pool gap 7 &times; 10<sup>&minus;6</sup>.
- H177 refuted: *A*, k = 10: the 90th percentile beats *phys* on 8 of 12 LG M50 pools. The four that miss are the pools where *phys* was already closest (`kappa/OKane2022@1.40625x` phys 0.059, q90 0.093; `consistent/OKane2022@1.4375x` 0.064 against 0.083; `loaded/OKane2022@1.375x` 0.071 against 0.083; `loaded/Chen2020@1.375x` 0.080 against 0.093). On the seven LG M50 pools where *phys* errs by more than 0.09, the k = 10 90th percentile is 0.061 to 0.086.
- H178 supported: *A*, k = 10: the median over resamples is within +0.007 of the k = 160 error on every pool (largest gap `consistent/OKane2022@1.4375x`, 0.060 against 0.053). In the median, ten runs is the 160-run result.
- H179 supported: *A*, k = 1: the 90th percentile is above *phys* on all 24 pools (worst 0.178, `kappa/Ai2020@0.5875x`). One design's oracle exponent is not a calibration.
- H180 supported: *B*, k = 10 (ten designs from one earlier pool, one current) has its median within 0.01 of *A*, k = 10 on 24 of 24 pools; its 90th percentile is a little wider (worst 0.098 against 0.093). The current dependence 5zg found on LG M50 is too small, at these spacings, to bias a single-campaign calibration.
- H181 refuted: no k below 160 brings every pool's 90th percentile within 0.01 of its k = 160 error (at k = 80 two `Ai2020` pools stay out, `consistent/Ai2020@0.575x` 0.057 against 0.040 + 0.01 and `loaded/Ai2020@0.525x` 0.064 against 0.053 + 0.01; at k = 40 seven pools; 160 is trivially exact). Counting pools whose 90th percentile beats *phys*: 0 at k = 1, 5 at k = 3, 14 at k = 10, 18 at k = 20, 19 at k = 32, 21 at k = 80, 23 at k = 160.
- Reading: the calibration's typical value settles fast (the median &kappa;* of ten designs is close to the median of 160), so a search that can live with "usually better" needs about ten earlier long runs, from one campaign if that is what it has. What does not settle fast is the tail: &kappa;*'s spread within a set (LG M50 interquartile about 30 to 65 V<sup>&minus;1</sup>) makes a ten-run median land 13 to 16 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> or more from the set's full median one time in ten (5.3 on `Ai2020`; 2000 draws from all six pools, seed 1, a check run after the report), and on the pools where the physical exponent was already within 6 to 8% that miss costs more than the calibration gains. Guaranteeing no loss against physics needs about 80 runs. The cheaper route to reliability is to shrink the spread, which is the mechanism question still open from 5zf: if &kappa;*/(&alpha;F/RT) is a quantity readable from the first ten cycles (the separator-surface rise against the open-circuit rise), it would replace the calibration's scatter with a per-design measurement.
- Caveats: seen data throughout (20 of the 24 pools chose the *med* form in 5zg), so this measures the budget for a form already chosen; the earlier pools are at other currents of the same set, 32 designs each; the k-run draws are from the same Sobol bounds as the test pools; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/fewruns/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `fewruns.svg`; `rows.json` (6.6 MB) is regenerated by `rows` and not committed; tests `tests/test_fewruns.py`.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zh: how many earlier 100-cycle DFN runs does 5zg's per-set plating exponent need? Resampling on the 768 committed designs (plan, written and committed before the resampling was run; H177 to H181 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zg calibrated &kappa; once per parameter set as the median oracle &kappa;* over 160 earlier hundred-cycle DFN runs, which a design search would have to pay for before trusting its ten-cycle forecasts. Here 160 is the cost to cut: what does a budget of k earlier runs buy, and does it matter whether they come from one earlier campaign (one current) or are scattered across several?
- Data: no new simulation. All 24 committed pools of 5zb to 5zg (32 designs each, six pools per set). Each pool is the test pool in turn, and its set's other five pools (160 designs) are the earlier runs. &kappa;* and each design's error on 5zg's 0.25 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> grid are computed by 5zg's unchanged `design` and `errs_on_grid` (`experiments/fewruns/run.py rows`); the pool score at a resampled &kappa; is the median over the pool's designs of the linearly interpolated |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|, checked against the exact forecast at k = 160.
- Two ways to spend k runs, each resampled 500 times (seed 5): *A*, k designs without replacement from all 160; *B*, one of the five earlier pools at random, then k of its 32 designs. k = 1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 32 (both), 40, 80, 160 (*A*). &kappa; = the median &kappa;* of the k designs (5zg's *med*). For each cell: the median and the 90th percentile over resamples of the pool's error.
- Already seen before this plan: the per-pool medians and quartiles of &kappa;* (5zf, 5zg) and the *med* errors with 128 or 160 training designs; not seen: any error at k below 128 or from a single pool.
- H177 (ten runs suffice where it matters): *A*, k = 10: the 90th percentile below the *phys* error (&kappa; = 25.3) on all 12 LG M50 pools. Refuted if on fewer than 9.
- H178 (ten runs nearly match 160): *A*, k = 10: the median within 0.01 of the k = 160 error on all 24 pools. Refuted if more than 0.02 above it on any pool.
- H179 (one run is a gamble): *A*, k = 1: the 90th percentile above *phys* on at least 6 of the 24 pools. Refuted if on fewer than 3.
- H180 (one campaign is as good as scattered runs): *B*, k = 10: the median within 0.01 of *A*, k = 10 on at least 20 of the 24 pools. Refuted if on fewer than 16. (5zg found LG M50's &kappa;* falls with current, so one campaign at another current could carry a bias.)
- H181 (the budget): the smallest k (*A*) at which every pool's 90th percentile is at most its k = 160 error + 0.01 is at most 20. Refuted if above 40 or never reached.
- Caveats fixed now: the earlier pools are at other currents of the same set (no pool is calibrated on its own current); the test pools are all seen data, 20 of them already used to choose the *med* form in 5zg, so this measures the budget, not the form; one current per pool; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Script: `experiments/fewruns/run.py`; outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 5zg outcome: one plating exponent per parameter set, calibrated on that set's earlier 100-cycle runs, removes the ten-cycle forecast's bias on all four fresh pools. LG M50's cycle-99 lithium-loss error falls from 9.3% and 7.6% to 4.9% and 6.5%, the controls improve too (`Ai2020` 5.6% to 4.9%, `NCA_Kim2011` 1.7% to 0.9%), and the worst pool goes from 0.093 to 0.065. H172 to H176 supported
- Bench: 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, Sobol seed 25, 3 workers, about 20 minutes on 4 cores. Three runs (`Ai2020@0.5375x` designs 1, 6 and 19) were killed for memory (exit &minus;9, not the solver) and re-run with one worker, as the plan fixed; none was dropped, so there are 32 designs per pool. The report was run once, after the bench. `experiments/setkappa/run.py`'s `dev`, `design`, `calibrate` and the plan's calibration were not changed after the plan; `report`, `verdicts` and the figure were written while the bench ran, before any fresh result was read.
- Median |ln(L(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|, *phys* (25.3) against *med* (the set's median oracle &kappa;*): `Chen2020@1.265625x` 0.093 to 0.049 (&kappa; 49.7), `OKane2022@1.28125x` 0.076 to 0.065 (38.6), `Ai2020@0.5375x` 0.056 to 0.049 (21.0), `NCA_Kim2011@0.65625x` 0.017 to 0.009 (22.7). *med* beats *phys* on 23, 21, 18 and 21 of 32 designs. The signed median error goes from +0.091, +0.073, &minus;0.048, &minus;0.016 to +0.006, +0.018, +0.004, +0.004: the systematic over-prediction on LG M50 and under-prediction on the controls are gone. Spearman of the *med* forecast against the true loss is 0.959 and 0.966 on LG M50, 0.992 and 0.999 on the controls.
- H172 supported (0.049 &lt; 0.093, 0.065 &lt; 0.076). H173 supported (0.049, 0.065 &le; 0.07). H174 supported (controls improve). H175 supported (worst 0.065 against 0.093).
- H176 supported: *line* (&kappa;* a line in the C-rate) beats *med* on both LG M50 pools, 0.046 against 0.049 (`Chen2020`, &kappa; 53.5) and 0.058 against 0.065 (`OKane2022`, &kappa; 47.4); the fresh pools' own oracle medians were 51.7 and 43.3, so LG M50's exponent does fall with current. *err* (least pooled error) did as well here (0.043, 0.053) though it overfit `OKane2022` in dev; it was not in a hypothesis.
- Reading: the ten-cycle plating forecast is now inside 7% on all four fresh pools and its sign errors are gone, using the design's balance, its first ten cycles, and one number per parameter set from that set's earlier 100-cycle runs. On the controls the calibrated exponent is close to &alpha;F/RT (21 and 23 against 25.3), so it is a small correction there; on LG M50 it is 1.5 to 2 times larger, and it moves with current. That LG M50 needs a steeper effective lever is what the law cannot derive: the next step is the mechanism 5zf named (is the oracle ratio &kappa;*/(&alpha;F/RT) the ratio of the separator-surface potential rise to the open-circuit rise the law reads?), which would replace the calibration with something read from ten cycles, and a cost check of how few earlier 100-cycle runs the calibration needs (here 160 per set; a design search would want fewer than 10).
- Caveats: one current per set; the calibration used 160 earlier hundred-cycle runs per set; the form was chosen after a leave-one-pool-out dev on the same seen pools the calibration uses; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/setkappa/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `dev.txt`, `dev.json`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `setkappa.svg`; tests `tests/test_setkappa.py`.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5zg: does one plating exponent &kappa; per parameter set, calibrated on that set's earlier 100-cycle DFN runs, fix the ten-cycle plating forecast on fresh designs at new currents? Leave-one-pool-out on the 640 committed designs, then a pre-registered fresh bench (plan, written before any fresh run; H172 to H176 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zf showed &kappa; cannot be read from a design's own ten cycles on LG M50, and that the oracle &kappa;* sits at 30 to 57 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> there against &alpha;F/RT = 25.3. A design search has earlier 100-cycle runs of the same parameter set to hand, so it can calibrate &kappa; once per set and then forecast every new design from ten cycles. 5zd's law is unchanged (`experiments/kappa/run.py`: `tab`, `forecast`, `kappa_oracle`); only &kappa; moves (`experiments/setkappa/run.py`).
- Three calibrations per set, each from the set's training designs: *med*, the median oracle &kappa;*; *err*, the &kappa; on a 0.25 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> grid with the least pooled median |ln(L<sub>pred</sub>(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|; *line*, &kappa;* a least-squares line in the C-rate multiplier, read at the target current. *phys* is 25.3.
- Seen (dev; `python experiments/setkappa/run.py dev`, `experiments/setkappa/dev.txt`): leave one pool out over the 20 committed pools of 5zb to 5zf (640 designs, five pools per set), each pool forecast with &kappa; calibrated on its set's other four pools. *med* beats *phys* on 19 of 20 pools, worst pool 0.078 against 0.109; LG M50 0.041 to 0.078 against 0.059 to 0.109; `Ai2020` 0.040 to 0.068 against 0.063 to 0.081 (worse on one pool, 0.067 against 0.065); `NCA_Kim2011` 0.008 to 0.016 against 0.018 to 0.029. *line* wins 18 of 20, worst 0.069; it beats *med* most at the low end of the LG M50 range (`kinetic/OKane2022@1.25x` 0.039 against 0.075), because LG M50's &kappa;* falls with current (slopes &minus;89 and &minus;95 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> per unit C-rate multiplier on `Chen2020` and `OKane2022`). *err* wins 14 and overfits on `OKane2022` (0.080 to 0.094 on three pools). *med* is the primary form because it is the simplest and won most pools; *line* is the secondary.
- Calibration for the fresh bench, fixed now (all 160 seen designs per set): *med* 20.98 (`Ai2020`), 22.74 (`NCA_Kim2011`), 49.68 (`Chen2020`), 38.61 (`OKane2022`); *line* &kappa;* = 10.0 + 23.3c, 18.5 + 7.2c, 165.5 &minus; 88.5c, 169.4 &minus; 95.3c.
- Fresh pools, fixed now: 32 scrambled Sobol designs (seed 25, used by no milestone; energy-cell bounds) on each of `Ai2020@0.5375x`, `NCA_Kim2011@0.65625x`, `Chen2020@1.265625x`, `OKane2022@1.28125x`, currents no milestone has used, inside each set's dev range; the LG M50 currents sit at the low end, where *med* and *line* part most (*line* reads 53.5 and 47.3 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> there). 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100 with 5zc's `simulate` unchanged, 3 workers; memory kills retried with one worker; failed runs dropped and counted.
- H172 (the per-set exponent helps LG M50): *med*'s median |ln err| below *phys*'s on both LG M50 pools. Refuted if on neither.
- H173 (LG M50 inside 7%): *med* at most 0.07 on both LG M50 pools. Refuted above 0.10 on either.
- H174 (controls not harmed): *med* at most *phys* + 0.01 on both control pools. Refuted if worse by more than 0.03 on either.
- H175 (the worst pool improves): *med*'s worst pool below *phys*'s worst pool and at most 0.08. Refuted if *med*'s worst pool is no better than *phys*'s.
- H176 (current matters on LG M50): *line* below *med* on both LG M50 pools. Refuted if above *med* on both.
- Caveats fixed now: one current per set; calibration uses five earlier pools per set (160 hundred-cycle runs), which a real search would have to pay for; the form was chosen after the leave-one-pool-out dev; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only. The mechanism question 5zf also raised (whether LG M50's steeper lever is the ratio of the separator-surface rise to the open-circuit rise) is not tested here.
- Script: `experiments/setkappa/run.py`; outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5zf outcome: the plating law's kinetic exponent cannot be read from a design's first ten cycles on LG M50, and fitting it per design makes the cycle-99 forecast worse on all four fresh pools. But the &kappa; each set would need is not the same: LG M50 needs a steeper potential lever (median oracle &kappa;* 45 and 32 V<sup>&minus;1</sup>, against &alpha;F/RT = 25.3), while `Ai2020` and `NCA_Kim2011` need about the physical value (20 and 23). H167 to H171 supported
- Bench: 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, Sobol seed 24, 3 workers, about 33 minutes on 4 cores. One run (`Ai2020@0.5875x` design 1) was killed for memory (exit &minus;9, not the solver) and re-run with one worker, as the plan fixed; none was dropped, so there are 32 designs per pool. The report was run once, after the bench. `experiments/kappa/run.py` was not changed after the plan.
- 5zd at &kappa; = 25.3 replicates again: median |ln(L(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))| 0.099 (`Chen2020@1.34375x`) and 0.059 (`OKane2022@1.40625x`), 0.081 (`Ai2020@0.5875x`, signed &minus;0.059), 0.019 (`NCA_Kim2011@0.71875x`). &kappa; = 0 is 0.25 to 0.39.
- H167 supported: the lever rises by 0.7 and 0.6 mV over cycles 2 to 10 on the LG M50 pools; the ten-cycle *fit* sits at the 100 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> bound on 21 of 32 `Chen2020` designs (median 100) and 13 of 32 `OKane2022` designs (interquartile 15 to 98).
- H168 supported: *fit* forecasts worse than &kappa; = 25.3 on both controls (`Ai2020` 0.090 against 0.081, `NCA_Kim2011` 0.054 against 0.019), and on both LG M50 pools too (0.127 against 0.099, 0.122 against 0.059). *slope* is no better (0.092, 0.054, 0.127, 0.090).
- H169 supported: median &kappa;* 45.4 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> (interquartile 29.7 to 65.4) on `Chen2020` and 31.7 (23.9 to 47.7) on `OKane2022`.
- H170 supported: median &kappa;* 20.4 (17.3 to 33.3) on `Ai2020` and 22.9 (22.2 to 25.9) on `NCA_Kim2011`.
- H171 supported: Spearman(*fit*, &kappa;*) 0.721 on `NCA_Kim2011` (lever +46 mV in ten cycles), 0.083 and 0.589 on the LG M50 pools; `Ai2020` (+6.5 mV) &minus;0.126.
- Reading: on all ten LG M50 pools now checked (eight seen, two fresh), the law with the physical &kappa; over-predicts because, at a constant &eta;, its potential lever is too shallow there: the oracle's pool medians are 30 to 57 V<sup>&minus;1</sup>, 1.2 to 2.3 &times; &alpha;F/RT. On the two control sets the physical &kappa; is about right, and `Ai2020`'s error is design-to-design scatter in &kappa;* around 20 (interquartile 17 to 33), not a bias the exponent can remove. A per-design &kappa; from ten cycles is a dead end where it matters most, because LG M50's lever barely moves in ten cycles; on `NCA_Kim2011`, where it moves 40 to 50 mV, the estimate tracks &kappa;* but reads 6 to 8 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> high. The next test is the one a design search can afford: one &kappa; per parameter set, calibrated on that set's earlier 100-cycle runs (the seen pools give LG M50 about 30 to 57), checked on fresh designs at new currents; and, for the mechanism, whether the steeper lever LG M50 needs equals the ratio of the separator-surface potential rise to the open-circuit lever rise.
- Caveats: one current per set; &kappa;* uses the cycle-99 loss, so it diagnoses and does not forecast; the estimators were fixed before dev, the hypotheses after it; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/kappa/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `dev.txt`, `dev.json`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `kappa.svg`; tests `tests/test_kappa.py`.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5zf: is the plating law's remaining error in its kinetic exponent &kappa;? Can &kappa; be estimated per design from its first ten cycles, and which &kappa; would each set need? Seen-data diagnosis on the 512 committed designs, then a pre-registered fresh bench (plan, written before any fresh run; H167 to H171 are this entry's)
- Question: every milestone since 5zb has used &kappa; = &alpha;F/RT = 25.3 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> on every set, from OKane2022's plating transfer coefficient (&alpha; = 0.65, 298 K). 5ze put the law's remaining error on its structure and named &kappa; as the first candidate. In 5zd's law R<sub>&kappa;</sub>(L) = &int; exp(&kappa; dU<sub>&eta;</sub>(l)) Q<sub>&eta;</sub>(0)/Q<sub>&eta;</sub>(l) dl the lever dU<sub>&eta;</sub> and the window Q<sub>&eta;</sub> do not depend on &kappa;, so they are tabulated once per design and R<sub>&kappa;</sub> is cheap for any &kappa; (`experiments/kappa/run.py`).
- Two ten-cycle estimators, fixed before dev: *fit*, the &kappa; in [0, 100] V<sup>&minus;1</sup> that makes R<sub>&kappa;</sub>(L<sub>n</sub>), n = 2..10, most nearly a line in n (least 1 &minus; r<sup>2</sup>); *slope*, the least-squares slope of &minus;[ln &Delta;L<sub>n</sub> + ln Q<sub>&eta;</sub>(0)/Q<sub>&eta;</sub>(L)] on dU<sub>&eta;</sub>(L) at each cycle's midpoint, n = 3..10. Both use a table over [0, 1.05 L(10)]: 5zd's table spans 30 L(10) in 60 cells, about two cells inside the first ten cycles, which a synthetic test showed biases the slope estimator by 15% (found while writing the tests, before dev; the forecast itself still uses 5zd's table, so &kappa; = 25.3 reproduces 5zd exactly). And one diagnostic that is not a forecast: the oracle &kappa;*, the &kappa; whose cycle-99 forecast is exact (bisection on [0, 100]).
- Seen (dev; `python experiments/kappa/run.py dev`, `experiments/kappa/dev.txt`; 16 pools, 512 designs, every form listed):
  - The ten-cycle &kappa; does not help. *fit* forecasts worse than &kappa; = 25.3 on 12 of 16 pools, on all 8 control pools (`Ai2020` 0.088 to 0.135 against 0.063 to 0.076; `NCA_Kim2011` 0.057 to 0.065 against 0.018 to 0.029), and better on 4 LG M50 pools by at most 0.020. *slope* is within 0.03 of *fit* everywhere. &kappa; = 0 (charge-time lever alone) is 0.25 to 0.41 everywhere, so the potential lever matters.
  - On LG M50 &kappa; cannot be read from ten cycles: the lever rises by 0.5 to 1.4 mV over cycles 2 to 10 (pool medians), so R is nearly a line for any &kappa;; *fit*'s interquartile range is 49 to 84 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> wide on seven of the eight LG M50 pools, and on the eighth (`loaded/Chen2020@1.375x`, 16 V<sup>&minus;1</sup>) because more than a quarter of its designs sit at the 100 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> bound (the per-pool count at the bound is the `kedge` column of `dev.txt`). Spearman(*fit*, &kappa;*) is &minus;0.31 to 0.55.
  - Where the lever moves it can: on `NCA_Kim2011` the lever rises 39 to 52 mV in ten cycles and Spearman(*fit*, &kappa;*) is 0.72 to 0.85, but *fit* is biased high (29 to 33 against &kappa;* 21 to 24), which is why its forecast is worse. `Ai2020` (6 to 8 mV) is in between: Spearman &minus;0.06 to 0.16.
  - The oracle: LG M50 needs a larger &kappa;, median &kappa;* 30 to 57 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> on all eight LG M50 pools (1.2 to 2.3 &times; &alpha;F/RT); the controls need about the physical one, 20 to 25 (`Ai2020`) and 21 to 24 (`NCA_Kim2011`). So the constant &eta; is not the only compensation on LG M50: the law also asks for a steeper potential lever there, and on `Ai2020` the oracle median is within 6 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> of 25.3, so its error is scatter in &kappa;* across designs (interquartile 17 to 31) rather than a bias.
- Fresh pools, fixed now: 32 scrambled Sobol designs (seed 24, used by no milestone; energy-cell bounds) on each of `Ai2020@0.5875x`, `NCA_Kim2011@0.71875x`, `Chen2020@1.34375x`, `OKane2022@1.40625x`, currents no milestone has used, inside each set's dev range. 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100 with 5zc's `simulate` unchanged, 3 workers; memory kills retried with one worker; failed runs dropped and counted.
- H167 (LG M50 &kappa; is not identifiable from ten cycles): on both LG M50 pools the median lever rise over cycles 2 to 10 is below 2 mV and *fit* is uninformative, its interquartile range wider than 40 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> or at least a quarter of the designs at a bound of [0, 100]. Refuted if on either the range is under 20 V<sup>&minus;1</sup> with fewer than a quarter at a bound. (The bound clause was added after a first draft of this plan, committed in this PR, used the range alone, which `loaded/Chen2020@1.375x` already fails in dev; no fresh run had started.)
- H168 (no ten-cycle gain where it was tried hardest): *fit* forecasts worse than &kappa; = 25.3 on both control pools. Refuted if better on either.
- H169 (LG M50 needs a steeper lever): median &kappa;* above 25.3 on both LG M50 pools and above 30 on at least one. Refuted if at most 25.3 on either.
- H170 (the controls need about the physical &kappa;): median &kappa;* within [17, 28] V<sup>&minus;1</sup> on both control pools. Refuted if outside [12, 35] on either.
- H171 (identifiable only where the lever moves): Spearman(*fit*, &kappa;*) at least 0.6 on `NCA_Kim2011` and below 0.6 on both LG M50 pools. Refuted if below 0.4 on `NCA_Kim2011` or above 0.75 on either LG M50 pool.
- Caveats fixed now: one current per set; &kappa;* uses the cycle-99 loss, so it diagnoses and does not forecast; the estimators were chosen before dev, the hypotheses after it (all 16 dev pools are in `dev.txt`); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Script: `experiments/kappa/run.py`; outcome to be logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5ze outcome: neither of 5zd's proposed fixes nor a split overpotential removes the `Ai2020` cost, and on fresh pools the reason replicates: knowing &eta;'s true drift to cycle 99 makes the forecast worse on all four pools (`Ai2020` 0.111 against 0.063). 5zd's LG M50 result replicates at a new seed (0.109 and 0.095), and the charge-side overpotential from the discharge energy sits within 2 to 5 mV of 5zc's symmetric &eta;, so the symmetric reading was already right. H162 to H166 supported
- Bench: 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, Sobol seed 23, 3 workers, about 25 minutes on 4 cores. Three runs (`Ai2020@0.6125x` designs 3 and 12, `OKane2022@1.3125x` design 1) were killed for memory (exit &minus;9, not the solver) and re-run with one worker, as the plan fixed; none was dropped, so there are 32 designs per pool. The report was run once, after the bench. `experiments/asym/run.py` was not changed after the plan.
- H162 supported (5zd replicates): the &eta;-window law's median |ln(L(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))| is 0.109 (`Chen2020@1.28125x`) and 0.095 (`OKane2022@1.3125x`), against 0.191 and 0.160 for 5zc; `Ai2020@0.6125x` is 0.063 against 0.024 at open circuit, so 5zd's cost on `Ai2020` is still there (signed &minus;0.020, an under-prediction). `NCA_Kim2011@0.5625x` 0.023 against 0.013: at this lower current the &eta; window is worse than open circuit there too, unlike 5zd's 0.8125&times; pool.
- H163 supported: asym's error is within 0.007 of 5zd's on every pool (0.059, 0.023, 0.102, 0.093). It wins 18, 25, 28 and 24 of 32 designs, so the small gain is consistent in sign but not material.
- H164 supported: median |&eta;<sub>c</sub> &minus; &eta;| is 2.3, 0.8, 5.3 and 3.4 mV.
- H165 supported: median &eta;<sub>d</sub> is 156, 242 and 256 mV against &eta;<sub>c</sub> 230, 360 and 368 mV on `Ai2020`, `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` (`NCA_Kim2011` 97 against 86, outside the claim as planned). The discharge end carries about two thirds of the charge end's overpotential, and moving it costs little capacity because the OCV is steep there.
- H166 supported: the oracle drift (&eta; a line in the lost lithium from its ten-cycle mean to the &eta; the cycle-99 charge Ah needs) is worse than a constant &eta; on 4 of 4 pools: 0.111, 0.040, 0.155, 0.126 against 0.063, 0.023, 0.109, 0.095.
- Descriptive (in the table, not in the plan): from cycles 2 to 10 to cycle 99 the charge-side &eta;<sub>c</sub> falls by 41 mV on `Ai2020` (230 to 189), 14 mV on `Chen2020` and 7 mV on `OKane2022`, and rises 32 mV on `NCA_Kim2011`; &eta;<sub>d</sub> moves by at most 13 mV. So the drift 5zc found is on the charge side. Spearman of the asym forecast against the true cycle-99 loss is 0.981, 1.000, 0.987 and 0.987.
- Reading: three readings of the overpotential (symmetric, split, and the true drift) give the same or worse forecasts, so the remaining `Ai2020` error is not in how &eta; is measured. It sits in the law's structure: a constant &eta; makes the R line through cycles 2 to 10 consistent with the later cycles on LG M50, and putting the true drift in breaks that on every set, which says the constant &eta; is compensating for something the law leaves out (candidates: the kinetic exponent &kappa;, taken from OKane2022's plating parameters on every set, or the electrolyte's share of the potential at the separator). The next test is a ten-cycle estimate of &kappa; per design, from the slowdown of the per-cycle plating over cycles 2 to 10 against the &eta;-window potential rise, checked on the 512 committed designs before a fresh bench.
- Caveats: one current per set; the oracle uses the cycle-99 charge Ah, so it diagnoses and does not forecast; &eta;<sub>d</sub> assumes the discharge window shares its top with the previous charge (no rest step, as in the protocol); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/asym/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `dev.txt`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `asym.svg`; tests `tests/test_asym.py`.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5ze: is 5zd's `Ai2020` under-prediction caused by its overpotential drifting, or by the symmetric overpotential 5zc assumed? Seen-data diagnosis of four candidate fixes, then a pre-registered replication on fresh pools of all four sets (plan, written before any fresh run; H162 to H166 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zd put its `Ai2020` cost (2.5% to 7.6%) on &eta; drifting by about &minus;20 mV over 100 cycles and proposed two fixes: let &eta; follow its own ten-cycle trend, or pick open circuit or the &eta; window per design from the first ten cycles. A third assumption is untested: 5zc's &eta; is symmetric, so the charge Ah fixes the window's length but not where it sits, and the potential lever is read at v<sub>hi</sub> &minus; &eta;. The discharge energy fixes the rest: under constant current the mean discharge voltage (Wh over Ah) is the mean open-circuit voltage over the window less &eta;<sub>d</sub>, so two measured numbers per cycle give &eta;<sub>d</sub> and &eta;<sub>c</sub> (`experiments/asym/run.py`, `solve_asym`), and the lever is read at v<sub>hi</sub> &minus; &eta;<sub>c</sub>.
- Seen (dev; `python experiments/asym/run.py dev`, `experiments/asym/dev.txt`): 5zb's, 5zc's and 5zd's 384 designs. Five forms, all listed: 5zc (open circuit), 5zd (&eta; window), drift (&eta; a line in the lost lithium through cycles 2 to 10, both levers read at &eta;(L)), select (open circuit or &eta; window, whichever forecasts cycle 10 better from cycles 2 to 7), asym (this plan's split). Plus an oracle that is not a forecast: &eta;(L) a line from its ten-cycle mean to the &eta; the measured cycle-99 charge Ah needs (5zc's and 5zd's pools only).
  - Drift over-extrapolates: the ten-cycle trend puts LG M50's cycle-99 &eta; at 327 to 347 mV where it needs 347 to 382, and the LG M50 error rises from 0.064 to 0.104 (5zd) to 0.145 to 0.181. `Ai2020` improves only to 0.054 to 0.061 from 0.065 to 0.076.
  - The oracle is worse than a constant &eta; on all eight pools it can be run on, `Ai2020` included (0.094 and 0.102 against 0.076 and 0.065; LG M50 0.101 to 0.143 against 0.064 to 0.104). So knowing &eta;'s true drift does not fix `Ai2020`: 5zd's drift explanation does not hold on seen data. The law's constant &eta; works on LG M50 partly by compensation.
  - Select picks the wrong form: the &eta; window on 28 to 30 of 32 `Ai2020` designs (where open circuit is better) and open circuit on 2 to 12 of 32 `OKane2022` designs (where the &eta; window is better). Ten cycles do not separate the two readings; they part only later.
  - Asym: &eta;<sub>c</sub> comes out within 1 to 6 mV of the symmetric &eta; (pool medians), while &eta;<sub>d</sub> is 70 to 150 mV lower on LG M50 and `Ai2020` (the bottom of the window is steep in OCV, so it costs little capacity). The forecast moves by at most 0.012 (`Chen2020@1.3125x` 0.104 to 0.092); `Ai2020` is unchanged (0.076 to 0.078).
  - Also checked (scratch, not committed): plating is 98 to 100% of the lithium lost over cycles 2 to 99 on every seen pool once `pl_mol` is scaled by the electrodes in parallel (34 on `Ai2020`), so `Ai2020` is a plating cell too; an unscaled first look read 3% and was wrong.
- No new law is proposed. The fresh bench tests whether these four seen findings replicate at a new seed and new currents, so they can be stated as results rather than dev observations.
- Fresh pools, fixed now: 32 scrambled Sobol designs (seed 23, used by no milestone; energy-cell bounds) on each of `Ai2020@0.6125x`, `NCA_Kim2011@0.5625x`, `Chen2020@1.28125x`, `OKane2022@1.3125x`, currents no milestone has used, inside each set's dev range. 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100 with 5zc's `simulate` unchanged, 3 workers; memory kills retried; failed runs dropped and counted.
- H162 (5zd replicates): the &eta;-window law's median |ln(L(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))| is at most 0.12 on both LG M50 pools, and `Ai2020` is above 0.05. Refuted if either LG M50 pool is above 0.15, or if `Ai2020` is no worse than open circuit (the cost 5zd found is gone).
- H163 (asym changes nothing that matters): asym's error is within 0.02 of 5zd's on every pool. Refuted if more than 0.04 away on any.
- H164 (the charge side carries the overpotential the lever needs): median |&eta;<sub>c</sub> &minus; &eta;| at most 10 mV on every pool. Refuted above 25 mV on any.
- H165 (the split is real): median &eta;<sub>d</sub> below median &eta;<sub>c</sub> on both LG M50 pools and `Ai2020`. Refuted if on at most one of the three. (`NCA_Kim2011` is not in the claim: dev has &eta;<sub>d</sub> above &eta;<sub>c</sub> at 0.5&times; and below at the higher currents.)
