- H166 (drift is not the gap): the oracle drift's error is above the constant-&eta; error on at least 3 of 4 pools. Refuted if on at most 1.
- Caveats fixed now: one current per set; the oracle uses the cycle-99 charge Ah, so it diagnoses and does not forecast; &eta;<sub>d</sub> assumes the discharge window shares its top with the previous charge (no rest step, as in the protocol); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Script: `experiments/asym/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5zd outcome: reading the plating law's potential lever at the same &eta;-shortened charge window as its charge-time lever halves the LG M50 forecast error again, from 5zc's 20% and 17% to 10% and 6% on fresh pools (59 of 64 designs won), with no new parameter. The &eta; window also forecasts the cycle-99 separator-surface potential rise better than open circuit there (21 and 17 mV against 35 and 27 mV). The cost is on `Ai2020`, 2.5% to 7.6%, just past the pre-registered harm bar. H157, H158, H160, H161 supported; H159 mixed
- Bench: 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, Sobol seed 22, 3 workers, about 20 minutes on 4 cores. Five `Ai2020@0.575x` runs were killed for memory (exit &minus;9, not the solver) and re-run with one worker, as the plan fixed; none was dropped, so there are 32 designs per pool. The report was run once, after the bench. `experiments/consistent/run.py` was not changed after the plan.
- H157 supported: median |ln(L(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))| 0.104 and 0.064 against 5zc's 0.202 and 0.167 on `Chen2020@1.3125x` / `OKane2022@1.4375x` (30 and 29 of 32 designs won; 5zb: 0.270, 0.259). The over-prediction shrinks from +0.202 and +0.165 (signed) to +0.095 and +0.042.
- H158 supported: 0.104 and 0.064, both within 0.12.
- H159 mixed: `NCA_Kim2011@0.8125x` improves, 0.033 to 0.020 (26 of 32 won). `Ai2020@0.575x` worsens from 0.025 to 0.076 (signed +0.006 to &minus;0.060, now an under-prediction), by 0.051: past the 0.05 bar and inside the 0.10 refutation line. Dev had said about 0.04.
- H160 supported: worst pool 0.104 (`Chen2020`) against 5zc's 0.202 (`Chen2020`). Over the programme's fresh pools the worst-pool error has gone 0.346 (5y), 0.264 (5zb), 0.210 (5zc), 0.104 (here).
- H161 supported: median absolute error of the predicted cycle-99 separator-surface OCP rise (measured median 39 and 36 mV) is 21.1 and 16.6 mV against 35.0 and 27.3 mV at open circuit. On the controls the &eta; window is about as poor as open circuit (`Ai2020` 75 against 80 mV of a 118 mV rise) or worse (`NCA_Kim2011` 40 against 23 mV).
- Descriptive (not in the plan; computed from `summary.json` after the verdicts): Spearman of the predicted against the true cycle-99 loss falls on LG M50, 0.994 to 0.971 (`Chen2020`) and 0.964 to 0.942 (`OKane2022`), and is 0.990 and 1.000 on the controls (5zc 0.996, 0.997). So the &eta; lever fixes the size of the loss more than its order across designs.
- Reading: on LG M50 the programme's ten-cycle plating forecast is now inside 10% with every input either the parameter set, the design's balance, or its own first ten cycles of loss and charge Ah. The two levers of 5zb's kinetics are both read where the loaded charge actually ends, set by one effective overpotential. On `Ai2020` the same reading over-corrects: its &eta; drifts by about &minus;20 mV over 100 cycles (5zc, H156), so the shortened window over-states the late potential rise and the law under-predicts. The next test is whether letting &eta; follow its own ten-cycle trend (one more number from the same cycles) removes the `Ai2020` under-prediction without giving back the LG M50 gain, or whether a per-set rule (open circuit where the charge Ah already matches, the &eta; window where it does not) can be stated in advance from the first ten cycles.
- Caveats: one current per set; one symmetric &eta; held constant; the form was chosen after 256 seen designs and seven forms (all in `dev.txt`); four pools; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/consistent/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `dev.txt`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `consistent.svg`; tests `tests/test_consistent.py`.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5zd: does 5zc's loaded law forecast the 100-cycle lithium loss better when its potential lever is read at the same &eta;-shortened charge window as its charge-time lever? Fresh pools of all four sets (plan, written before any fresh run; H157 to H161 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zc moved the charge-time lever to the loaded window (the charge stops when the open-circuit voltage is &eta; short of the upper cut-off) and cut LG M50's over-prediction to 21% and 16%, but kept the potential lever dU at the open-circuit upper cut-off. Its post hoc put the remaining gap on the surface potential at the separator. If the charge stops at OCV = v<sub>hi</sub> &minus; &eta;, the negative electrode's open-circuit stoichiometry at the end of each charge is the top of that shortened window, so the consistent reading of the potential lever is dU<sub>&eta;</sub>(L) = U<sub>n</sub>(x<sub>hi</sub>(n<sub>Li</sub> &minus; L; v<sub>hi</sub> &minus; &eta;)) &minus; U<sub>n</sub>(x<sub>hi</sub>(n<sub>Li</sub>; v<sub>hi</sub> &minus; &eta;)). One &eta; (5zc's, from the charge Ah of cycles 2 to 10) then sets both levers; nothing is fitted beyond 5zc's line in n.
- Model, fixed now (`experiments/consistent/run.py`): R = &int; exp(&kappa; dU<sub>&eta;</sub>) Q<sub>&eta;</sub>(0)/Q<sub>&eta;</sub>(l) dl, &kappa; = 25.3 V<sup>&minus;1</sup>, a line in n through cycles 2 to 10, read at 99, inverted. Inputs: the parameter set, the design's balance, its first ten cycles' loss and charge Ah. The separator-surface OCP is not an input; it is used only to score H161.
- Seen (dev; `python experiments/consistent/run.py dev`, `experiments/consistent/dev.txt`): 5zb's and 5zc's 256 designs. Seven potential-lever forms were tried, all listed in `dev.txt`: open circuit (5zc), open circuit plus 5za's stoichiometry offset, the &eta; window (this plan), the &eta; window plus an offset, a line in L through the measured surface OCP, and the open-circuit and &eta; rises scaled through the origin to the measured cycles 2 to 10 rise. Median |ln(L(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|, 5zc against the &eta; window: `Chen2020@1.25x` 0.192 to 0.102, `Chen2020@1.375x` 0.210 to 0.080, `OKane2022@1.25x` 0.161 to 0.097, `OKane2022@1.375x` 0.161 to 0.071; controls `Ai2020@0.525x` 0.031 to 0.065, `Ai2020@0.65x` 0.021 to 0.065, `NCA_Kim2011@0.5x` 0.025 to 0.029, `NCA_Kim2011@0.6875x` 0.023 to 0.018. The &eta; window forecasts the cycle-99 surface OCP rise better than open circuit on all four LG M50 pools (16 to 20 mV against 26 to 30 mV median error) but worse on three of four controls. The offset forms forecast the rise best (5 to 13 mV on LG M50) yet give worse loss forecasts, so a better rise forecast alone does not buy a better loss forecast; the &eta; window was chosen because it adds no parameter and follows from 5zc's own assumption, not only because it scored best.
- Fresh pools, fixed now: 32 scrambled Sobol designs (seed 22, used by no milestone; energy-cell bounds) on each of `Ai2020@0.575x`, `NCA_Kim2011@0.8125x`, `Chen2020@1.3125x`, `OKane2022@1.4375x`, currents no milestone has used, inside each set's dev range. 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100 with 5zc's `simulate` unchanged, 3 workers; memory kills retried; failed runs dropped and counted.
- H157 (LG M50, better): the &eta;-window law's median |ln err| is below 5zc's on both LG M50 pools. Refuted if on neither.
- H158 (LG M50, good enough): at most 0.12 on both LG M50 pools. Refuted above 0.15 on either (5zc's H153 bar, missed by 5zc).
- H159 (controls, bounded harm): no worse than 5zc by more than 0.05 on either control pool. Refuted if worse by more than 0.10 on either. Dev says `Ai2020` will be worse by about 0.04.
- H160 (one law): the worst pool error over the four pools is below 5zc's worst. Refuted otherwise.
- H161 (the lever itself): the &eta; window forecasts the cycle-99 separator-surface OCP rise u(99) &minus; u(2) with a smaller median absolute error than the open-circuit reading on both LG M50 pools. Refuted if on neither.
- Caveats fixed now: one current per set; one symmetric &eta; held constant over 100 cycles (5zc's H156 found it drifts by 20 to 35 mV on the controls); the form was chosen after seeing 256 designs and seven forms; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Script: `experiments/consistent/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5zc outcome: reading the charge time from the loaded charge capacity (an effective overpotential &eta; measured in the design's first ten cycles) cuts 5zb's LG M50 over-prediction of the 100-cycle lithium loss from 28% and 25% to 21% and 16%, wins on all 64 LG M50 designs, and leaves the controls at 3.0% and 2.3%. A constant &eta; predicts the cycle-99 charge Ah within 4 to 5% on three pools (7% on `Ai2020`), where the open-circuit capacity is off by 20 to 86%. Post hoc, the measured charge Ah plus the measured separator-surface potential rise make LG M50's per-cycle plating steady, so the gap left is forecasting that surface rise. H152, H154, H155 supported; H153, H156 mixed
- Bench: 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, Sobol seed 21, 3 workers, about 17 minutes on 4 cores, none failed or killed; 32 designs per pool. The report was run once, after the bench. `experiments/loaded/run.py` was changed after the plan only to add the post-hoc command below; the analysis is untouched.
- H152 supported: median |ln(L(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))| 0.210 and 0.161 against 5zb's 0.279 and 0.248 on `Chen2020@1.375x` / `OKane2022@1.375x`, 32 of 32 designs won on each. Both still over-predict (signed +0.210, +0.161).
- H153 mixed: `OKane2022` 0.161 and `Chen2020` 0.210, above the 0.15 bar and below the 0.22 refutation line (dev: 0.193, 0.161).
- H154 supported: `Ai2020@0.525x` 0.030 against 0.031 (18 of 32 won; signed &minus;0.004 against +0.027), `NCA_Kim2011@0.6875x` 0.023 against 0.018 (+0.005, inside the 0.02 margin; 8 of 32 won).
- H155 supported: worst pool 0.210 (`Chen2020`) against 5zb's 0.279 (`Chen2020`); 5y's worst was 0.346. Spearman of the predicted against the true cycle-99 loss 0.982, 0.995, 0.995, 0.996.
- H156 mixed: with &eta; held at its cycles 2 to 10 value, Q<sub>&eta;</sub> at the cycle-99 lithium misses the cycle-99 charge Ah by a median |ln| 0.069, 0.047, 0.048 and 0.040 (`Ai2020`, `NCA_Kim2011`, `Chen2020`, `OKane2022`); the open-circuit capacity misses by 0.287, 0.196, 0.831 and 0.856. The &eta; the cycle-99 charge needs is 162, 147, 372 and 377 mV against 182, 112, 378 and 383 mV from cycles 2 to 10, so &eta; is nearly constant on LG M50 and drifts on the controls (&minus;20 mV on `Ai2020`, +35 mV on `NCA_Kim2011`).
- Descriptive (in the plan): with 5za's surface offset as well, 0.141, 0.113, 0.239 and 0.153; it helps neither control and only `OKane2022`.
- Post hoc (after the verdicts; `python experiments/loaded/run.py posthoc`, `experiments/loaded/posthoc.txt`): median ln of the per-cycle loss at cycle 99 over cycle 2 is &minus;1.91, &minus;4.43, &minus;1.33, &minus;1.30. Removing the measured charge-Ah change and &kappa; times the open-circuit potential rise leaves &minus;0.23, &minus;0.07, &minus;0.78, &minus;0.58; removing the charge Ah and &kappa; times the measured separator-surface OCP rise (5z's lever) leaves +1.35, &minus;0.49, &minus;0.14, +0.06. So on LG M50 the charge time and the surface potential together carry the whole slowdown (the rate is steady to within 15%), while on the controls the open-circuit rise does. The `NCA_Kim2011` open-circuit figure is sensitive to which cycle's lithium dU is read at (&minus;0.07 at the lithium before each cycle, &minus;0.49 one cycle later), because its loss per cycle falls 80-fold.
- Reading: the charge-time lever is now read correctly. A constant effective overpotential turns ten cycles of charge Ah into a 100-cycle charge capacity within 5% on LG M50, where the open-circuit balance is off by a factor of 2.3. Its cost on the controls is nil. What remains on LG M50 is the potential lever: the plating slows through the loaded surface OCP at the separator, which the open-circuit dU under-reads and 5za's ten-cycle offset predicts poorly. The next test is a ten-cycle forecast of that surface rise, checked against the 128 recorded `u_end` curves here before any fresh bench.
- Caveats: one current per set; one symmetric &eta;, an effective number, not a measured overpotential; open-circuit dU; the form was proposed by 5zb's post hoc and checked on 5zb's pools (seen; one form tried); four pools; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/loaded/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `dev.jsonl`, `dev.txt`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `posthoc.txt`, `loaded.svg`; tests `tests/test_loaded.py`.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5zc: does 5zb's kinetic law forecast the 100-cycle lithium loss better when the charge time is read from the loaded charge capacity, measured in the design's own first ten cycles, instead of the open-circuit capacity? Fresh pools of all four sets (plan, written before any fresh run; H152 to H156 are this entry's)
- Question: 5zb's post hoc found the loaded charge on LG M50 shrinks faster than the open-circuit capacity Q(L) its law uses (discharge-energy ratio 0.61 against Q ratio 0.72 by cycle 100), so the charge-time lever is under-read and the law over-predicts by 24 to 26%. 5zb proposed the loaded capacity in place of Q.
- Model, fixed now (`experiments/loaded/run.py`): under constant-current charge the charge stops when the open-circuit voltage is &eta; short of the upper cut-off, and the discharge before it stopped &eta; above the lower one. Q<sub>&eta;</sub>(L) is the open-circuit capacity between v<sub>lo</sub> + &eta; and v<sub>hi</sub> &minus; &eta; with n<sub>Li</sub> &minus; L of lithium (5r's balance). Per design, &eta; is solved (bisection) so that Q<sub>&eta;</sub> equals the measured charge Ah of cycle n, n = 2..10, and averaged; then 5zb's law unchanged with Q<sub>&eta;</sub> for Q: R = &int; exp(&kappa; dU) Q<sub>&eta;</sub>(0)/Q<sub>&eta;</sub>(l) dl, &kappa; = 25.3 V<sup>&minus;1</sup>, open-circuit dU, a line in n through cycles 2 to 10, read at 99, inverted. Inputs: the parameter set, the design's balance, its first ten cycles' loss and charge Ah. One symmetric &eta; per design is the assumption under test (H156); it is an effective number, not a measured overpotential.
- Seen (dev; `python experiments/loaded/run.py dev`, then `devreport`, `experiments/loaded/dev.txt`): 5zb's 128 designs re-run to cycle 10 for the charge Ah (their lithium loss over cycles 1 to 10 reproduces 5zb's committed curves exactly, max relative difference 0), scored against 5zb's committed 100-cycle curves. Median |ln(L(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))|, 5zb against loaded: `Ai2020@0.65x` 0.076 to 0.021 (24 of 32 designs win), `NCA_Kim2011@0.5x` 0.024 to 0.025 (6 of 32), `Chen2020@1.25x` 0.264 to 0.193 (32 of 32), `OKane2022@1.25x` 0.243 to 0.161 (32 of 32). Median &eta; 251, 74, 345 and 351 mV. Only this one form was tried. With 5za's surface offset as well (descriptive): 0.173, 0.025, 0.165, 0.139.
- Fresh pools, fixed now: 32 scrambled Sobol designs (seed 21, used by no milestone; energy-cell bounds) on each of `Ai2020@0.525x`, `NCA_Kim2011@0.6875x`, `Chen2020@1.375x`, `OKane2022@1.375x`, currents no milestone has used, inside each set's dev range. 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100 with 5za's `simulate` plus the charge and discharge Ah per cycle, 3 workers; memory kills retried; failed runs dropped and counted.
- H152 (LG M50, better): the loaded law's median |ln err| is below 5zb's on both LG M50 pools. Refuted if on neither.
- H153 (LG M50, good enough): at most 0.15 on both LG M50 pools. Refuted above 0.22 on either. Dev (0.19, 0.16) says this is not expected to pass on `Chen2020`.
- H154 (controls, no harm): no worse than 5zb by more than 0.02 on either control pool. Refuted if worse by more than 0.05 on either.
- H155 (one law): the worst pool error is below 5zb's worst pool error over the four pools. Refuted otherwise.
- H156 (the model's own check): with &eta; held at its cycles 2 to 10 value, Q<sub>&eta;</sub> at the cycle-99 lithium predicts the cycle-99 charge Ah within median |ln| 0.05 on all four pools. Refuted above 0.10 on any.
- Caveats fixed now: one current per set; one symmetric &eta; held constant over 100 cycles; open-circuit dU, which 5z showed is the wrong place on LG M50; the form was proposed by 5zb's post hoc and checked on 5zb's pools (seen); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Script: `experiments/loaded/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5zb outcome: a ten-cycle law whose shape is fixed by the plating kinetics (plating per cycle proportional to the charge capacity, slowed by the negative potential rise, so a line in the cycle count) beats 5y's power law on all four fresh pools, 122 of 128 designs, and halves or better its error on the controls (`Ai2020@0.65x` 0.150 to 0.076, `NCA_Kim2011@0.5x` 0.078 to 0.024). One law now serves every set, with the worst pool at 0.264 against 0.325. LG M50 is still over-predicted by 24 to 26%. H147, H149, H150, H151 supported; H148 mixed
- Bench: 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, Sobol seed 20, 3 workers, about 16 minutes on 4 cores. One `Ai2020@0.65x` run was killed for memory (exit &minus;9, not the solver) and re-run with one worker. No design was dropped, so there are 32 per pool. The report was run once, after the bench. `experiments/kinetic/run.py` was not changed after the plan.
- H147 supported: median |ln(L(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))| is 0.076 against 5y's 0.150 on `Ai2020@0.65x` (31 of 32 designs won) and 0.024 against 0.078 on `NCA_Kim2011@0.5x` (30 of 32).
- H148 mixed: `NCA_Kim2011@0.5x` is within the 0.06 bar. `Ai2020@0.65x` is 0.076 (signed +0.062, an over-prediction), above the bar and below the 0.10 refutation line. 0.65&times; is outside the dev range, and the over-prediction grows with current, as it did on the dev pools (`Ai2020` signed &minus;0.008 at 0.3&times; to +0.032 at 0.6&times;; `NCA_Kim2011` &minus;0.024 at 0.5&times; to +0.059 at 1&times;).
- H149 supported: 0.264 and 0.243 against 0.308 and 0.325 on `Chen2020@1.25x` / `OKane2022@1.25x` (29 and 32 wins), both above 0.15.
- H150 supported: the worst pool is 0.264 (`Chen2020@1.25x`) against 5y's 0.325 (`OKane2022@1.25x`). Across the four pools the kinetic law wins 4 of 4 (one-sided sign p = 0.0625) and 122 of 128 designs.
- H151 supported: Spearman of the predicted against the true cycle-99 loss is 0.977, 0.999, 0.991 and 0.993 (5y: 0.962, 1.000, 0.995, 0.994).
- Descriptive (in the plan): with 5za's surface offset the kinetic law gives 0.204 and 0.179 on the LG M50 pools, the best so far there (5za: 0.21 to 0.23 on other pools), but 0.142 on `Ai2020` (signed &minus;0.142), so the surface reading still hurts the control.
- Post hoc (after the verdicts): the discharge energy at cycle 100 over cycle 2 is 0.605 and 0.607 on the LG M50 pools, while the open-circuit capacity ratio the law uses is 0.723 and 0.732. On `Ai2020` the two agree to within 0.03 (0.556 against 0.579), and on `NCA_Kim2011` they are 0.461 against 0.425. So on LG M50 the loaded charge shrinks faster than the open-circuit capacity says, the charge-time lever is under-read, and the law over-predicts, which is the direction of the residual.
- Reading: once the charge time is in the feedback, the plating kinetics fix the law's shape, and no curvature needs fitting. That removes 5za's double count on `Ai2020` and works at a current and seed no milestone had seen. The 5y power law worked through compensation, and so the kinetic law transfers better (worst pool 0.26 against 0.33, controls 2 to 3&times; better). The LG M50 gap is now two named under-reads: the potential rise at the separator surface (5z/5za) and the loaded charge capacity (here). Both are visible in the first ten cycles of the design's own run. The next test is the law with the loaded charge capacity per cycle in place of the open-circuit Q, plus the surface offset, pre-registered on all four sets.
- Caveats: one current per set; Q is the open-circuit capacity; open-circuit dU; the form was chosen after seeing 5za's pools and all 13 dev pools (8 forms tried, all listed in the plan); four pools (sign p = 0.0625); OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/kinetic/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `dev.txt`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `kinetic.svg`; tests `tests/test_kinetic.py`.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5zb: does a ten-cycle law whose shape comes from the plating kinetics, not from a fitted power law, forecast the 100-cycle lithium loss on fresh pools of all four parameter sets? One law, a fitted level and rate only (plan, written before any fresh run; H147 to H151 are this entry's)
- Question: 5za found 5y's open-circuit predictor works on `Ai2020` and `NCA_Kim2011` partly by compensation: the power law fitted to R through ten cycles carries part of the deceleration, so an accurate feedback counts it twice. 5za proposed fixing the law's shape from the kinetics. The kinetics give two levers, not one: PyBaMM's irreversible plating current, slowed by the negative potential rise (5x), integrated over a charge that lasts Q(L)/I at constant current, Q(L) the open-circuit capacity between the cut-offs with n<sub>Li</sub> &minus; L of lithium. With an otherwise steady rate, dL/dn = r<sub>0</sub> (Q(L)/Q(0)) exp(&minus;&kappa; dU(L)), so R<sub>Q</sub>(L) = &int;<sub>0</sub><sup>L</sup> exp(&kappa; dU(l)) Q(0)/Q(l) dl is linear in the cycle count, and the law's curvature is no longer free.
- Diagnostic on 5za's committed curves (seen; scratch script, not committed): with the measured separator-surface OCP as the feedback, the per-cycle plating divided by exp(&minus;&kappa; &Delta;U<sub>meas</sub>) still falls from cycle 2 to 99 by ln &minus;0.59 and &minus;0.52 on the LG M50 pools and &minus;1.71 on `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x` (+0.21 on `Ai2020@0.45x`); dividing also by the discharge-energy ratio (a charge-time proxy, ln &minus;0.42 to &minus;0.67) leaves &minus;0.18, &minus;0.08, &minus;1.07, +0.99. So the potential alone does not make the rate steady, and the charge time is a lever of the same size.
- Forms tried on 5za's 128 seen curves, all disclosed (median |ln(L(99)/L<sub>DFN</sub>(99))| on `Chen2020@1.5x`, `OKane2022@1.5x`, `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x`, `Ai2020@0.45x`): 5y 0.351, 0.358, 0.030, 0.085; 5za 0.231, 0.209, 0.065, 0.220; a line in n through 5za's surface R, cycles 2 to 10: 0.236, 0.195, 0.299, 0.135; the same through cycles 5 to 10: 0.258, 0.223, 0.256, 0.197; a line through the open-circuit R: 0.401, 0.362, 0.088, 0.196; a line through R<sub>Q</sub> with the surface offset: 0.200, 0.175, 0.206, 0.098; the power law on that R<sub>Q</sub>: 0.210, 0.191, 0.058, 0.084; **a line through the open-circuit R<sub>Q</sub>: 0.297, 0.250, 0.043, 0.031**. The last is the only form that fixes the shape and needs nothing but the parameter set, the design's balance and its first ten cycles' loss, so it is the predictor tested here; the surface variant is reported, not counted.
- Dev on all 13 committed 100-cycle pools (seen; `python experiments/kinetic/run.py dev`, `experiments/kinetic/dev.txt`): the kinetic law against 5y, median |ln err|: `Ai2020` at 0.3, 0.35, 0.4, 0.45, 0.5, 0.55, 0.6&times; 0.026 to 0.033 against 0.080 to 0.105, winning on 53/53, 60/60, 64/64, 61/62, 62/63, 64/64, 64/64 designs; `NCA_Kim2011` 0.017 and 0.022 against 0.056 and 0.038 at 0.625 and 0.75&times;, but 0.043 and 0.059 against 0.031 and 0.024 at 0.875 and 1&times; (now over-predicting, +0.04, +0.06); LG M50 at 1.5&times; 0.305 and 0.256 against 0.358 and 0.351.
- Predictor, fixed now (`experiments/kinetic/run.py`, `kinetic_loss`): per design, R<sub>Q</sub> tabulated on 0 &le; L &le; min(0.95 n<sub>Li</sub>, 30 L(10)) with 5x's open-circuit dU at the upper cut-off and Q from 5r's balance (60-point midpoint rule), &kappa; = 25.3 V<sup>&minus;1</sup>; a least-squares line through R<sub>Q</sub>(L(n)) for n = 2..10, read at n = 99, inverted. Baseline: 5y's power law on R, unchanged.
- Fresh pools, fixed now: 32 scrambled Sobol designs (seed 20, used by no milestone; energy-cell bounds) on each of `Ai2020@0.65x`, `NCA_Kim2011@0.5x`, `Chen2020@1.25x`, `OKane2022@1.25x`, a current per set that no milestone has run to cycle 100 (`Ai2020` at 0.65&times; is outside the dev range of 0.3 to 0.6&times;). 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100 with 5za's `simulate`, unchanged, 3 workers; memory kills retried; failed runs dropped and counted.
- H147 (controls, better): the kinetic law's median |ln err| is below 5y's on both control pools (`Ai2020`, `NCA_Kim2011`). Refuted if on neither.
- H148 (controls, good): at most 0.06 on both control pools. Refuted above 0.10 on either.
- H149 (LG M50, better but not enough, as dev says): below 5y's on both LG M50 pools and above 0.15 on both. Refuted if below 5y's on neither.
- H150 (one law): the kinetic law's worst pool error is below 5y's worst pool error over the four pools. Refuted otherwise.
- H151 (order): Spearman of the predicted against the true cycle-99 loss at least 0.9 on all four pools. Refuted below 0.8 on any.
- Caveats fixed now: one current per set; Q is the open-circuit capacity, not the loaded charge time; open-circuit dU, which 5z showed is the wrong place on LG M50; the form was chosen after seeing 5za's pools and all 13 dev pools; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Script: `experiments/kinetic/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5za outcome: read at the separator-side surface, the plating feedback cuts 5y's over-prediction of the 100-cycle lithium loss on fresh LG M50 pools from 35% to 21 to 23%, with no constant fitted, but not to the 15% bar; on the controls it does harm, and on `Ai2020` it does harm while predicting the surface rise exactly, so 5y's open-circuit reading works there partly by compensation. The `Ai2020` bookkeeping gap is the parallel-electrode count (5p), confirmed on 32 fresh designs. H143, H145, H146 supported; H144 mixed
- Bench: 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, Sobol seed 19, 3 workers, about 25 minutes on 4 cores. Four `Ai2020@0.45x` runs were killed for memory (exit &minus;9, not the solver) and re-run with one worker; none dropped, 32 designs per pool. The report was run once, after the bench; `experiments/surface/run.py` was changed after the plan only to retry memory kills (the analysis is untouched).
- H143 supported: median |ln(L(99)/L_DFN(99))| 0.231 and 0.209 against 5y's 0.351 and 0.358 on `Chen2020@1.5x` / `OKane2022@1.5x`; the surface predictor wins on 20 and 23 of 32 designs. Both still over-predict (signed +0.231, +0.209).
- H144 mixed: above the 0.15 bar on both pools, below the 0.25 refutation line on both (dev: 0.128, 0.130; the fresh pools are worse than the seen ones).
- H145 supported (the predicted failure): worse than 5y on both controls, `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x` 0.065 against 0.030 and `Ai2020@0.45x` 0.220 against 0.085 (wins 4 and 1 of 32).
- H146 supported: Spearman of the predicted against the true cycle-99 loss 0.959 and 0.962 (5y: 0.987, 0.984, so order was never the problem).
- Bookkeeping, as planned: plating is 99.8%, 99.8%, 98.9% and 99.6% of the cycle-99 loss once PyBaMM's booked plating and SEI are multiplied by the parallel-electrode count (34 on `Ai2020`, 1 elsewhere); total lost / (count &times; booked) is 1 to five decimals on all 128 runs.
- Post hoc (after the verdicts, from the recorded surface OCP): the measured rise of the separator-node surface OCP from cycle 2 to 99 is a median 26.4 and 25.8 mV on the LG M50 pools, against 18.8 and 18.1 mV predicted by the shifted OCP table; on `Ai2020` 88.7 mV measured against 89.2 predicted; on `NCA_Kim2011` 102 against 59. The open-circuit reading predicts 0.6, 8.9, 130 and 39 mV.
- Reading: on LG M50 the surface offset puts the predictor on the right lever (a rise appears where the open-circuit reading sees none) and removes a third of the error, but it under-reads the rise (19 against 26 mV) because the offset taken from cycles 2 to 10 shrinks as the charge shortens, so the loss is still over-predicted. On `Ai2020` the predicted rise is right and the forecast is worse: the power law fitted to R over cycles 2 to 10 already carries part of the deceleration, so an accurate feedback counts it twice, the same double count 5x met with the &radic;n law. So 5y's open-circuit predictor is not the physics read correctly on `Ai2020` and `NCA_Kim2011`; it is a feedback that is too weak, paired with a law that is too curved. A predictor faithful to the mechanism needs the law's shape fixed by the plating kinetics (a feedback-free rate taken from cycle 1 or 2 only, not a power law fitted through ten cycles), which is the next test.
- Caveats: two LG M50 pools at one current; &delta; averaged over cycles 2 to 10 at one node; the form was chosen on 5z's seen designs; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/surface/bench.jsonl`, `dev.jsonl`, `dev.txt`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `surface.svg`; tests `tests/test_surface.py`.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5za: does 5y's ten-cycle predictor work on the LG M50 sets once the plating feedback is read at the separator-side particle surface instead of at open circuit? Fresh Sobol pools, no constant fitted (plan, written before any fresh run; H143 to H146 are this entry's)
- Question: 5z found the plating slows on the LG M50 sets (`Chen2020`, `OKane2022`) through the negative OCP at the loaded surface by the separator (+38 mV by cycle 99), where 5x/5y's open-circuit reading sees 0.5 to 9 mV and 5y over-predicts the cycle-99 loss by about 35%. 5z proposed predicting that surface shift from the first ten cycles.
- Correction to 5z, found while planning: 5z's descriptive note that on `Ai2020` only 3% of the loss is booked to plating or SEI is the parallel-electrode bookkeeping that 5p already measured and documented (`experiments/attribution/UPSTREAM.md`): PyBaMM 26.10.1.1 books SEI and plating per electrode pair (`L_y L_z`) but totals lithium over `A_cc = L_y L_z n_parallel`, and `Ai2020` has 34 electrodes in parallel. A 3-cycle re-run of 5z's `Ai2020@0.45x` design 7 gives lost 0.0053285 mol, booked 0.00015672 mol, ratio 34.000; plating alone (SEI off) gives the same ratio. Times 34, plating is 99.7% of that loss. So the `Ai2020` loss 5u to 5y fitted is plating, as on the other sets, and 5z's "needs resolving before `Ai2020` counts as a second cell" is withdrawn. This bench records the count and reports the corrected share per pool.
- Seen (dev; `python experiments/surface/run.py dev`, `experiments/surface/dev.txt`): 5z's 39 designs re-run to cycle 10 for the separator-node surface stoichiometry at end of charge, scored against 5z's committed 100-cycle curves. The 5z trajectories show the surface OCP flat for the first 10 to 20 cycles on LG M50 and rising between cycles 30 and 75, so a slope fitted to ten cycles of the surface OCP would see nothing; the predictor instead takes the surface-over-bulk offset at end of charge and lets the set's OCP table say when the shifted stoichiometry leaves the plateau. Median |ln(L(99)/L_DFN(99))|: 5y 0.340 and 0.346, surface 0.128 and 0.130 on `Chen2020@1.5x` / `OKane2022@1.5x` (wins 8/12, 11/12; offset &delta; &minus;0.098, &minus;0.111; still over-predicting, +0.115, +0.101). On the controls the surface reading is worse: `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x` 0.020 to 0.047, `Ai2020@0.45x` 0.077 to 0.229 (&delta; +0.17, +0.21). Only this one form was tried.
- Predictor, fixed now (`experiments/surface/run.py`): per design, &delta; = mean over cycles 2 to 10 of x<sub>surf</sub>(separator node, end of charge) &minus; x1(n<sub>Li</sub> &minus; L), x1 the open-circuit upper-cut-off stoichiometry (5r's balance table); dU<sub>s</sub>(L) = U<sub>n</sub>(x1(n<sub>Li</sub> &minus; L) + &delta;) &minus; U<sub>n</sub>(x1(n<sub>Li</sub>) + &delta;); then 5y unchanged: R = &int; exp(&kappa; dU<sub>s</sub>) dl, &kappa; = 25.3 V<sup>&minus;1</sup>, power law on R over cycles 2 to 10, read at cycle 99, inverted. Inputs: the parameter set, the design's balance, its first ten cycles.
- Fresh pools, fixed now: 32 scrambled Sobol designs (seed 19, used by no milestone; energy-cell bounds) on each of `Chen2020@1.5x`, `OKane2022@1.5x` and controls `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x`, `Ai2020@0.45x`, 128 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, one fresh process per run, 3 workers. Failed runs are dropped and counted.
- H143 (better): the surface predictor's median |ln err| is below 5y's on both LG M50 pools. Refuted if on neither.
- H144 (good enough): at most 0.15 on both LG M50 pools. Refuted if above 0.25 on either.
- H145 (not universal, as dev says): worse than 5y by more than 0.03 on both controls. Refuted if no worse than 5y on either.
- H146 (order): the surface predictor ranks the true cycle-99 loss at Spearman 0.9 or above on both LG M50 pools. Refuted below 0.8 on either.
- Caveats fixed now: two LG M50 pools at one current; &delta; averaged over cycles 2 to 10 at one node; the form was chosen on 5z's seen designs; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Script: `experiments/surface/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5z outcome: on the LG M50 sets the plating slows for the same reason as on `NCA_Kim2011`, the negative electrode's open-circuit potential rising, but at the loaded surface by the separator (+38 mV at end of charge by cycle 99), not in the open-circuit window 5x read (0.5 to 9 mV). Plating is 99.8% of the loss there; a one-node rate index tracks the per-cycle plating change within 0.015. On `Ai2020` only 3% of the recorded loss is booked to plating or SEI. H139, H140, H142 supported; H141 refuted
- Bench: 40 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, 3 workers, about 4 minutes on 4 cores; 1 killed for memory (`Ai2020@0.45x` design 7, exit &minus;9, not retried), so 12, 12, 8 and 7 designs scored. The report was run once, after the bench finished; `experiments/mechanism/run.py` was not changed after the plan.
- H139 supported: plating is 99.8% (median) of the lithium lost by cycle 99 on both LG M50 pools, at least 90% on every design; SEI 0.2%.
- H140 supported: median |ln(index<sub>99</sub>/index<sub>2</sub>) &minus; ln(&Delta;P<sub>99</sub>/&Delta;P<sub>2</sub>)| is 0.015 and 0.014 on `Chen2020@1.5x` and `OKane2022@1.5x` (the per-cycle plating falls by ln &minus;1.23 and &minus;1.18, about 3.3&times;). Reported, not counted: 0.069 on `Ai2020@0.45x`, 0.49 on `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x`, where the single node misses part of the decay.
- H141 refuted: the surface-OCP term carries the change everywhere. Median &minus;&kappa;&Delta;U<sub>surf</sub> is &minus;0.95 and &minus;0.97 on the LG M50 pools, 1.16 and 1.20 times the end-of-charge exponent change (&minus;0.77, &minus;0.73), against 1.03 on `NCA_Kim2011` (&minus;2.70 of &minus;2.64). The surface OCP at the separator rises 37.5 and 38.4 mV by cycle 99 on LG M50 (5x's open-circuit &Delta;U: 0.5 and 8.9 mV), 107 mV on `NCA_Kim2011`, 78 mV on `Ai2020`. Partly offsetting: the intercalation overpotential grows more negative (&minus;7.6, &minus;9.4 mV, +0.19 and +0.24 log units); film and c<sub>e</sub> terms are under 0.07. The charge gets shorter (ln t &minus;0.43), which the index carries apart from the end-of-charge terms.
- H142 supported: every re-run reproduces the committed cycle-99 lithium loss to better than 0.01% (largest |difference| 0.0000 at four places).
- Descriptive, not planned: on `Ai2020@0.45x` the lithium booked to plating is 2.9% of PyBaMM's "Total lithium lost [mol]" at cycle 99 and SEI 0.0%. A 3-cycle check on design 0 (after the report) shows 0.0053 mol lost from the particles against 0.00016 mol to side reactions with SEI + plating on, and none lost with both off. So the loss 5u to 5y fitted on `Ai2020` is mostly lithium leaving the particles without being booked to any side reaction in PyBaMM 26.10.1.1's accounting; on the other three sets the two agree (plating + SEI 98.7 to 100%). Its source is not identified here.
- Reading: the open-circuit window is the wrong place to read the plating feedback on a cell whose graphite is on a plateau at rest. Under charge the separator-side surface runs ahead of the bulk, past the plateau, and lost lithium moves it back down a steep part of the OCP curve; that is a 38 mV lever where the open-circuit window shows 0.5 mV. 5x's mechanism is then one law for all three plating-dominated sets, with the surface stoichiometry at the separator at end of charge in place of the open-circuit x1. The next step is to predict that surface shift from the first ten cycles (it is observed in each of them) and rerun 5x/5y's predictor with it, pre-registered on fresh LG M50 pools. On `Ai2020` the fitted loss is not plating, so the 5u to 5y results there are about a quantity whose physics this milestone has not named; that needs resolving before `Ai2020` counts as a second cell.
- Caveats: 39 designs, all seen at the aggregate level; one node at end of charge summarises a profile in x and t (it misses 0.49 of 4.4 log units on `NCA_Kim2011`); the shares are of the end-of-charge exponent, not of the time-integrated rate; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/mechanism/bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`; tests `tests/test_mechanism.py`.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 5z: on the LG M50 sets, what slows the lithium loss? A decomposition of PyBaMM's plating exponent at the separator-side node, cycle 2 against cycle 99, on re-run designs of committed pools (plan, written before the bench; H139 to H142 are this entry's)
- Question: 5x's parameter-free correction (the plating exponential through the negative electrode's open-circuit potential at top of charge) works on `NCA_Kim2011` and predicts no feedback on `Chen2020` / `OKane2022` (&Delta;U 0.5 and 8.9 mV), yet on 5w's two LG M50 pools the per-cycle loss falls about threefold from cycle 2 to cycle 99 (dev below). The ten-cycle laws miss by &minus;33% (&radic;n) and +44% (power law), so neither carries the shape. To derive a law for these sets the mechanism must be named first.
- Dev, seen (stdlib, the 13 committed 100-cycle pools, scratch scripts not committed): fitted to cycles 2 to 10 and read at cycle 99, an exponential decay of the per-cycle loss gives a median |ln err| of 0.155 and 0.144 on the LG M50 pools (signed &minus;0.13, &minus;0.12) but 0.21 to 0.34 elsewhere; a linear state feedback dL/dn = a + cL gives the same on LG M50. A charge-throughput feedback (the plating rate scaled by the cycle's charge capacity, Q(L) fitted linearly to cycles 1 to 10, combined with or without 5x's OCP factor, power law on the transformed loss) is badly wrong there: &minus;0.74 and &minus;0.79 signed, and 0.12 to 0.23 worse than 5y on `Ai2020`. The per-cycle loss divided by the cycle's charge falls by half from cycle 2 to 99 on LG M50, so throughput is at most a part. A 3-cycle smoke run on `Chen2020@1.5x` design 0 shows the separator-node intercalation overpotential shrinking by about 0.4 mV per cycle at end of charge while the surface OCP moves 0.05 mV.
- Bench, fixed now (`experiments/mechanism/run.py`): DFN (SEI solvent-diffusion limited + irreversible plating, as every 100-cycle milestone) to cycle 100 on committed designs, one fresh process per run: 12 from each LG M50 pool of 5w (`Chen2020@1.5x`, `OKane2022@1.5x`) and controls, 8 from 5w's `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x` (where 5x works) and 8 from 5v's `Ai2020@0.45x` (where it half-works); design indices drawn with `random.Random(18)` from 0 to 63. Recorded per cycle: lithium lost to SEI and to plating, the charge time, and at the separator-side node of the negative electrode at end of charge the surface potential difference &Delta;&phi;, the surface open-circuit potential U, the intercalation overpotential &eta;<sub>int</sub> = &Delta;&phi; &minus; U, the SEI film overpotential &eta;<sub>SEI</sub> and c<sub>e</sub>; and a rate index &int; c<sub>e</sub> exp(&minus;&kappa;(&Delta;&phi; + &eta;<sub>SEI</sub>)) dt over the charge at that node (PyBaMM's irreversible plating current, j0 &prop; c<sub>e</sub>, &kappa; = &alpha;F/RT = 25.3 V<sup>&minus;1</sup>, &alpha; = 0.65 from OKane2022).
- H139 (plating, not SEI): plating is at least 90% of the lithium lost by cycle 99 on every LG M50 design. Refuted if under 75% on either pool's median. (5p found SEI negligible over 20 cycles; 100 cycles of &radic;t SEI could change that, and SEI would be a self-limiting term of its own.)
- H140 (a one-node summary is enough): median |ln(index<sub>99</sub>/index<sub>2</sub>) &minus; ln(&Delta;P<sub>99</sub>/&Delta;P<sub>2</sub>)| at most 0.20 on both LG M50 pools, &Delta;P the per-cycle plating loss. Refuted above 0.40 on either.
- H141 (the mechanism differs by set): the surface-OCP term &minus;&kappa;&Delta;U is under half of the end-of-charge exponent change (OCP + &eta;<sub>int</sub> + film + ln c<sub>e</sub> terms) on both LG M50 pools and over half on `NCA_Kim2011`. Refuted if over half on both LG M50 pools or under half on `NCA_Kim2011`.
- H142 (reproduction): each re-run's cycle-99 lithium loss is within 1% of the committed run of the same design. Refuted above 5% on any.
- Caveats fixed now: 40 designs, all seen at the aggregate level; end-of-charge values at one node summarise a profile in x and t; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only. This milestone names a mechanism; it does not yet give a predictor.
- Script: `experiments/mechanism/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5y outcome: on three fresh pools the plating feedback, with no constant fitted, gets the 100-cycle lithium loss to within 2.4% and 3.8% on `NCA_Kim2011` and 10.2% on `Ai2020` (the uncorrected ten-cycle law: 31%, 30%, 18%), and one law (a power law on R) replaces 5x's per-pool choice. But on `Ai2020` the smaller error ranks worse than 5x's larger, uniform one, and on `NCA_Kim2011` raw cycle-10 energy still ranks best. H135, H137, H138 supported; H136 mixed
- Bench: 192 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, Sobol seed 17, 2 workers, about 55 minutes on 4 cores; no solver drops and no memory kills, so 64 designs per pool (8 calibration, 56 held out for ranks). The report was run once, after the bench finished; `experiments/rlaw/run.py` was not changed after the plan.
- H135 supported: median |ln(L_R(99) / L_DFN(99))| 0.102, 0.038, 0.024 on `Ai2020@0.55x`, `NCA_Kim2011@0.75x`, `@1x`, below the uncorrected law's 0.178, 0.297, 0.311 on all three (sign p = 0.25).
- H136 mixed: at most 0.10 on 2 of 3; `Ai2020@0.55x` is 0.102 (signed +0.102, an over-prediction, as on all six dev pools), above the bar and below the 0.15 refutation line.
- H137 supported: its map ranks the held-out DFN@100 Wh/kg at 0.875, 0.819, 0.933, above 5n's &radic;cycle energy fit (0.739, &minus;0.080, &minus;0.561) on all three, and above the one-constant map calibrated on 8 hundred-cycle runs on both `NCA_Kim2011` pools (0.623, 0.839). On `Ai2020` it trails the one-constant map (0.958), as dev predicted.
- H138 supported: equal to 5x on both `NCA_Kim2011` pools (5u's selector picks the power law there, so the two predictors coincide) and better on `Ai2020` (0.102 against 0.323; 5x signed &minus;0.323, the double count replicated on a fresh pool).
- Descriptive (in the plan's table): raw cycle-10 Wh/kg ranks the 100-cycle energy at 0.947, 0.933, 0.958, at or above every fade-aware list on all three pools (best fade-aware: 5x 0.974 on `Ai2020`, power-on-R 0.819 and 0.933 on `NCA_Kim2011`); the map fed DFN's own loss ranks at 0.96 on all three. The power-on-R loss itself ranks the true cycle-99 loss at 0.984 to 0.999. Median DFN fade from cycle 10 to 100 is 0.44 on `Ai2020@0.55x` and 0.32 to 0.34 on `NCA_Kim2011`; median lost fraction of lithium 0.30 and 0.44 to 0.48.
- Reading: the fresh `NCA_Kim2011` pools confirm 5x out of sample at two currents no milestone ran to cycle 100: the plating law's own exponential, with &alpha; and T from the parameter set and nothing fitted, takes a 30% error to 2 to 4% and outranks an 8-run calibration. On `Ai2020` the double count goes away when the law on R is the power law, so one rule now serves both sets, but the size gain does not buy ranking: 5x's 32% under-prediction is nearly uniform across designs and the map, which 5v found amplifies how the error varies between designs, ranks better with it (0.974) than with a 10% error that varies (0.875). Size and order are separate targets, and a fade forecast and a design screen should not be scored on one number. On these pools the cycle-10 energy alone already carries the 100-cycle order (5w's limit again), so none of this tests a screen; that needs pools where fade reorders designs after cycle 10.
- Caveats: three pools on two sets (sign p = 0.25); the law form was chosen on the ten seen pools; LG M50 sets left out (dev: R carries no feedback there and the power law over-predicts by 35%); SEI treated as plating; open-circuit x1; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/rlaw/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rlaw.svg`; tests `tests/test_rlaw.py`.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5y: with the plating feedback taken out, does one law (a power law fitted to R over cycles 2 to 10, on every pool) predict the 100-cycle lithium loss on fresh pools with no constant fitted and no per-pool law choice? (plan, written before any fresh run; H135 to H138 are this entry's)
- Question: 5x's parameter-free correction fixed `NCA_Kim2011` (power law) but double-counted on `Ai2020`, where 5u's selector picks a + b&radic;n, a curve that already bends like the feedback. Its next step asks for a fit with the feedback present from cycle 1. R is that quantity: if the plating feedback is the only reason the loss decelerates, R should follow a feedback-free law whose shape does not need choosing per pool.
- Dev, seen (`python experiments/rlaw/run.py dev`, stdlib, the ten committed 100-cycle pools; three forms were tried on R: linear, a + b&radic;n and a n<sup>b</sup>, so this is a choice made on seen data): the power law on R gives a median |ln(L / L_DFN)| at cycle 99 of 0.080 to 0.105 on the six `Ai2020` pools (signed +0.08 to +0.105; 5x 0.30 to 0.32; uncorrected law 0.11 to 0.18), 0.056 and 0.031 on `NCA_Kim2011` (equal to 5x, which already used the power law there), and 0.35 to 0.36 on `Chen2020` / `OKane2022` (law 0.33, 0.34; over-predicting by 35%, where both laws on L under-predict). Linear R: 0.18 to 0.21 on `Ai2020`; &radic;n R is 5x. On ranks the gain does not follow: the power-on-R map ranks held-out DFN@100 Wh/kg at 0.78 to 0.86 on `Ai2020` (5x 0.80 to 0.98, one-constant map 0.89 to 0.98, sqrt fit 0.58 to 0.72) and 0.69, 0.91 on `NCA_Kim2011` (one-constant 0.51, 0.76). So on `Ai2020` a smaller median error ranks worse than 5x's larger one, which is uniform in sign; the map is sensitive to how the error varies across designs, not to its median (5v's conditioning result).
- Predictor, fixed now (`experiments/rlaw/run.py`): per design, 5x's R(L) table (&kappa; = 25.3 V<sup>&minus;1</sup>, the open-circuit upper cut-off, the set's committed OCP), the power law a n<sup>b</sup> fitted to R over cycles 2 to 10, read at cycle 99, inverted to L, fed to 5s's map. No constant is fitted and no law is chosen; every input is the parameter set, the design's balance and its first ten cycles.
- Fresh pools, fixed now: 64 scrambled Sobol designs (seed 17, used by no milestone; energy-cell bounds) on `Ai2020@0.55x` (a current no milestone ran to cycle 100), `NCA_Kim2011@0.75x` and `NCA_Kim2011@1x` (run to cycle 10 only, on other designs, in 5n), 192 designs. DFN with SEI + plating to cycle 100 with the lithium curve, one fresh process per run, 2 workers. Designs that fail before cycle 100 are dropped and counted. Balance tables were built (no simulation) before this entry.
- Comparators: the uncorrected pool law (5u), 5x (pool law on R), the one-constant map (5w, 8 calibration designs with `random.Random(0)`), 5n's &radic;cycle energy fit, raw cycle-10 Wh/kg. Ranks are on 5w's held-out split on every pool.
- H135 (size, against the law): median |ln(L_R(99) / L_DFN(99))| below the uncorrected law's on all 3 pools. Refuted if on 1 or fewer.
- H136 (size, absolute): that median at most 0.10 on all 3. Refuted if above 0.15 on any.
- H137 (ranks): its map ranks the held-out DFN@100 Wh/kg above the sqrt fit on all 3 and within 0.05 of (or above) the one-constant map on both `NCA_Kim2011` pools. Refuted if above the sqrt fit on 1 or fewer. Dev expects it to trail the one-constant map on `Ai2020`, so that pool is reported, not counted, for the second part.
- H138 (one law, not two): no more than 0.03 worse than 5x on size on all 3 and better than 5x on the `Ai2020` pool. Refuted if within 0.03 on 1 or fewer, or not better on `Ai2020`.
- Caveats fixed now: three pools on two sets (3 of 3 gives sign-test p = 0.25); the law form was chosen on the ten seen pools; the LG M50 sets are left out because dev says R carries no feedback there (5x) and the power law over-predicts by 35%; lost lithium includes SEI, treated as plating; open-circuit x1; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Script: `experiments/rlaw/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5x outcome: the plating law's own exponential, with no constant fitted, gets the 100-cycle lithium loss right on `NCA_Kim2011` (median error 5.6% and 3.1%, against the ten-cycle law's 27% and 30%) and orders the cell sets' self-limiting slopes correctly (Spearman 0.92 over ten pools), but it double-counts on `Ai2020` (under-predicts the loss by 26 to 28%) and predicts no feedback on the LG M50 sets, whose graphite sits on its plateau at top of charge. H134 supported; H131, H132, H133 refuted
- Bench: none. Stdlib, 19 s on the ten committed 100-cycle pools (5u, 5v, 5w; 622 designs). The report was run once; `experiments/derived/run.py` was not changed after it.
- H131 refuted: the derived loss is closer to DFN than the uncorrected law on 2 of 10 pools, the two `NCA_Kim2011` pools (median |ln err| 0.056 and 0.031 against 0.275 and 0.303). On the six `Ai2020` pools it is worse (0.30 to 0.32 against 0.11 to 0.18); on `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` it is the law (0.33, 0.40 against 0.33, 0.34).
- H132 refuted: at most 0.06 on 2 of 10 (both `NCA_Kim2011`).
- H133 refuted on the bar, supported on the order: the derived slope is within 0.15 of the fitted one on 1 of 10 (`NCA_Kim2011@0.875x`, 0.60 against 0.71; `@0.625x` 0.63 against 0.81), but the Spearman of derived against fitted slope over the ten pools is 0.915. Derived: 0.29 to 0.33 on `Ai2020` (fitted 0.57 to 0.60), 0.02 and &minus;0.01 on `Chen2020` / `OKane2022` (fitted 0.35, 0.34).
- H134 supported: on 5w's held-out split the derived map ranks the 100-cycle DFN energy above 5n's &radic;cycle fit on 10 of 10 pools and within 0.05 of the one-constant calibrated map on 8 of 10 (the two misses: `Ai2020@0.3x` 0.800 against 0.886, `@0.35x` 0.867 against 0.943). It beats the one-constant map on both `NCA_Kim2011` pools (0.692 against 0.514, 0.911 against 0.758) and needs no 100-cycle run.
- Descriptive (in the plan's table): the median rise of the negative electrode's open-circuit potential at top of charge by cycle 99 is 38 to 42 mV on `Ai2020`, 132 to 161 mV on `NCA_Kim2011`, 0.5 mV on `Chen2020` and 8.9 mV on `OKane2022`, so the feedback factor exp(&minus;&kappa;&Delta;U) is about 0.36, 0.02 to 0.04 and 0.99 to 0.80.
- Post hoc (after the verdicts, read off `summary.json`): the signed median ln(L / L_DFN) is &minus;0.30 to &minus;0.32 derived against &minus;0.10 to +0.05 for the law on `Ai2020`, so there the &radic;n law fitted to cycles 2 to 10 already carries most of the feedback in its curvature and the derivation applies it twice; on `NCA_Kim2011` the power law over-predicts (+0.28, +0.30) and the derivation removes it (&minus;0.06, &minus;0.03); on `Chen2020` / `OKane2022` both under-predict by about 30% (5t's finding), so what slows loss there is not this term, and the loss is larger than either law says.
- Reading: the self-limiting correction is, on `NCA_Kim2011`, PyBaMM's plating exponential acting through the negative electrode's OCP rise at top of charge: a parameter-free prediction fixes a 30% error and outranks a calibration that costs 8 hundred-cycle runs. On `Ai2020` the mechanism has the right sign and order (slope 0.3 against 0.6) but the law already absorbs part of it, so the next step is to fit the law to R with the feedback present from cycle 1 (a joint fit, not a transform after a free curvature), or to let the law's exponent be fixed by the set. On the LG M50 sets the plateau leaves no OCP lever, and the fitted slope there (0.35) needs another mechanism (the positive electrode's top-of-charge shift, or SEI); that is now a testable claim, not a calibration.
- Caveats: ten pools, all seen at the aggregate level (their fitted slopes were in 5v and 5w); lost lithium includes SEI growth, treated as plating; open-circuit x1 at the cut-off, no charge overpotential; OKane2022's plating parameters on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/derived/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `derived.svg`; tests `tests/test_derived.py` (closed form on linear OCPs, recovery of a self-limited power law, no-feedback limit).

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5x: can the correction's slope be derived instead of fitted? The plating law's own exponential with the negative electrode's OCP rise at top of charge, no constant fitted, on the ten committed 100-cycle pools (plan, written before the derived predictor was computed on any design; H131 to H134 are this entry's)
- Question: 5w found the self-limiting slope S is cell-specific (pooled fits 0.34 to 0.37 on `Chen2020` / `OKane2022`, 0.58 on `Ai2020`, 0.71 to 0.90 on `NCA_Kim2011`) and asked for a derivation with those values as its test. A practitioner with a new cell would then need no 100-cycle calibration runs for the slope.
- Mechanism, fixed now: PyBaMM 26.10.1.1's irreversible plating current is j0 exp(&minus;&alpha; F (&Delta;&phi; + &eta;_SEI) / RT) with &alpha; = 0.65 (OKane2022's lithium plating transfer coefficient, used on every set since 5b) and T = 298.15 K (every set's ambient). Lost lithium lowers the negative electrode's stoichiometry at the upper cut-off, x1(n_Li &minus; L) &lt; x1(n_Li), so its open-circuit potential at the end of charge rises by &Delta;U(L) = U_n(x1(n_Li &minus; L)) &minus; U_n(x1(n_Li)), and to first order &Delta;&phi; rises with it. So dL/dn = r(n) exp(&minus;&kappa; &Delta;U(L)), &kappa; = &alpha;F/RT = 25.3 V<sup>&minus;1</sup>. With R = &int;<sub>0</sub><sup>L</sup> exp(&kappa; &Delta;U(l)) dl, dR/dn = r(n): R is the loss the cell would have had without the feedback, which the pool's law (5u's choice) should extrapolate, and L(99) follows by inverting R.
- Predictor, fixed now (`experiments/derived/run.py`, stdlib): per design, map each end-of-cycle loss of cycles 2 to 10 to R through the design's own balance (x1 from 5r's `window` at the open-circuit upper cut-off, U_n from the set's committed OCP table), fit the pool's law to R, read R at cycle 99, invert to L_der(99), and feed 5s's map unchanged. Nothing is fitted to any 100-cycle number; every input is the parameter set, the design's balance and its first ten cycles.
- Pools: the ten committed 100-cycle pools, all seen (their pooled S values are in 5v and 5w): 5u `Ai2020` 0.3&times;, 0.4&times;, 0.5&times;, `NCA_Kim2011@0.625x`; 5v `Ai2020` 0.35&times;, 0.45&times;, 0.6&times;; 5w `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x`, `Chen2020@1.5x`, `OKane2022@1.5x`. The per-design derived losses have not been computed before this entry. Ranks against the one-constant map are on 5w's held-out designs (8 calibration designs drawn with `random.Random(0)`) on every pool; the other metrics use every design.
- H131 (size, against the law): median |ln(L_der(99) / L_DFN(99))| below the uncorrected law's on all 10 pools. Refuted if on 7 or fewer.
- H132 (size, absolute): that median at most 0.06 on at least 7 of 10 pools. Refuted if on 3 or fewer.
- H133 (the slope): the derived correction's implied slope (ln(L_law / L_der) = A + S ln(L_law / n_Li) fitted per pool) within 0.15 of the slope fitted to the DFN truth on at least 7 of 10 pools, and Spearman of the two over the 10 pools at least 0.6. Refuted if within 0.15 on 3 or fewer.
- H134 (ranks): the derived map ranks the held-out DFN@100 Wh/kg above 5n's &radic;cycle energy fit on at least 8 of 10 pools and within 0.05 of (or above) the one-constant calibrated map on at least 7 of 10. Refuted if above the sqrt fit on 5 or fewer.
- Caveats fixed now: all ten pools were seen at the aggregate level, so this is a test of a parameter-free mechanism against known pools, not a fresh-pool test; the lost lithium also includes SEI growth, which the feedback does not slow in this form (it is treated as plating); the open-circuit x1 ignores the charge overpotential at the cut-off; plating parameters are OKane2022's on every set (5p); one PyBaMM version; modelled fade only.
- Script: `experiments/derived/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5w outcome: the corrected map's ranking carries to three other cell sets with one constant recalibrated on 8 designs (held-out Spearman 0.76, 0.94, 0.94 against the &radic;cycle fit's &minus;0.42, 0.80, 0.80), but the slope does not: `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` want about 0.36, not `Ai2020`'s 0.577, so the loss size misses the 0.06 bar there (0.087, 0.085). H126, H128, H129 supported; H127, H130 mixed
- Bench: 192 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, Sobol seed 16, 2 workers, about 33 minutes on 4 cores; no solver drops and no memory kills, so 64 designs per pool, 8 to calibrate and 56 held out. The report was run once, after the bench finished.
- H126 supported: held-out Spearman(one-constant map, DFN@100 Wh/kg) 0.758, 0.939, 0.936 on `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x`, `Chen2020@1.5x`, `OKane2022@1.5x`, above the sqrt fit's &minus;0.420, 0.796, 0.801 on all three (sign p = 0.25). Over 50 calibration draws it beats the sqrt fit in 50, 50 and 49 of 50 (worst draw 0.672, 0.923, 0.923).
- H127 mixed: median |ln(L_corr(99) / L_DFN(99))| 0.052, 0.087, 0.085 (bar 0.06; refuted above 0.10). The intercept closes most of the frozen constants' error (0.084, 0.232, 0.253) but not all of it on the NMC811 sets.
- H128 supported: with 5v's frozen `Ai2020` constants that error is above 0.06 on all three pools. Yet the frozen map ranks almost as well as the recalibrated one (0.804, 0.935, 0.932): on these pools a common size error moves every design's loss together and leaves the order nearly intact, where on `Ai2020` (5v) it did not.
- H129 supported, and weak: the union of raw top 4 and one-constant top 4 found the held-out DFN best on all three (gap 0), but so did the raw top 4 alone. The best design is ranked 3rd, 1st and 1st by raw cycle-10 Wh/kg, so these pools do not test the fade list as a screen (the same limit 5t disclosed for its `Chen2020`, `NCA_Kim2011` and `OKane2022` pools).
- H130 mixed: the one-constant map ranks at least as well as the two-constant refit on 2 of 3 (`Chen2020` 0.939 against 0.919, `OKane2022` 0.936 against 0.917; `NCA_Kim2011` 0.758 against 0.813). Over 50 draws its worst case is better on all three (0.672, 0.923, 0.923 against 0.625, 0.851, 0.859).
- Descriptive (in the plan): the pool's in-sample slope S is 0.709 (`NCA_Kim2011`), 0.349 (`Chen2020`) and 0.339 (`OKane2022`), against 0.577 on `Ai2020`. Raw cycle-10 Wh/kg ranks the 100-cycle energy at 0.959, 0.932 and 0.927, above every fade list on `NCA_Kim2011` and within 0.01 of the one-constant map on the other two; the map fed DFN's own loss ranks at 0.964, 0.922 and 0.917. Median DFN fade from cycle 10 to 100 is 0.32 to 0.33 on all three.
- Post hoc (after the verdicts, `/tmp` scratch, not committed): the two-constant refit on the same 8 designs brings the held-out lithium error to 0.069, 0.026 and 0.026, with S = 0.90, 0.37 and 0.36. On the NMC811 sets the ratio L_law / L_DFN rises with the true lost fraction as on `Ai2020` (Spearman 0.88, 0.89); on `NCA_Kim2011` it does not (&minus;0.18), as 5v's dev found (&minus;0.49).
- Reading: for ranking, the correction is portable: one constant set from 8 designs is enough, the slope from another cell does no harm, and a stable one-constant fit is safer than a two-constant one on 8 points. For the size of the loss, which a fade forecast needs, the slope is set-specific (0.34 to 0.37 on the two LG M50 parameterisations, 0.58 on `Ai2020`, 0.7 to 0.9 on `NCA_Kim2011`). That is a reason to derive S from the plating law rather than to fit it, and a test for any derivation: it must give about 0.36 on `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` and about 0.58 on `Ai2020`. On these three pools the cycle-10 energy alone already carries the order, so a screen test needs pools where the best design is not near the top at cycle 10 (fade-limited `Ai2020` currents, or a slower set).
- Caveats: three pools, one per set (sign p = 0.25); the screen part is untested here (best design in raw top 3); `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` are two parameterisations of one cell (LG M50; identical positive-electrode OCP in the tables here, different negative-electrode OCP), not two independent cells; plating parameters are OKane2022's on every set (5p); open-circuit map; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/crossset/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `crossset.svg`.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5w: does 5v's self-limiting correction carry to other chemistries when only its intercept is recalibrated, on 8 designs per pool, with the slope kept from Ai2020? (plan, written before any fresh run; H126 to H130 are this entry's)
- Question: 5v's corrected map beat 5n's &radic;cycle energy fit on three fresh `Ai2020` pools with A = 0.658, S = 0.577 frozen, but on the set the constants came from. Its next step asks whether the correction carries to `NCA_Kim2011`, `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` with constants self-calibrated on 8 designs. A practitioner with a new cell needs to know how many 100-cycle runs that costs and which constant must move.
- Dev, seen (`python experiments/crossset/run.py dev`, stdlib, 50 random draws of 8 calibration designs on each committed 5u and 5v pool, ranks on the 45 to 56 held-out designs): refitting both A and S on 8 designs is unstable, with S from &minus;0.42 to 2.08 on 5u's `NCA_Kim2011@0.625x` pool (held-out map rank median 0.616, worst &minus;0.081) and 0.39 to 0.82 on the six `Ai2020` pools (worst 0.78 to 0.90). Refitting only A with S held at 0.577 gives held-out medians 0.922 to 0.974 on `Ai2020` (worst 0.875) and 0.537 on `NCA_Kim2011` (worst 0.475), against the &radic;cycle fit's 0.59 to 0.75 and &minus;0.15. On `NCA_Kim2011` raw cycle-10 Wh/kg ranks at 0.914, above any fade list. On the 5s diagnostic designs (8 `Chen2020@0.5x`, 6 `OKane2022@1x`; too few to split) the frozen `Ai2020` constants leave a median |ln error| of 0.054 and 0.128 on the cycle-99 lithium loss, and the in-sample slopes are 0.47 and 0.38.
- Predictor, fixed now (`experiments/crossset/run.py`): per pool, 5u's law choice and the cycle-99 law loss as in 5v; 8 completed designs drawn with `random.Random(0)` are run to cycle 100 and set A = mean of ln(L_law / L_DFN) &minus; 0.577 ln(L_law / n_Li); then L_corr and 5s's map as in 5v. Everything is scored on the other designs only (held out).
- Fresh pools, fixed now: 64 scrambled Sobol designs (seed 16, used by no milestone; energy-cell bounds) on `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x`, `Chen2020@1.5x` and `OKane2022@1.5x`, 192 designs; DFN with SEI + plating to cycle 100 with the lithium curve, one fresh process per run, 2 workers. Designs that fail before cycle 100 are dropped and counted. Balance tables were built (no simulation) before this entry.
- Comparators: 5n's sqrt energy fit and union (raw top 4 plus sqrt top 4), 5v's frozen map, the two-constant refit on the same 8 designs, raw cycle-10 Wh/kg.
- H126 (ranks): Spearman(one-constant map, DFN@100 Wh/kg) on held-out designs above the sqrt fit's on all 3 pools. Refuted if on 1 or fewer.
- H127 (lithium size): median |ln(L_corr(99) / L_DFN(99))| at most 0.06 on every pool. Refuted if above 0.10 on any.
- H128 (the intercept must move): with 5v's frozen `Ai2020` constants the same error exceeds 0.06 on at least 2 of 3 pools. Refuted if at most 0.06 on all 3.
- H129 (screen): the union of raw top 4 and one-constant top 4 within 0.5% of the held-out pool's DFN best on every pool. Refuted if above 2% on any.
- H130 (one constant is enough): the one-constant map ranks at least as well as the two-constant refit on all 3. Refuted if on 1 or fewer.
- Reported beside, not counted: the same metrics over 50 calibration draws (median and worst), the pool's in-sample slope, the map fed DFN's own loss.
- Caveats fixed now: three pools, one per set (3 of 3 gives sign-test p = 0.25); the slope is still fitted, not derived; plating parameters are OKane2022's on every set (5p); open-circuit map, which 5t found under-predicts fade on the NMC811 sets; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); the calibration designs cost 8 extra 100-cycle runs per new pool, on top of the union's resumed designs.
- Script: `experiments/crossset/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5v outcome: the map's failure was conditioning, and one self-limiting correction fixes it out of sample. With A = 0.658, S = 0.577 frozen from 5u's pools, the corrected map ranks the 100-cycle DFN energy at 0.92, 0.96 and 0.96 on three fresh `Ai2020` pools (5n's &radic;cycle energy fit 0.59 to 0.67, 5u's uncorrected map 0.61 to 0.82) and gets the cycle-99 lithium loss to a median 4% (uncorrected 11 to 18%). H122, H123, H124 supported, H125 mixed
- Bench: 192 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, Sobol seed 15, 2 workers, about 54 minutes on 4 cores; no memory kills. 3 designs dropped by the solver (IDA failures, 2 on `Ai2020@0.35x`, 1 on `@0.45x`), so 62, 63 and 64 were scored. The report was run once, after the bench finished.
- H122 supported: Spearman(corrected map, DFN@100 Wh/kg) 0.918, 0.955, 0.963 on `Ai2020` 0.35&times;, 0.45&times;, 0.6&times;, above the sqrt fit's 0.670, 0.618, 0.590 on all three (sign test p = 0.25 at n = 3) and within 0.01 to 0.04 of the map fed DFN's own loss (0.961, 0.963, 0.969). The uncorrected 5u map ranks at 0.610, 0.681, 0.816.
- H123 supported: median |ln(L_corr(99) / L_DFN(99))| 0.039, 0.036, 0.040 (bar 0.06); uncorrected 0.112, 0.158, 0.182.
- H124 supported: the union of raw top 4 and corrected-map top 4 found the pool's DFN best on all three (gap 0), as did 5n's union, at 6.3 resumed designs on average against 7.7.
- H125 mixed: a 10% lognormal error on the true loss lowers the map's Spearman by 0.52, 0.37 and 0.19 (bar 0.2 on every pool; refuted below 0.1). The map is ill-conditioned at low current and less so at 0.6&times;, where the fade is smaller (median 0.43 against 0.50).
- Descriptive (in the plan, not counted): the uncorrected law ranks the cycle-99 lithium loss itself at 0.989, 0.976, 0.970 (corrected 0.993, 0.990, 0.989), so the order of the loss was never the problem; its error ratio rises with the true lost fraction (Spearman 0.89, 0.86, 0.79), as on dev. Refitted on 8 random designs per pool run to cycle 100 the constants are A = 0.61 to 0.72, S = 0.57 to 0.60 (frozen 0.658, 0.577) and the self-calibrated map ranks at 0.934, 0.947, 0.961: a user calibrating on a new pool loses nothing measurable here.
- Not in the plan, read off the same table: raw cycle-10 Wh/kg alone ranks the 100-cycle energy at 0.534, 0.884 and 0.976, above the corrected map on `Ai2020@0.6x` (0.963). As fade shrinks with current, the cycle-10 energy carries the order and any fade list matters less; the correction matters where fade decides (0.35&times;: 0.918 against 0.534).
- Reading: 5u's diagnosis was half right. The per-design extrapolation was the problem, but not its ranking: the map amplifies a size error (a 10% random error costs 0.2 to 0.5 of Spearman), and the ten-cycle law's size error is systematic, larger for designs that lose more, as a self-limiting loss would be. A two-constant line in log space removes it, and constants fitted on one set of three pools carry to three fresh pools of the same set, two at currents no milestone ran. The map with this correction is the first ten-cycle fade predictor in the programme to beat 5n's energy fit out of sample on every pool; 5n's union now has a cheaper equal (6.3 designs resumed, not 7.7).
- Caveats: three pools of one set, the set the constants came from; `NCA_Kim2011` needs its own slope (0.81 on dev) and `Chen2020` / `OKane2022` are untested; the correction is empirical, with no mechanism fitted (the slope 0.58 is not derived); plating parameters are OKane2022's (5p); open-circuit map; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/calmap/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `calmap.svg`.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5v: is the balance map's ranking failure a conditioning problem, and does one self-limiting correction of the ten-cycle lithium law, fitted on 5u's Ai2020 pools and frozen, carry to fresh Ai2020 pools? (plan, written before any fresh run; H122 to H125 are this entry's)
- Question: 5u's reading was that the per-design ten-cycle law gets the cycle-99 lithium loss wrong and the next predictor "should be judged on the lithium Spearman first". Dev on 5u's committed pools (seen; `python experiments/calmap/run.py dev`, stdlib) says that is the wrong target: the &radic;n / power law already ranks the cycle-99 DFN lithium loss at Spearman 0.98 to 0.99 on all four pools (the cycle-10 loss alone ranks it at 0.98 to 0.998). The map is ill-conditioned: fed the true loss times exp(N(0, 0.1)) it ranks the 100-cycle energy at 0.38, 0.54, 0.67 and 0.48 (mean of 20 draws) against 0.95 to 0.97 with the exact loss, and a uniform 1.25&times; over-prediction alone drops it to 0.65 to 0.74. So what decides the ranking is the per-design size of the loss to within a few percent, not its order.
- Dev, seen: the law's error is systematic, not noise. L_law(99) / L_DFN(99) rises with the predicted fraction of lithium lost (Spearman with the true fraction 0.93, 0.88, 0.82 on `Ai2020` 0.3&times;, 0.4&times;, 0.5&times;; &minus;0.49 on `NCA_Kim2011@0.625x`): designs that lose more slow down more. One line, ln(L_law / L_DFN) = A + S ln(L_law / n_Li), fitted pooled on the three `Ai2020` pools gives A = 0.658, S = 0.577 (per-pool slopes 0.57, 0.60, 0.59). In sample it cuts the median |ln error| from 0.12 to 0.18 down to 0.026 to 0.038 and lifts the map's energy ranking from 0.64 to 0.76 to 0.90, 0.97 and 0.97 (true-loss map 0.97, 0.97, 0.96). On `NCA_Kim2011` the slope is 0.81 and the pooled `Ai2020` constants give 0.58; the correction is a per-set calibration, and this milestone tests it on `Ai2020` only. Also seen: refitting the law to cycles 2 to 20 (a cycle-20 rung, 5u's other suggestion) narrows the ratio spread a little but ranks the energy worse (0.60 to 0.71 on `Ai2020`), so it is not taken further. 5u's first suggestion, one curvature per pool (the law fitted to the pool's median normalised curve) with a per-design scale (the cycle-10 loss, or a least-squares scale over cycles 2 to 10), ranks the energy at 0.62 to 0.75 on `Ai2020` and 0.37 to 0.40 on `NCA_Kim2011`, no better than the per-design law, because it keeps the same size error. Nothing beyond these numbers was computed before this entry (the dev scripts are in `experiments/calmap/run.py dev` for the correction; the curvature and cycle-20 checks were scratch and are not committed).
- Predictor, fixed now (`experiments/calmap/run.py`): 5u's per-pool law (median ten-cycle exponent &ge; 0.6 takes a + b&radic;n, else a n<sup>b</sup>), read at cycle 99, then L_corr = L_law / exp(0.658 + 0.577 ln(L_law / n_Li)), then 5s's map unchanged (cycle-10 Wh/kg &times; E_ocv(n_Li &minus; L_corr) / E_ocv(n_Li &minus; lost by end of cycle 9), floored at the cycle-9 loss). Only cycles 1 to 10 of each design are read; A and S are frozen.
- Fresh pools, fixed now: 64 scrambled Sobol designs (seed 15, used by no milestone; energy-cell bounds) on `Ai2020@0.35x` (a current 5s ran, new designs), `Ai2020@0.45x` and `Ai2020@0.6x` (currents no milestone ran), 192 designs. DFN with SEI + plating to cycle 100 with the lithium curve, one fresh process per run, 2 workers (5u: three ran out of 15 GB). Designs that fail before cycle 100 are dropped and counted.
- Comparators: 5n's sqrt energy fit (cycles 5 to 10, read at 100), 5n's union (raw top 4 plus sqrt top 4), 5u's uncorrected map.
- H122 (ranks): Spearman(corrected map, DFN@100 Wh/kg) above the sqrt fit's on all 3 pools and at least 0.85 on each. Refuted if below the sqrt fit on 2 or more, or below 0.75 on any.
- H123 (lithium size): median |ln(L_corr(99) / L_DFN(99))| at most 0.06 on every pool. Refuted if above 0.10 on any.
- H124 (screen): the union of raw top 4 and corrected-map top 4 within 0.5% of the pool's DFN best and no more than 0.5 points worse than 5n's union on every pool. Refuted if above 2% on any.
- H125 (conditioning, replicated): a 10% lognormal error on the true cycle-99 loss lowers the map's Spearman by at least 0.2 on every pool (mean of 20 draws). Refuted if by less than 0.1 on any.
- Reported beside, not counted: a self-calibrated variant (A, S refitted on 8 random designs of the same pool run to cycle 100, the cost a user would pay on a new cell set), the uncorrected map, the true-loss map, the lithium Spearman of each.
- Caveats fixed now: three pools (3 of 3 gives sign-test p = 0.25); one set (`Ai2020`), the set the constants came from; the correction is empirical, a line in log space with no mechanism fitted; plating parameters are OKane2022's (5p); open-circuit map; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1).
- Script: `experiments/calmap/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5u outcome: on four fresh pools (256 unseen designs) the per-pool law choice works and the &radic;n law gets the median cycle-99 lithium loss right out of sample (0.94 to 1.05 on `Ai2020`), but the map still ranks the 100-cycle DFN energy worse than 5n's &radic;cycle energy fit on 3 of 4 pools. Fed DFN's own loss the same map ranks at 0.95 to 0.97 on all four, so what is left is the per-design lithium extrapolation (10th to 90th percentile 0.77 to 1.45 of the truth). H121 supported, H118 mixed, H117, H119 and H120 refuted. 5n's union stays the procedure
- Bench: 256 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, Sobol seed 14, about 90 minutes on 4 cores. Three workers ran out of memory (two runs killed by the kernel, exit &minus;9; amended before any result was read: 2 workers, killed runs retried, the predictor untouched). 15 designs dropped by the solver (IDA failures, 11 on `Ai2020@0.3x`, 4 on `Ai2020@0.4x`, the same first-cycle failures 5l and 5n saw below 0.4&times;), so 53, 60, 64 and 64 designs were scored. The report code was run once on the finished `Ai2020@0.3x` pool with its output discarded, to check it ran, before the other pools finished.
- H121 supported: the selector picked &radic;n on the three `Ai2020` pools (b_med 0.86, 0.88, 0.90) and the power law on `NCA_Kim2011@0.625x` (b_med 0.25), as 5s's pools predicted. Median L_law(99) / L_DFN(99): 1.05, 0.99 and 0.94 on `Ai2020` 0.3&times;, 0.4&times;, 0.5&times; (bar 0.8 to 1.25), 1.32 on `NCA_Kim2011` with the power law (5t's &radic;n law was 1.66 there on dev). The law is right on the median on designs nobody had looked at.
- H117 refuted: Spearman(map, DFN@100) above the sqrt fit's on 1 of 4 (`Ai2020@0.5x`, 0.764 against 0.666). `Ai2020@0.3x` 0.641 against 0.667, `@0.4x` 0.662 against 0.745, `NCA_Kim2011@0.625x` &minus;0.352 against &minus;0.157 (both below the raw cycle-10 energy's 0.914 there).
- H118 mixed, H119 refuted: the union of raw top 4 and map top 4 found the DFN best on three pools and missed by 1.25% on `Ai2020@0.4x`, where the best ranks 22nd by the map and 1st by the sqrt fit; 5n's union is exact there and misses by 0.87% on `Ai2020@0.5x` (where the map's union is exact). Excess over 5n's union: +1.25 points at worst (bar 1).
- H120 refuted: median |fade error| from cycle 10 to 100 below the sqrt fit's on 1 of 4 (`NCA_Kim2011`, 0.40 against 0.70); 0.080, 0.103 and 0.123 against 0.075, 0.079 and 0.107 on `Ai2020`.
- Descriptive (in the plan, not counted): fed DFN's own cycle-99 lithium loss the map ranks the 100-cycle energy at 0.974, 0.968, 0.961 and 0.952, with median fade error 0.016 to 0.033 on all four pools, including `NCA_Kim2011`, where every energy extrapolation ranks negatively. 5s's decomposition, checked on 27 designs, holds on 241 unseen ones. The law the selector did not pick ranks worse on every pool (0.28 to 0.43 on `Ai2020` with the power law; &minus;0.49 on `NCA_Kim2011` with &radic;n). The three-list union (raw + sqrt + map, 4 each) is exact on all four pools at 11.0 resumed designs against 7.8 for 5n's; the top 8 by raw cycle-10 energy alone misses by 2.74% and 1.44% on `Ai2020` 0.3&times; and 0.4&times;.
- Post hoc (after the verdicts): the per-design ratio L_law(99) / L_DFN(99) runs from 0.77 to 0.89 at the 10th percentile to 1.28 to 1.45 at the 90th on `Ai2020` (1.19 to 1.50 on `NCA_Kim2011`). Getting the pool median right is not enough: this per-design spread is what separates the map's 0.64 to 0.76 from its 0.95 to 0.97 when fed the true loss.
- Reading: the decomposition is right and now tested out of sample: energy fade at cycle 100 is the electrode-balance map of the lithium lost (Spearman 0.95 to 0.97 on four fresh pools). The pool-level law choice from the ten-cycle exponent works. What fails is ranking designs by a two-parameter law fitted per design to cycles 2 to 10: the curvature that decides the late loss is not identifiable from ten cycles for each design. The next predictor should share information across a pool (one curvature per pool, fitted to the median curve, with a per-design scale) or read one later rung (cycle 20) where the deceleration has begun, and should be judged on the lithium Spearman first, since the map turns a good lithium ranking into a good energy ranking.
- Caveats: four pools (4 of 4 would give sign-test p = 0.125); one Sobol pool per condition; 15 solver drops on the two lowest-current pools; plating parameters are OKane2022's on both sets (5p); open-circuit map; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` not tested.
