- Label, as milestone 1: untrusted = SPMe@100 score 0 or more than 25% below DFN@100. r_min = the minimum over SPMe's 100-cycle run (milestone 2 found r set in the first discharge).
- H29 (H2 as registered): the threshold t* minimising misclassification of untrusted by r_min <= t on `OKane2022`'s 256 designs (milestones 2 and 5a, grid 0.00 to 0.50 step 0.01, ties to the smallest) misclassifies at most 20% of designs on each of Ecker2015 and Ai2020. Refuted if above 20% on either.
- H30 (H1 transfers): ROC AUC of -r_min for untrusted is at least 0.90 on each set. Refuted below 0.80 on either; inconclusive if a set has fewer than 3 untrusted or 3 trusted designs.
- H31 (short beats long): on each set, Spearman(SPMe@10, DFN@100) exceeds Spearman(SPMe@100, DFN@100) with the 95% bootstrap interval of the difference (2000 resamples, seed 0) above 0. Refuted if the point difference is at or below 0 on either set.
- H32 (method ranking): 9 frozen methods (GP-EI at DFN@100; the seven whole-pool screens SPM@2, SPM@100, DFN@2, SPMe@2, SPMe@10, SPMe@30, SPMe@100; cheap-start co-kriging MF-UCB over DFN@2), replayed on 30 pools of 32 designs per set under that set's baseline costs (milestone 4's T1 rule, 1% target). The Spearman between the 9 methods' median T1 on `OKane2022` (5a's fresh designs, pools of 32) and on each set is at least 0.7. Refuted below 0.5 on either; between is inconclusive.
- Also reported, no verdicts: each set's own best threshold (H2's "within a factor of 2" of t*, which is undefined if t* is 0); the r_min distribution; fail counts; Spearman of every cell against DFN@100; the fastest method per set. Caveats fixed now: 64 designs per set; pools of 32 out of 64 overlap heavily, so p-values overstate independence; two sets, both graphite negatives.
- Script: `experiments/transfer/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 5a outcome: on 128 fresh designs the short SPMe screen holds (123 s against GP-EI's 220 s on pools of 64) and the method ranking transfers (Spearman 0.92); the cheap-start search beats GP-EI everywhere but trails the best screen on small pools
- Bench: 384 PyBaMM runs (Sobol seed 1; nearest seed-0 design at normalised distance 0.07 to 0.34, median 0.18). SPMe fails 5 of 128, SPM and DFN none. DFN@100 best 163.5 and median 128.5 Wh/kg (seed 0: 162.3, 128.8). Two DFN children were killed by the session's memory limit at 4 workers; they were dropped and rerun at 2 workers (`run.py` now refuses to record an exit -9 as a model failure).
- Reproduction: the seed-0 replay in the same script gives milestone 4b's and part 2b's medians exactly (GP-EI 220.4, SPMe@10 122.9, cheap-start MF-UCB 144.0 and 173.9 s).
- H25 supported: SPMe@10 screen on pools of 64, median T1 122.9 s against GP-EI 220.4 (p = 2.2e-6) and the SPM@2 screen 205.0 (p = 0.0008).
- H26 supported: Spearman with DFN@100, SPMe@10 0.668 against SPMe@100 0.575; difference 0.093 (95% CI 0.045 to 0.154). SPMe ranks the fresh DFN-best design 2nd at 10 cycles and 48th at 100 (seed 0: 1st and 41st).
- H27 refuted: cheap-start MF-UCB over DFN@2 beats GP-EI at every size (62.4 / 89.6 / 123.6 / 140.6 s against 147.0 / 195.9 / 220.4 / 232.7, p <= 0.0032), but is 1.36 x the best screen at 16 (DFN@2, 46.0 s) and 1.71 x at 32 (SPMe@2, 52.5 s); 1.18 x at 64 and 0.94 x at 96, where it is the fastest method.
- H28 supported: the 15 methods' median T1 on pools of 64 correlate at Spearman 0.924 between seed-0 and fresh designs.
- Extra, after the verdicts (section C): the worst ratio of each method's median to the fastest method's, over the four pool sizes. SPMe@2 screen 1.37 (seed 0: 1.34), SPMe@10 1.40 (1.40), DFN@2 1.44 (1.46), SPM@100 1.58 (1.81), cheap-start MF-UCB over DFN@2 1.71 (1.72); GP-EI 3.73; SPMe@100 8.68. No single method is fastest at every size on either set, and the fastest per size moved (fresh: DFN@2 at 16, SPMe@2 at 32 and 64, cheap-start MF-UCB at 96).
- Reading: what parts 2b and 4b found after the fact survives new designs on the same parameter set. A 2- or 10-cycle SPMe screen stays within 1.4 x of the best method at every pool size, and SPMe's short run ranks DFN's long run better than its own long run does. The cheap-start search is the safe choice only for large pools. Transfer to other parameter sets (H2, milestone 5b) is untested.
- Output: `experiments/fresh/bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `fresh.svg`.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 3 outcome (theory, part 2b): SPMe's error follows -ln of its own minimum salt ratio, but that neither plans nor repairs the search; post hoc, a plain 10-cycle SPMe screen is the fastest yet on pools of 64 and 96 (exploratory)
- Rerun check: 128 SPMe 100-cycle runs reproduce `bench.jsonl` per-cycle energies exactly (max relative difference 0), the same 7 failures, r_min within 5e-7.
- H21 supported: 5-fold CV RMSE on SPMe@100, f ~ a + b s + beta g: g_log 11.34, g_quad 12.31, g_flag 16.80, none 21.27 Wh/kg (log / none 0.53). The four registered forms rank the same way at 2, 10 and 30 cycles. g_eta (extra) 13.25.
- H22 refuted: location model from 16-design pilots, E[R] / observed mean R on pools of 64: SPMe@2 2.64, SPMe@10 9.07, SPMe@30 1.04, SPMe@100 0.71 (2 of 4 within 2x). The location-free model on the same pilots: 2.30, 7.44, 0.85, 0.59. Residual sd after the location is about 7 Wh/kg against a 1% tolerance of about 1.6.
- H23 refuted: online g_log correction over SPMe@100, median T1 528.1 s against SPMe@100 + flag 417.8 s (p = 0.0001; median 12 against 7.5 DFN calls). With no feature the online rule is the plain screen (699.5 s).
- H24 supported by its bar, empty in substance: online over SPMe@2 has median T1 129.4 s against SPM@2's 168.2 s (p = 0.038), but every feature and none give 129.4 s, because the target is found inside the 4 warm-up calls. The win is the plain SPMe@2 screen, which milestone 4 never ran.
- Extra, post hoc (section E), plain whole-pool screens, median T1 at 16 / 32 / 64 / 96: SPMe@2 38.5 / 52.5 / 129.4 / 230.9 s; SPMe@10 49.1 / 73.7 / 122.9 / 172.1 s; SPMe@30 75.4 / 89.7 / 424.2 / 648.6 s. At 64, SPMe@10 against SPM@2, SPM@100, DFN@2 and GP-EI: p = 0.0005, 5e-5, 1e-4, 7e-6 (all faster). SPMe@30 is significantly slower than all of them.
- Extra, post hoc: the DFN-best design (j = 12, r = 0.074) is ranked 8 / 1 / 27 / 41 by SPMe at 2 / 10 / 30 / 100 cycles. Without it, mean DFN calls on pools of 64: SPMe@10 1.10, SPMe@2 1.13, DFN@2 1.13, DFN@10 1.30, SPMe@30 1.60, SPMe@100 2.07, SPM@100 3.90, SPM@2 4.67. r is set in the first discharge (median 0.297 and 10th percentile 0.038 at every cycle count).
- Reading: the physics says where SPMe errs (salt-depleted designs, in a -ln r shape), but the residual left over is too large to plan from, and a 4-point online fit cannot use it. A practitioner gets more from a short SPMe run than from correcting a long one. Next: pre-register the short-SPMe screens on fresh designs and other parameter sets, and explain why SPMe's 10-cycle ranking tracks DFN's 100-cycle ranking.
- Output: `experiments/location/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `location.svg`, `spme_cycles.jsonl`; `THEORY.md` part 2b.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 3 (theory, part 2b): can SPMe's own salt-depletion output say *where* its error falls, well enough to plan the DFN budget and to correct the screen? (plan, written before the run; H21 to H24 are this entry's)
- Question: part 2a found that no independent-noise model predicts SPMe's screening cost (it under-predicts SPMe@30 and SPMe@100 by 3.6x and 4.4x), because SPMe's large residuals sit on particular designs, and asked for the residual's location from the physics. Here the location is modelled from SPMe's own solution and tested three ways: as a fit, as a planner, and as a correction to the screen.
- Mechanism (to write out in `THEORY.md`, part 2b; a scaling argument, not a theorem): in PyBaMM 26.10's SPMe the concentration overpotential is chi RT/F times the difference of the x-averages of log c_e over the two electrodes (`electrolyte_conductivity.Composite`, log clipped at a small tolerance), with one reaction rate for the whole electrode. As the cheap solution's minimum salt ratio r = min c_e / c_e0 falls to zero, that term grows like -ln r and pulls the voltage to the 2.5 V cut-off early. The DFN has the same log, but its reaction moves away from the depleted region, so its voltage does not follow ln of the minimum. So SPMe's extra energy loss, and hence its affine residual e = f - (a s + b), should scale with g_log(r) = -ln max(r, 0.01), not with the asymptotic order (1 - r)^2 of the expansion's correction.
- Data: cached scores (`experiments/cycles/bench.jsonl`, `cost.json`, milestone 4's 30 pools of 64). One new PyBaMM pass: SPMe, 100 cycles, on the same 128 designs, same protocol and code path as `cycles/run.py`, recording r_min per cycle (and the most negative x-averaged concentration overpotential per cycle) so that SPMe@n's feature comes from its own first n cycles; its per-cycle energies must reproduce `bench.jsonl` (check reported). The 7 designs whose SPMe run fails have r = 0 by convention (clipped to 0.01).
- Features, fixed now: g_log = -ln max(r, 0.01) (primary); g_quad = (1 - r)^2; g_flag = 1[r <= 0.1] (milestone 1's flag); g_eta = -min eta_c in volts (extra, no verdict).
- H21 (form): on SPMe@100 over all 128 designs, 5-fold cross-validated RMSE (folds fixed by `random.Random(0)`) of the regression f ~ a + b s + beta g: g_log beats g_quad and g_flag, and is at most 0.7 of the regression with no g. Refuted if g_quad or g_flag is lower than g_log. Not blind: part 1's H13 already found Spearman(e, r) = -0.82.
- H22 (planning): the location model e = beta g_log + N(0, sigma0^2), with a, b, beta, sigma0 fitted on a pilot of 16 random designs (20 pilots, median prediction), gives E[R] by part 2a's quadrature with per-design means f - beta g_log. On pools of 64, within a factor of 2 of the observed mean R on at least 3 of the 4 SPMe cells (2, 10, 30, 100 cycles). Refuted if 2 or fewer. Same convention as part 2a: the formula sees the pool's true f; only the residual model is fitted on the pilot.
- H23 (corrected screen, SPMe@100): online corrected screen-then-verify: screen the whole pool, verify in descending s for the first 4 DFN@100 calls, then refit f ~ a + b s + beta g_log by least squares on the verified designs after every call and verify the unverified design with the largest prediction. On pools of 64 under milestone 4's costs, median T1 below SPMe@100 + flag's (417.8 s), paired Wilcoxon p < 0.05. Refuted if its median T1 is higher.
- H24 (corrected screen, cheap rung): the same online correction over SPMe@2 (with its 2-cycle r) has median T1 at most the SPM@2 screen's 168.2 s on pools of 64. Refuted if above 1.2 x 168.2 s.
- Also reported, no verdicts: the same online rule with g_quad, g_flag and no g (ablations) on every SPMe cell; GP-EI at DFN@100 as reference (220.4 s); the pool sweep 16, 32, 64, 96 for the best corrected cell. Caveats fixed now: pools overlap, so p-values overstate independence; H21's form was suggested by H13's correlation; one parameter set.
- Script: `experiments/location/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 4b outcome: co-kriging BO as planned loses to GP-EI at every pool size, because its 5 random full-model starts cost more than whole screens; started from cheap runs instead, it keeps pace with the best screen everywhere (exploratory)
- References reproduce milestone 4 exactly on pools of 64 (GP-EI 220.4 s, SPM@2 screen 168.2, SPM@100 153.2, DFN@2 159.6), so the replay and its costs are the same.
- H18 refuted: co-kriging with augmented EI over SPM@2 has median T1 240.3 s against GP-EI's 220.4 s on pools of 64 (p = 0.25). It needs 9.5 DFN@100 calls to GP-EI's 9; its 27 SPM@2 calls cost about 9 s.
- H19 refuted: the best planned variant at 64, MF-UCB over DFN@2 (204.6 s, p = 0.23 against GP-EI), takes 267.1 s at 96 (1.21 x GP-EI, bar 1.1) and 157.1 s at 16 (3.4 x the DFN@2 screen's 46.0, bar 2.0). No planned variant beats GP-EI significantly at any pool size, and all four are significantly slower at 96 (p 0.002 to 0.035).
- H20 supported: charging each design its own measured time (DFN 19 to 67 s a design in the benchmark) keeps the order of the 8 methods at Spearman 0.90 against baseline-cost charging. Every method gets slower (GP-EI 220 to 259 s) and the SPM@2 screen loses its significant lead over GP-EI (p = 0.34); SPM@100 and DFN@2 screens keep theirs.
- Diagnosis: the planned start puts 5 random designs through DFN@100 (122.5 s) before the low rung can say anything, more than half of the best screen's whole T1 on pools of 64. The low-rung calls are cheap (under 25 s a search); the cost is in DFN calls, 7 to 11 for co-kriging against 3 to 6 for a screen.
- Extra, not in the plan, chosen after seeing the above (so exploratory): the same four searches started from 10 random low-rung runs and DFN@100 on the best 2. All four beat GP-EI at 16, 32 and 64 (p <= 0.005), and MF-UCB over DFN@2 at 96 too (173.9 against 220.4 s, p = 0.0003). Against the best whole-pool screen at each size, MF-UCB over DFN@2 with the cheap start is 38.0 / 90.3 / 144.0 / 173.9 s against 46.0 / 67.6 / 153.2 / 199.3 s (wins 18 / 13 / 19 / 18 of 30, p 0.19 / 0.40 / 0.047 / 0.052): a tie with the best screen at each size, while no single screen is best at every size. The rung matters: with SPM@2 as the cheap rung the cheap-start searches are slower than the DFN@2 screen at 16 and 32 designs (wins 8 to 10 of 30, p 0.009 to 0.06). The cost split on pools of 64 (median calls x baseline cost): planned MF-UCB 183.7 s of DFN@100 and 19.5 s of DFN@2, cheap start 122.5 and 21.5 s. Measured against H19's bars it would pass (0.79 x GP-EI at 96, 0.83 x the best screen at 16), but the start was chosen post hoc and its two numbers (10, 2) were not tuned or varied, so this needs a pre-registered test on fresh designs (milestone 5's other parameter sets are the natural place).
- Reading: in a pool replay the cheap rung pays off only if it is allowed to pick the first full-model runs; spending the first DFN budget at random throws away what made screens win. For a practitioner: start a multi-fidelity search with a handful of 2-cycle DFN runs, not with random full runs.
- Output: `experiments/mfbo/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `mfbo.svg`.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 4b: does a co-kriging search that picks design and fidelity each step keep the screens' small-pool lead and GP-EI's large-pool one? (plan, written before the run; H18 to H20 are this entry's)
- Question: milestone 4 found that whole-pool screening with a cheap rung (SPM@2, DFN@2) beats GP-EI on DFN@100 on pools of 16 to 64 but loses by 96, because screening pays per candidate. The proposal's evaluation asks for a two-fidelity co-kriging BO (Kennedy and O'Hagan) as the multi-fidelity baseline; this step runs it, with two published fidelity-selection rules, on the cached benchmark (no new PyBaMM runs).
- Model: autoregressive co-kriging, f_h(x) = rho f_l(x) + delta(x), independent GPs (squared-exponential, isotropic, inputs scaled to [0, 1]) for f_l and delta, constant means per fidelity, signal variance profiled out, length scales, rho and the delta variance ratio by grid maximum likelihood, refit every 5 observations. Low rung SPM@2 or DFN@2, high rung DFN@100. Start: the same 5 random designs as GP-EI at DFN@100, plus those 5 at the low rung.
- Rules: (i) augmented EI (Huang, Allen, Notz and Miller 2006): EI of f_h at x times, for a low-rung query, the posterior correlation of f_l(x) and f_h(x) and the cost ratio c_h / c_l; the (design, rung) pair with the largest value is run. (ii) MF-UCB after Kandasamy et al. (2016): pick x by the upper confidence bound of f_h (beta_t = 0.2 d log 2t, their practical setting); run it at the low rung if sqrt(beta_t) times the posterior sd of f_l(x) exceeds gamma = sqrt(c_l / c_h) times the fitted delta sd, else at DFN@100.
- Replay: as milestone 4 (same `random.Random(s).sample` pools, 30 seeds, T1 = charged seconds until a design within 1% of the pool's best DFN@100 has been run at DFN@100), at pool sizes 16, 32, 64, 96. References re-run in the same script: GP-EI at DFN@100 and the SPM@2, SPM@100 and DFN@2 whole-pool screens. A search stops once it hits the 1% target (T1 does not depend on what comes after).
- Costs: (A) each evaluation charged the cell's median baseline fresh-run cost (`experiments/cycles/cost.json`), as milestone 4; (B) each design charged its own cost, cost_base(cell) x seconds_j(model) / median over designs of seconds(model), from the 100-cycle benchmark timings (`bench.jsonl`, run three at a time, so the ratio, not the level, is used). DFN designs range 19 to 67 s there.
- H18: on pools of 64, co-kriging with augmented EI and SPM@2 as the low rung has lower median T1 than GP-EI at DFN@100 (220 s in milestone 4), paired Wilcoxon p < 0.05. Refuted if its median T1 is higher than GP-EI's.
- H19 (the envelope): the better of the four co-kriging variants (2 rules x 2 low rungs), chosen once on pools of 64 under cost A, has median T1 at most GP-EI's on pools of 96 and at most 1.5 times the best whole-pool screen's on pools of 16. Refuted if more than 10% above GP-EI at 96 or more than 2 times the best screen at 16.
- H20 (cost heterogeneity): under cost B the order of the methods' median T1 on pools of 64 agrees with cost A's at Spearman >= 0.8. Refuted below 0.6.
- Also reported: the 95% bootstrap interval of the median, DFN@100 and low-rung calls to T1, and the paired Wilcoxon p of every method against GP-EI. Caveats fixed now: pools share designs, so p-values overstate independence; the grid of hyperparameters is coarse; the pool replay is not a continuous search.
- Plan change before the outcome run (logged before it): a 2-seed smoke test put the fitted rho at the bottom of the grid (0.6) and the low-rung length scale at its top (0.8), and with 40 DFN@100 points the co-kriging mean predicted held-out designs worse than plain GP (RMSE 5.4 against 2.9 Wh/kg). DFN@100 keeps about 60% of early-cycle energy, so rho below 0.6 is physical. The grid is widened to rho 0.2 to 1.2, length scales up to 1.6, delta variance ratio down to 0.005; the check then gives 7.1 against 11.8 Wh/kg at 10 points and 3.1 against 2.9 at 40 (SPM@2 low rung, 10 splits). Hypotheses unchanged; the smoke-test T1 values (2 seeds) were seen.
- Script: `experiments/mfbo/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 3 outcome (theory, part 2a): an independent-noise model plans screen against GP-EI right on 14 of 16 pool sizes, but cannot rank SPMe, whose residual sits on particular designs
- Proposition written out with proof in `THEORY.md` (part 2a): with independent residuals, E[R] = 1 + the sum over designs outside tolerance of the chance each outscores every design inside it; exact for Gaussian residuals by one-dimensional quadrature. Unit-checked against a closed form, Monte Carlo and the random-order limit (`tests/test_average.py`).
- H15 refuted: with sigma from the whole space, E[R] against the observed mean calls on pools of 64 has Spearman 0.51 over the 11 cells, within 2x on 7 of 11 (bars 0.8 and 8). It predicts about 10 calls for every SPMe cell; observed 1.0 (SPMe@10) to 14.0 (SPMe@100).
- Extra, no verdict: sigma from part 1's top set ranks the cells at Spearman 0.86 (within 2x still 7 of 11); it under-predicts SPMe@30 and SPMe@100 by 3.6x and 4.4x.
- H17 refuted: the design-independent resampled residual (model B) predicts 10.4 calls for SPMe@100 against 14.0 observed (ratio 0.74, registered bar for refutation within 1.5x). DFN@10 and DFN@30 are also over-predicted by more than 1.5x (2.2, 1.7), so the "B within 2x for SPM and DFN" half fails too.
- H16 supported: simulated median T1 = N c_s + c_h median R under the Gaussian model calls screen against GP-EI right on 14 of 16 (screen, N) pairs, against 8 of 16 for part 1's worst-case corollary; median relative error in T1 0.24. Misses: SPM@2 and DFN@2 at N = 96 (predicted wins, observed losses). Optimistic for SPM@2 throughout (70 against 168 s at 64).
- Reading: for screens whose residual is roughly design-independent, sigma near the top and the crowding of the top 1% plan the DFN budget; for SPMe the location of the large residuals (on the best designs at 100 cycles, below them at 10) decides the cost, so part 2b should derive where SPMe errs from the expansion, not how much.
- Output: `experiments/average/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `average.svg`; `THEORY.md` part 2a.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 3 (theory, part 2a): does an average-case noise model predict screen-then-verify calls where the worst-case bound failed? (plan, written before the run; H15 to H17 are this entry's)
- Question: part 1's worst-case corollary (H14) called screen against GP-EI right on only 8 of 16 pool sizes, because one large residual widens its band for every design. Here the screen's affine residual is treated as noise. Proposition (to prove in `THEORY.md`): if the affine-mapped screen is s' = f + e with e independent across designs and density g, and T is the set of designs within tolerance, then exactly E[R] = 1 + sum over x not in T of P(s'_x > max over y in T of s'_y), and for Gaussian e with sd sigma that probability is the one-dimensional integral of phi((t - f_x)/sigma)/sigma times the product over y in T of Phi((t - f_y)/sigma). Ranking only needs sigma relative to the spacing of f near the top, so sigma / (density of f in the top few percent) is the screen's average-case cost.
- Data: cached only (`experiments/cycles/bench.jsonl`, `cost.json`, milestone 4's pools), no new PyBaMM runs. Same 11 screen cells and target DFN@100 as part 1, tau = 1%.
- Residual (fixed now): least-squares affine fit of f on s over all 128 designs, e = f - (a s + b); the primary sigma is RMS(e) over the 128, failures (s = 0) included. Extras, no verdicts: sigma from the fit on part 1's top set S_q, and a robust sigma (1.4826 x MAD).
- Models: G, Gaussian e with the primary sigma (exact E[R] by quadrature); B, e drawn with replacement from the cell's own 128 residuals, independent of the design (2000 Monte Carlo draws per pool). B keeps the residual's distribution and drops its link to the design; observed R keeps both.
- H15 (the noise model predicts calls): on milestone 4's 30 pools of 64, model G's mean E[R] over the pools against the observed mean R, over the 11 cells: Spearman >= 0.8 and within a factor of 2 on at least 8 of 11. Refuted if Spearman < 0.6 or fewer than 6 within a factor of 2.
- H16 (planning): predicted median T1 = N c_s + c_h x median R, with R simulated under G on 2000 pools of N drawn from the 128, against GP-EI's observed median T1 at each N, for H14's 16 (screen, N) pairs (SPM@2, SPM@100, DFN@2, DFN@10 at N = 16, 32, 64, 96). Supported if at least 13 of 16 win/lose calls are right, refuted if 10 or fewer (H14's bar). Also reported against H14's fixed 220.4 s. Not blind: the pools come from the same 128 designs the sweep used, and the sweep's medians were in milestone 4's outcome.
- H17 (design-linked residual): for SPMe@100 the independent-residual model B under-predicts the observed mean R on pools of 64 by at least a factor of 2, because SPMe's residual is largest on the designs DFN scores highest (salt depletion in thick, high-energy designs, H13); for SPM@100 and every DFN cell B is within a factor of 2. Refuted if B for SPMe@100 is within a factor of 1.5 of observed. Also reported: Spearman(e, f) per cell.
- Script: `experiments/average/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry. Part 2b (the salt-depletion residual from the SPMe expansion) stays open.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 3 outcome (theory, part 1): affine error is free, the residual is what costs DFN calls; SPMe's residual is salt depletion
- Theorem written out with proof in `THEORY.md`: if the screen matches the target up to an affine map with worst residual kappa, every design screened ahead of the best is within 2 kappa of it, the DFN calls to 1% are bounded by the designs in that band, and a whole-pool screen costs at most c_h + N (c_s + p c_h).
- C1: no violation on 11 cells x 30 pools (330 checks), plus 200 random instances in `tests/test_theory.py`.
- H11 supported: across the 11 screen cells, the top-25% residual kappa_q ranks median DFN@100 calls at Spearman 0.72; the raw median |s - f| at -0.07. The whole-pool kappa_all gives only 0.53 (single outliers dominate it).
- H12 supported: on the 121 designs SPMe completes, kappa_all is 11.0 Wh/kg for SPM@100 against 42.8 for SPMe@100 (ratio 0.26), with similar raw errors (7.1 against 9.5).
- H13 supported: SPMe@100's affine residual has Spearman -0.82 with its own r_min (lower salt, larger under-score); SPM@100's +0.26. Residual RMS 17.7 against 4.4 Wh/kg. This is the quantitative form of "the SPMe correction hurts ranking": its design-dependent error is the salt-depletion term.
- H14 refuted: the worst-case corollary calls screen against GP-EI right on 8 of 16 (screen, pool-size) pairs (bar 13): it calls SPM screens losers at every pool size (band fraction 0.53, N* about 14) though they win up to 64. Right on DFN@10 at all four sizes. A sup-norm residual is too pessimistic for planning; an average-case model is next.
- Also: SPMe@10 has median R = 1 and top-1 regret 0 on the 128, though SPMe@100 needs 19: the SPMe residual grows with cycling (milestone 2), and the DFN-best design is outside SPMe@100's top 25%, so kappa_q is not a valid bound for that cell.
- Next (part 2): derive the salt-depletion residual from the SPMe expansion, and an average-case R(N) to replace H14's worst-case corollary.
- Output: `experiments/theory/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `theory.svg`; `THEORY.md`.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 3 (theory, part 1): what kind of screen error costs DFN calls, and when does whole-pool screening lose? (plan, written before the run; H11 to H14 are this entry's)
- Question: milestones 2 and 4 found that SPM, whose raw error against DFN is as large as SPMe's (median +7.1 against -8.7 Wh/kg), screens far better, and that every whole-pool screen loses to GP-EI as the pool grows. This step states the theory that should explain both and tests it on the cached data (`experiments/cycles/bench.jsonl`, no new PyBaMM runs).
- Theorem (to write out in `THEORY.md`): pool P, target f (DFN@100), screen s, x* = argmax f, screen-then-verify runs f in descending s. For any u >= 0 and c, let kappa = max over S of |u s(x) - c - f(x)|, where S contains x* and every x with s(x) >= s(x*). Then every design screened ahead of x* has f(x) >= f* - 2 kappa, so (i) top-1 regret <= 2 kappa, (ii) the number R of DFN@100 calls to reach (1 - tau) f* is at most 1 + #{x in P : f* - 2 kappa <= f(x) < (1 - tau) f*}, and (iii) R = 1 when 2 kappa <= tau f*. The best kappa is an affine Chebyshev fit (a linear programme). Corollary: T1 = N c_s + R c_h <= c_h + N (c_s + p(2 kappa) c_h), with p(w) the fraction of the pool within w of the top but outside tolerance, so a screen has an effective cost per candidate c_s + p c_h and loses to a search needing m DFN calls once N > (m - 1) c_h / (c_s + p c_h).
- Diagnostics computed: kappa over all designs (kappa_all) and over S_q = the top q = 25% by s together with the top 25% by f (kappa_q; the theorem applies when x* is in the screen's top 25%, reported), for the 11 (model, cycles) screen cells other than DFN@100, on the 128 and on each pool; raw error = median |s - f|; R per cell over the 30 pools of 64 of milestone 4 (same `random.Random(s).sample` pools).
- C1 (a check, not a hypothesis): bounds (i) and (ii) hold on every (cell, pool) with kappa_all of that pool. Any violation means the derivation or the code is wrong.
- H11 (the residual, not the raw error, predicts screening cost): over the 11 cells, Spearman(kappa_q on the 128, median R over pools) >= 0.7 and exceeds Spearman(raw median |s - f|, median R) by at least 0.2. Refuted if Spearman(kappa_q, median R) < 0.5.
- H12 (SPM against SPMe): on the 121 designs both completed, SPM@100's kappa_all is at most half SPMe@100's. Refuted if SPM's is not smaller.
- H13 (mechanism): the least-squares affine residual of SPMe@100 against DFN@100 (121 designs) is correlated with SPMe's whole-run r_min more strongly than SPM@100's residual is: |Spearman| for SPMe exceeds SPM's by at least 0.2. Refuted if SPM's is at least as large. Not blind: milestone 2 already found r_min flags SPMe's large gaps (AUC 0.994); the SPM side and the residual form are new.
- H14 (crossover): the corollary with p from kappa_q on the 128 and m = GP-EI's median DFN@100 calls on pools of 64 (9) predicts whether each of the four screens in milestone 4's pool sweep (SPM@2, SPM@100, DFN@2, DFN@10) has lower median T1 than GP-EI at pool sizes 16, 32, 64, 96. Supported if at least 13 of 16 calls are right, refuted if 10 or fewer. Not blind: the sweep's medians are in milestone 4's outcome; the formula has no fitted parameter, but the bound is conservative and may call losses that are wins.
- Script: `experiments/theory/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 4 outcome (search replay): a whole-pool DFN@10 screen is slower than plain GP-EI; the cheapest screens win on small pools, and GP-EI catches up by 96 designs
- Replay of 12 methods on 30 pools of 64 of the 128 Sobol designs, each evaluation charged its median baseline fresh-run cost (`experiments/search/results.txt`). T1 = charged seconds until a design within 1% of the pool's best DFN@100 has been run at DFN@100.
- H7 refuted: DFN@10 screen-then-verify has median T1 253.3 s against 220.4 s for GP-EI on DFN@100 (ratio 1.15, pre-registered bar 1/3), and at a budget of 73.5 s (GP-EI/3) it has not finished screening (regret 100%). Its first verified design is always within 1% (one DFN@100 call), but screening 64 designs at 3.6 s costs 229 s, more than GP-EI's median 9 DFN@100 calls.
- H8 refuted by its letter, not in substance: successive halving over DFN cycles reaches 1% at 246.8 s against 253.3 s, lower on every pool (Wilcoxon p = 1e-7), a 2.6% saving. Charging resumed runs only the extra cycles brings halving to 184.8 s.
- H9 supported: the SPM@2 screen (0.33 s a design) has median T1 168.2 s against 253.3 s for DFN@10 (p = 0.0003), and beats GP-EI (p = 0.01). Fastest overall: SPM@100 screen 153.2 s (95% 141.0 to 165.5) and DFN@2 screen 159.6 s, about 30% under GP-EI.
- H10 supported: the trust flag speeds SPMe screening, 417.8 s against 699.5 s (p = 8e-5), because the DFN-best designs are among those SPMe flags. SPMe stays the slowest screen, slower than random DFN at the median (612 s).
- MF-GP-EI (screen score as a fifth GP input) adds nothing on this pool: with DFN@10 it equals the screen (its start is already within 1%), with SPM@2 it is slower than the plain SPM@2 screen (192.7 against 168.2 s), because EI leaves the screen's order.
- Not in the plan, pool-size sweep (30 seeds each): median T1 for GP-EI / SPM@2 / SPM@100 / DFN@2 / DFN@10 screens is 147 / 54 / 66 / 46 / 82 s at 16 designs, 220 / 168 / 153 / 160 / 253 s at 64 and 220 / 240 / 199 / 252 / 368 s at 96. Screening cost grows with the pool and GP-EI's barely does, so whole-pool screening is the right tool only for small candidate sets; a continuous search needs a model on the cheap rung, which is what the BOCA-style baseline (not run yet) should test.
- Caveats: pools are drawn from one set of 128, so seeds share designs and the Wilcoxon p-values overstate independence; costs are one design's timings, so the screens' constant per-design cost hides that thick designs run up to 67 s under DFN.
- Output: `experiments/search/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `search.svg`.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 4: which multi-fidelity search finds the DFN-best design for the least wall time? (plan, written before the run; H7 to H10 are this entry's)
- Question: milestones 2 and 2a ranked single screens; a design loop also pays to screen. On the cached benchmark (`experiments/cycles/bench.jsonl`, no new PyBaMM runs), which search reaches a design within 1% of the best 100-cycle DFN score for the least simulated wall time: plain GP-EI on DFN, a screen with a cheap (model, cycles) cell then DFN verification in screen order, successive halving over DFN cycle counts, or GP-EI on DFN using a cheap screen as an extra input?
- Replay: 30 seeds; seed s draws a pool of 64 of the 128 Sobol designs (`random.Random(s).sample`), so methods are compared paired on the same pools. Each evaluation is charged the median baseline wall time of a fresh run of that (model, cycles) cell from `experiments/cycles/cost.json` (e.g. DFN@100 24.5 s, DFN@10 3.6 s, SPM@2 0.3 s); a design counts as found only once run at DFN@100. Target: within 1% of the pool's best DFN@100. Primary metric T1: charged seconds until the best DFN@100 seen is within 1%. Every method continues until the whole pool is DFN@100-verified, so T1 is finite.
- Methods: (a) random order at DFN@100; (b) GP-EI at DFN@100 (5 random starts, isotropic RBF on the 4 inputs scaled to [0, 1], length scale chosen from a grid by marginal likelihood, standardised y); (c) screen-then-verify for SPM@2, SPM@100, SPMe@100, DFN@2, DFN@10 (screen the whole pool, then DFN@100 in descending screen score); (d) SPMe@100 plus milestone 1's flag: the top 5 by SPMe, then every design with whole-run `r_min <= 0.1` in SPMe order, then the rest; (e) successive halving over DFN at 2, 10, 30, 100 cycles with eta = 3 from the whole pool (Hyperband's most exploratory bracket), every rung charged a fresh run, then the remaining designs verified in order of their last rung's score; an incremental-cost variant (a resumed run pays only the extra cycles) is reported beside it; (f) GP-EI at DFN@100 with the DFN@10 or SPM@2 screen score as a fifth input (input-augmented multi-fidelity GP, after Kennedy and O'Hagan's autoregressive idea), the screen's top design as the start.
- H7 (proposal H3, in wall time): DFN@10 screen-then-verify reaches T1 at median at most a third of GP-EI on DFN's median T1. Refuted if its median regret at a budget of a third of GP-EI's median T1 exceeds 2%.
- H8: successive halving over DFN cycles does not beat the DFN@10 screen. Refuted if its T1 is lower in a paired Wilcoxon test at p < 0.05.
- H9: the cheapest screen, SPM@2 (0.3 s a design), has a lower median T1 than the DFN@10 screen. Refuted if higher at p < 0.05 (paired Wilcoxon).
- H10: the trust flag does not slow SPMe screening: SPMe@100 with the flag has median T1 no higher than SPMe@100 alone. Refuted if higher at p < 0.05.
- Also reported: median and 95% bootstrap interval of T1 per method, median regret at budgets of 60, 120, 240 and 480 s, the number of DFN@100 calls to T1, and the paired Wilcoxon p for every method against GP-EI on DFN.
- Limits fixed now: a pool replay, not a continuous search; costs from one design's timings; BOCA (continuous-fidelity BO) is not run in this step.
- Script: `experiments/search/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 2 outcome (dense benchmark): on 128 unbiased designs 10-cycle DFN beats 100-cycle SPMe at the same cost, and SPM beats SPMe
- Context: this ran in parallel with milestone 2a (#14800, below), neither run seeing the other; 2a asked the same question on the 49 stratified designs, this asks it on a fresh 128-design Sobol sample, so it is the out-of-sample replication 2a's plan change called for. Both agree: DFN@10 against SPMe@100 is 0.977 against 0.834 on the 49 and 0.962 against 0.641 on the 128.
- Benchmark: 128 Sobol designs x SPM, SPMe, DFN at 100 cycles, 384 runs, all committed per cycle (`experiments/cycles/bench.jsonl`). DFN completed all 128; SPMe failed on 7 (5%); SPM on none. Prefix check exact (relative difference 0 for all three models) and the baseline reproduces `energy-cell-dfn` exactly (SPMe 109.14, DFN 116.26 Wh/kg).
- H1 supported: Spearman with DFN@100 is 0.962 for DFN@10 against 0.641 for SPMe@100, a difference of +0.32 (paired bootstrap 95% +0.18 to +0.48).
- H2 supported, narrowly: DFN@10 takes 3.6 s and SPMe@100 3.7 s on the baseline (median of 3, idle machine), ratio 0.98.
- H3 supported: DFN@10 screen plus top-10 DFN@100 verify finds the DFN best of the 128 (162.3 Wh/kg, regret 0%; in fact from k = 1). The SPMe@100 screen misses it at every k up to 20 (regret 1.98%) because SPMe ranks the DFN-best design 41st, scoring it 121.0 (gap 0.25).
- Not in the plan, and the finding to follow up: the plain SPM, which has no electrolyte at all, ranks better than SPMe (Spearman 0.955 at 100 cycles), and still does over the 121 designs SPMe completed (0.984 against 0.769), so SPMe's problem is not only its solver failures. SPM over-scores by a median +7.1 Wh/kg and SPMe under-scores by -8.7, but SPM's error is closer to a uniform shift. SPM is not a safe screen for the single best design (top-1 regret 9.4%, it scores the DFN best at 182.7), only for a shortlist (regret 0 from k = 5).
- Also not in the plan: on this unbiased sample milestone 1's label marks 15 of 128 designs (12%) untrusted under SPMe, against 7 of 49 in the stratified sample; SPMe's minimum salt concentration over the 100-cycle run flags them with AUC 0.994, and `r_min <= 0.1` catches all 15 with 8 false flags. (Milestone 1's flag used a 2-cycle run; this one is the whole run's minimum, so it is a replication of the signal, not of the exact test.)
- SPMe's agreement falls with cycle count (0.736 at 2 cycles, 0.641 at 100): its error grows as degradation accumulates, while DFN's short runs converge to the 100-cycle ranking (0.942, 0.962, 0.980 at 2, 10, 30).
- Plan change (agrees with 2a's): the milestone 4 search uses (model, cycles) as a two-axis fidelity space with DFN at short horizons and SPM as the cheapest rung; SPMe stays only as the source of the trust flag. Milestone 3's theory should explain why the SPMe correction hurts design ranking when it is meant to help voltage.
- Output: `experiments/cycles/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `cost.json`, `prefix.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `cycles.svg`.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 2 (dense benchmark): is the cycle count a better fidelity axis than the model? (plan, written before the run; numbered H1 to H3 within this entry)
- Question: milestone 1 found the cost is in the cycle count (100-cycle DFN 27.9 s, 2-cycle DFN 1.3 s), not in the model. Does a short DFN run rank designs by their 100-cycle DFN score as well as a full 100-cycle SPMe run does, at lower cost? The answer decides which axis the multi-fidelity search of milestone 4 spends its budget on.
- Data: a new paired benchmark, 128 designs from a scrambled Sobol sequence over the `energy-cell` bounds (`scipy.stats.qmc.Sobol`, d = 4, seed 0, `random_base2(7)`), not chosen by any search, so it is not the stratified sample of milestone 1. Each design runs once per model (SPM, SPMe, DFN) for 100 cycles of the `energy-cell` protocol (OKane2022, 5 A discharge to 2.5 V, 5 A charge to 4.2 V, SEI and plating on), one fresh process per run. Each run stores the discharge energy of every cycle, so the score at cycle n (Wh/kg of cycle n, `energy_cell.cell.evaluate`'s rule: 0 when the run stops before cycle n) comes from the same run for n = 2, 10, 30, 100. Prefix validity (an n-cycle run equals the first n cycles of a 100-cycle run) is checked on the baseline before use. The baseline's 100-cycle SPMe and DFN scores must reproduce `energy-cell-dfn`'s 109.14 and 116.26 Wh/kg within 0.5%.
- Cost: wall time of an n-cycle run on the baseline for each model and n, fresh process, median of 3, on an otherwise idle machine (the benchmark runs three at a time, so its own timings are not used for cost).
- Target: DFN at cycle 100 (DFN@100). Designs DFN fails on (score 0) stay in, as ties at the bottom.
- H1: Spearman(DFN@10, DFN@100) is at least Spearman(SPMe@100, DFN@100). Refuted if it is lower by more than 0.05.
- H2 (cost): a 10-cycle DFN run costs no more than a 100-cycle SPMe run on the baseline. Refuted if it costs more than 1.5 times as much.
- H3 (screen then verify): screening all 128 with DFN@10 and DFN-verifying the top 10 at 100 cycles finds a design within 1% of the DFN@100 best of the 128; refuted if the regret exceeds 2%. The same for SPMe@100 as the screen is reported beside it.
- Also reported (no hypothesis): the full grid (SPM, SPMe, DFN) x (2, 10, 30, 100 cycles) of Spearman and top-10 overlap against DFN@100 with bootstrap intervals, and each cell's cost, so the cheapest cell that ranks as well as SPMe@100 can be read off.
- Script: `experiments/cycles/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-11 · Outcome 2a: a 10-cycle DFN run ranks 100-cycle DFN far better than a 100-cycle SPMe run, at the same wall time
- H4 supported: Spearman(DFN at cycle 10, DFN at cycle 100) = 0.977 against 0.834 for 100-cycle SPMe; paired bootstrap of the difference over designs 0.138 (95% 0.057 to 0.271). DFN at cycle 30 reaches 0.990, and even DFN at cycle 2 (0.915) beats every SPM and SPMe rung (best: SPMe at cycle 10, 0.883).
- H5 at the boundary, read as equal cost, not cheaper: median DFN wall time to the end of cycle 10 is 4.0 s against 4.1 s for a whole SPMe 100-cycle run (ratio 0.98, supported by the plan's measure); cycle-end to cycle-end it is 4.0 against 3.7 s (1.07). Not refuted (threshold 1.5).
- H6 supported: DFN-verifying the top 5 by DFN at cycle 10 finds the DFN best of the 49 (regret 0) where the top 5 by SPMe 100 misses by 2.1 Wh/kg. At k = 1 DFN at cycle 10 picks a design 2.5 Wh/kg short (SPMe 100: 2.1).
- The reduced models get *worse* with horizon: SPMe's Spearman peaks at cycle 10 (0.883) and falls to 0.834 at 100, so its degradation path drifts from DFN's. DFN keeps only 58 to 74% (median 61%) of its cycle-10 energy by cycle 100, so the short run ranks well despite heavy fade, not because nothing fades.
- The 7 milestone-1 untrusted designs: mean absolute rank error 4.6 places under DFN at cycle 10 against 16.3 under SPMe 100; the three SPMe solver failures solve under DFN.
- Negative: a linear fade extrapolation of DFN from cycles 2 and 10 to cycle 100 ranks at Spearman 0.01 (early fade is not linear); rank on the raw cycle-10 score instead.
- Reproduction: the SPMe 100-cycle re-runs match the committed `spme_now` scores exactly (max difference 0) and a DFN 100-cycle re-run of design 0 matches its committed 116.256 Wh/kg.
- Plan change for milestone 2 (dense Sobol benchmark): cache DFN per cycle up to 100 (one run gives every rung) plus SPMe 100, and run the search comparison with cycle count as the fidelity axis; the trust flag from milestone 1 stays as a check for searches that do use SPMe.
- Output: `experiments/horizon/results.txt`, `horizon.jsonl`, `summary.json`, `check.json`, `horizon.svg`.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 2a: is a short DFN run a better screen than a full SPMe run? (plan, written before the run)
- Question: milestone 1 found that cycle count, not model reduction, carries most of the cost (DFN 100 cycles 27.9 s against 1.3 s for 2 cycles on the baseline). If the ranking of designs by DFN at cycle 10 already matches the 100-cycle DFN ranking, a short-horizon DFN screen beats the full-horizon SPMe screen the multi-fidelity literature would reach for. This is the fidelity grid the outcome entry below asked for (model x cycles), run before the dense Sobol benchmark (proposal milestone 2) so that benchmark caches the right rungs.
- Data: the same 49 designs, the same protocol and OKane2022 parameters, PyBaMM 26.10.1.1 (as committed). New runs, one process per design and model: SPM 100 cycles, SPMe 100 cycles, DFN 30 cycles, recording the discharge energy of every cycle and the wall time at the end of every cycle (PyBaMM `on_cycle_end` callback, set-up included). Score at cycle n = discharge Wh of cycle n / stack mass, the `energy-cell` score with `cycles = n`; 0 if the run did not complete n cycles. Reference: the committed 100-cycle DFN scores (`energy-cell-dfn/experiments/dfn.jsonl`); a check re-runs DFN 100 cycles on design 0 against its committed 116.26 Wh/kg.
- Comparator: 100-cycle SPMe against 100-cycle DFN, Spearman 0.834 on the committed scores (computed before this plan; it is the milestone 1 data).
- H4 (ranking): Spearman(DFN at cycle 10, DFN at cycle 100) is at least 0.90. Refuted if below 0.834 (no better than full SPMe).
- H5 (cost): the median over the 49 of DFN wall time to the end of cycle 10 is at most the median SPMe 100-cycle wall time. Refuted if above 1.5 times it.
- H6 (screen): DFN-verifying the top 5 by DFN cycle 10 has regret to the DFN best of the 49 no larger than the top 5 by SPMe 100 (2.1 Wh/kg). Refuted if larger.
- Also reported (no hypothesis): Spearman and top-k regret for every rung (SPM, SPMe at 2, 10, 30, 100; DFN at 2, 10, 30), a linear extrapolation of DFN to cycle 100 from cycles 2 and 10, and where the 7 milestone-1 untrusted designs rank under DFN at cycle 10.
- Script: `experiments/horizon/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-11 · Outcome: the flag works (AUC 0.997), but cycle count, not model reduction, is where the cost is
- H1 supported: SPMe's 2-cycle minimum electrolyte concentration (`r_min`) separates the 7 untrusted designs from the 42 trusted with ROC AUC 0.997 (bootstrap 95% 0.980 to 1.000). The 3 solver failures count as `r_min = 0` by the plan; over the 46 designs SPMe solved, AUC is 0.994 (4 untrusted). A flag at `r_min <= 0.10` catches all 7 with 1 false flag (design 30, gap 0.17); at `r_min <= 0` it catches 6 with none.
- H2 supported: Spearman(`r_min`, relative gap) = -0.833 over 49.
- H3 refuted, and this is the finding that changes the plan: a 2-cycle run costs SPM 0.3 s, SPMe 1.2 s, DFN 1.3 s (median of 3, baseline design, fresh process, set-up included), so SPMe/DFN = 0.88. Extra run, not in the plan: at 100 cycles, SPM 1.0 s, SPMe 3.9 s, DFN 27.9 s (one run each), SPMe/DFN = 0.14. Going from 100 cycles to 2 saves 21x on DFN; going from DFN to SPMe at 100 cycles saves 7x. A 2-cycle *DFN* run costs no more than the 2-cycle SPMe flag, so the cheapest trustworthy screen may be short-horizon DFN, not SPMe.
- Replay: DFN-verifying the top 10 by SPMe already returns the DFN best of the 49 (163.0); the top 1 to 5 returns 160.9 (regret 2.1, 1.3%). Adding the flagged designs costs 8 DFN calls and finds nothing better, because the designs SPMe mis-scores are not the DFN optimum in this sample. The flag's value is in not pruning a region, not in the best design here.
- Plan change: milestone 2 adds the cycle count as a fidelity axis (2, 10, 30, 100 cycles x SPM, SPMe, DFN) and asks whether early-cycle DFN ranks 100-cycle DFN as well as 100-cycle SPMe does.
- Output: `experiments/trust/results.txt`, `probe.jsonl`, `cost.json`, `cost100.json`, `summary.json`, `trust.svg`.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 1: can the cheap model flag where it cannot be trusted? (plan, written before the run)
- Question: `energy-cell-dfn` found that SPMe and DFN agree on most of 49 `energy-cell` designs (median gap 9%) but disagree badly on a few (SPMe 0 to 27.6 Wh/kg where DFN gives 120 to 146), and traced the failures to electrolyte salt depletion. A multi-fidelity search needs to know *before* paying for DFN which cheap scores to distrust. The candidate indicator is free: SPMe's own minimum electrolyte concentration, read from a short SPMe run.
- Data: the 49 designs of `energy-cell-dfn/experiments/selected.json` with their committed 100-cycle SPMe and DFN scores (`dfn.jsonl`, not re-run). New runs: each design under SPMe for 2 cycles (5 A discharge to 2.5 V, 5 A charge to 4.2 V; the `energy-cell` protocol and OKane2022 parameters), recording the minimum electrolyte concentration over space and time divided by the initial 1000 mol/m3 (`r_min`), whether the solve failed, and wall time. A solver failure counts as `r_min = 0`.
- Label (fixed now): a design is *untrusted* when SPMe's cycle-100 score is below DFN's by more than 25% of DFN's (`(DFN - SPMe) / DFN > 0.25`), or SPMe scored 0.
- H1: `r_min` separates untrusted from trusted designs with ROC AUC at least 0.90. Refuted if AUC < 0.80.
- H2: across all 49, `r_min` and the relative gap `(DFN - SPMe) / DFN` have Spearman correlation at most -0.5. Refuted if above -0.3.
- H3 (cost): one 2-cycle SPMe run costs under a tenth of a 2-cycle DFN run on the baseline design (wall time, 3 repeats each, one process). Refuted if the ratio is above 0.25.
- Also reported (no hypothesis): the screen-then-verify replay on the committed scores (DFN on the top-k by SPMe, with and without the flagged designs added), and its regret to the DFN best of the 49.
- Script: `experiments/trust/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.
